# Fork audit 2026-06-12 — confirmed findings

Baseline: `8a744c1b31` (upstream Modern.js v3.2.1, last merged upstream commit). 21 area investigations + 4 critic follow-ups; every finding adversarially verified (evidence re-read, fork-ownership re-checked against the baseline diff). 241 confirmed / 5 refuted.

Machine-readable version: `fork-audit-2026-06-12.json`. Severity: CRITICAL = a gate/feature is silently broken today; MAJOR = real defect/debt with concrete blast radius; MINOR = confirmed but contained.

## plugin-tanstack  (11 confirmed)

@modern-js/plugin-tanstack is a working, reasonably unit-tested (codegen/routeTree) integration lane, and the ADR-0017 consolidation genuinely moved all TanStack code here. However the package carries heavy copied-code debt: verbatim copies of plugin-runtime router types/utilities, a duplicated ~100-name TanStack re-export allowlist maintained in two packages, near-identical browser/node plugin bodies, and a ~175-line runtime preamble re-generated into every app's src that duplicates routeTree.ts logic and contains a real redirect bug. The decontamination is also incomplete on the runtime side: @modern-js/runtime still hard-depends on @tanstack/* for the deprecated alias. Several CLI exports are dead experiments, and the sizable RSC composite/slot subsystem has zero integration coverage or in-repo consumers.

### plugin-tanstack#1 [MAJOR/duplication/refactor] Verbatim copies of plugin-runtime router types and route-object builder instead of importing through the existing context seam

**Files:** `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/types.ts:9-78`, `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/utils.tsx:32-162`, `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/DefaultNotFound.tsx`, `packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/router/runtime/types.ts:28-101`, `packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/router/runtime/utils.tsx:77-246`, `packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/context/index.ts:12-23`

**Evidence:** plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/lifecycle.ts:7-16 correctly re-exports the canonical router-state FUNCTIONS from '@modern-js/runtime/context', but the TYPES they operate on (InternalRouterRuntimeState, InternalRouterServerSnapshot, RouterFramework, RouterServerPrepareResult, RouterRouteMatchSnapshot, LoaderFunction*) are copied verbatim into src/runtime/types.ts:39-78 because context/index.ts only exports RouterLifecycleContext/RouterLifecyclePhase. RouterConfig (types.ts:9-25) is also copied including react-router-only vestiges that are dead in a TanStack plugin: oldVersion (line 15) and future.v7_startTransition (lines 20-22) have zero usages in the package. src/runtime/utils.tsx is a line-for-line copy of plugin-runtime/src/router/runtime/utils.tsx getRouteObjects/createRouteObjectsFromConfig/urlJoin/standardSlash (only !! -> Boolean() changed) — including the synthetic '{ path: "*", element: <DefaultNotFound/> }' push (utils.tsx:109-112) that both plugin.tsx:305-317 and plugin.node.tsx:304-316 then immediately strip via stripSyntheticNotFoundRoute. getRouteObjects exists UPSTREAM in plugin-runtime (fork diff shows only 11 changed lines there), so upstream fixes to it will silently not propagate to the tanstack copy.

**Direction:** Export InternalRouterRuntimeState/InternalRouterServerSnapshot/RouterFramework/RouterServerPrepareResult from '@modern-js/runtime/context' (one-line addition to the fork-owned context/index.ts) and delete the copies in plugin-tanstack types.ts. Drop oldVersion/future.v7_startTransition from the tanstack RouterConfig. For utils.tsx, either export createRouteObjectsFromConfig through the same seam, or (better) build TanStack-native route objects directly and delete both the synthetic-notfound push and the two stripSyntheticNotFoundRoute copies — TanStack handles 404 via notFoundComponent (routeTree.ts:944,1025) so the add-then-strip dance is pure waste.

**Verifier:** Fully verified. plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/types.ts:39-96 duplicates InternalRouterRuntimeState/InternalRouterServerSnapshot/RouterFramework/RouterServerPrepareResult/RouterRouteMatchSnapshot/LoaderFunction* verbatim from plugin-runtime/src/router/runtime/types.ts; context/index.ts exports only RouterLifecycleContext/RouterLifecyclePhase as types, so the seam genuinely lacks them. utils.tsx getRouteObjects/createRouteObjectsFromConfig/urlJoin/standardSlash is a line-for-line copy (only !!->Boolean() and an else-branch flattening differ); DefaultNotFound.tsx is byte-identical (diff exit 0). oldVersion and future.v7_startTransition have zero usages in the package (grep-verified). The synthetic '*' push (utils.tsx:109-112) is immediately stripped by identical stripSyntheticNotFoundRoute copies in plugin.tsx:305-317 and plugin.node.tsx:304-316. Upstream-fix divergence risk is real: plugin-runtime/src/router/runtime/utils.tsx fork diff is only 11 changed lines, so getRouteObjects is upstream-owned and the tanstack copy will silently drift. notFoundComponent: DefaultNotFound confirmed at routeTree.ts:944 and 1025, so the add-then-strip dance is indeed redundant. All flagged files are fork-added (A status). Direction is additive to fork-owned files and does not touch upstream hunks.

### plugin-tanstack#2 [MAJOR/architecture/consolidate] Consolidation incomplete: @modern-js/runtime still hard-depends on @tanstack/* and duplicates the ~100-name re-export allowlist plus Link/Form/Fetcher types

**Files:** `packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/package.json:236-237`, `packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/exports/tanstack-router.ts:21-191`, `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/index.tsx:3-126`, `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/prefetchLink.tsx:9-50`, `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/dataMutation.tsx:14-20,315-385`

**Evidence:** ADR-0017's scope note and the tanstack-single-source-consolidation changeset claim 'all TanStack code lives in @modern-js/plugin-tanstack' and the runtime is 'decontaminated'. But the deprecated '@modern-js/runtime/tanstack-router' alias (fork-added file, A status) does value re-exports of ~100 names directly from '@tanstack/react-router' (tanstack-router.ts:21-126), forcing @modern-js/runtime to keep hard deps on @tanstack/react-router 1.170.15 and @tanstack/router-core 1.171.13 (package.json:236-237). Every react-router-only app installs the full TanStack stack transitively, and bundling the alias pulls TanStack code into @modern-js/runtime bundles — only the 6 Modern-specific bindings delegate lazily. The same ~100-name allowlist is independently maintained in plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/index.tsx:4-103, and PrefetchBehavior/LinkProps/NavLinkProps/SubmitOptions/FetcherState/FormProps/Fetcher type definitions are hand-copied into tanstack-router.ts:127-191 — three surfaces that must be edited in lockstep on every pinned TanStack upgrade.

**Direction:** Finish the decontamination: in the next major, reduce '@modern-js/runtime/tanstack-router' to the 6 lazy Modern bindings plus 'export type' declarations sourced from @modern-js/plugin-tanstack (types-only import does not require a runtime dep), and delete the @tanstack/* dependencies from plugin-runtime/package.json. Alternatively drop the alias entirely — the changeset already documents the migration. In plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/index.tsx, replace the hand-maintained named list with 'export * from "@tanstack/react-router"' (local explicit exports like Link/Outlet/Form automatically shadow star re-exports), eliminating the allowlist maintenance.

**Verifier:** Core claims verify: @tanstack/react-router 1.170.15 and @tanstack/router-core 1.171.13 are in plugin-runtime's `dependencies` (fork-added per diff, lines 236-237), so every app installs them transitively; exports/tanstack-router.ts (fork-added, A) value re-exports ~100 names (lines 22-121) so importing the alias bundles TanStack code, with only the 6 Modern bindings delegating lazily via the Symbol.for slot; the identical ~100-name list is independently maintained in plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/index.tsx:4-103; PrefetchBehavior/LinkProps/NavLinkProps/SubmitOptions/FetcherState/FormProps/Fetcher are hand-copied at tanstack-router.ts:127-191 (originals confirmed in prefetchLink.tsx/dataMutation.tsx). One correction: the changeset explicitly documents that the deprecated alias 'still re-exports the @tanstack/react-router surface', so the alias itself is intended behavior, not a hidden seam — but it nowhere acknowledges the hard-dependency cost on react-router-only apps or the three lockstep-maintained surfaces, which are the substantive findings. Direction caveat: type-only imports from @modern-js/plugin-tanstack would introduce a (types-level) dependency cycle since plugin-tanstack depends on @modern-js/runtime; the 'export *' simplification for plugin-tanstack's own index is sound (explicit exports shadow star re-exports).

### plugin-tanstack#9 [MAJOR/testing/move] RSC composite/slot subsystem (~1,070 lines, 3 public export subpaths) has no integration coverage and no in-repo consumer

**Files:** `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/rsc/`, `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/package.json:50-61`, `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/tests/router/rsc.test.tsx`, `tests/integration/routes-tanstack/modern.config.ts`

**Evidence:** The rsc/ subtree (server.tsx 304, payloadRouter.ts 319, createRscProxy.tsx 190, ReplayableStream.ts 156, symbols.ts 106, plus 6 more files) implements a custom server-component proxy system: CompositeComponent slots, dev-only slot-usage telemetry streams, seroval-trap proxies, replayable flight streams, and a payload-router navigation cache. It is exposed as three public subpaths ('./runtime/rsc', './runtime/rsc/client', './runtime/rsc/server') and wired into both plugins behind getGlobalEnableRsc(). Yet none of the three tanstack integration fixtures (routes-tanstack, routes-tanstack-create-routes, routes-tanstack-mf) enables RSC, and grep finds zero consumers of CompositeComponent/renderServerComponent/createCompositeComponent outside the package's own 256-line unit test — no app fixture, no docs example fixture, nothing. plugin.tsx/plugin.node.tsx (611+585 lines of SSR orchestration: redirect/interrupt handling, hydration records, script-tag serialization) likewise have no unit tests at all; only routeTree/cli/codegen are unit-covered. The subsystem also duplicates selectTreePath/EmptyFallback between CompositeComponent.tsx and RscNodeRenderer.tsx.

**Direction:** Either add an integration fixture (routes-tanstack-rsc with enableRsc + a CompositeComponent page, SSR + client-navigation assertions) to make the lane real, or quarantine it: drop the three public export subpaths, mark the API unstable_* and keep only what plugin.node needs internally (the serialization adapters). Shipping an untested 1k-line public RSC API invites breakage on every pinned TanStack/React bump.

**Verifier:** Core verifies, with two corrections that do not weaken it. The rsc/ subtree is actually ~1,420 lines across 12 files (the finding undercounted at ~1,070); the three public subpaths './runtime/rsc', './runtime/rsc/client', './runtime/rsc/server' are in package.json exports (lines 50-61); none of the tanstack integration fixtures (routes-tanstack, routes-tanstack-create-routes, routes-tanstack-mf host/remote) sets enableRsc (grep on their modern.config.ts returns nothing); zero executable consumers of CompositeComponent/renderServerComponent/createCompositeComponent exist outside the package's 256-line rsc.test.tsx. Correction 1: docs DO document the API (docs/{en,zh}/guides/basic-features/render/tanstack-rsc.mdx with code examples importing the three subpaths) — so 'no docs example' is wrong, but a documented public API with no executable coverage strengthens rather than weakens the finding. Correction 2: plugin.tsx/plugin.node.tsx (611/585 lines) have no unit tests as claimed, though their non-RSC SSR paths are exercised end-to-end by the routes-tanstack stream/string fixtures; the RSC lane specifically is the uncovered one. selectTreePath/EmptyFallback duplication between CompositeComponent.tsx and RscNodeRenderer.tsx confirmed (lines 12-16 vs 14-18). Fork-owned; direction (fixture or quarantine) is reasonable.

### plugin-tanstack#3 [MINOR/duplication/refactor] plugin.tsx and plugin.node.tsx duplicate route assembly, type plumbing, and hook registries; runtime plugin uses a third name

**Files:** `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/plugin.tsx:97-114,169-199,305-317,323,348-375`, `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/plugin.node.tsx:69-86,304-316,348,358-401`, `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/hooks.ts:6-16`, `packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/router/runtime/internal.ts:17-46`, `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/cli/index.ts:282,305-309`

**Evidence:** The browser and node plugin files duplicate: stripSyntheticNotFoundRoute verbatim (plugin.tsx:305-317 = plugin.node.tsx:304-316), the TanstackRouterRuntimeConfig/Extends/PluginAPI/Plugin type quadruple (plugin.tsx:97-114 = plugin.node.tsx:69-86), the merged-config + createRoutes/createRouteObjectsFromConfig + modifyRoutes assembly sequence (plugin.tsx:341-375 vs plugin.node.tsx:359-397), and pickRouteModuleComponent is a verbatim copy of routeTree.ts:256-286 at plugin.tsx:169-199. Both register their OWN createSyncHook instances for modifyRoutes/onBeforeCreateRouter/etc (hooks.ts:6-16) — semantically the same hooks plugin-runtime's built-in router registers. Because the plugin reaches apps via two paths (provider registry through built-in '@modern-js/plugin-router' which only calls setup() and uses plugin-runtime's hook instances, vs the CLI-pushed runtime plugin named '@modern-js/plugin-router-tanstack' at plugin.tsx:323 which registers plugin-tanstack's instances), a consumer hooking onBeforeCreateRouter against one registry will not fire when the router is created through the other path. The plugin also answers to three identifiers: package '@modern-js/plugin-tanstack', runtime plugin '@modern-js/plugin-router-tanstack', CLI-injected entry plugin 'tanstackRouter' (cli/index.ts:306).

**Direction:** Extract a shared pluginShared.ts (types, stripSyntheticNotFoundRoute, route-object assembly, getMergedConfig) imported by both variants; reuse routeTree's pickRouteModuleComponent. Make hooks.ts re-export the hook instances from '@modern-js/runtime/router/internal' (RouterExtendsHooks is already exported there) so both code paths share one registry, and align the runtime plugin name with the package name.

**Verifier:** Duplication claims verify: stripSyntheticNotFoundRoute is verbatim in both files (plugin.tsx:305-317 = plugin.node.tsx:304-316), the TanstackRouterRuntimeConfig/Extends/PluginAPI/Plugin quadruple is duplicated (plugin.tsx:97-114 = plugin.node.tsx:69-86), the merged-config + createRoutes/createRouteObjectsFromConfig + stripSynthetic + modifyRoutes assembly is repeated (plugin.tsx:341-375 vs plugin.node.tsx:362-397), pickRouteModuleComponent is a verbatim copy of routeTree.ts:256-286, hooks.ts:6-16 creates its own createSyncHook instances, and the three identifiers exist ('@modern-js/plugin-tanstack' cli/index.ts:282, '@modern-js/plugin-router-tanstack' plugin.tsx:323/plugin.node.tsx:348, 'tanstackRouter' cli/index.ts:306). However the headline hook-registry misfire claim is REFUTED: @modern-js/plugin merges registryHooks by NAME into a single per-run extendsHooks map (toolkit/plugin/src/runtime/context.ts:29-35), and both consumer taps (initPluginAPI wires api.<hookName> to extendsHooks[name].tap) and provider calls (plugin.tsx uses api.getHooks()) resolve through that same map — within any run there is exactly one live instance per hook name. Moreover the two paths are mutually exclusive per entry: plugin-runtime/src/router/cli/index.ts:143-154 skips injecting the built-in 'router' plugin for plugin-owned route entrypoints (tanstack entries carry routesOwner '@modern-js/plugin-tanstack'). Neither package publicly exports the hook instances for direct tapping. So no consumer-visible misfire exists; the dual instances are only a latent hazard. With the correctness sub-claim gone this is a maintainability/duplication finding — downgraded to minor. Direction correction: '@modern-js/runtime/router/internal' exports only the RouterExtendsHooks TYPE, not the instances; plugin-runtime would have to export the instances first (fork-owned hunk, safe).

### plugin-tanstack#4 [MINOR/correctness/rewrite] Generated router.gen.ts embeds a ~175-line runtime preamble that duplicates routeTree.ts logic and contains a broken absolute-redirect handler

**Files:** `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/cli/tanstackTypes.ts:589-764`, `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/cli/tanstackTypes.ts:621-629`, `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/routeTree.ts:326-344`

**Evidence:** generateTanstackRouterTypesSourceForEntry inlines isResponse/isRedirectResponse/throwTanstackRedirect/mapParamsForModernLoader/createRouteStaticData/modernLoaderToTanstack into every entry's src/modern-tanstack/<entry>/router.gen.ts (tanstackTypes.ts:589-764) — a third copy of the loader-wrapping/redirect logic already implemented twice in routeTree.ts (wrapModernLoader/wrapRouteObjectLoader). The generated file already imports createRouter/notFound/redirect from '@modern-js/plugin-tanstack/runtime' (line 592-600), so the helpers could come from the same module. Worse, the emitted throwTanstackRedirect is buggy: `try { void new URL(target); throw redirect({ href: target }); } catch { throw redirect({ to: target }); }` — when target IS a valid absolute URL, the thrown `redirect({href})` is immediately caught by its own catch block and replaced with `redirect({ to: absoluteUrl })`, which TanStack treats as an internal path. The hand-written equivalent in routeTree.ts:326-344 does this correctly with a separate isAbsoluteUrl helper. The exported `router` const (line 783-790) makes this live code in any app that imports the generated module at runtime.

**Direction:** Export modernLoaderToTanstack and createRouteStaticData from '@modern-js/plugin-tanstack/runtime' and have codegen emit imports instead of inlining ~175 lines into every entry of every app (which also means bugfixes ship via package update instead of regeneration). Fix the try/catch so the redirect({href}) throw isn't swallowed (mirror routeTree.ts isAbsoluteUrl), and add a codegen test asserting absolute-URL redirect behavior.

**Verifier:** Both claims verify against the code: generateTanstackRouterTypesSourceForEntry inlines ~175 lines of loader-wrapping helpers (tanstackTypes.ts:589-764) duplicating routeTree.ts's wrapModernLoader/wrapRouteObjectLoader, and the emitted throwTanstackRedirect (lines 621-629) is genuinely broken — `throw redirect({href})` inside the try is caught by its own catch and replaced with `redirect({to: absoluteUrl})`; routeTree.ts:326-344 does it correctly with isAbsoluteUrl. The file already imports from '@modern-js/plugin-tanstack/runtime' so the helpers could be imported. Severity downgraded from major: in the supported flow router.gen.ts is a typing artifact only — register.gen.d.ts references it via `import type` (cli/index.ts:132), no value import exists anywhere in src, codegen-emitted entry code, or the three integration fixtures, and the generated router uses createMemoryHistory(['/']). The bug only fires if a user manually value-imports the generated `router`, which the exported const invites (TanStack convention) but nothing in the framework does. Direction is sound and fork-local.

### plugin-tanstack#5 [MINOR/dead-code/delete] Dead CLI exports: abandoned regex heuristic, test-only route-splitting metadata, and an unused route-tree builder

**Files:** `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/cli/tanstackTypes.ts:250-271`, `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/cli/routeSplitting.ts:13-27,29-40,63-80`, `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/routeTree.ts:912-994`, `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/cli/index.ts:28,36-44`

**Evidence:** (1) isTanstackRouterFrameworkEnabled (tanstackTypes.ts:250-271) regex-greps the user's runtime config for framework:'tanstack' — exactly the 'old regex heuristic' the tanstack-single-source-consolidation changeset says was removed; it is imported and re-exported by cli/index.ts but never called anywhere in src or tests (grep-verified). (2) createTanstackRsbuildRouteSplittingProfile's modernRouteChunks/builderChunkSplit/tanstackStartRspackSplitter fields (incl. prose 'reason' strings and clientDeleteNodes arrays) plus isTanstackStartRouteModuleSource are consumed ONLY by tests/router/cli.test.ts:509-693; production code uses only profile.defaultConfig (cli/index.ts:321). (3) createRouteTreeFromModernRoutes (routeTree.ts:912-994, ~83 lines plus dedicated helpers like createRouteFromModernRoute/wrapModernLoader at 423-507/816-910) has no caller outside its own tests — both runtime plugins use only createRouteTreeFromRouteObjects.

**Direction:** Delete isTanstackRouterFrameworkEnabled and the descriptive profile fields (keep resolveTanstackRouteCodeSplittingEnabled + defaultConfig); delete createRouteTreeFromModernRoutes and the modern-route-only half of routeTree.ts (createRouteFromModernRoute, wrapModernLoader, toModernRouteComponent) along with their tests — roughly 250+ lines of unreachable production code. If the NestedRoute-direct path is planned for a future lane, gate it behind an explicit ADR reference instead of shipping it exported and unused.

**Verifier:** All three claims verify. (1) isTanstackRouterFrameworkEnabled (tanstackTypes.ts:250+) is imported at cli/index.ts:28 and re-exported at :43 but never called anywhere in src or tests (grep-verified, including the tests directory) — it is exactly the regex-grep-the-runtime-config heuristic the consolidation changeset says was removed. (2) routeSplitting.ts's modernRouteChunks/builderChunkSplit/tanstackStartRspackSplitter fields and isTanstackStartRouteModuleSource are consumed only by tests/router/cli.test.ts; production uses only profile.defaultConfig (cli/index.ts:321). (3) createRouteTreeFromModernRoutes (routeTree.ts:912-994) plus its dedicated helpers (createRouteFromModernRoute 816-910, wrapModernLoader 423-507) have no caller outside routeTree.test.ts; both runtime plugins call only createRouteTreeFromRouteObjects (plugin.tsx:387, plugin.node.tsx:433). All fork-added files. Minor caveat on direction: these are published exports of the package, so deletion is API-breaking, but the package is fork-new so that is the fork's call.

### plugin-tanstack#6 [MINOR/correctness/small-fix] source.include path computed via first-occurrence string replace on an absolute path; wrong directory when CLI is loaded as ESM

**Files:** `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/cli/index.ts:320-328`

**Evidence:** api.config() adds `path.resolve(__dirname, '../runtime').replace('cjs', 'esm')` to source.include so the package's dist runtime gets transpiled for the app's browser targets. Two defects: (a) .replace replaces the FIRST 'cjs' substring anywhere in the absolute path — a workspace path containing 'cjs' (e.g. /home/cjs-team/...) corrupts the include path and silently disables down-leveling; (b) when the CLI plugin is loaded through the ESM condition (package.json exports '.' -> node.import -> dist/esm-node/cli/index.mjs), the shimmed __dirname yields dist/esm-node/runtime and the replace is a no-op (verified in built dist/esm-node/cli/index.mjs:145), but browsers bundle dist/esm/runtime per the './runtime' export map — so the include never matches the files actually bundled and the runtime ships untranspiled es2021. The include also covers @tanstack/react-router but not its runtime deps @tanstack/router-core and @tanstack/react-store.

**Direction:** Replace the string surgery with a resolution-based include, e.g. path.dirname(require.resolve('@modern-js/plugin-tanstack/runtime', { paths:[appDirectory] })) or a package-scoped regex like /[\\/]@modern-js[\\/]plugin-tanstack[\\/]dist[\\/]/, and add @tanstack/router-core and @tanstack/react-store alongside react-router.

**Verifier:** Verified. cli/index.ts:325 is `path.resolve(__dirname, '../runtime').replace('cjs', 'esm')`: (a) String.replace replaces the first 'cjs' substring anywhere in the absolute path — a workspace path containing 'cjs' corrupts the include; (b) the built dist/esm-node/cli/index.mjs (~line 144) contains the same call with the shimmed cli_dirname, yielding dist/esm-node/runtime with a no-op replace, while package.json maps './runtime' to dist/esm/runtime/index.mjs for bundling — so under the node.import condition the include never matches the bundled runtime. Include regex covers only @tanstack/react-router (line 324), not router-core/react-store. One contextual correction: the identical replace('cjs','esm') idiom exists UPSTREAM in plugin-runtime/src/router/cli/index.ts:170 (absent from the fork diff), so this is an inherited upstream pattern copied into a fork-added file — still in scope since the flagged file is wholly fork-owned and plugin-tanstack's dist layout makes the esm-node mismatch concrete. Proposed direction is fork-local and merge-safe.

### plugin-tanstack#7 [MINOR/correctness/small-fix] register.gen.d.ts emits a plugin-i18n module augmentation based on a path-segment heuristic, breaking typecheck for non-i18n apps with :lang params

**Files:** `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/cli/index.ts:139-151`, `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/cli/tanstackTypes.ts:119-126,200-247`

**Evidence:** collectCanonicalRoutesForEntry sets hasI18nSurface=true when the first route segment is one of ':lang'/':locale'/':language' (LOCALE_PARAM_SEGMENTS, tanstackTypes.ts:119-126; check at 211-214) — regardless of whether @modern-js/plugin-i18n is installed. createRegisterDtsContent then emits `declare module '@modern-js/plugin-i18n/runtime' { interface UltramodernCanonicalRoutes {...} }` (cli/index.ts:142-150) into the user's src. An app that hand-rolls a top-level /:lang/ param without plugin-i18n gets TS2664 ('Invalid module name in augmentation; cannot find module') on every typecheck from a file marked 'Do not edit manually'. The reliable signal (routes carrying modernCanonicalPath, which only plugin-i18n produces) is already checked at tanstackTypes.ts:203-205; the segment-name heuristic is the only unreliable trigger.

**Direction:** Gate the augmentation on actual plugin-i18n presence (e.g. the CLI plugin list in api.getAppContext(), or resolvability of '@modern-js/plugin-i18n/runtime' from appDirectory) instead of, or in addition to, the locale-segment-name heuristic; keep modernCanonicalPath as the primary signal.

**Verifier:** Verified. collectCanonicalRoutesForEntry sets hasI18nSurface=true when the first segment of a top-level route path is in LOCALE_PARAM_SEGMENTS (':lang'/':locale'/':language'/'$lang'/'$locale'/'$language', tanstackTypes.ts:119-126, check at 211-214) with no check that @modern-js/plugin-i18n is installed; createRegisterDtsContent then unconditionally emits the `declare module '@modern-js/plugin-i18n/runtime'` augmentation when canonical entries exist (cli/index.ts:139-151). Augmenting an unresolvable module produces TS2664 on every typecheck, in a generated 'Do not edit manually' file. The reliable modernCanonicalPath signal is indeed already checked at tanstackTypes.ts:203-205, and the function's own doc comment (170-177) claims plain TanStack apps never get the augmentation — contradicted by the segment-name trigger. Fork-owned; direction is sound.

### plugin-tanstack#8 [MINOR/duplication/refactor] routeTree.ts builds the same route options four times and duplicates 80-line loader wrappers

**Files:** `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/routeTree.ts:423-507,536-624,740-814,816-910,938-994,1000-1075`

**Evidence:** wrapModernLoader (423-507) and wrapRouteObjectLoader (536-624) are ~80-line near-verbatim twins (same signal/baseRequest/href/request extraction, same RSC payload branch, same response/redirect/notFound normalization in both try and catch). The route-option assembly block (getParentRoute/component/pendingComponent/errorComponent/validateSearch/loaderDeps/staticData/loader + ssr/shouldReload/path-vs-id) is repeated four times: createRouteFromRouteObject (740-814), createRouteFromModernRoute (816-910), and the two root-route builders (938-994, 1000-1075). This is the main reason the file is 1,114 lines; finding 5 already removes the modern-route half, after which one loader wrapper plus one option-assembly helper suffices.

**Direction:** Extract a single buildTanstackRouteOptions(adapter) helper taking a small accessor adapter (id/component/loader/children getters) and one wrapLoader implementation parameterized by the params-mapping function. Combined with deleting the unused modern-route path, this should roughly halve the file.

**Verifier:** Verified by direct reading. wrapModernLoader (423-507) and wrapRouteObjectLoader (536-624) are near-verbatim twins (identical signal/baseRequest/href/request extraction, RSC payload branch, and response/redirect/notFound normalization in the catch), differing only in the params-mapping function and loader source. The getParentRoute/component/pendingComponent/errorComponent/validateSearch/loaderDeps/staticData/loader + ssr/shouldReload/path-vs-id assembly repeats in createRouteFromRouteObject (740-814), createRouteFromModernRoute (816-910), and both root builders (938-994, 1000-1075). File is exactly 1,114 lines. Fork-owned. Note the proposed consolidation dovetails with finding 5's deletion of the modern-route half (createRouteFromModernRoutes path), which is unreachable in production.

### plugin-tanstack#10 [MINOR/upstream-compat/small-fix] Duck-typed coupling to @modern-js/runtime/cli validates 2 of 5 required helpers before casting to the full interface

**Files:** `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/cli/index.ts:56-106`

**Evidence:** getRuntimeRouterCli() requires five helpers from '@modern-js/runtime/cli' (getEntrypointRoutesDir, handleFileChange, handleGeneratorEntryCode, handleModifyEntrypoints, isRouteEntry — the RuntimeRouterCliHelpers shape at cli/index.ts:56-87), but the runtime guard at lines 98-101 checks only handleGeneratorEntryCode && getEntrypointRoutesDir before casting the Partial to the full type. This is the package's only seam into plugin-runtime CLI internals (fork-added exports on an upstream-modified package); if an upstream sync or refactor drops handleModifyEntrypoints/handleFileChange/isRouteEntry, the nice 'requires @modern-js/runtime/cli router helper exports' error never fires and the failure surfaces later as an opaque 'not a function' TypeError inside api.modifyEntrypoints/api.onFileChanged.

**Direction:** Validate all five function properties in the guard (a small loop over a const list keeps it in sync with the type), so the contract violation fails fast with the actionable error message. Optionally note this seam in FORK-DIVERGENCE.md's plugin-runtime entry since it constrains the runtime/cli export surface during merges.

**Verifier:** Verified exactly. RuntimeRouterCliHelpers (cli/index.ts:56-87) requires five functions; getRuntimeRouterCli (96-105) guards only `cli.handleGeneratorEntryCode && cli.getEntrypointRoutesDir` before casting Partial to the full type. All five are genuinely used: getEntrypointRoutesDir (288), isRouteEntry (317), handleModifyEntrypoints (331), handleGeneratorEntryCode (346), handleFileChange (363-364) — so a missing unvalidated helper surfaces later as an opaque TypeError inside api.modifyEntrypoints/api.onFileChanged instead of the actionable error. This is the package's only seam into the fork-added '@modern-js/runtime/cli' router-helper exports, making it a real merge-hazard tripwire. Fork-owned (file A). Direction (validate all five via a const list) is a safe small fix.

### plugin-tanstack#11 [MINOR/security/small-fix] SSR script-tag reconstruction emits router script children unescaped, relying on upstream seroval escaping

**Files:** `packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/plugin.node.tsx:262-297,421-426`

**Evidence:** routerManagedTagToHtml hand-rolls HTML serialization of TanStack's buffered SSR scripts: attributes are escaped for & and " only (htmlEscapeAttr, line 262-264 — adequate for double-quoted attrs), but children are concatenated raw: `${open}${children}</script>` (lines 289-291). Safety therefore depends entirely on TanStack/seroval pre-escaping '</script>' and '<!--' sequences in dehydrated loader data; if a future pinned upgrade changes that contract, loader data containing '</script>' becomes an XSS injection into the SSR HTML. Nearby, the SSR Request clone creates an AbortController whose signal is attached to ssrRequest (lines 421-426) but controller.abort() is never called anywhere — loaders that honor the signal will never see cancellation, so the plumbing is currently inert.

**Direction:** Defensively escape script-breaking sequences in children (e.g. replace /<\//g with '<\\/' or reject tags whose children contain '</script') before emitting, and add a unit test pinning the behavior against the vendored TanStack version. Either wire controller.abort() to response close/error in the SSR pipeline or delete the dead AbortController.

**Verifier:** Verified. htmlEscapeAttr (plugin.node.tsx:262-264) escapes only & and " — adequate for the double-quoted attributes it serves — but children are concatenated raw into `${open}${children}</script>` (289-291), so safety against '</script>' / '<!--' breakout in dehydrated loader data rests entirely on TanStack/seroval's pre-escaping contract with the pinned versions (1.170.15/1.171.13). A pinned-version bump that changes that contract would turn loader data into an SSR HTML injection vector — minor as a defense-in-depth gap, correctly rated. The AbortController claim also verifies: `controller` appears only at lines 421 and 425; .abort() is never called anywhere in the file, so signal-honoring loaders never observe cancellation (inert plumbing). Fork-owned (file A). Direction (escape script-closing sequences + pin with a test; wire or delete the controller) is sound and fork-local.

## plugin-garfish  (10 confirmed)

packages/runtime/plugin-garfish is a zombie package: upstream deleted plugin-garfish before the v3.2.1 baseline (#7416, commit 1131344faa), and the fork resurrected its source plus ~1.5k lines of new trust/compatibility/cache/telemetry code — but never restored package.json, never re-added the garfish dependency (0 hits in pnpm-lock.yaml), and both its CLI and runtime halves target plugin APIs that no longer exist, so the package cannot build, publish, install, or even register with the framework. It is kept "green" by a self-referential harness: test shims re-implement the removed legacy plugin manager and stub Garfish in 13 lines, while CI release gates grep the dead source for contract strings. The ADR-0011 MF-vs-Garfish parity machinery is therefore aspirational on the client side: the only emitter of parity telemetry lives in this unshippable package, and the "canonical baseline" Garfish runtime is never exercised against real Garfish anywhere in the repo. The honest options are to delete the package (plus its gate/profile entries and the dead config surfaces that reference it) or to genuinely re-platform it onto the current plugin API — the current state is the worst of both.

### plugin-garfish#1 [CRITICAL/dead-code/delete] Entire package is unshippable dead weight: no package.json, garfish dependency absent from lockfile, zero consumers

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/rstest.config.mts:6`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/plugin.tsx:2`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/index.ts`

**Evidence:** Upstream removed plugin-garfish in #7416 (1131344faa, ancestor of baseline 8a744c1b31 — `git ls-tree 8a744c1b31` shows 0 files at this path); the fork resurrected 32 files (~4.5k src + test lines) as A-status fork-owned files but never restored package.json/tsconfig (directory contains only rstest.config.mts, src/, tests/). The package is not in the pnpm workspace, not built, not typechecked (no nx/tsconfig/package.json reference at root), not publishable. `grep -c garfish pnpm-lock.yaml` = 0, so `import GarfishInstance from 'garfish'` (plugin.tsx:2) is unresolvable outside tests, where rstest.config.mts:62 aliases garfish to a 13-line stub (tests/shims/garfish.ts). No package or integration test in the repo imports `@modern-js/plugin-garfish` (only scripts/boundary-guards/profile.json and scripts/release-gates/rc-contract-profile.json reference its file paths). The rstest config itself documents the situation: "this package was removed from the workspace upstream (#7416) but its fork-retained tests still run through the root rstest sweep" and resolves react straight out of node_modules/.pnpm.

**Direction:** Decide explicitly: (a) delete the whole package, its rstest config, its entries in scripts/release-gates/rc-contract-profile.json and scripts/boundary-guards/profile.json, and update SUNSET-DECISION-0001/ADR-0011 to say the Garfish compat lane is retired (MF-only); or (b) if the Garfish compat lane is genuinely wanted, restore a package.json with the garfish dependency, wire it into the workspace/build, and re-platform both halves onto the current plugin API (see companion findings). Option (a) removes ~6k lines of unmaintainable code and several CI gate commands at zero functional cost — nothing today can load this code.

**Verifier:** Verified. Directory holds only rstest.config.mts + src/ + tests/ (no package.json/tsconfig), so despite the packages/runtime/* workspace glob it is not a workspace package, never built/published/typechecked (zero references in nx.json, root tsconfigs, root package.json). git ls-tree 8a744c1b31 -- packages/runtime/plugin-garfish is empty and all 32 files are A-status in the baseline diff — fully fork-owned. grep -c garfish pnpm-lock.yaml = 0; the only repo references to @modern-js/plugin-garfish are two ADR docs and a CHANGELOG; gate profiles reference file paths only. One evidence correction: 1131344faa is NOT an ancestor of 8a744c1b31 (merge-base --is-ancestor exits 1; likely different lineage), but the substantive claim — package absent at baseline — independently verifies via ls-tree. Proposed direction (a) is upstream-merge-friendly: upstream already deleted this package, so deletion shrinks divergence to zero for this path.

### plugin-garfish#2 [MAJOR/correctness/delete] CLI half targets the removed legacy plugin API — every hook it returns is never invoked by current app-tools

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/cli/index.ts:43`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/tests/shims/modern-js-core.ts:1`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/solutions/app-tools/src/compat/hooks.ts:39`

**Evidence:** src/cli/index.ts:43 uses `setup: ({ useAppContext, useResolvedConfigContext, useConfigContext }) =>` returning a legacy hook object (`validateSchema`, `resolvedConfig`, `config`, `addRuntimeExports`, `modifyEntryImports`, `modifyEntryRuntimePlugins`, `modifyEntryRenderFunction`, `modifyAsyncEntry`, `modifyEntryExport`). Grep for modifyEntryImports/modifyEntryRenderFunction/modifyAsyncEntry/modifyEntryExport/addRuntimeExports across packages/solutions/app-tools/src returns ZERO hits — those hooks were removed with the v2->v3 plugin-system finalization (#7373/#7381); the compat layer (src/compat/hooks.ts) only bridges modifyEntrypoints/modifyFileSystemRoutes/modifyServerRoutes/modifyResolvedConfig/modifyHtmlPartials. Tests pass only because tests/shims/modern-js-core.ts re-implements the deleted `@modern-js/core` plugin manager (its own header admits this) and cli.test.tsx calls `garfishPlugin().setup!({...mock api})` directly. The string-rewrite hooks (cli/utils.ts makeRenderFunction replacing `bootstrap(AppWrapper, mountNode, root`, `createApp({`, `router(`) are additionally coupled to entry-template text that the fork's plugin-runtime has since changed.

**Direction:** If the package is kept, rewrite src/cli as a real `CliPlugin<AppTools>` using the current api-registration style (api.config / api.modifyEntrypoints / template hooks app-tools actually runs) and delete tests/shims/modern-js-core.ts; otherwise delete src/cli, cli.test.tsx, runtimeExport.test.tsx and the shim together with the package. Testing a plugin exclusively against a hand-rolled re-implementation of a deleted framework API verifies nothing about the product.

**Verifier:** Core claim verified: grep for modifyEntryImports/modifyEntryRenderFunction/modifyAsyncEntry/modifyEntryExport/addRuntimeExports/modifyEntryRuntimePlugins across app-tools src returns zero hits, and the compat bridge (compat/utils.ts transformHookRunner + hooks.ts handleSetupResult, which requires api[newAPI] to exist) maps none of them — the entry-codegen hooks that constitute the plugin's function are dead. tests/shims/modern-js-core.ts header confirms it re-implements the deleted legacy plugin manager, and runtimeExport.test.tsx calls plugin.setup!({mock api}) directly. Minor overstatement: 'every hook' — `config` and `resolvedConfig` (→ api.config / modifyResolvedConfig, both real at toolkit/plugin cli/api.ts:89 and compat) WOULD bridge if the plugin were ever loaded; moot today since nothing can load the package. Severity downgraded critical→major: finding 1 already establishes the code is unreachable, so this is decision-support for the revive-vs-delete call rather than an independent critical defect. Direction is sound either way.

### plugin-garfish#3 [MAJOR/correctness/rewrite] Runtime half registers a 'hoc' hook the current runtime never calls, and imports exports that do not exist in @modern-js/runtime

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/plugin.tsx:193`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/utils/apps.tsx:2`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/compat/hooks.ts:19`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/tests/runtimePlugin.test.tsx:61`

**Evidence:** plugin.tsx:192-280 returns `{ hoc({App}, next) }` from setup. The current runtime plugin API exposes `wrapRoot` (toolkit/plugin/src/runtime/api.ts:90; used by every live runtime plugin, e.g. plugin-runtime/src/router/runtime/plugin.tsx:133), and plugin-runtime's handleSetupResult (core/compat/hooks.ts:19-34) only bridges setup-result keys for which `api[key]` exists — there is no `api.hoc`, so the returned hoc is silently dropped: in a real app the GarfishProvider never wraps the tree and the plugin is a no-op. The test proves the gap: runtimePlugin.test.tsx:61 manually drives `runtime.hoc?.({ App: Shell }, input => input)`. Worse, apps.tsx:2 does a value import of `RuntimeReactContext` from '@modern-js/runtime' — zero hits in plugin-runtime/src (only RuntimeContext/ReactRuntimeContext exist), and apps.tsx:59 calls `context?.router?.useRouteMatch?.()` (react-router v5 API) against a context shape (TRuntimeContext, core/context/runtime.ts:11) that has no `router` member. plugin.tsx:1 imports `type { Plugin }` which plugin-runtime also does not export (only RuntimePlugin). None of this is caught because the package has no tsconfig and is excluded from repo typecheck, and the only test that would exercise apps.tsx mocks it out (runtimePlugin.test.tsx:10).

**Direction:** If kept: port plugin.tsx to `RuntimePlugin` with `api.wrapRoot`, fix the imports to real exports, and add at least one unmocked render test through registerPlugin so the integration is exercised by the actual hook dispatcher instead of a hand-called hoc. If the package is deleted per finding 1, this goes with it.

**Verifier:** Verified in full. plugin.tsx:192-280 returns {hoc}; plugin-runtime core/compat/hooks.ts:19-34 bridges only setup-result keys with an existing api method ('hoc' maps to itself via default case and no api.hoc exists — toolkit/plugin runtime api.ts:90 exposes wrapRoot only), so the hoc is silently dropped and the plugin would be a no-op even if loadable. runtimePlugin.test.tsx:61 manually drives runtime.hoc and :10 mocks apps.tsx out. Import claims verified: '@modern-js/runtime' index exports RuntimeContext + type RuntimePlugin — no RuntimeReactContext value export (ReactRuntimeContext exists only in core/context/runtime.ts:44, not re-exported) and no `Plugin` type; TRuntimeContext (runtime.ts:11-21) has no typed router member (apps.tsx:59-61 calls v5-style context.router.useRouteMatch). Severity downgraded critical→major for the same unreachability reason as the CLI finding. Proposed direction (wrapRoot port + real exports) matches how live plugins (router plugin.tsx:133) integrate.

### plugin-garfish#4 [MAJOR/architecture/move] ADR-0011 parity machinery is aspirational: the only client emitter of runtime-parity telemetry is this dead package, and Garfish is never exercised

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/fallbackTelemetry.ts:13`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:207`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/contracts.ts:133`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/docs/super-app-rfc-adr/ADR-0011-mf-vs-garfish-runtime-parity-contract.md`

**Evidence:** ADR-0011 declares Garfish the canonical baseline and requires parity telemetry (runtimeSurface, parityClaimId, fallback codes) plus garfish-observed evidence before MF can be canonical. Code reality: `runtimeSurface` appears in no package outside plugin-garfish (repo-wide grep); the only code that emits `modernjs:mv-runtime-parity` events or POSTs to `/_modern/contract-gates/runtime-fallback` is fallbackTelemetry.ts in this unbuildable package. The server half is real and hardened (server-runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:207 with auth/rate-limit/dedupe), but no shipping client can ever send it these signals; generated SuperApp workspaces only embed the event NAME as topology metadata (create contracts.ts:133) with no emitter. The canonical baseline itself is untestable: garfish is absent from pnpm-lock.yaml, tests/shims/garfish.ts is a 13-line stub, and `grep -rln garfish tests/` (integration suite) returns nothing. The evidence dirs contain no garfish-observed parity records (grep over docs/super-app-rfc-adr/evidence finds only prose references).

**Direction:** Make the contract honest. Either (a) re-scope ADR-0011: declare MF the only runtime surface, retire the Garfish baseline, and move the fallback-telemetry client (fallbackTelemetry.ts + the trust/compatibility taxonomies — the genuinely good, reusable ~1.1k lines here) into a fork-owned package the MF lane actually ships (e.g. server-runtime-extensions sibling or a new @modern-js/mv-runtime-telemetry consumed by the create templates / plugin-runtime MF path), or (b) actually revive the Garfish lane end-to-end. Today the parity contract gates a comparison in which one side cannot run and the other side never emits.

**Verifier:** Verified with one correction. runtimeSurface appears in no package outside plugin-garfish (repo-wide grep confirms); fallbackTelemetry.ts is the only client-runtime emitter of modernjs:mv-runtime-parity events; create contracts.ts:133 and add-vertical.ts:367 embed only the event NAME as topology metadata with no emitter; server endpoint is real (runtime-extensions telemetry.ts:207 ff, auth header/rate-limit/dedupe constants present); garfish absent from lockfile, tests/shims/garfish.ts is exactly 13 lines, no integration test under tests/ mentions garfish; ADR-0011 confirms Garfish-as-canonical-baseline and required parity telemetry fields. Correction: app-tools ships a `modern runtime fallback-signal` CLI command (src/commands/runtime.ts:196, 'emit runtime fallback signal for canary gate mutation') that POSTs synthetic signals to the endpoint — so 'the only code that POSTs' is technically overstated, but it is an operator/CI tool whose payload carries no runtimeSurface/parityClaimId, so the core claim (no shipping application client emits parity telemetry, and the canonical Garfish side cannot run) stands. ADR-0011 and all flagged files are fork-added. Direction (a)/(b) choice is honest and merge-safe.

### plugin-garfish#5 [MAJOR/correctness/small-fix] Garfish-lane telemetry defaults runtimeSurface to 'module-federation', corrupting the parity dataset by design

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/fallbackTelemetry.ts:18`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/plugin.tsx:184`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/tests/runtimePlugin.test.tsx:92`

**Evidence:** fallbackTelemetry.ts:18 sets `DEFAULT_RUNTIME_SURFACE = 'module-federation'` inside the GARFISH runtime plugin, and no emit site in plugin.tsx/apps.tsx/MApp.tsx passes a runtimeSurface override (plugin.tsx:184-191 builds the default telemetry config with only reportToServer/reportEndpoint). ADR-0011 §6 requires telemetry to carry "the runtime surface" — i.e., the concrete path used to load the remote — so every event from the Garfish lane mislabels itself as MF, making Garfish-vs-MF correlation impossible at the gate. The test suite asserts the mislabel as expected behavior (runtimePlugin.test.tsx:92 expects `runtimeSurface: 'module-federation'` for a Garfish bootstrap failure), so this is encoded, not accidental drift.

**Direction:** Default runtimeSurface to 'garfish' in this package (or derive it from the loading path) and update the test expectations; if the telemetry module is extracted for the MF lane per the previous finding, make runtimeSurface a required constructor parameter of the emitter rather than a constant default.

**Verifier:** Verified. fallbackTelemetry.ts:18 sets DEFAULT_RUNTIME_SURFACE = 'module-federation' inside the Garfish runtime package; createFallbackEvent (lines 298-301) uses baseEvent.runtimeSurface ?? config?.runtimeSurface ?? that default, and the plugin's auto-built telemetry config (plugin.tsx:184-191) sets only reportToServer/reportEndpoint — no emit site in plugin.tsx/apps.tsx/MApp.tsx passes runtimeSurface. runtimePlugin.test.tsx:91 asserts runtimeSurface: 'module-federation' for a Garfish bootstrap failure, encoding the mislabel. Caveat for severity context: a user COULD override via fallbackTelemetry.runtimeSurface config, and no data flows today since the package is unshippable, so the corruption is latent — but the flaw is real in the code and the test, and CI gates certify this file as the fallback-telemetry contract. Keeping major per investigator; if the telemetry module is extracted to the MF lane per the architecture finding the default becomes correct there, which the proposed direction (required constructor parameter) handles well.

### plugin-garfish#6 [MAJOR/correctness/rewrite] Auto-derived runtime-compatibility policy is self-satisfying: manifest digest is used as both host digest and remote-digest fallback

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/plugin.tsx:81`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/compatibility.ts:35`

**Evidence:** In the default production profile ('balanced', plugin.tsx:35-38), when no explicit `runtimeCompatibility.hostDigest` is configured, plugin.tsx:81-98 synthesizes a strict policy with `hostDigest = manifest.runtimeDigest || modernManifest.runtimeDigest` and `requireRemoteDigest: true`. Validation then calls validateRuntimeCompatibility with `manifestRuntimeDigest: manifest.runtimeDigest, globalRuntimeDigest: modernManifest?.runtimeDigest` (plugin.tsx:132-136), and compatibility.ts:35-46 resolves a remote's digest by falling back to those SAME values. For any remote app record lacking its own runtimeDigest, remoteDigest === hostDigest by construction: the strict handshake passes vacuously and `requireRemoteDigest: true` can never trigger — exactly the case (digest-less remote) the handshake exists to block per ADR-0002. The only failure mode left is an app that explicitly declares a mismatching digest, i.e. an honest attacker. tests/compatibility.test.ts:44,77 codify the fallback as a feature without covering the conflated-derivation case.

**Direction:** Separate the two digest sources: host digest must come from host-side config/build define (e.g. the MODERN_MF_RUNTIME_DIGEST define the CLI half already plumbs) and must never fall back to the remote-describing manifest; when requireRemoteDigest is true, a remote without an app-level or runtimeMetadata digest must fail, with no manifest-level fallback. Add a test asserting that the auto-derived balanced policy rejects digest-less remotes.

**Verifier:** Verified mechanically. plugin.tsx:81-98: with no explicit hostDigest in the balanced/strict profiles, hostDigest := manifest.runtimeDigest || modernManifest.runtimeDigest with mode 'strict' and requireRemoteDigest true; plugin.tsx:132-136 then passes those SAME values as manifestRuntimeDigest/globalRuntimeDigest, and compatibility.ts:35-46 resolveRemoteDigest falls back to them for any app lacking app.runtimeDigest/runtimeMetadata.runtimeDigest — so remoteDigest === hostDigest by construction, the missing_remote_digest branch (compatibility.ts:88-101) is unreachable in the auto-derived case, and digest-less remotes pass vacuously. tests/compatibility.test.ts ('uses manifest digest fallback...' / 'uses global manifest digest fallback...') codify the fallback with hostDigest deliberately equal to the fallback, never covering the conflated auto-derivation. Latent (package unshippable) but it is the exact behavior the RC 'runtime-compatibility-contract' gate certifies. Proposed direction (separate host-side digest source, fail digest-less remotes under requireRemoteDigest) is correct and fork-local.

### plugin-garfish#7 [MAJOR/security/rewrite] Integrity 'verification' is a fetch-twice TOCTOU — verified bytes are not the executed bytes

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/trust.ts:206`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/trust.ts:294`

**Evidence:** trust.ts verifyIntegrity (206-313) fetches app.entry, hashes response.text() (294-299), compares to the declared SRI token, then DISCARDS the content; Garfish later performs its own independent fetch of the same URL to actually execute the remote. A compromised remote/CDN can serve clean bytes to the verification request and malicious bytes to the execution request (or simply vary by cache state/headers) — the check provides no binding between verification and execution, while ADR-0011 §7 and the release-gate snippet 'plugin-garfish-remote-trust-contract' present it as an integrity gate. Secondary issues: hashing the UTF-8 re-encoding of response.text() (encodeText, 194-204) rather than raw bytes can produce false mismatches for non-UTF-8 assets, and the double fetch doubles remote-entry load cost.

**Direction:** Either verify-and-use (fetch once, hash the raw ArrayBuffer, and hand the verified bytes to the loader — Garfish supports custom loaders, and an MF re-platform could use real <script integrity> SRI which delegates binding to the browser), or downgrade the claim: rename/document it as advisory integrity *sampling* in the ADR and trust taxonomy so release gates do not overstate the guarantee. Use response.arrayBuffer() instead of text() regardless.

**Verifier:** Verified. trust.ts verifyIntegrity (206-313) performs its own fetch(app.entry) (239-254), hashes via subtle.digest over encodeText(response.text()) (294-299), and discards the content; execution happens via Garfish's independent loader fetch after GarfishInstance.registerApp (plugin.tsx:237), so there is no binding between verified and executed bytes — classic TOCTOU, plus the secondary text()/UTF-8-re-encode and double-fetch points are accurate. Fairness note: this is additive hardening that vanilla Modern.js/Garfish lacks entirely, so the defect is an overstated guarantee (presented as the 'plugin-garfish-remote-trust-contract' RC gate and ADR-0011 integrity machinery) rather than a regression; and the code is currently unreachable in any built app. Major is acceptable given the gates certify it as an enforced integrity contract; the verify-and-use / downgrade-the-claim direction is sound.

### plugin-garfish#8 [MAJOR/testing/refactor] CI release gates and boundary guards pin string-presence contracts on dead source files — contract theater

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/release-gates/rc-contract-profile.json:125`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/release-gates/rc-contract-profile.json:404`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/boundary-guards/profile.json:27`

**Evidence:** rc-contract-profile.json:125-160 defines four RC 'contracts' (runtime-compatibility, remote-trust, fallback-telemetry, cache-policy) as `includes` string checks against plugin-garfish source files that are compiled into no shipped artifact, and :404 runs the package's shim-driven unit tests as a gateCommand; boundary-guards/profile.json:27-47 requires snippets ('mf-runtime-trust-before-registration', 'mf-fallback-telemetry-load-unmount') in plugin.tsx/apps.tsx. Both profiles are wired into CI (.github/workflows/release-contract-gates.yml:102, boundary-anti-patterns.yml:51, plus validate:bun-smoke). The gates therefore certify at release-candidate time that trust-before-registration and fallback telemetry are 'enforced' by code that cannot execute in any built application — a false green signal precisely where ADR-0011 demands evidence, and ongoing CI cost maintaining it.

**Direction:** Re-point these gate entries at the live MF lane (server-runtime-extensions telemetry/trust modules, create-workspace contracts, and whatever client emitter replaces fallbackTelemetry.ts per finding 4), or delete them together with the package. A contract gate must reference code on the shipping path; keep the gate *mechanism*, fix its targets.

**Verifier:** Verified in full. rc-contract-profile.json:124-163 defines the four garfish contracts as `includes` string checks against src files compiled into no artifact; :404 gateCommand runs the package's shim-driven unit tests; boundary-guards/profile.json:26-48 requires the 'mf-runtime-trust-before-registration' and 'mf-fallback-telemetry-load-unmount' snippets in plugin.tsx/apps.tsx. CI wiring confirmed: release-contract-gates.yml:102 passes rc-contract-profile.json, boundary-anti-patterns.yml:51 runs validate:boundary-guards, and the root validate:bun-smoke script runs both profiles. Both profile files are A-status fork additions. The 'false green at RC time' characterization is accurate — the gates assert source strings and shim-mediated tests for code no application can execute. Proposed direction (re-point gate targets at the live MF lane or delete with the package) keeps the mechanism and is merge-neutral (all fork-owned scripts).

### plugin-garfish#9 [MINOR/upstream-compat/consolidate] Fork-added deploy.microFrontend trust fields in app-tools (M file) serve only the dead package — shrinkable divergence

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/solutions/app-tools/src/types/config/deploy.ts:14`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/cli/index.ts:121`

**Evidence:** deploy.ts is an M file vs baseline; the fork-added hunk is the `runtimeDigest`/`integrity`/`attestation` fields on MicroFrontend (deploy.ts:14-27, absent in `git show 8a744c1b31:.../deploy.ts`), whose JSDoc points users at "runtime `remoteTrust.attestations`" — a feature of the dead garfish runtime. Repo-wide, the ONLY consumer of `deploy.microFrontend` (let alone the new fields) is plugin-garfish/src/cli/index.ts:121-134, which converts them into MODERN_MF_* defines that nothing else reads (grep MODERN_MF_RUNTIME_DIGEST hits only plugin-garfish src+tests). grep for `deploy.microFrontend` in app-tools src returns zero consumers. FORK-DIVERGENCE.md:138 records this as the ADR-0002 §10-11 'trust contract', overstating it: it is an inert public config surface that silently does nothing for users, and an upstream-merge friction point in a shared types file.

**Direction:** If the garfish lane is deleted, revert the MicroFrontend hunk in deploy.ts to upstream shape (shrinking the M-file divergence) and carry remote trust metadata where the MF lane actually defines it (create-workspace topology contracts / MF manifest). If kept, move the type extension into the fork-owned package's own config surface instead of the upstream types file, and correct the FORK-DIVERGENCE entry.

**Verifier:** Verified. deploy.ts is M vs baseline; git show 8a744c1b31 confirms upstream MicroFrontend has only enableHtmlEntry/externalBasicLibrary/moduleApp — runtimeDigest/integrity/attestation (deploy.ts:14-27) are fork-added, with JSDoc pointing at the dead runtime's remoteTrust.attestations. Repo-wide (excluding dist/node_modules), the only consumer of the new fields is plugin-garfish/src/cli/index.ts:121-134 (the only other microFrontend reference is a test-helper default in server/utils/tests/helpers.ts); MODERN_MF_RUNTIME_DIGEST hits only plugin-garfish src+tests; app-tools' own src/plugins/deploy contains zero microFrontend/runtimeDigest/attestation usage despite FORK-DIVERGENCE.md:138 attributing the 'trust contract (ADR-0002 §10-11)' to it — the overstatement claim is accurate. Proposed direction reverts only the fork-added hunk to upstream shape, which reduces M-file merge friction without touching vanilla behavior. Minor is the right severity.

### plugin-garfish#10 [MINOR/hygiene/small-fix] Accumulated rot in test/infra: pnpm-store spelunking aliases, dead plugin options in tests, and React-19-removed APIs in generated code

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/rstest.config.mts:12`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/tests/runtimeExport.test.tsx:26`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/cli/utils.ts:39`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/cli/index.ts:303`

**Evidence:** Three symptoms of the package being outside the workspace: (1) rstest.config.mts:12-26 resolves react/react-dom/hoist-non-react-statics by sorting node_modules/.pnpm and taking the first `react@` prefix match — if a second react major ever lands in the store the tests silently bind to the lexicographically-lowest version, and `definedAliases` silently drops missing entries instead of failing. (2) runtimeExport.test.tsx:26 passes `mfPackagePath` to garfishPlugin(), an option the plugin no longer accepts (signature is `{pluginName, runtimePluginName}`, cli/index.ts:38-40) — the test exercises only defaults while appearing to test custom paths. (3) The code-generation templates emit `unmountComponentAtNode` usage and import it from 'react-dom' (cli/utils.ts:39, cli/index.ts:299-309): removed in react-dom 19, while the workspace standard is react ^19.2.7 (plugin-runtime package.json:249) — even a revived CLI lane would generate entries that fail to build.

**Direction:** All three disappear if the package is deleted. If kept: restore a package.json with proper devDependencies (eliminating the .pnpm spelunking), fix or delete the mfPackagePath test, and update the generated provider/destroy path to react-dom/client root.unmount() only.

**Verifier:** All three symptoms verified. (1) rstest.config.mts:12-26 resolvePnpmPackageEntry sorts node_modules/.pnpm and takes the first 'react@'-prefixed entry (lexicographically lowest if multiple majors land), and definedAliases (28-36) silently filters out null entries rather than failing. (2) runtimeExport.test.tsx:24-26 passes mfPackagePath to garfishPlugin() whose actual signature is { pluginName, runtimePluginName } (cli/index.ts:38-41) — the option is ignored, so the test exercises defaults while appearing to test custom paths. (3) The generated provider template emits unmountComponentAtNode (cli/utils.ts provider destroy branch) and cli/index.ts:299-309 adds an import of unmountComponentAtNode from 'react-dom' — an API removed in react-dom 19 — while the workspace pins react ^19.2.7 (plugin-runtime package.json:249), so even a revived CLI lane would generate broken entries. All files fork-added; minor severity and the delete-or-fix direction are appropriate.

## server-runtime-extensions  (10 confirmed)

The @modern-js/server-runtime-extensions package itself is well-built — clean per-concern modules, a typed env pass, real unit tests, and the extraction genuinely decoupled server-core (zero residual telemetry/gate imports there; registration happens through prod-server's applyPlugins seam used by both prod and dev). The problems cluster around the extraction's leftovers and lost parity: a 1,000-line dead legacy Server harness in prod-server still duplicates the whole telemetry/canary/signal lane, three behaviors silently fell out of the live path (SLO alert wiring, MF asset cache headers, the workerLane feature whose config surface still ships), and the package's two largest classes are tested only from the consuming package's test directory. Separately, the runtime-fallback signal endpoint defaults to enabled+unauthenticated with attacker-controlled rate-limit keys, making it an unauthenticated canary rollback lever, and telemetry.ts has grown into a 2.2k-line monolith that fuses telemetry with contract-gate domain logic.

### server-runtime-extensions#1 [MAJOR/dead-code/delete] Dead legacy Server harness in prod-server duplicates the entire telemetry/canary/runtime-signal lane (~1,200 lines)

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/prod-server/src/server/index.ts`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/prod-server/src/server/modernServerSplit.ts:1`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/prod-server/tests/runtimeSignals.test.ts:12`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:1839`

**Evidence:** prod-server/src/server/index.ts (1,004 lines, fork-added) is unreachable in production: it is not exported from src/index.ts or the package exports map, and its serverImpl is modernServerSplit.ts whose createProdServer unconditionally throws ('not available in this source runtime', modernServerSplit.ts:1-5), so Server.init() can never complete (index.ts:169). Its only consumer is tests/runtimeSignals.test.ts, which bypasses init and pokes private fields via `as any` (test line ~79: server.runtimeFallbackSignalConfig = {...}). The class duplicates the live runtime-extensions plugin nearly line-for-line: initTelemetry (index.ts:249-425) vs injectTelemetryPlugin (telemetry.ts:1905-2221); persistRuntimeFallbackContractGate (index.ts:855-922) vs telemetry.ts:1839-1874; emitCanaryDecisionMetric (index.ts:441-460) vs telemetry.ts:1876-1895; resolveContractGateSnapshotPath (index.ts:427-439) vs contractGateSnapshotStore.ts:305-317; constants (index.ts:70-73) vs telemetry.ts:209-215; runtime status payload builder (index.ts:731-842) vs telemetry.ts:2062-2141. It also reads process.env.MODERN_CONTRACT_GATES_FILE and MODERN_ENV/NODE_ENV raw (index.ts:266-268, 379, 433), violating the package's own env.ts single-typed-pass contract. Commit 12705325a4 already deleted 1,148 lines of this lane (modernServer.ts, libs/render/) as 'verified-unreachable' but left this half behind.

**Direction:** Delete prod-server/src/server/ (index.ts + modernServerSplit.ts) and tests/runtimeSignals.test.ts. Any behavior in it worth keeping (worker lane, SLO wiring, close()/shutdown — see separate findings) should be ported into the runtime-extensions plugin first, then the harness removed. This eliminates a drift hazard where two copies of security-sensitive signal handling can diverge silently.

**Verifier:** Verified. prod-server/src/server/index.ts is 1,004 lines (fork-added, A); serverImpl=createProdServer from modernServerSplit.ts which unconditionally throws (lines 1-5), so Server.init() at index.ts:169 can never complete. Not exported from prod-server/src/index.ts (which exports from ./apply) nor from package.json exports (only '.' and './netlify'). Only consumer is tests/runtimeSignals.test.ts, which casts `createServer() as any` and assigns server.runtimeFallbackSignalConfig directly (lines 79-80) bypassing init. Duplication confirmed line-for-line: initTelemetry (index.ts:249-425) vs injectTelemetryPlugin (telemetry.ts:1905-2221); persistRuntimeFallbackContractGate (index.ts:855-922) vs telemetry.ts:1839-1874; emitCanaryDecisionMetric (index.ts:441-460) vs telemetry.ts:1876-1895; constants (index.ts:70-73) vs telemetry.ts:209-215; status payload builder (index.ts:731-842) vs telemetry.ts:2062-2126. Raw env reads confirmed: MODERN_ENV/NODE_ENV (index.ts:266-268), MODERN_CONTRACT_GATES_FILE (index.ts:433). Commit 12705325a4 deleted the sibling modernServer.ts/libs/render lane as verified-unreachable but kept this. Fork-owned. Proposed deletion is safe (nothing imports it but the dead test) and does not affect vanilla Modern.js, which uses the new createServerBase/applyPlugins path. Note: the live runtime-extensions plugin currently lacks the harness's SLO sink, close() teardown, and worker lane (see findings 4/7/2), so those must be ported before deletion as the direction already states.

### server-runtime-extensions#3 [MAJOR/dead-code/move] mfCache.ts is dead code: MF cache-header policy lost its only consumer, regressing the documented behavior

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/mfCache.ts`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/runtime-extensions/tests/mfCache.test.ts`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/core/src/adapters/node/plugins/static.ts:288`

**Evidence:** resolveMfAssetCacheHeaders/isMfManifestAsset/isMfRemoteEntryAsset have zero non-test consumers (repo-wide grep matches only mfCache.ts and its test). The pre-extraction consumer was prod-server/src/server/modernServer.ts:428 (verified via `git grep 5f9dff870e^`), deleted in 12705325a4 as unreachable — but the helpers and tests were kept. The live MF static-serving path (server-core static.ts:288-507, which serves mf-manifest.json/remoteEntry) sets no cache-control headers at all; no other package emits the 'no-cache, no-store' / 'immutable' policy (grep for must-revalidate|immutable finds only mfCache.ts and vercel.ts's generic static rule). Yet the package README explicitly advertises 'MF asset cache-header policies (resolveMfAssetCacheHeaders)' and FORK-DIVERGENCE.md:120 claims 'MF cache headers now live in @modern-js/server-runtime-extensions' — code-vs-docs drift, and a silent behavioral regression: hosts can now cache a stale mf-manifest.json, the exact failure ADR-0002's no-store policy existed to prevent.

**Direction:** Either re-wire it: add a tiny injectMfAssetCacheHeadersPlugin (middleware applying resolveMfAssetCacheHeaders to matching paths) to the package and register it in prod-server applyPlugins next to injectModuleFederationCssPlugin; or delete mfCache.ts + mfCache.test.ts and correct README/FORK-DIVERGENCE. Given the manifest no-store policy matters for MF-SSR correctness, re-wiring is the better option.

**Verifier:** Verified. resolveMfAssetCacheHeaders/isMfManifestAsset/isMfRemoteEntryAsset have zero non-test consumers repo-wide (grep). Pre-extraction consumer confirmed at git 5f9dff870e^: prod-server/src/server/modernServer.ts:40 imported and :428 used resolveMfAssetCacheHeaders; modernServer.ts was deleted in 12705325a4, orphaning the helpers (kept along with mfCache.test.ts). The live MF static-serving path (server-core static.ts serveFile, lines ~573-633) emits CORS/Content-Type/Content-Length/Content-Encoding but NO cache-control on mf-manifest.json; no other package emits no-store/immutable (grep finds only mfCache.ts). README line 16 advertises 'MF asset cache-header policies (resolveMfAssetCacheHeaders)' and FORK-DIVERGENCE.md:120 states they 'now live in @modern-js/server-runtime-extensions' — confirmed code-vs-docs drift. Fork-owned (mfCache.ts and the static.ts MF-serving block are both fork-added). The 'regression' framing is fair: the prior fork prod server (modernServer.ts) applied no-store to manifests; the current architecture serving them via static.ts does not. Re-wiring vs delete-and-correct-docs both viable; neither breaks vanilla Modern.js (which has no MF manifest serving in upstream static.ts).

### server-runtime-extensions#4 [MAJOR/correctness/small-fix] server.telemetry.slo config is silently dropped by the live plugin — SLO alerts never fire

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:1928`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/core/src/types/config/server.ts:374`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/prod-server/src/server/index.ts:274`

**Evidence:** TelemetryRegistry fully implements SLO alerting (telemetry.ts:71-77 options, 595-605 thresholds, 669-704 maybeEmitSloAlert) and the config surface exposes server.telemetry.slo (server-core config server.ts:374). But injectTelemetryPlugin constructs the registry without the slo option (telemetry.ts:1928-1937 passes only service/module/environment/samplingRate/flushIntervalMs/maxBatchSize/maxQueueSize/redactionKeys), so queueUtilizationWarnThreshold/queueDroppedWarnThreshold/alertCooldownMs are read by nobody and no onAlert sink exists. The dead Server harness DID wire it with a logger sink (prod-server/src/server/index.ts:274-285) — direct evidence the extraction lost parity. SLO behavior is only exercised in tests that construct the registry by hand (prod-server/tests/telemetry.test.ts:191).

**Direction:** Pass telemetryConfig.slo through to the TelemetryRegistry constructor in injectTelemetryPlugin with an onAlert handler that logs via the server logger/monitors (mirroring the dead harness's sink), and add an assembly-level test asserting an alert fires through the plugin path.

**Verifier:** Verified. TelemetryRegistry implements SLO end-to-end: options.slo (telemetry.ts:71-77), thresholds in constructor (595-605), maybeEmitSloAlert (669-704). Config surface exposes server.telemetry.slo (server-core server.ts: `slo?: ServerTelemetrySloUserConfig`). But injectTelemetryPlugin's registry construction (telemetry.ts:1928-1937) passes only service/module/environment/samplingRate/flushIntervalMs/maxBatchSize/maxQueueSize/redactionKeys — no slo, so onSloAlert is undefined and maybeEmitSloAlert early-returns. The dead harness DID wire slo with a logger onAlert sink (index.ts:274-285), confirming the extraction dropped parity. SLO is only exercised by hand-built registries in prod-server/tests/telemetry.test.ts:191. All accurate and fork-owned. Proposed small-fix (thread telemetryConfig.slo through with a logger/monitors sink) is low-risk and does not touch vanilla behavior.

### server-runtime-extensions#5 [MAJOR/security/rewrite] Runtime fallback signal endpoint is enabled by default, unauthenticated, with attacker-controlled rate-limit keys — an unauthenticated canary kill switch

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:1974`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:1702`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:1540`

**Evidence:** When canary is enabled, autopilot defaults on (telemetry.ts:1974 `autopilot?.enabled ?? true`) and the signal endpoint defaults on (telemetry.ts:1989 `enabled ?? true`); auth requires explicit opt-in (telemetry.ts:1517 `enabled === true`); trust policy defaults are allow-all (normalizeRuntimeFallbackTrustPolicy: empty allowedApps/allowedEntryOrigins lists skip both checks, telemetry.ts:1665-1685; enforceRuntimeDigest defaults false). So any client that can reach the server can POST to /_modern/contract-gates/runtime-fallback and persist a failing 'runtime-mf-fallback-health' gate (telemetry.ts:1839-1874), forcing the canary orchestrator into rollback and holding it there by re-posting (failureHoldMs renewable). The rate limit cannot mitigate this: the source key is `${appName}@${entryOrigin}` derived entirely from the attacker's payload (telemetry.ts:1722), so rotating appName bypasses both the 30/min window and dedupe fingerprints (telemetry.ts:1702-1708). Additionally, the auth token compare is non-constant-time (`token !== authConfig.expectedValue`, telemetry.ts:1540), and /_modern/runtime/status discloses queue stats, exporter health, gate names, trust-policy shape and endpoints with no auth unless signal auth is enabled (telemetry.ts:2062-2126).

**Direction:** Make the endpoint fail closed: require auth (or a non-empty trust policy) to enable runtimeFallbackSignal, or flip the default to enabled:false. Rate-limit by connection/remote address as a floor in addition to payload-derived keys. Use crypto.timingSafeEqual for token comparison. Gate /_modern/runtime/status behind the same auth by default.

**Verifier:** Attack chain verified end-to-end in telemetry.ts. Defaults: autopilot enabled (1974 `?? true`), signal endpoint enabled (1989 `?? true`), auth opt-in only (1517 `enabled === true`), trust policy allow-all (normalizeRuntimeFallbackTrustPolicy yields empty allowedApps/allowedEntryOrigins which skip the length>0 guards at 1665-1685; enforceRuntimeDigest defaults false at 1599). Unauthenticated POST to /_modern/contract-gates/runtime-fallback persists a failing gate (persistRuntimeFallbackContractGate 1839-1874); ContractGateAutopilot.start syncs it into the orchestrator (addRequiredContractGate + setContractGate, contractGateAutopilot.ts:98/104) and collectFailures pushes contract_gate_failed -> rollback. Rate-limit sourceKey is `${appName}@${entryOrigin}` derived from payload (1722), and dedupe fingerprint includes appName (1702-1708), so rotating appName bypasses both. Non-constant-time compare confirmed (1540 `token !== authConfig.expectedValue`). /_modern/runtime/status discloses queue/exporter/gate/trust-policy/endpoint detail with auth enforced only when signal auth is enabled (2069-2074). All fork-owned. Caveat for accuracy: the endpoint registers ONLY when server.telemetry.canary.enabled is set (1947), itself opt-in — so this is default-on once canary is configured, not on a stock app; the title's 'enabled by default' is correct in that bounded sense. Severity kept major (not critical) given that precondition. timingSafeEqual + fail-closed defaults + remote-addr rate-limit floor are all sound, vanilla-safe hardening.

### server-runtime-extensions#8 [MAJOR/correctness/rewrite] MF remote CSS warmup caches forever in prod — stale/broken CSS links after any remote redeploy

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/moduleFederationCss.ts:309`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/moduleFederationCss.ts:238`
