**Evidence:** injectModuleFederationCssPlugin computes `warmupPromise = isProd() ? collectDirectRemoteModuleFederationCss(pwd) : undefined` once at onPrepare (moduleFederationCss.ts:309-312) and every request reuses it (line 319). Remote manifests are fetched over HTTP from live remotes whose CSS asset names are content-hashed; after a remote redeploys, the host SSR keeps injecting the old (now 404) CSS URLs and misses new ones until the host process restarts. A transient timeout at boot (1.5s default, line 12) likewise pins an empty/partial list for the process lifetime because per-remote fetch errors are swallowed (lines 275-282). The comment says it mirrors injectResourcePlugin's warmup, but that warms local files which only change on host redeploy — remote manifests are mutable independent of the host, which is the whole point of Module Federation. In dev the opposite extreme: every request sequentially re-fetches all remote manifests (lines 238-283, awaited in a for-loop), adding up to remotes*1500ms to SSR latency.

**Direction:** Replace the boot-time-forever cache with a TTL/stale-while-revalidate cache (e.g., serve cached list, refresh in background every N seconds, keep last-good on fetch failure), and parallelize the per-remote fetches with Promise.allSettled. This also fixes the boot-failure-pins-empty-list case.

**Verifier:** Verified. injectModuleFederationCssPlugin computes `warmupPromise = isProd() ? collectDirectRemoteModuleFederationCss(pwd) : undefined` once in onPrepare (moduleFederationCss.ts:310-312) and every request awaits `warmupPromise || collect(...)` (319-322), so in prod the remote CSS list is pinned for the process lifetime. Per-remote fetch errors are swallowed (try/catch at 260-282, warn+continue), so a boot-time timeout (DEFAULT_REMOTE_MANIFEST_TIMEOUT=1500, line 12) pins an empty/partial list forever. Remote manifests are fetched over HTTP from content-hashed remotes, so after a remote redeploy the host injects stale/404 CSS until restart — accurate. Dev path: warmupPromise is undefined, so each request re-runs collectDirectRemoteModuleFederationCss which loops remotes sequentially with await (238-283), adding ~remotes*timeout latency per SSR — accurate. Entire module is fork-added (A). The comment 'mirroring injectResourcePlugin warmup' is real but the analogy is flawed since remote manifests mutate independent of the host. Major is fair for MF-SSR correctness. TTL/stale-while-revalidate + Promise.allSettled direction is reasonable and self-contained.

### server-runtime-extensions#2 [MINOR/correctness/move] workerLane config surface (EPIC-7) is advertised but silently ignored by every reachable server path

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/core/src/types/config/server.ts:175`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/prod-server/src/libs/runtimeFallbackWorkerLane.ts`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:259`

**Evidence:** server-core's fork-added config hunk defines ServerTelemetryCanaryRuntimeFallbackSignalWorkerLaneUserConfig (server.ts:175-192, 'Optional worker-lane pilot for runtime fallback snapshot persistence'), and runtimeFallbackWorkerLane.ts implements off-main-thread gate persistence honoring MODERN_RUNTIME_FALLBACK_WORKER_LANE. But the only code that reads workerLane config is the dead Server harness (prod-server/src/server/index.ts:377-418, 633-674). The live injectTelemetryPlugin's RuntimeFallbackSignalConfig (telemetry.ts:259-268) has no workerLane field and persistRuntimeFallbackContractGate (telemetry.ts:1839-1874) always persists on the calling thread. A user setting server.telemetry.canary.autopilot.runtimeFallbackSignal.workerLane.enabled=true gets nothing — no warning, no behavior. The package README even acknowledges this oddity: 'MODERN_RUNTIME_FALLBACK_WORKER_LANE is consumed by the @modern-js/prod-server legacy harness ... not by this package.' Bonus divergence-shrink: deleting the unimplemented option removes a fork hunk from the M-tagged upstream file server.ts.

**Direction:** Decide the feature's fate: either move runtimeFallbackWorkerLane.ts into runtime-extensions and wire it into injectTelemetryPlugin's persist path (making config real and shrinking the prod-server fork surface), or delete the worker-lane module, its test, the workerLane config type in server-core types, and the README note. Do not ship config that is a no-op.

**Verifier:** Verified mechanically. server-core/types/config/server.ts:175 defines workerLane on the runtime-fallback-signal config and the dedicated interface ServerTelemetryCanaryRuntimeFallbackSignalWorkerLaneUserConfig directly below it (fork-added hunk; upstream server.ts had no telemetry block). runtimeFallbackWorkerLane.ts (A) implements off-thread persistence. The only reader of workerLane config is the dead harness (index.ts:377-418, 633-674). The live RuntimeFallbackSignalConfig type (telemetry.ts:259-268) has NO workerLane field and persistRuntimeFallbackContractGate (telemetry.ts:1839-1874) always writes via gateSnapshotStore on the calling thread. README lines 56-57 admit the env var is consumed only by the legacy harness. All accurate and fork-owned. Severity downgraded to minor: the option's interface documents @default false and is described as a 'pilot' — it is opt-in and off by default, so the real-world impact is a no-op config rather than a default-on misbehavior. Direction is sound; deleting the type also removes a fork hunk from the M-tagged server.ts, slightly shrinking divergence.

### server-runtime-extensions#6 [MINOR/testing/consolidate] Unit-test suites for runtime-extensions live in prod-server/tests — partly verbatim duplicates, partly the only coverage

Worse, substantial coverage exists ONLY in the consumer package: SLO alerts (prod-server telemetry.test.ts:191), startup health check (:224, :248), the entire TelemetryCanaryOrchestrator suite (:273-393), and the entire ContractGateAutopilot behavior suite (sync/staleness/expiry) — runtime-extensions' own tests cover only the http snapshot store (contractGateSnapshotStore.test.ts has a single test). ### server-runtime-extensions#7 [MINOR/correctness/small-fix] Live telemetry lane has no shutdown path: final flush on exit and dev-restart teardown were lost in the extraction

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:607`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/prod-server/src/server/index.ts:462`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/server/src/createDevServer.ts:88`

**Evidence:** TelemetryRegistry starts an unref'd flush interval in its constructor (telemetry.ts:607-613) and injectTelemetryPlugin starts canary/autopilot pollers in onPrepare (telemetry.ts:2201-2218), but nothing ever calls registry.shutdown(), canaryOrchestrator.stop() or autopilot.stop() — telemetry.ts has no process.on/beforeExit handler and the server plugin API has no close hook. Consequences: (1) on SIGTERM/exit, up to flushIntervalMs (default 1s) plus any backlogged envelopes are silently dropped — registry.shutdown() with its final flush (telemetry.ts:998-1011) exists but is dead in the live path; (2) each dev-server (re)creation re-runs applyPlugins (createDevServer.ts:88, dev.ts:108/131), creating a fresh registry + pollers while old ones keep flushing/evaluating in-process. The deleted-in-all-but-name Server harness had a proper close() doing exactly this teardown (prod-server/src/server/index.ts:462-473), confirming the parity loss.

**Direction:** Have injectTelemetryPlugin register a disposer: hook nodeServer close in prod-server applyPlugins (it already has the nodeServer handle) or install a once process 'beforeExit'/'SIGTERM' final-flush; ensure re-running setup tears down the previous registry/orchestrator/autopilot before creating new ones.

**Verifier:** Verified. TelemetryRegistry starts an unref'd flush interval in its constructor (telemetry.ts:607-613); injectTelemetryPlugin starts orchestrator/autopilot pollers in onPrepare (2201-2218). No registry.shutdown()/orchestrator.stop()/autopilot.stop() is ever invoked in the live path (grep: telemetry.ts has only the shutdown() definition at 998 and no process.on/beforeExit/SIGTERM). The ServerPlugin hook surface (server-core types/plugins/plugin.ts:37-52) offers prepareWebServer/fallback/prepareApiServer/afterMatch/afterRender/afterStreamingRender plus base Hooks — no close/dispose hook — so the plugin cannot self-deregister. registry.shutdown() with final flush exists (998-1011) but is dead in the live path. Dead harness had close() doing exactly this teardown (index.ts:462-473). Dev re-create: createDevServer.ts:88 re-runs applyPlugins per invocation; timers are unref'd (so they won't pin the process) but old registry/pollers do keep running until GC, causing duplicate flush/evaluation during a session. Minor is appropriate (bounded telemetry-data loss on exit; resource churn on dev restart). Fork-owned. Direction (nodeServer-close disposer in prod-server applyPlugins, or process beforeExit) is vanilla-safe.

### server-runtime-extensions#9 [MINOR/architecture/refactor] telemetry.ts is a 2,221-line monolith fusing telemetry, canary orchestration, and contract-gate signal-endpoint domains

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/index.ts:39`

**Evidence:** telemetry.ts is 43% of the package (2,221 of ~5.2k lines) and contains at least six separable concerns: envelope model + redaction (lines 26-420), OTLP/VictoriaMetrics exporters (495-559), TelemetryRegistry queue/SLO (561-1012), TelemetryCanaryOrchestrator (1014-1273), metrics wrapper (1343-1407), and the ~850-line runtime-fallback-signal subsystem (auth/trust/rate-limit/dedupe/persist + endpoints, 1409-2141) that is contract-gate domain logic (it writes to contractGateSnapshotStore) rather than telemetry. index.ts re-exports 35 symbols from this single module; the canary orchestrator type-imports back from contractGateAutopilot.ts creating a two-way conceptual knot. By contrast the rest of the package is cleanly one-concern-per-file (env.ts, mfCache.ts, moduleFederationCss.ts, contractGateSnapshotStore.ts, contractGateAutopilot.ts). Package cohesion overall is acceptable (telemetry+gates is one cluster, MF server helpers a second, both registered through the same prod-server assembly seam), but this file is where future drift and merge pain will concentrate. Note also @modern-js/server-core in dependencies is used type-only (all imports are `import type`) and could be a peer/dev dependency.

**Direction:** Split telemetry.ts into exporters.ts, registry.ts, canary.ts, metricsWrapper.ts, runtimeFallbackSignal.ts and plugin.ts (injectTelemetryPlugin assembly), keeping index.ts re-exports stable so consumers are untouched. Move runtimeFallbackSignal next to the contract-gate modules it actually belongs with. Demote @modern-js/server-core to a peerDependency or type-only devDependency.

**Verifier:** Verified. telemetry.ts is 2,221 lines, by far the largest file (src total ~3,411; 43% of the ~5.2k-line package incl. tests). The six concerns are all present and separable as described: envelope/redaction, OTLP/VM exporters, TelemetryRegistry+SLO (561-1012), TelemetryCanaryOrchestrator (1014+), metrics wrapper, and the ~850-line runtime-fallback-signal subsystem (1409-2141) which is contract-gate domain logic (it writes via gateSnapshotStore). index.ts re-exports ~36 symbols from this one module (lines 39-75). server-core IS used type-only: every import from @modern-js/server-core in the package is `import type` (telemetry.ts:2-8, moduleFederationCss.ts:2-6); the other occurrences in contractGateSnapshotStore.ts:75-76 are string literals in a builtin-alias Set, not imports — so the peer/devDependency suggestion is valid. Minor accurately reflects this as an architecture/maintainability concern. One phrasing correction: the two-way knot is contractGateAutopilot.ts type-importing TelemetryCanaryOrchestrator (line 7) while telemetry.ts value-imports ContractGateAutopilot (line 15) — the finding states it slightly backwards ('orchestrator type-imports back') but the circular coupling is real. Fork-owned; index-stable split is merge-safe.

### server-runtime-extensions#10 [MINOR/correctness/small-fix] Pluggable stateStore modules resolve via require() from the framework package, breaking bare-specifier stores under pnpm

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/contractGateSnapshotStore.ts:372`

**Evidence:** resolveContractGateSnapshotStore loads user-configured stores with a bare `require(modulePath)` (contractGateSnapshotStore.ts:376). Relative paths are resolved against appDirectory (resolveStoreModulePath, lines 288-303), but bare package specifiers (e.g. stateStore.module: 'my-gate-store') resolve from @modern-js/server-runtime-extensions' own location — in the esm-node build this is a createRequire(import.meta.url) shim (scripts/rslib/src/index.ts:29-35), so resolution starts inside the framework's package directory. Under pnpm's strict (non-hoisted) node_modules layout, packages installed by the app are not visible from there, so any npm-published custom snapshot store fails to load with a misleading 'Failed to load stateStore.module' error.

**Direction:** Resolve bare specifiers from the app: use `createRequire(path.join(appDirectory, 'package.json'))` (or require.resolve with `paths: [appDirectory]`) before falling back to local resolution. Add a test installing a fixture store package under a temp app dir.

**Verifier:** Verified. resolveContractGateSnapshotStore loads user stores with `mod = require(modulePath)` (contractGateSnapshotStore.ts:376). resolveStoreModulePath (288-303) resolves '.'-prefixed/absolute paths against appDirectory but returns bare specifiers unchanged, so require() resolves them from the framework package's own location. The esm-node build injects a createRequire shim (rslib config shims.esm.require:true), anchoring resolution to runtime-extensions' dir; under pnpm strict node_modules an app-installed custom store would not be visible, failing with the 'Failed to load stateStore.module' message (line 379). All accurate and fork-owned (A file). Minor is appropriate (niche: requires a published bare-specifier custom store under pnpm; relative-path and built-in 'http' stores work). Direction (require.resolve with paths:[appDirectory] / createRequire from app package.json) is correct and vanilla-safe.

## toolkit-create  (11 confirmed)

The ultramodern workspace generator is functional and has decent manifest/snapshot tests, but it carries roughly a thousand lines of dead or landmine code left over from a removed demo topology ('workspace'/'records'/'actions'), and its flagship `--vertical` flow generates a page that fails its own `pnpm check` (verified by running the built generator). Several CLI affordances (--workspace flag) are documented but wired to nothing, the create flow and generated postinstall scripts silently run sudo package installs and hit the live npm registry, and version pins are duplicated between versions.ts and static template files with comment-only discipline. The root architectural weakness is that ~4k lines of generated TS/TSX live as untooled inline template literals, which is exactly how the undeclared-identifier bug shipped unnoticed.

### toolkit-create#1 [CRITICAL/correctness/small-fix] Generated vertical page references undeclared `supportedLanguages` — every `--vertical` workspace fails typecheck and would crash at render

**Files:** `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/demo-components.ts:278`

**Evidence:** createRemotePage emits `{supportedLanguages.map(code => (...))}` (demo-components.ts:278) but the generated file only destructures `const { i18nInstance, language } = useModernI18n();` and has no other import/declaration of `supportedLanguages`. Verified by running the built generator: `addUltramodernVertical({name:'catalog'})` produces verticals/catalog/src/routes/[lang]/page.tsx whose line 47 uses the bare identifier with no declaration. `useModernI18n()` does return `supportedLanguages` (packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/runtime/context.tsx:77-85), so the value exists but is never destructured. The generated package tsconfig includes src (package-json.ts:365-377) and scripts.typecheck runs tsgo --noEmit, so `pnpm check` in the generated workspace fails with TS2304; at runtime it is a ReferenceError. No test catches it: workspace-content.test.ts snapshots only shell-frame/route-head/validate-script/api.ts, and tests/integration/create-ultramodern-workspace/tests/index.test.ts:1518-1560 only asserts file existence and package.json shape.

**Direction:** In createRemotePage, destructure `supportedLanguages` from useModernI18n() (`const { i18nInstance, language, supportedLanguages } = useModernI18n();`). Add a regression test that typechecks (or at least greps for undeclared identifiers in) a generated vertical page — ideally add the catalog [lang]/page.tsx to the content-snapshot fixture set.

**Verifier:** Verified. demo-components.ts:272 destructures only { i18nInstance, language } from useModernI18n() while line 278 emits bare `supportedLanguages`; no import or declaration reaches the generated [lang]/page.tsx (the const in ultramodern-route-head.tsx.handlebars:9 is module-private to a different file, not exported). useModernI18n does return supportedLanguages (plugin-i18n context.tsx — actual lines are 57 and 314, not 77-85 as cited; immaterial). Generated app tsconfig includes src (package-json.ts:365-377) and typecheck runs effect-tsgo --noEmit (package-json.ts:43-44,418), so pnpm check fails with TS2304 and SSR render throws ReferenceError. Confirmed no test coverage: workspace-content.test.ts snapshots only shell-frame/route-head/validate-script/catalog effect api.ts; the create-ultramodern-workspace and create-tailwind integration tests for --vertical assert only file existence and package.json shape, never building or typechecking. createRemotePage is the only vertical page path (write-workspace.ts:195), so every --vertical workspace is affected. Proposed one-line fix is correct and fork-local.

### toolkit-create#2 [MAJOR/dead-code/delete] Dead demo-topology branches keyed on magic ids 'workspace'/'records'/'actions' — unreachable in default scaffold and verified to generate broken workspaces when a vertical happens to use those names

**Files:** `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/demo-components.ts:319-686`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/routes.ts:43-196`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/locales.ts:48-281`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/effect-api.ts:522-605`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/write-workspace.ts:239-253`, `packages/toolkit/create/templates/app/action-queue-store.ts`

**Evidence:** The default scaffold always uses `initialVerticals: WorkspaceApp[] = []` (write-workspace.ts:324) and createVerticalDescriptor only ever emits exposes './Route' and './Widget' (descriptors.ts:103-106), so the branches for `app.id === 'workspace'` exposes './Header'/'./Highlights'/'./DirectoryPanel'/'./Footer' (demo-components.ts:372-460), './RecordPage'/'./ActionQueue'/'./StartAction'/'./StatusBadge'/'./ActionReviewPage'/'./ActionSuccessPage' (demo-components.ts:319-326,470-598), the hardcoded route tables for domains workspace/records/actions (routes.ts:43-196), the full en+cs demo locale copy (locales.ts:48-281), and createEffectDomainOperations stems 'actions'/'records' (effect-api.ts:530-580) are unreachable. Worse, they are landmines: verified by running the built generator — `addUltramodernVertical({name:'records'})` writes src/components/vertical-components.tsx importing `@<scope>/workspace/Highlights` and `@<scope>/actions/StartAction` (write-workspace.ts:241-246 → createRecordsRemoteComponents at demo-components.ts:618-686), packages that do not exist and are not dependencies → hard build failure; it also writes phantom demo routes /workspaces, /records/[slug], /unavailable the user never asked for. A vertical named 'actions' gets an action-queue-store.ts nothing imports (write-workspace.ts:247-253). The duplicated ~60-line createHydratedRemote template (demo-components.ts:104-157 vs 624-685) exists only to serve this dead lane.

**Direction:** Delete the demo-trio branches wholesale: the workspace/records/actions cases in createRemoteExposeComponent and createRemoteEntry, createRecordsRemoteComponents, the records/actions hooks in writeApp, the workspace/records/actions route tables in createRouteOwnedI18nPaths, the actions/records/workspace entries in generatedLocaleResources (keep commonLocaleMessages + createFallbackLocaleMessages), the actions/records cases in createEffectDomainOperations, and templates/app/action-queue-store.ts. Every vertical should go through the single generic path regardless of its name.

**Verifier:** Verified. Default scaffold uses initialVerticals=[] (write-workspace.ts:324) and createVerticalDescriptor only emits ./Route+./Widget exposes (descriptors.ts:103-106), making the workspace Header/Highlights/DirectoryPanel/Footer branches (demo-components.ts:372-460), the RecordPage/ActionQueue createRemoteEntry branches (319-327), and the records/actions expose branches (470-598) unreachable. Landmines confirmed in code: writeApp's id==='records' branch (write-workspace.ts:241-246) writes createRecordsRemoteComponents importing @scope/workspace/Highlights and @scope/actions/StartAction (demo-components.ts:629-630) — packages that don't exist and aren't dependencies, failing the tsgo typecheck/build; id==='actions' writes an action-queue-store.ts nothing imports (247-253); routes.ts:43-196 generates phantom /workspaces, /records/:slug, /unavailable route-meta and alias pages for those domain names; locales.ts:48-281 demo copy and effect-api.ts:530-580 actions/records stems confirmed. Duplicated ~60-line createHydratedRemote confirmed (104-157 live shell copy vs 624-685 dead-lane copy). One correction to the proposed direction: createFallbackLocaleMessages reads generatedLocaleResources[lang].workspace/.records (locales.ts:283-299), so those entries must have their referenced strings inlined into the fallback before wholesale deletion. Fork-local, no upstream-merge impact.

### toolkit-create#3 [MAJOR/correctness/rewrite] `--workspace` flag is parsed and documented (and recommended in an error message) but never honored

**Files:** `packages/toolkit/create/src/index.ts:487`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/index.ts:312-319`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/index.ts:354-368`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/locale/en.ts:45-46`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/locale/zh.ts:41-42`

**Evidence:** `'--workspace'` appears only in the optionWithoutValue set (index.ts:487) so it is swallowed by arg parsing; no code path checks `args.includes('--workspace')`. Yet locale strings document it ('Use workspace protocol for @modern-js dependencies', en.ts:45-46, example6 at en.ts:65) and the hard-failure path in readBleedingDevFrameworkVersionFromRegistry tells the user to 'Pass --workspace to use local workspace protocol dependencies' (index.ts:315) — advice that does nothing. The only working spelling is `--ultramodern-package-source=workspace` (resolveWorkspacePackageSource → hasExplicitUltramodernPackageSource, index.ts:354-368). Related confusion: detectUltramodernPackageSource defaults strategy to 'workspace' for non-bleedingdev installs (index.ts:242-243) only for resolveWorkspacePackageSource to override it to 'install' unless the flag was explicit — a misleading two-phase resolution.

**Direction:** Wire `--workspace` as a real alias that forces strategy 'workspace' (skipping resolveInstallBackedPackageSource), or remove it from locale strings and the registry error message. Collapse the detect/resolve double-resolution into one function with an explicit default.

**Verifier:** Verified. The only code occurrence of '--workspace' is the arg-swallowing optionWithoutValue set (index.ts:487); grep confirms no args.includes('--workspace') anywhere. en.ts:45-46/65 and zh.ts:41-42/61 document it; the hard-failure path of readBleedingDevFrameworkVersionFromRegistry (index.ts:312-319) recommends 'Pass --workspace...' which does nothing, leaving an offline user stuck despite following the printed remedy. The two-phase resolution is accurately described: detectUltramodernPackageSource defaults non-bleedingdev installs to 'workspace' (index.ts:242-243), then resolveWorkspacePackageSource overrides to install-backed resolution unless --ultramodern-package-source=workspace was explicit (354-368). All fork-added (upstream baseline index.ts/en.ts have none of this). Proposed direction (wire alias or remove the references) is safe and fork-local.

### toolkit-create#4 [MAJOR/security/delete] create CLI and generated postinstall scripts silently install git with sudo and clone GitHub repos during `pnpm install`

**Files:** `packages/toolkit/create/src/index.ts:391-420`, `packages/toolkit/create/template-workspace/scripts/bootstrap-agent-skills.mjs:44-77`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/package-json.ts:265-266`

**Evidence:** installGitForGeneratedProject (index.ts:391-420) runs `sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y git` / `sudo dnf` / `sudo yum` / `brew install git` with stdio inherit and no confirmation, as part of every default `create <name>` run (index.ts:636). The identical sudo-install logic is duplicated in the generated workspace at template-workspace/scripts/bootstrap-agent-skills.mjs:44-77, which the generated root package.json runs on **postinstall** (`node ./scripts/bootstrap-agent-skills.mjs --postinstall && node ./scripts/setup-agent-reference-repos.mjs`, package-json.ts:265-266) — so a plain `pnpm install` in a generated project can attempt sudo system-package mutation and clones multiple GitHub repos (rstackjs/agent-skills, module-federation/agent-skills, TechsioCZ/skills per contracts.ts:693-714). A scaffolding tool escalating to sudo from an npm lifecycle hook is a supply-chain and consent problem, and breaks non-interactive/sudo-less CI.

**Direction:** Delete the auto-install paths in both places: if git is missing, fail with actionable instructions (the error message already exists). Move repo cloning out of postinstall into an explicit `pnpm skills:install` step (the script already exists as a named script); postinstall should at most print a hint.

**Verifier:** Verified. installGitForGeneratedProject (index.ts:391-420) runs `sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y git` / sudo dnf / sudo yum / brew with stdio inherit and no prompt, invoked unconditionally on every default create via initializeGeneratedGitRepository (index.ts:636). The same logic is duplicated in template-workspace/scripts/bootstrap-agent-skills.mjs:45-79 (cited 44-77, close enough) and runs from the generated root package.json postinstall (package-json.ts:265-266) along with setup-agent-reference-repos.mjs; clone sources confirmed at contracts.ts (rstackjs/agent-skills, module-federation/agent-skills, TechsioCZ/skills). Two softeners the finding omits: ULTRAMODERN_SKIP_AGENT_SKILLS/ULTRAMODERN_AGENT_SKILLS env opt-out exists (bootstrap script lines 13-15), and in --postinstall mode clone failures are warned-and-skipped (line 249) — but installGit() runs before both checks' effect on it and still attempts sudo, so the core consent/CI claim stands. Fork-owned (file added by fork). Proposed deletion is safe.

### toolkit-create#5 [MAJOR/correctness/small-fix] Generated Effect client silently drops operationContext/traceparent/locale — the operation-context contract surface it generates is never wired

**Files:** `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/effect-api.ts:399-461`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/runtime/effect-client/index.ts:48-52`

**Evidence:** createEffectClient generates a client options interface advertising `locale`, `operationContext`, `traceparent` (effect-api.ts:399-404) and every helper (listX/getX/createX/readiness) threads `operationContext: options.operationContext ?? <group>OperationContexts.<op>` into the options (effect-api.ts:413-461) — but the generated `create<X>Client` passes only `{ baseUrl: ... }` to makeEffectHttpApiClient (effect-api.ts:406-411), discarding everything else. plugin-bff's makeEffectHttpApiClient supports exactly this via `EffectHttpApiClientOptions.requestContext: RequestContextInput` ({headers, locale, traceparent, operationContext}, packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/runtime/effect-client/index.ts:48-52 and packages/server/create-request/src/requestContext.ts:7-12), which maps to request headers (index.ts:213-236). Net effect: generated workspaces export rich OperationContexts (effect-api.ts:194-219) and a contract claiming propagatedHeaders ['traceparent','x-correlation-id',...] (createEffectRequestContextContract, effect-api.ts:507-520), but no header is ever attached — the ADR-0005-style operation-context propagation is dead on arrival in scaffolded apps.

**Direction:** Generate `makeEffectHttpApiClient(api, { baseUrl: ..., requestContext: { locale: options.locale, operationContext: options.operationContext, traceparent: options.traceparent } })` in create<X>Client, and add the generated client file to the content-snapshot fixtures so the wiring is pinned.

**Verifier:** Verified end to end. createEffectClient emits an options interface with locale/operationContext/traceparent (effect-api.ts:399-404); every helper threads operationContext into the create<X>Client options (413-461); but create<X>Client passes only { baseUrl } to makeEffectHttpApiClient (406-411), discarding the rest. plugin-bff's makeEffectHttpApiClient supports exactly this via EffectHttpApiClientOptions.requestContext (effect-client/index.ts:48-57) which createRequestContextHeaders maps onto request headers (index.ts:209-246); RequestContextInput shape confirmed (create-request/requestContext.ts:7-12). Generated OperationContexts (effect-api.ts:194-219) and the propagatedHeaders contract (507-520) confirmed, so the generated workspace advertises propagation that never happens. The cited generator file is fork-added; the supporting plugin-bff effect-client file is also fork-added (A in baseline diff). Proposed fix is a small fork-local change.

### toolkit-create#6 [MAJOR/dead-code/delete] appendEffectSharedApiContract is never called and the scaffolded shared-effect-api package is an orphan stub carrying an unused plugin-bff dependency

**Files:** `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/add-vertical.ts:73-96`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/effect-api.ts:230-243`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/package-json.ts:481-488`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/write-workspace.ts:311-315`

**Evidence:** Grep shows appendEffectSharedApiContract (add-vertical.ts:73-96) has zero callers — addUltramodernVertical never invokes it; vertical Effect contracts instead live in each vertical's own shared/effect/api.ts (write-workspace.ts:217-237). Its `service` parameter is untyped (implicit any, add-vertical.ts:73), a sign the function predates the per-vertical design and escapes strictness. Consequently the scaffolded packages/shared-effect-api ships forever as a stub (`export const sharedEffectApiPackage = { scope: 'external-effect-api-contracts' }`, effect-api.ts:239-243) with a dependency on @modern-js/plugin-bff (package-json.ts:481-488) that nothing imports — appDependencies adds only shared-contracts and shared-design-tokens to apps (package-json.ts:147-148), and createEffectServiceEntry's default contractImportPath pointing at shared-effect-api (effect-api.ts:248) is always overridden by both call sites (write-workspace.ts:226-231).

**Direction:** Delete appendEffectSharedApiContract and the unused contractImportPath default. Either drop packages/shared-effect-api from the scaffold (and from sharedPackages in descriptors.ts:48-64, the topology, and the manifest test) or actually wire it as the cross-vertical contract home — the current half-state generates a misleading package in every workspace.

**Verifier:** Verified. appendEffectSharedApiContract (add-vertical.ts:73-96) has zero callers repo-wide (grep; not re-exported from ultramodern-workspace/index.ts or used in tests), and its `service` param is indeed untyped. addUltramodernVertical writes per-vertical contracts via writeApp instead. The scaffolded packages/shared-effect-api ships the stub `export const sharedEffectApiPackage = { scope: 'external-effect-api-contracts' }` (effect-api.ts:239-243 via write-workspace.ts:311-315) with an @modern-js/plugin-bff dependency (package-json.ts:481-488) that no generated code imports (app deps add only shared-contracts/shared-design-tokens, package-json.ts:147-148). createEffectServiceEntry's default contractImportPath (effect-api.ts:248) is dead — minor correction: there is exactly one call site (write-workspace.ts:226-231), not 'both call sites'; it serves both the scaffold and add-vertical flows and always overrides the default. Fork-owned; deletion direction is safe but should also update sharedPackages (descriptors.ts:48-64) and the manifest/integration assertions that list the package.

### toolkit-create#7 [MAJOR/testing/small-fix] Default create path (and version.test.ts smoke) depends on live npm registry lookup of @bleedingdev/modern-js-create@latest

**Files:** `packages/toolkit/create/src/index.ts:285-320`, `packages/toolkit/create/tests/version.test.ts:97-145`

**Evidence:** When the running create package is not named @bleedingdev/modern-js-create (i.e. the in-repo @modern-js/create, package.json name) and no explicit --ultramodern-package-version/--ultramodern-package-source is passed, resolveWorkspacePackageSource → resolveInstallBackedPackageSource → readBleedingDevFrameworkVersionFromRegistry executes `npm view @bleedingdev/modern-js-create@latest ultramodern.frameworkVersion --json` (index.ts:297-303), failing hard offline (index.ts:312-319). tests/version.test.ts 'built CLI resolves workspace template for default scaffold' (lines 97-145) spawns the built CLI with no flags and no MODERN_CREATE_ULTRAMODERN_FRAMEWORK_VERSION env (the env var is referenced nowhere else in the repo per grep), so the unit test requires network access to npm — and the same spawn also runs initializeGeneratedGitRepository, reaching the sudo-git-install path if git is absent. The other tests (workspace-content/manifest) correctly pin packageSource and stay deterministic.

**Direction:** Set MODERN_CREATE_ULTRAMODERN_FRAMEWORK_VERSION (or pass --ultramodern-package-version) in version.test.ts so the suite is hermetic, and consider defaulting the repo-local package to workspace strategy instead of dialing out to the BleedingDev registry when run from a monorepo checkout.

**Verifier:** Core claim verified with one evidence correction. package.json name is @modern-js/create (not @bleedingdev/modern-js-create), so the default scaffold path resolveWorkspacePackageSource → resolveInstallBackedPackageSource → readBleedingDevFrameworkVersionFromRegistry runs `npm view @bleedingdev/modern-js-create@latest ultramodern.frameworkVersion --json` (index.ts:297-303) and hard-exits offline (312-319). version.test.ts 'built CLI resolves workspace template for default scaffold' (96-147) spawns the built CLI with no flags and sets no env, so it inherits whatever the runner has — nothing in the package test config or CI workflows sets MODERN_CREATE_ULTRAMODERN_FRAMEWORK_VERSION, making the unit test network-dependent; the spawn also reaches initializeGeneratedGitRepository's sudo path if git is absent. CORRECTION: the claim 'the env var is referenced nowhere else in the repo per grep' is false — tests/integration/create-tailwind, create-ultramodern-workspace, and create-bff-runtime all set MODERN_CREATE_ULTRAMODERN_FRAMEWORK_VERSION for their own spawns (which is in fact the precedent the proposed fix should copy). The conclusion (this unit test is non-hermetic) is unaffected. Fork-owned.

### toolkit-create#8 [MAJOR/duplication/consolidate] Version pins duplicated between versions.ts and static template files, guarded only by a comment

**Files:** `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/versions.ts:1-5`, `packages/toolkit/create/template-workspace/pnpm-workspace.yaml:30`, `packages/toolkit/create/template-workspace/AGENTS.md:9`, `packages/toolkit/create/template-workspace/README.md.handlebars:56`

Alternatively add a versions-lockstep unit test that greps the template trees for every pin in versions.ts.

**Verifier:** Verified. versions.ts:1-5 comment mandates manual lockstep; TANSTACK_ROUTER_VERSION '1.170.15' (versions.ts:6) is re-pinned verbatim as a pnpm override in template-workspace/pnpm-workspace.yaml:30 (overrides win over package.json deps in generated workspaces, so a versions.ts bump silently leaves a conflicting forced resolution); PNPM_VERSION '11.5.3' (versions.ts:30) is hardcoded prose in AGENTS.md:9 and README.md.handlebars:56 even though README is a rendered handlebars template that already receives pnpmVersion/nodeVersion (copyRootTemplate, write-workspace.ts:328-334) and .mise.toml.handlebars uses the placeholders correctly. No lockstep test exists (grep over tests/ found nothing). Fork-owned (all files fork-added). Both proposed remedies (templatize or add a lockstep test) are fork-local; the lockstep-test variant is the lower-risk option.

### toolkit-create#9 [MAJOR/architecture/refactor] ~4k lines of generated TS/TSX maintained as inline template literals while a dedicated templates/ mechanism exists — the root cause that let the undeclared-identifier bug ship

**Files:** `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/demo-components.ts`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/effect-api.ts`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/module-federation.ts:22-237`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/fs-io.ts:17-26`, `packages/toolkit/create/templates/app/shell-frame.tsx`

**Evidence:** The package uses three template mechanisms: (1) template-workspace/ copied with a regex {{placeholder}} renderer (fs-io.ts:129-141), (2) templates/ per-file readFileTemplate/renderFileTemplate (fs-io.ts:17-26) used for shell-frame.tsx, route-head, and the 6 workspace scripts, and (3) giant inline TS template literals for everything else — demo-components.ts (696 lines), effect-api.ts (670), module-federation.ts (489), much of package-json.ts. Inline literals get no syntax checking, linting, or formatting as code, which is precisely how the `supportedLanguages` bug (finding 1) and the stray-tab indentation in createShellRemoteComponents output (demo-components.ts:166-171 emits tab-indented lines) shipped. The mostly-static bodies — modern.config.ts (module-federation.ts:43-236, ~190 lines with a handful of interpolations), the MF config files, the Effect server entry and client — are exactly the shape the templates/*.handlebars mechanism already handles, as the workspace-content test's own 'risk class' taxonomy acknowledges.

**Direction:** Move the large code-shaped generated files (modern.config.ts, module-federation.config.ts shell/remote, effect server entry, effect client, remote/shell pages) into templates/*.handlebars with named placeholders, keeping TS functions only for data-driven JSON (contracts, topology, package.json). Add the rendered versions to the content-snapshot fixtures. This also shrinks the generator source by roughly a third.

**Verifier:** Core claim verified, with the headline number overstated. The three coexisting mechanisms are exactly as described: copyRootTemplate regex renderer (fs-io.ts:129-141), readFileTemplate/renderFileTemplate for templates/ (fs-io.ts:17-26; used for shell-frame, route-head, shared-contracts index, and 6 workspace scripts), and inline literals for the rest. File sizes confirmed: demo-components.ts 696, effect-api.ts 670, module-federation.ts 489, package-json.ts 495. The stray-tab emission is real (demo-components.ts:168-173 emit tab-indented lines inside createShellRemoteComponents output), and the workspace-content.test.ts header does define a 'risk class' taxonomy distinguishing verbatim/rendered/pure-codegen files. CORRECTION: the literal-heavy cited files total ~1.9-2.5k lines, not ~4k, so 'shrinks the generator source by roughly a third' is optimistic. The causal link to finding 1 is fair (an inline TSX literal gets no syntax/identifier checking; a checked-in .tsx template would not have either unless linted, so the direction should include linting/snapshotting the templates). Fork-owned; refactor is fork-local and has no upstream-merge cost since upstream's template/ tree was deleted by the fork.

### toolkit-create#10 [MINOR/dead-code/consolidate] Always-empty public-surface stubs: assets and content sources that are computed, deleted, and asserted but can never exist

**Files:** `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/public-surface.ts:96-100`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/public-surface.ts:135-160`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/public-surface.ts:30-34`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/routes.ts:10-25`

**Evidence:** workspaceAssetsForApp returns `{}` unconditionally (public-surface.ts:135-140) yet writeApp iterates it (write-workspace.ts:186-190) and rewriteWorkspaceAssetsForApp rm-forces the publicSurfaceManagedSourceAssetPaths files (config/public/robots.txt etc., public-surface.ts:30-34, 142-160) that nothing ever writes; the validate-script template then asserts they don't exist. createPublicSurfaceContentSources returns `[]` unconditionally (public-surface.ts:96-100). Because privateAppRoutePublicness `{public:false, indexable:false}` is spread into every generated route (routes.ts:10-25 and all branches), createPublicRouteMetadata is always [] at scaffold time, so routeEntries/concreteUrlPaths in the generated contract are always empty and sitemap.xml/site.webmanifest are never in the output-files list (public-surface.ts:112-119). The runtime script in the generated workspace can recompute these when users flip routes to public, so the workspace-side machinery is fine — but the generator-side computation is constant-folding dead code.

**Direction:** Delete workspaceAssetsForApp/createPublicSurfaceContentSources and inline their constant results; replace the managed-asset rm/assert dance with a one-line tombstone list shared with the validate template. Keep generate-public-surface-assets.mjs (the runtime side) as is.

**Verifier:** Verified. workspaceAssetsForApp returns {} unconditionally (public-surface.ts:135-140) yet writeApp iterates it (write-workspace.ts:186-190); rewriteWorkspaceAssetsForApp rm-forces publicSurfaceManagedSourceAssetPaths (public-surface.ts:30-34, 142-160) which nothing generator-side ever writes; createPublicSurfaceContentSources returns [] (96-100); privateAppRoutePublicness {public:false,indexable:false} is spread into every route via `base` (routes.ts:10-26 and all branches), so createPublicRouteMetadata is always [] at scaffold time and routeEntries/concreteUrlPaths are constant-empty, with sitemap.xml/site.webmanifest never in output files (112-119). The managed-path list also feeds the validate script (workspace-scripts.ts:115-116) whose template asserts the private-surface invariants. One nuance: the rm-force in rewriteWorkspaceAssetsForApp has a live tombstone purpose when add-vertical reruns over a user-modified workspace, which the proposed direction already preserves via the shared tombstone list. Correctly scoped as generator-side constant-folding dead code; minor is the right severity. Fork-owned.

### toolkit-create#11 [MINOR/hygiene/small-fix] CLI help/locale hygiene: dead example keys, never-printed options, always-true guards, undocumented flag, and misbranded --version output

**Files:** `packages/toolkit/create/src/index.ts:91-140`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/locale/en.ts:45-75`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/locale/zh.ts:41-72`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/write-workspace.ts:46`

**Evidence:** showHelp wraps every fork option in `if (localeKeys.help.optionX)` guards (index.ts:102-135) that are always true since localeKeys is initialized from EN_LOCALE (locale/index.ts:7); en/zh both carry empty placeholder keys example7-example11 (en.ts:66-70) and example3/optionWorkspace that are never printed; `--ultramodern-package-registry` is parsed (index.ts:252-254,477) but absent from help. version.message hardcodes '@bleedingdev/modern-js-create version:' (en.ts:75, zh.ts:71) even though this exact code ships as @modern-js/create (package.json name) — `--version` misidentifies the package, and tests/version.test.ts:88-95 pins the wrong-name output. write-workspace.ts:46 imports createAppLocaleMessages but never uses it.

**Direction:** Drop the always-true guards and empty example keys, document --ultramodern-package-registry or remove it, render the version line from the actual package.json name instead of a hardcoded alias, and remove the unused import.

**Verifier:** Verified on every sub-claim. localeKeys comes from i18n.init → getObjKeyMap (i18n-utils), i.e. a key-path string map, so every `if (localeKeys.help.optionX)` guard in showHelp (index.ts:102-135) is an always-truthy string. example7-example11 are empty placeholders in both en.ts:66-70 and zh.ts and are never printed; example3 and optionWorkspace exist in both locales but showHelp never prints them. --ultramodern-package-registry is parsed (index.ts:252-254, optionWithValue at 476) and absent from help. version.message hardcodes '@bleedingdev/modern-js-create version:' (en.ts:75, zh.ts) while the package ships as @modern-js/create (package.json name; upstream baseline said '@modern-js/create version:' — the misbranding is a fork edit), and version.test.ts:90-93 pins the misbranded output. write-workspace.ts:46 imports createAppLocaleMessages and never uses it (only createAppPublicLocaleMessages is called). Caveat for the direction: the bleedingdev branding is plausibly intentional for the published alias package, so deriving the version line from the runtime package.json name (as proposed) is the right fix rather than hardcoding either name. Fork-owned.

## plugin-runtime-fork-lane  (11 confirmed)

The fork lane inside packages/runtime/plugin-runtime is ambitious but mostly coherent: the provider registry and router-lifecycle snapshot machinery are documented, tested, and genuinely decouple SSR from react-router. The main problems are (1) a fully dead 4-file SSR scaffold under src/ssr that imports modules which do not exist, (2) a ~310-line hand-rolled Helmet SSR collector with semantics that diverge from the react-helmet-async client path it pairs with, (3) residual TanStack contamination (hard deps + a 320-line proxy alias) that contradicts the fork's own ADR-0017 decontamination goal, and (4) substantial feature hunks (hydration-script ordering, MF CSS, router snapshots) spread across six upstream SSR files instead of behind the existing htmlModifiers/collector seams, which is the biggest upstream-merge pain multiplier in the repo.

### plugin-runtime-fork-lane#1 [MAJOR/dead-code/delete] src/ssr is a dead, non-compiling scaffold of the legacy SSR architecture — delete it

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/ssr/index.node.tsx`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/ssr/serverRender/renderToString/entry.ts:27-37`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/ssr/serverRender/renderToStream/buildTemplate.after.ts`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/ssr/serverRender/types.ts`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/tsconfig.json:12`

**Evidence:** All four files are fork-added (A vs 8a744c1b31). entry.ts imports './buildHtml', './render', './loadable', './styledComponent', './type', '../prefetch', '../tracker', '../utils' — none exist (renderToString/ contains only entry.ts; src/ssr has exactly 4 files total). tsconfig.json:12 explicitly excludes "src/ssr/**/*" so it never typechecks; no exports-map entry, no import anywhere in src/ or tests/ (grep across the package returns zero referencers). The tests/ssr/serverRender/* test files that mirror these paths actually import src/core/server and src/router/runtime/lifecycle, not src/ssr. This is the abandoned 'ultramodern ssr scaffold proof' (commit 321c336d2d) left behind after the real work landed in src/core/server.

**Direction:** Delete the entire src/ssr directory (4 files, ~418 lines) and remove the "exclude": ["src/ssr/**/*"] line from tsconfig.json. Keep the tests/ssr/serverRender suites — they test live code and just need no change.

**Verifier:** Verified. All four files are A vs 8a744c1b31; renderToString/ holds only entry.ts whose imports ('./buildHtml', './render', './loadable', './styledComponent', '../tracker', '../../prefetch') resolve to nothing; tsconfig.json:12 excludes src/ssr/**/*; zero referencers in src/ or tests/ (tests/ssr/* import src/core/server and src/router/runtime/lifecycle, e.g. tests/ssr/serverRender/renderToString/entry.test.ts imports src/core/server/string/ssrData). Commit 321c336d2d 'fix ultramodern ssr scaffold proof' confirmed. Evidence is actually understated: the rslib build still emits dist/esm/ssr/serverRender/renderToString/entry.mjs with unresolvable relative imports, so broken modules land in the build output (package.json has no 'files' allowlist). Deletion is pure fork-owned cleanup; no upstream-merge impact.

### plugin-runtime-fork-lane#2 [MAJOR/correctness/rewrite] Hand-rolled Helmet SSR collector duplicates react-helmet-async with divergent semantics and render-phase mutation

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/exports/head.ts:137-310`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/react/wrapper.tsx:49`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/context/helmetContext.ts`

**Evidence:** head.ts (M file, fully rewritten by the fork) reimplements Helmet server collection by hand: on the server the component mutates the per-request context during render (head.ts:300-306) and returns null, never rendering AsyncHelmet — even though wrapper.tsx:49 already wraps the tree in <HelmetProvider context={helmetContext}> with the same slot object, which would give real react-helmet-async server semantics for free. Divergences from the client path it hydrates against: (a) collectHelmetProps appends meta/link/script tags into hidden __metaTags/__linkTags accumulators with no name/property dedup (head.ts:248-251,282), so nested Helmet overrides emit duplicate tags in SSR HTML while the client dedupes after hydration; (b) <html>/<body> children are silently dropped (head.ts:174-176) although react-helmet-async maps them to html/body attributes — SSR loses e.g. <html lang>; (c) the accumulate-on-render design double-collects if React replays a subtree (Suspense retry in streaming SSR); (d) the state object stuffs 10 private __-prefixed fields into HelmetServerState via `as any` (head.ts:229-294), and priority/toComponent are stubs (head.ts:69,99,256). Mixing this Helmet with a direct react-helmet-async Helmet writes the same helmetContext.helmet field from two competing mechanisms, last-writer-wins.

**Direction:** Delete the manual collector (~250 lines): on the server render AsyncHelmet through the already-installed HelmetProvider and read context.helmet in getHelmetData (core/server/helmet.ts already consumes HelmetServerState). Keep only the fork-owned per-request helmetContext slot. If react-helmet-async server rendering is genuinely broken under React 19/streaming, document that in the file header and add a dedup pass + html/body-children support plus a test pinning nested-Helmet override semantics against client behavior.

**Verifier:** Verified. Upstream head.ts was a 15-line react-helmet re-export; the fork rewrote it (+320 lines). Server path mutates the per-request helmetContext during render and returns null (head.ts:297-307) while wrapper.tsx:49 already installs <HelmetProvider context={helmetContext}> with the same slot (core/context/helmetContext.ts). Confirmed: no name/property dedup in the __metaTags/__linkTags accumulators (248-251, 281-282); <html>/<body> children silently dropped on server only (174-176) while the client AsyncHelmet honors them — real SSR/hydration divergence; non-idempotent accumulate-on-render; 10 __-prefixed fields smuggled through `as any` (272-294); priority/toComponent stubs (69, 99, 116, 256). Mixing with a direct react-helmet-async Helmet does write the same context.helmet field from two mechanisms. Direction's hedge is appropriate: react-helmet-async server collection under React 19/streaming may be the reason the collector exists, so the rewrite must be validated there first.

### plugin-runtime-fork-lane#3 [MAJOR/architecture/delete] @modern-js/runtime still hard-depends on TanStack despite the ADR-0017 decontamination; @tanstack/router-core is a dead dependency

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/package.json:236-237`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/exports/tanstack-router.ts:21-121`

**Evidence:** package.json (M) declares hard deps @tanstack/react-router 1.170.15 and @tanstack/router-core 1.171.13. router-core is imported nowhere in src/, types/, or scripts/ (grep exits 2) — a dead dep, and version-skewed against react-router 1.170.15. react-router is imported only by the deprecated alias src/exports/tanstack-router.ts (A file), which value-re-exports ~100 symbols plus ~200 lines of Symbol.for/Proxy delegate machinery for 6 compat bindings. The FORK-DIVERGENCE ledger and commit d1b1dcf1a5 state the goal was to 'single-source TanStack in plugin-tanstack, decontaminate core context', yet every consumer of @modern-js/runtime — including pure react-router apps — installs two TanStack packages because of one deprecated subpath kept 'so the published subpath keeps resolving'.

**Direction:** Remove @tanstack/router-core from dependencies now (dead). Then either drop the ./tanstack-router subpath in the next major (the compat bindings already live in @modern-js/plugin-tanstack/runtime), or reduce it to a types-only re-export plus throw-with-migration-message shim so @tanstack/react-router can move to an optional peerDependency.

**Verifier:** Verified. package.json:236-237 (fork hunk) declares @tanstack/react-router 1.170.15 and @tanstack/router-core 1.171.13 as hard deps; @tanstack/router-core has zero imports anywhere in src/ or types/ (only stale, git-untracked dist/types/router/runtime/tanstack/rsc/*.d.ts from before commit d1b1dcf1a5 reference it — src/router/runtime/tanstack no longer exists). Version skew vs react-router confirmed. @tanstack/react-router is imported only by the fork-added deprecated alias src/exports/tanstack-router.ts (~100 re-exported symbols + Symbol.for/Proxy delegates). ADR-0017 exists (docs/super-app-rfc-adr/ADR-0017-superapp-composition-router-framework-coordination.md) and d1b1dcf1a5's message matches the quote. Removing router-core is safe (plugin-tanstack carries its own pin; react-router brings it transitively); the peer-dep move is correctly scoped to a major.

### plugin-runtime-fork-lane#4 [MAJOR/correctness/refactor] Built-in router installation decided by regex-sniffing the user's modern.runtime.ts source

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/router/cli/index.ts:51-65`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/router/cli/index.ts:127-148`

**Evidence:** Fork hunk in the upstream CLI plugin: hasRouterConfigInRuntimeFile reads the runtime-config file and tests /router\s*:/ against its raw text (index.ts:62) to decide whether to inject the '@modern-js/runtime/router/internal' runtime plugin (index.ts:143-152). False positives: any comment or string containing 'router:' (e.g. '// router: handled by plugin X') installs the react-router runtime into the entry. False negatives: runtime config composed via spread/imported objects never matches, silently leaving routing uninstalled — a hard-to-debug failure. It also does a synchronous readFileSync inside the _internalRuntimePlugins hook, re-run per entrypoint per build.

**Direction:** Replace the source sniff with a deterministic signal: the normalized config check (hasUserRouterConfig) already exists at index.ts:130-132 — make runtime.router in modern.config.ts the documented way to opt in for non-file-route entrypoints, or parse the runtime file's exported config with es-module-lexer/AST instead of a substring regex. At minimum cache the read and anchor the regex to an actual config key position.

**Verifier:** Verified and fully fork-owned: upstream injected the runtime router plugin only `if (nestedRoutesEntry)`; the fork added hasRouterConfigInRuntimeFile (index.ts:52-67) testing /router\s*:/ against the raw runtime-config source, consumed in _internalRuntimePlugins (133-135, 143-154). False positives (any 'router:' substring in comments/strings) and false negatives (config composed via spread/imports) are real; findExists+readFileSync re-runs per entrypoint per hook invocation. Note for fairness: the regex path only widens installation relative to upstream (built-in route entrypoints always install), so the false-negative failure mode applies to custom-entry apps configuring router solely in modern.runtime.ts. Direction (use normalized config / AST parse, cache the read) is sound and fork-local.

### plugin-runtime-fork-lane#11 [MAJOR/upstream-compat/consolidate] Shrink divergence: hydration-script/asset/MF-CSS injection hunks spread across six upstream SSR files instead of the existing htmlModifiers seam

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/server/string/index.ts:171-189`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/server/string/ssrData.ts:87-133`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/server/string/loadable.ts:30-216`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/server/stream/afterTemplate.ts:48-149`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/server/stream/beforeTemplate.ts:80-100`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/server/requestHandler.tsx:292-310`

**Evidence:** The fork already owns the right primitives (scriptOrder.ts, federatedCss.ts, lifecycle.ts — all A files), but the call sites are large M-hunks woven through upstream rendering files: createReplaceChunkJs's remove-placeholder/inject-early/restore dance (string/index.ts:171-189) is duplicated in buildShellAfterTemplate (afterTemplate.ts:67-82); hydration-script appending appears in both ssrData.ts:125-129 and afterTemplate.ts:139-148; MF CSS link injection appears in beforeTemplate.ts:80-86, beforeTemplate.worker.ts:67-72 and loadable.ts emitStyleAssets; loadable.ts grew ~190 fork lines (orderHydrationScriptChunks, getMatchedRouteChunks). Both render paths already expose an extension seam the fork uses elsewhere: htmlModifiers (consumed at string/index.ts generateHtml and threaded through buildHtml callbacks in the stream path). Every upstream change to these six files now conflicts with fork feature logic rather than a one-line seam.

**Direction:** Extract the injections into a fork-owned module (e.g. core/server/forkHtmlModifiers.ts) that, given runtimeContext + resource, returns BuildHtmlCb[] (hydration scripts, early-entry script reordering, MF CSS links), and register it once per render path via the existing htmlModifiers array. Upstream files then shrink to passing runtimeContext where they don't already. The remaining irreducible hunks (router-snapshot reads in requestHandler/ssrData) stay, but the conflict surface drops by hundreds of lines.

**Verifier:** Verified. All flagged hunks are fork-owned: scriptOrder.ts (+52), federatedCss.ts (+29), lifecycle.ts are A files, and the call sites are fork hunks (diff stat: requestHandler.tsx 190 changed lines, beforeTemplate.ts +109, loadable.ts 161 changed, afterTemplate.ts +60, string/index.ts +47, ssrData.ts +48; the remove-placeholder/inject-early/restore dance in string/index.ts:171-190 is fork-added per diff and duplicated in afterTemplate.ts:68-82; hydration scripts appended in both ssrData.ts #getSSRDataScripts and afterTemplate.ts:142-145; MF CSS in beforeTemplate.ts:80-86, beforeTemplate.worker.ts:67-72, loadable.ts:340). Two corrections: (1) loadable.ts gained ~131 added lines, not ~190; (2) the htmlModifiers seam is consumed ONLY by the string path today (string/index.ts:103,150-157) — requestHandler.tsx:200 initializes it empty and the stream path never reads ssrContext.htmlModifiers, so the consolidation needs a small fork-owned addition to thread htmlModifiers into the stream buildHtml callback lists, not a zero-cost registration. With that adjustment the direction is sound and is the single biggest upstream-merge pain saver in this area; severity major stands.

### plugin-runtime-fork-lane#5 [MINOR/architecture/refactor] Router provider contract silently drops provider registryHooks; the same six hooks are declared in four places

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/router/runtime/internal.ts:39-55`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/router/runtime/plugin.tsx:86-93`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/router/runtime/plugin.node.tsx:92-97`

**Evidence:** The wrapper plugin in internal.ts declares the six RouterExtendsHooks in registryHooks and then invokes the resolved provider via `pluginFactory(userConfig).setup?.(api)` (internal.ts:55) — only setup is called, so the provider plugin's own registryHooks object is ignored. The identical six-hook block is nevertheless redeclared in plugin.tsx:87-93, plugin.node.tsx:93-97, and again in @modern-js/plugin-tanstack's plugin.tsx:324-331 and plugin.node.tsx:349-356. Today this works only because all five declarations are byte-identical; a provider that ships a seventh hook will find it silently unregistered, and the duplication invites drift.

**Direction:** Make the wrapper merge the factory plugin's registryHooks into its own before setup (or change RouterProviderFactory to return { registryHooks, setup } and have the wrapper compose them), then delete the four redundant declarations so the hook set is defined once in hooks.ts.

**Verifier:** Core claim verified: the wrapper in internal.ts declares the six hooks (26-33) and invokes only `pluginFactory(userConfig).setup?.(api)` (line 42), discarding the resolved provider's own registryHooks; the hook block is redeclared in plugin.tsx:86-93, plugin.node.tsx:93-98, and plugin-tanstack plugin.tsx:324-331 / plugin.node.tsx:349-356. Evidence correction: the declarations are NOT byte-identical — plugin.node.tsx declares only four hooks (onAfterHydrateRouter and onBeforeHydrateRouter missing), i.e. the predicted drift has already happened; nothing breaks only because the wrapper's six-hook superset is the effective registration. Severity downgraded to minor: RouterProviderPlugin is typed as RuntimePlugin<{extendHooks: RouterExtendsHooks}>, so the six-hook set is the typed contract and a 'seventh hook' provider is already outside it; today's impact is duplication/drift, not breakage. The merge-registryHooks + single-source-in-hooks.ts direction remains correct and fork-local.

### plugin-runtime-fork-lane#6 [MINOR/duplication/consolidate] beforeTemplate.worker.ts is a 151-line copy of beforeTemplate.ts with a dead conditional

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/server/stream/beforeTemplate.worker.ts:13-27`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/server/stream/beforeTemplate.worker.ts:140-146`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/server/stream/beforeTemplate.ts:43-173`

**Evidence:** The fork-added worker variant duplicates buildShellBeforeTemplate nearly verbatim; the only intended difference (no fs-based style inlining on edge) degenerated into `if (checkIsInline(...)) { return link; } return link;` (worker:142-145) — both branches identical, making checkIsInline (worker:13-27) and the inlineStyles handling entirely dead in this file. Any future fix to the CSS-chunk/MF-CSS logic must now be applied twice (the federatedCss + matchedRouteIds hunks were already copy-synced once).

**Direction:** Collapse to a single shared implementation parameterized by an optional readAsset/inline capability (the node entry passes the fs-backed inliner, the worker entry passes none), mirroring how createReadableStream.worker.ts stays thin upstream. Delete the dead checkIsInline branch either way.

**Verifier:** Fully verified. Fork-added (A) worker file duplicates buildShellBeforeTemplate near-verbatim (same getCssChunks/matchedRouteIds/federatedCss logic); the inline-style branch degenerated into `if (checkIsInline(chunk, inlineStyles)) { return link; } return link;` (worker:140-146), making checkIsInline (worker:13-27) and the inlineStyles config dead in the worker path. The node variant (beforeTemplate.ts:153-167) shows what the branch was supposed to do (readAsset + <style> inlining). Consolidation via an injected readAsset/inline capability is sound and matches the createReadableStream.worker.ts pattern.

### plugin-runtime-fork-lane#7 [MINOR/architecture/refactor] Realm-global router-provider registry conflicts with the SuperApp multi-app premise

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/router/runtime/provider.ts:47-103`

**Evidence:** The v2-keyed Symbol.for registry isolation (commit bb0c77bb17) is well-documented and pinned by tests, but the registry remains global per JS realm: (a) keep-first semantics (provider.ts:69-85) mean that in an MF page hosting multiple independent apps, the first-loaded copy of a provider wins for every app — silent version capture across apps with only a one-time console.warn; (b) there is a single nonDefaultProvider slot (provider.ts:87-100), so two unrelated apps in one realm registering different non-default routers throw, which contradicts the fork's own SuperApp/MicroVerticals model of independent verticals sharing a page; (c) resolveRouterProvider reads the shared map, so app B can resolve a factory created by app A's different plugin-tanstack copy.

**Direction:** Scope provider resolution to the runtime instance: register providers into the runtime-context extension slot (extensions.ts already exists for exactly this) with the global registry only as a fallback for module-eval-time registration, and replace the single nonDefaultProvider slot with a per-name conflict check so unrelated apps cannot crash each other.

**Verifier:** Verified on provider.ts (fork-added A file): keep-first semantics with one-time console.warn (69-85), single nonDefaultProvider slot that throws on a second different non-default provider (87-100), and resolveRouterProvider reading the shared Symbol.for('@modern-js/runtime:router-providers:v2') map (105-139). The design is deliberate and documented (old-copy isolation, commit bb0c77bb17), but the finding correctly identifies that isolation is per-realm, not per-app. Practical caveat: only two providers exist today (react-router default, tanstack non-default), so the throw scenario needs a third provider or two different non-defaults — latent, hence minor. Direction caveat: registration happens at module-eval time (internal.ts:17; plugin-tanstack runtime entry import side effect), before any runtime context exists, so the proposed context-scoped registry genuinely needs the global registry as the registration buffer — the finding's 'global as fallback' phrasing should be 'global as registration buffer, context-scoped resolution'.

### plugin-runtime-fork-lane#8 [MINOR/architecture/refactor] lifecycle.ts snapshot normalization is over-engineered relative to its four consumers

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/router/runtime/lifecycle.ts:101-214`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/router/runtime/types.ts:69-101`

**Evidence:** InternalRouterRuntimeState and InternalRouterServerSnapshot each carry hydrationScript (singular), hydrationScripts (plural), matchedRouteIds AND matches; createRouterRuntimeState (lifecycle.ts:101-154) re-derives all four at both the state and nested-snapshot level with three layers of ?? fallbacks, and getRouterHydrationScripts (185-201) is a five-way fallback chain. Actual consumers (afterTemplate.ts, ssrData.ts, beforeTemplate.ts, loadable.ts, requestHandler.tsx, Entry) only ever read plural hydrationScripts, matchedRouteIds, statusCode, errors and routerData. No producer sets the singular hydrationScript (grep: only lifecycle.ts and type defs reference it). RouterServerPrepareResult.redirect (types.ts:99) is never consumed — applyRouterServerPrepareResult ignores it. @modern-js/plugin-tanstack additionally redeclares these types by hand (plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/types.ts:55-73) instead of importing from '@modern-js/runtime/context', so the contract is maintained twice.

**Direction:** Collapse to one canonical snapshot shape: plural-only hydrationScripts, matchedRouteIds derived once at write time; delete the singular field, the dead redirect field, and the double-level re-derivation. Export the types from @modern-js/runtime/context and make plugin-tanstack import them.

**Verifier:** Verified: both InternalRouterServerSnapshot and InternalRouterRuntimeState carry hydrationScript+hydrationScripts+matchedRouteIds+matches (types.ts:69-94, fork-added); createRouterRuntimeState double-derives at state and snapshot level (lifecycle.ts:101-154); getRouterHydrationScripts is a five-way ?? chain (185-201); no in-repo producer sets singular hydrationScript (grep: only lifecycle.ts, both types.ts files); RouterServerPrepareResult.redirect (types.ts:99) is ignored by applyRouterServerPrepareResult (172-183) — tanstack handles redirects via serverRouter.state.redirect/interrupt instead; plugin-tanstack hand-redeclares the types (plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/types.ts:55-73). Direction correction: the context-extension slots use Symbol.for (extensions.ts:16,65), so snapshots written by OLDER published copies of plugin-tanstack/runtime in MF setups can land in the same slot — the singular-field READ tolerance may be deliberate cross-version compat (same concern as the v2 registry key). Safe version of the fix: stop writing the singular field and delete the dead redirect field, but keep read-side normalization (toHydrationScripts) until old copies are out of support.

### plugin-runtime-fork-lane#9 [MINOR/correctness/small-fix] Router cleanup runs before streamed response bodies finish, and swallows all errors

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/server/requestHandler.tsx:292-388`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/router/runtime/lifecycle.ts:216-222`

**Evidence:** The fork hunk wraps the whole request in try { ... return response } finally { await cleanupRouterRuntimeState(context) }. For streaming SSR the returned Response carries a live ReadableStream — the finally clause executes when `return response` is evaluated, i.e. while React is still rendering into the body, so a provider cleanup that disposes the router (plugin-tanstack registers `cleanup: () => serverRouter.serverSsr?.cleanup?.()`, plugin.node.tsx:539) can run against a router still in use by the in-flight render. Separately, cleanupRouterRuntimeState (lifecycle.ts:219-221) is `try { await ...cleanup?.() } catch {}` — failures vanish without reaching the monitors/onError plumbing that the same file's request path uses.

**Direction:** Defer cleanup until body completion for streaming responses (hook it to onAllReady/pipe completion in core/server/stream, or wrap response.body in a stream that triggers cleanup on close/cancel), and report cleanup failures through options.onError/monitors instead of an empty catch.

**Verifier:** Verified, fork-owned (the try/finally + cleanupRouterRuntimeState import are fork hunks in requestHandler.tsx; lifecycle.ts is A). The stream path returns the Response at shell-ready (createReadableStream.worker.ts:54 onShellReady; allReady resolves later at 58), so `finally { await cleanupRouterRuntimeState(context) }` (requestHandler.tsx:385-387) runs while React is still writing into the returned body; plugin-tanstack registers `cleanup: () => serverRouter.serverSsr?.cleanup?.()` (plugin.node.tsx ~539), which can dispose server-SSR state under an in-flight streaming render. Empty catch confirmed (lifecycle.ts:219-221). Direction (tie cleanup to body close/cancel/allReady, route failures through onError/monitors) is correct and fork-local.

### plugin-runtime-fork-lane#10 [MINOR/architecture/move] boundary-debugger (SuperApp dev overlay) is app-flavored code living inside @modern-js/runtime

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/boundary-debugger/index.tsx:45-52`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/package.json:64-67`

**Evidence:** A fork-added 409-line dev overlay (team-boundary highlighting for MicroVerticals) ships as a subpath of @modern-js/runtime — the package with the repo's largest upstream divergence (98 M + 33 A files). It hardcodes Czech/English UI labels ('zobrazit hranice týmů', index.tsx:48-51), localStorage keys and a color palette; its only consumer is the create-workspace template (packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/app-files.ts). Nothing in it touches runtime internals — it imports only `type RuntimePlugin` from '../core'.

**Direction:** Move it to a fork-owned package (e.g. alongside @modern-js/plugin-tanstack or a new @modern-js/superapp-devtools) and update the template import. This removes a whole feature directory plus exports-map entries from the upstream-mirrored package, shrinking future merge surface, and makes the labels injectable instead of hardcoded.

**Verifier:** Verified: 409-line fork-added (A) overlay exported as @modern-js/runtime subpath (package.json:64-67, 173-174); only consumer is packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/app-files.ts; imports only `type RuntimePlugin` from '../core' (index.tsx:2), so it has no real coupling to runtime internals and moves cleanly. Evidence correction: the Czech/English strings (index.tsx:48-51) are DEFAULTS — BoundaryDebuggerPluginOptions.labels (index.tsx:28) already makes labels injectable, so the 'hardcoded labels' point is overstated; localStorage key and palette are likewise overridable defaults/constants. Core architecture claim (app-flavored feature inflating the most upstream-divergent package's export surface) stands; move-to-fork-owned-package direction is sound and shrinks merge surface.

## plugin-i18n-lane  (10 confirmed)

The i18n lane is feature-rich and has real test depth (5 unit suites in-package, ~15 integration suites under tests/integration/i18n), and the SSR no-backend-poll fix from the changeset is genuinely implemented (the 20x100ms poll loop in i18n/utils.ts was removed). The main problems are policy-by-convention rather than plugin-level guarantees: localisedUrls is default-on and build-breaking for upstream-style configs, the ADR-0002 MF skip-list is stamped into every app config instead of living in the plugin, and the node SSR locale default path disagrees with the auto-detection that enables it. The hand-rolled path matcher in shared/localisedUrls.ts has a crash-on-malformed-URL bug and the localize/canonicalize sequence is copy-pasted in three places.

### plugin-i18n-lane#1 [MAJOR/upstream-compat/small-fix] localisedUrls is default-on with an empty map, turning localePathRedirect into a build-breaking change for upstream-style configs

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/shared/localisedUrls.ts:66-78`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/shared/localisedUrls.ts:108-132`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/cli/index.ts:215-247`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/shared/type.ts:14-24`

**Evidence:** resolveLocalisedUrlsConfig(undefined) returns {enabled: true, map: {}} (localisedUrls.ts:73-77), and cli/index.ts:234-246 then runs applyLocalisedUrlsToRoutes, whose validateLocalisedUrls throws for every non-root route missing a map entry (localisedUrls.ts:114-119). Net effect: any vanilla Modern.js app config with localePathRedirect + languages (which cannot know about the fork-only localisedUrls option) fails its build. Every fork fixture that does not use the feature had to add the opt-out — tests/integration/i18n/{app-csr,app-ssr,routes-csr,routes-ssr,custom-i18n-wrapper,mf/*}/modern.config.ts all contain 'localisedUrls: false'. The shared/type.ts JSDoc and .changeset/localised-url-routing.md confirm 'default-on' is intentional, but the cost is paid by every migrating app.

**Direction:** Treat an empty/absent map as disabled: only an explicit object map (or explicit `true`) should enable validation and route expansion. Keep the hard fail-fast for partially-defined maps (missing languages on a defined route), which is the valuable part. This deletes the `localisedUrls: false` line from 7+ fixtures and restores upstream config compatibility.

**Verifier:** Verified. resolveLocalisedUrlsConfig(undefined) returns {enabled:true, map:{}} (shared/localisedUrls.ts:66-78, fork-added file) and the fork-added modifyFileSystemRoutes hook in cli/index.ts:215-247 then runs applyLocalisedUrlsToRoutes, whose ensureLocalisedUrlsForPath throws for every localisable route absent from the map (localisedUrls.ts:108-132). 12 fixture configs carry 'localisedUrls: false'; the diffs of upstream fixtures routes-csr/routes-ssr/app-ssr show exactly one added line each — that opt-out — proving an upstream-style config (localePathRedirect + languages, e.g. routes/[lang]/about) fails the build without it. The changeset .changeset/localised-url-routing.md confirms default-on is intentional, which doesn't excuse breaking upstream configs. One scoping correction: only file-system-routed apps hit the build throw (modifyFileSystemRoutes); self-controlled-route apps don't crash at build, though they still get default-enabled canonical-redirect middleware. Proposed direction (empty/absent map = disabled) restores vanilla compat and eases merges; small caveat: explicit `true` with no map must not re-enable the always-throw validation.
