### plugin-i18n-lane#2 [MAJOR/correctness/small-fix] matchPathPattern crashes on malformed percent-encoding: any request URL like /cs/produkty/%E0%A4%A 500s the server redirect middleware and crashes client Link rendering

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/shared/localisedUrls.ts:343`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/server/index.ts:481-501`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/runtime/Link.tsx:285-309`

**Evidence:** matchPathPattern does params[name] = decodeURIComponent(match[index + 1] || '') (localisedUrls.ts:343) with no try/catch; decodeURIComponent('%E0%A4%A') throws URIError (verified with node). new URL(...).pathname preserves invalid percent sequences raw, so when localisedUrls patterns contain params, the i18n-server-middleware (server/index.ts:481-501 -> buildLocalizedUrl -> resolveLocalisedPath -> matchPathPattern) throws on any crafted request, producing 500s instead of redirect/404. The same code runs in the browser for every <Link> active-state computation via canonicalPath (Link.tsx:290-291), so landing on a malformed URL crashes render. Additionally, normalisePathPattern is applied to concrete pathnames (localisedUrls.ts:337,376,426) even though it rewrites '[x]' to ':x' — a pattern-normalizer misused on runtime paths.

**Direction:** Wrap the decode in a safe helper that returns null (no-match) on URIError, and add a pathname-normalizer that does not apply the bracket->param rewrite. Add unit tests for malformed-encoding requests in localisedUrls.test.ts and a server middleware test asserting next() is called instead of throwing.

**Verifier:** Verified, including by execution: decodeURIComponent('%E0%A4%A') throws URIError and new URL('http://x/cs/produkty/%E0%A4%A').pathname preserves the raw sequence (reproduced in node). matchPathPattern at localisedUrls.ts:343 has no try/catch, nor does any caller. Server chain confirmed: i18n-server-middleware (server/index.ts:481-501, fork hunk) -> buildLocalizedUrl -> resolveLocalisedPath -> matchPathPattern; an async handler throw becomes a 500 instead of redirect/next. Client chain confirmed: Link isActive useMemo (Link.tsx:285-309, fork-added file) calls canonicalPath -> resolveCanonicalLocalisedPath -> matchPathPattern on adapter.location.pathname, so a malformed location crashes render. Precondition (a param-bearing pattern in the map matching the segment) is correctly stated in the finding. The secondary claim about normalisePathPattern's [x]->:x rewrite being applied to concrete pathnames at lines 337/376/426 also verifies. Proposed direction is safe and fork-local.

### plugin-i18n-lane#3 [MAJOR/architecture/consolidate] ADR-0002 MF locale-redirect exemptions are stamped into every app config and 'verified' by string-matching fixture source, instead of being a plugin guarantee

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/module-federation.ts:172-178`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/i18n/mf/test/app-level-mf-ssr-contract.test.ts:41-49`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/i18n/mf/mf-app-provider/modern.config.ts:46-53`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/server/index.ts:341-509`

**Evidence:** ADR-0002 §9 mandates ignoring i18n locale redirect on /mf-manifest.json, /mf-stats.json, /remoteEntry.js. The implementation is: (a) the scaffolder hardcodes the three paths into each generated app's ignoreRedirectRoutes (create module-federation.ts:172-178); (b) test fixtures hand-copy the same list (mf-app-provider/modern.config.ts:50-52); (c) a 'contract' test asserts the fixture's modern.config.ts source text contains the literal strings (app-level-mf-ssr-contract.test.ts:45-48) — a tautology that tests repo content, not behavior. The plugin's own server middleware (server/index.ts) has a fork-added skip for API prefixes and static prefixes but nothing for MF endpoints, so any hand-written provider config silently serves an HTML locale-redirect to remote consumers requesting the manifest.

**Direction:** Move the guarantee into the plugin: export a WELL_KNOWN_MF_ENDPOINTS constant and skip them (or more generally, skip any last path segment containing a file extension) in the i18n-server-middleware/detector handlers, the same way matchesApiPrefix already works. Drop the stamped lists from the scaffolder and fixtures, and replace the source-string contract test with a behavioral test (GET /mf-manifest.json through the middleware returns next(), not a redirect).

**Verifier:** Verified on all four legs: (a) create/src/ultramodern-workspace/module-federation.ts (fork-added) hardcodes '/mf-manifest.json', '/mf-stats.json', '/remoteEntry.js' into generated ignoreRedirectRoutes; (b) tests/integration/i18n/mf/mf-app-provider/modern.config.ts hand-copies the list (fork hunk); (c) app-level-mf-ssr-contract.test.ts:41-49 (fork-added) literally string-matches the fixture source — it tests repo content, not middleware behavior; (d) grep confirms plugin server src has zero MF-endpoint handling while it does have fork-added matchesApiPrefix and static-prefix skips (server/index.ts:401,439). ADR-0002 lines 88-89 mandate the exemptions. Consequence is real: a hand-written provider config without the stamped list serves a 302-to-HTML for manifest fetches. Direction caveat: keep the built-in skip as an explicit well-known constant layered on the existing upstream ignoreRedirectRoutes mechanism rather than a generic 'any path with a file extension' heuristic, which would silently change redirect behavior for unrelated dotted paths.

### plugin-i18n-lane#4 [MAJOR/correctness/small-fix] Node SSR backend default loadPath (./config/public/locales) contradicts the auto-detection that enables it (root ./locales), and the only SSR-content assertion is CI-skipped

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/runtime/i18n/backend/defaults.node.ts:1-4`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/cli/index.ts:60-90`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/i18n/app-ssr/tests/index.test.ts:46-50`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/i18n/app-ssr/modern.config.ts:5-24`

**Evidence:** The fork changed defaults.node.ts loadPath/addPath from './locales/...' to './config/public/locales/...' (verified fork hunk vs 8a744c1b31). But detectLocalesDirectory (upstream logic, cli/index.ts:61-64) auto-enables the backend when JSON exists in root ./locales — the upstream convention. An app with root ./locales gets backend auto-enabled while the node fs-backend reads a path that does not exist, so SSR renders untranslated keys and the client recovers post-hydration over HTTP. The fork's own app-ssr fixture has exactly this shape (root ./locales plus publicDir './locales' to make HTTP work), and its SSR-body assertion only runs when process.env.LOCAL_TEST === 'true' (index.test.ts:47), so CI never validates SSR translation content.

**Direction:** Make the CLI plugin pass the detected locales directory into the runtime config (it already knows which of root/config-public/publicDir matched) and use it as the node loadPath default, instead of a single hardcoded guess; or have the node default try both conventional paths. Un-gate at least one SSR-HTML translation assertion from LOCAL_TEST.

**Verifier:** Core claim verified and empirically REPRODUCED. Fork hunk (commit 321c336d2d 'fix ultramodern ssr scaffold proof') changed defaults.node.ts to './config/public/locales/...' while upstream detectLocalesDirectory (cli/index.ts:61-64, unchanged) auto-enables the backend on root ./locales. Rebuilding the app-ssr fixture with modern.ssg.config.ts on current code renders '<div id="key">key</div>' in dist-ssg HTML; the stale May-18 artifact (pre-change) contained the translated strings. Two evidence corrections that strengthen rather than weaken: (1) 'CI never validates SSR translation content' is wrong — the upstream-unchanged ssg.test.ts asserts translated SSG HTML unconditionally and now FAILS, i.e. this is a red upstream test, not a hidden gap; (2) the LOCAL_TEST gate at index.test.ts:47 is upstream code (the fork hunk there is import reordering only), so the 'CI-skipped assertion' half of the title is not fork-owned — but the fork did un-skip conditionalTest, so the gap claim is mixed. Proposed direction (pass the detected directory into runtime config, or try both conventions) is sound and merge-safe.

### plugin-i18n-lane#5 [MAJOR/upstream-compat/small-fix] react-i18next demoted from optional peer to exact-pinned hard dependency (17.0.8) while the runtime still treats it as optional via dynamic-import-with-fallback

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/package.json:100`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/runtime/i18n/react-i18next.ts:10-25`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/runtime/index.tsx:87-106`

**Evidence:** Upstream lists react-i18next as an optional peerDependency; the fork hunk makes it a regular dependency pinned to exact '17.0.8' (package.json:100) and removes the peer entry. Yet the fork-added react-i18next.ts still does await import('react-i18next') inside try/catch with null fallbacks, and index.tsx keeps a reactI18next:boolean option — machinery that only makes sense for an optional dep. Worse, a hard dep means apps that install their own react-i18next at any other version get two module instances: the plugin renders I18nextProvider/initReactI18next from its pinned copy while app components call useTranslation from theirs, which reads a different React context and throws or silently falls back ('You will need to pass in an i18next instance'). All fork fixtures avoid this only by pinning the app side to the identical 17.0.8 (e.g. tests/integration/i18n/app-ssr/package.json:16), which real apps will not do.

**Direction:** Restore react-i18next as an optional peerDependency (>=17) with a devDependency for tests, keeping the existing dynamic-import fallback; or fully commit to bundling it and delete the try/catch fallback plus the reactI18next toggle. Either way, drop the exact pin.

**Verifier:** Verified via package.json fork diff: upstream had peerDependencies 'react-i18next': '>=15.7.4' with peerDependenciesMeta.optional=true; the fork removed both and added dependencies 'react-i18next': '17.0.8' (exact pin). The fork-added react-i18next.ts keeps try/catch dynamic import with null fallbacks, and the reactI18next:boolean plugin option (runtime/index.tsx:53,72,87-95) is fork-added (absent from baseline index.tsx, which used optional-peer getI18nextProvider). Fixtures pin the app side to identical 17.0.8 (app-ssr/package.json:16 and 6 more). The dual-instance hazard (plugin's pinned copy provides I18nextProvider/initReactI18next, app's differently-versioned copy reads a different React context) is a real failure mode under pnpm/npm. Restoring the optional peer matches upstream and eases merges; keeping the exact pin would also force a plugin release for every react-i18next patch.

### plugin-i18n-lane#6 [MINOR/duplication/consolidate] The strip-language/resolve-localised-path/re-prefix sequence is copy-pasted in three places, and the hand-rolled matcher recompiles every regex on every call

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/runtime/utils.ts:73-97`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/server/index.ts:301-339`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/runtime/localizedPaths.ts:33-53`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/shared/localisedUrls.ts:306-330`

**Evidence:** The fork-added ~15-line block (split segments -> strip leading language -> resolveLocalisedUrlsConfig -> resolveLocalisedPath -> rejoin with language prefix) appears verbatim in runtime/utils.ts:80-96 and server/index.ts:317-336 (both verified fork hunks), with the inverse in localizedPaths.canonicalPath:37-52. Server and client must agree exactly or canonical-redirect loops appear; today they only agree by copy discipline. Separately, compilePathPattern builds a fresh RegExp per pattern per call (localisedUrls.ts:306-330), and resolveLocalisedPath/resolveCanonicalLocalisedPath iterate the whole map (O(routes x languages) regex compiles) on every server redirect check and every <Link> render/active-state computation; matching also has no static-vs-param specificity ordering, so precedence is silently 'object insertion order'.

**Direction:** Add localiseTargetPathname(pathname, language, languages, config) and canonicalTargetPathname(...) to shared/localisedUrls.ts and make runtime/utils.buildLocalizedUrl, server/index.buildLocalizedUrl and localizedPaths.canonicalPath thin callers. Memoize compiled patterns in a module-level Map keyed by pattern string, and sort candidate patterns static-segments-first.

**Verifier:** Verified. The split/strip/resolve/rejoin block is near-verbatim in runtime/utils.ts:79-97 and server/index.ts:317-336 (both confirmed fork hunks), with the inverse in localizedPaths.ts canonicalPath:37-52 (fork-added file); server/client canonical-redirect agreement indeed rests on copy discipline (server/index.ts:490-503 redirects whenever its computation differs from the actual path). compilePathPattern constructs a fresh RegExp per pattern per matchPathPattern call (localisedUrls.ts:306-346, no memoization), and resolveLocalisedPath/resolveCanonicalLocalisedPath scan the entire map x languages per call from both the server middleware and every Link render. First-match Object.entries iteration means precedence is insertion order with no static-vs-param specificity — all confirmed. Proposed consolidation lives entirely in fork-owned files; no upstream-merge hazard. Minor is the right severity.

### plugin-i18n-lane#7 [MINOR/architecture/refactor] routerAdapter duck-types TanStack router internals and calls hooks conditionally despite the fork's own typed seams existing

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/runtime/routerAdapter.tsx:64-80`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/runtime/routerAdapter.tsx:212-226`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/runtime/routerAdapter.tsx:233-267`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/runtime/plugin.tsx:501-507`

**Evidence:** The fork-owned adapter defines an untyped RouterInstance shape (stores.location.get/subscribe, state.matches, subscribe('onBeforeNavigate')) at routerAdapter.tsx:64-80 and re-implements reactive location tracking with a manual store subscription plus a setRouterVersion re-render counter (233-267) — even though plugin-tanstack already publishes typed useLocation/useNavigate/useMatches/Link on context.router (plugin.tsx:501-507) and the fork built a router provider registry + getRouterRuntimeState exactly to avoid ad-hoc coupling. It also violates rules-of-hooks: inReactRouter ? useReactRouterNavigate() : null (212-214) and the conditional contextUseRouter({warn:false}) call (224-226) change hook order if router presence ever changes between renders. The duck-typed field names track @tanstack/router-core 1.171 internals and will drift on TanStack upgrades.

**Direction:** Extend the fork's router provider contract (plugin-runtime provider registry / InternalRouterRuntimeState) with framework-neutral useLocation/useNavigate/Link capabilities that each provider registers, and have useI18nRouterAdapter consume only that. This deletes the RouterInstance duck-typing, the manual subscription block, and the conditional hook calls.

**Verifier:** Verified. routerAdapter.tsx is fork-added (A). RouterInstance duck type with stores.location.get/subscribe, state.matches, subscribe(eventType) at lines 64-80; rules-of-hooks violations at 212-214 (inReactRouter ? useReactRouterNavigate() : null, plus useReactRouterLocation/useReactRouterParams) and 224-226 (conditional contextUseRouter({warn:false}) call); manual subscription + setRouterVersion re-render counter at 227,233-267. plugin-tanstack (fork-added) does publish context.router = { Link, useMatches, useLocation, useNavigate, useRouter } (plugin.tsx ~501-507), and plugin-runtime exports getRouterRuntimeState/registerRouterProvider, so typed seams exist as claimed. Repo pins @tanstack/router-core 1.171.13, matching the drift-risk claim. Mitigating context (not a refutation): router presence rarely flips between renders, so the hook-order hazard is mostly latent. Direction is a fork-internal refactor; no vanilla-behavior risk.

### plugin-i18n-lane#8 [MINOR/correctness/small-fix] Link's declared `prefetch` prop is a no-op on every branch; tests mask the leak with mocks that strip it

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/runtime/Link.tsx:122`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/runtime/Link.tsx:380-397`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/tests/link.test.tsx:49-66`

**Evidence:** LinkBaseProps declares prefetch?: 'intent' | 'render' | 'viewport' | 'none' (Link.tsx:122). For external/no-router targets it is destructured away (323-328, 340-345); for the TanStack and react-router branches it rides {...rest} into the underlying Link, but TanStack's prop is `preload` (no mapping exists) and react-router's Link has no prefetch at all, so the value either does nothing or lands as a meaningless DOM attribute. The unit tests only assert the stripped branches, and the TanstackLink test double itself strips prefetch before spreading (link.test.tsx:52-59), so the behavior gap is invisible to the suite.

**Direction:** Map prefetch -> preload for the TanStack branch ('none' -> false) and strip it from the react-router branch (or wire it to plugin-runtime's PrefetchLink behaviors); alternatively delete the prop until implemented. Add a test against the real prop contract instead of a stripping mock.

**Verifier:** Verified. Link.tsx (fork-added) declares prefetch?: 'intent'|'render'|'viewport'|'none' (line 122); external/no-router branches destructure it away (323-328, 340-345); the TanStack branch spreads {...rest} (389) with no prefetch->preload mapping (TanStack Link's real prop is preload, separately declared at line 123), and the react-router branch likewise forwards it though library-mode react-router Link has no prefetch prop. The TanstackLink test double strips prefetch before spreading (link.test.tsx:50-59), and the only prefetch assertions (332-342 external, 366-379 no-router) cover exactly the branches where Link itself strips it — so the suite cannot detect the no-op. Proposed mapping/removal is fork-local and safe.

### plugin-i18n-lane#9 [MINOR/hygiene/small-fix] Dead jest toolchain in devDependencies and contradictory test-script flags

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/package.json:85`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/package.json:118-125`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/i18n/test-utils.ts:5`

**Evidence:** The package's runner is rstest (rstest.config.mts defines two projects covering all 5 test files), yet the fork hunk adds jest ^30.4.2, ts-jest ^29.4.11 and @types/jest ^30.0.0 as devDependencies (package.json:118-125) with no jest config or jest import anywhere in the package (grep verified). The test script is 'rstest --passWithNoTests' (package.json:85) even though 5 suites exist, so a misconfigured include glob would green-pass silently. tests/integration/i18n/test-utils.ts:5 also keeps a vestigial `export const conditionalTest = test;` alias.

**Direction:** Delete jest/ts-jest/@types/jest from devDependencies, drop --passWithNoTests so an empty discovery fails loudly, and replace conditionalTest with a direct `test` import at the two call sites.

**Verifier:** Core claims verified: fork hunk adds jest ^30.4.2, ts-jest ^29.4.11, @types/jest ^30.0.0 to devDependencies; grep finds zero jest references in the package's src/tests/configs; rstest.config.mts's two projects enumerate exactly the 5 existing suites; test script is 'rstest --passWithNoTests' so an include-glob regression would pass silently. The conditionalTest alias in tests/integration/i18n/test-utils.ts:5 is fork-changed from 'LOCAL_TEST ? test : test.skip' to 'test', i.e. a deliberate un-skip shim, and is vestigial as claimed. Correction to the direction: it has 10+ call sites across mf/app fixtures, not 'two call sites'. Keep as minor hygiene.

### plugin-i18n-lane#10 [MINOR/dx/rewrite] Mixed British/American spelling across one public API surface: localisedUrls/resolveLocalisedPath vs localizePath/useLocalizedPaths/buildLocalizedUrl

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/runtime/index.tsx:296-319`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/shared/localisedUrls.ts:1-10`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-i18n/src/runtime/localizedPaths.ts:1-26`

**Evidence:** All of this naming is fork-owned. The config option and shared module use British spelling (localisedUrls, LocalisedUrlsMap, resolveLocalisedPath, shared/localisedUrls.ts) while the user-facing runtime helpers exported from the same entry use American spelling (localizePath, canonicalPath via localizedPaths.ts, useLocalizedPaths, useLocalizedLocation, buildLocalizedUrl — runtime/index.tsx:310-319). Users must remember which spelling each identifier uses within a single import statement.

**Direction:** Standardize on one spelling for everything new (the helpers' American spelling is the larger public surface), keep the existing config key as an accepted alias for one release with a deprecation note, and rename shared/localisedUrls.ts internals to match.

**Verifier:** Verified and entirely fork-owned: shared/localisedUrls.ts (A) exports British-spelled config surface (localisedUrls option in shared/type.ts, LocalisedUrlsMap, resolveLocalisedPath) while runtime/index.tsx:310-319 exports American-spelled helpers (canonicalPath, localizePath, useLocalizedPaths, useLocalizedLocation, buildLocalizedUrl) from the same entry, and useModernI18n exposes 'localisedUrls' consumed by 'useLocalizedPaths'. Real but cosmetic DX inconsistency; minor is right. Direction caveat: renaming the config key even with a deprecation alias churns 12+ fixture configs, the scaffolder, and docs — 'rewrite' boldness is disproportionate; an alias-only or docs-level convention fix is the cheaper corrected direction.

## bff-lane  (11 confirmed)

The Effect runtime lane itself (adapter/handler/edge/module) is solidly engineered, but the cross-project "hardening" stack is badly out of balance with its consumers: the policy evaluator that ADR-0005 §13 builds the whole story around is enforced only in the fork-added express/koa adapters, while the generated cross-project SDK pins apps to hono|effect lanes where the policy is dead config — producer clients pay the full strict-header tax for checks no default server performs. Two parallel envelope systems (bff-envelope vs data-envelope), a "schemaHash" that hashes route names instead of schemas, a hardcoded operationVersion that can never mismatch, and a dropped Upload client path round out a pattern of contract machinery built ahead of (and partially instead of) working consumers. There is also ~450 lines of verbatim fork-added duplication between create-request browser.ts and node.ts, and ~330 lines of untyped runtime JS embedded as codegen template strings.

### bff-lane#1 [MAJOR/security/rewrite] Cross-project policy is never enforced in the fork's own hono/effect lanes that the generated SDK mandates

**Files:** `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/utils/crossProjectApiPlugin.ts:30`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/utils/crossProjectApiPlugin.ts:58-84`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/server.ts:107-146`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/runtime/hono/adapter.ts:32-47`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/runtime/effect/adapter.ts:97-176`, `packages/server/bff-core/src/security/resolveCrossProjectPolicy.ts:50`, `packages/server/plugin-express/src/plugin.ts:158`, `packages/server/plugin-koa/src/plugin.ts:141`

**Evidence:** The generated cross-project SDK plugin force-enables the policy (`resolvedConfig.bff.crossProjectPolicy = { enabled: true, requireEnvelope: true, ... }`, crossProjectApiPlugin.ts:64-84) and pins `runtimeFramework` to 'hono' | 'effect' (line 30, throwing on mismatch at line 50). But repo-wide grep shows `checkCrossProjectPolicy`/`resolveCrossProjectPolicy` are consumed only by packages/server/plugin-express/src/plugin.ts and plugin-koa/src/plugin.ts — fork-added express/koa adapters reached via the prepareApiServer hook, which plugin-bff/src/server.ts only fires in hono mode (line 107) and which neither HonoAdapter (createHonoRoutes registers handlers with zero policy check) nor EffectAdapter (validates only the separate x-modernjs-data-envelope) participates in. Result: in every configuration the generated SDK permits, the producer server accepts traffic with no envelope/operation-context validation while config claims strict enforcement. ADR-0005 §13 documents express/koa enforcement only, confirming the gap is real, not an oversight in my reading.

**Direction:** Enforce the resolved policy in the fork's own lanes: HonoAdapter.setHandlers and EffectAdapter.registerMiddleware should call resolveCrossProjectPolicy({handlers, requestId, isCrossProjectServer, crossProjectPolicy}) once and prepend a middleware that runs checkCrossProjectPolicy(headers, policy) — both helpers are already framework-agnostic in bff-core/adapter-kit. Alternatively, stop force-enabling the policy in the generated crossProjectApiPlugin and document it as express/koa-only; the current state (config promises deny, server allows) is the worst option.

**Verifier:** Evidence verifies. The policy-forcing block (crossProjectApiPlugin.ts:55-84) and the hono|effect runtime pin are fork hunks (baseline file only set prefix/isCrossProjectServer). Repo-wide grep confirms checkCrossProjectPolicy/resolveCrossProjectPolicy are consumed only by fork-added plugin-express/plugin-koa (both absent at 8a744c1b31), reached via prepareApiServer which server.ts fires only in hono mode (line 107/118). HonoAdapter.setHandlers (createHonoRoutes) and EffectAdapter.registerMiddleware contain zero System-A checks; the effect handler validates only x-modernjs-data-envelope. ADR-0005 §13 confirms express/koa-only enforcement. One overstatement: 'in every configuration the generated SDK permits' — a consumer that ALSO registers plugin-express/koa in hono mode does get enforcement through the bind-bff handler; but the SDK neither requires nor documents that, and the default hono and all effect deployments enforce nothing. Severity downgraded critical→major: per the companion finding the envelope is client-forgeable, so no genuinely protective control is bypassed — the harm is a force-enabled config that is a silent no-op (false assurance, inert version-skew gate), not an exploitable hole. Proposed direction is fork-local (adapters are fork-owned) and does not worsen upstream merges.

### bff-lane#2 [MAJOR/security/rewrite] Cross-project 'security' layer is entirely client-asserted — namespace allowlists and contracts are forgeable by design

**Files:** `packages/server/bff-core/src/security/crossProjectPolicy.ts:89-90`, `packages/server/bff-core/src/security/crossProjectPolicy.ts:159-180`, `packages/server/create-request/src/node.ts:642-654`, `packages/server/create-request/src/browser.ts:583-595`

**Evidence:** The policy evaluator derives the producer namespace from `requestId.split(/[/:.]/)[0]` taken from the JSON envelope header the client itself constructs (crossProjectPolicy.ts:89, 159-180); operation context, schemaHash and operationVersion are likewise computed client-side (create-request node.ts:642-681) with no secret, signature, or binding to authenticated identity anywhere in the chain. Any caller that can read the open-source header format can pass every check, including `allowedNamespaces`. The module lives under src/security/ and ADR-0005 brands it 'cross-project BFF hardening', inviting operators to treat it as a trust boundary it cannot be.

**Direction:** Either (a) rename/document the layer as a version-skew and misconfiguration gate (it is good at that) and move it out of src/security/, or (b) make it a real control by binding namespace to an authenticated channel — e.g. derive the producer identity from mTLS/JWT claims at the server seam and compare against the envelope instead of trusting envelope.requestId. Pick one; the current ambiguity is the risk.

**Verifier:** Evidence verifies exactly. crossProjectPolicy.ts:89-90 derives namespace via requestId.split(/[/:.]/)[0] from the JSON envelope header; the whole evaluation (lines 103-313) reads only client-supplied headers; grep for hmac/sign/secret/jwt across bff-core/src/security and create-request/src finds nothing; node.ts:642-654 and browser.ts construct the envelope client-side with Date.now() and self-asserted origins. The module is fork-added (src/security/ is new) and ADR-0005 brands it 'cross-project BFF hardening'. allowedNamespaces is trivially satisfiable by any caller echoing an allowed requestId. Major is appropriate: it does not weaken anything relative to no policy, but the security/ placement and ADR framing invite operators to treat a forgeable gate as a trust boundary. Both proposed directions are fork-local; renaming exports would touch the fork-added express/koa plugins only.

### bff-lane#3 [MAJOR/correctness/rewrite] schemaHash hashes the route table, not schemas; operationVersion is hardcoded to 1 on both sides so version checks are inert

**Files:** `packages/server/bff-core/src/security/operationContracts.ts:48-63`, `packages/server/bff-core/src/security/operationContracts.ts:94-113`, `packages/server/bff-core/src/security/operationContracts.ts:23`, `packages/server/bff-core/src/client/generateClient.ts:76-81`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/utils/effectClientGenerator.ts:312-325`

**Evidence:** createOperationSchemaHash sha256s only sorted {name, httpMethod, routePath} entries plus requestId (operationContracts.ts:48-63) — changing a zod input/output schema (the actual API contract) does not change the hash, while renaming any handler does. Worse, buildOperationContractMap groups by source module (lines 77-96), so the hash is shared across every operation in a lambda file: adding one unrelated endpoint changes schemaHash for all of them, and with the SDK defaults (requireOperationSchemaHash: true, strict: true) every already-deployed consumer 403s (`operation_schema_hash_mismatch`) until regenerated. Meanwhile DEFAULT_OPERATION_VERSION = 1 (line 23) and both generators hardcode `operationVersion = 1` (generateClient.ts:77, effectClientGenerator.ts:312-315) with no API anywhere to set another value — `operation_version_mismatch` and `requireOperationVersion` machinery can never fire in real usage.

**Direction:** Make the hash mean what its name claims: hash each operation's actual schema (serialize the zod/Effect schema per endpoint) and key contracts per-operation, not per-module. Until that exists, rename to routeTableHash so the gate's real semantics are visible, and delete the operationVersion plumbing (evaluator branches, client validation, generated manifest field) — it is ~15 code paths that cannot ever trigger.

**Verifier:** Core claims verify. createOperationSchemaHash (operationContracts.ts:48-63) sha256s only sorted {name, httpMethod, routePath} entries + requestId — zod input/output schemas never enter the hash. buildOperationContractMap (65-116) groups by filename so all operations in a lambda module share one hash; adding any endpoint to the module rotates every operation's schemaHash. resolveCrossProjectPolicy overlays generated contracts (from the server's current handlers) over user-provided expectedOperationContracts, so a stale consumer's hash 403s with operation_schema_hash_mismatch on policy-enforced servers — note this cascade only manifests in the express/koa lanes since hono/effect never enforce (per finding 1). DEFAULT_OPERATION_VERSION=1 (line 23), generateClient.ts:77 and effectClientGenerator.ts:312-315 both hardcode 1. Two small corrections: (a) operation_version_mismatch is not literally unreachable — an operator can hand-write expectedOperationContracts with version!=1 via the bff.crossProjectPolicy config surface (and adapter-kit/parity.ts exercises +1 in its self-test), but since every client always emits 1 that configuration just denies all traffic permanently, so the mechanism is inert as a versioning gate, which is the finding's point; (b) client-side validateOperationContract only checks field presence, consistent with the claim. Direction is fork-local.

### bff-lane#4 [MAJOR/correctness/rewrite] Fork dropped the Upload client path from generateClient — Upload operator endpoints get broken generated clients

**Files:** `packages/server/bff-core/src/client/generateClient.ts:83-118`, `packages/server/bff-core/src/operators/http.ts:235`, `packages/server/create-request/src/browser.ts:648-671`, `packages/server/create-request/src/node.ts:707-722`, `tests/integration/bff-hono/src/routes/upload/page.tsx:74-79`

**Evidence:** Upstream generateClient emitted `createUploader(...)` for handlers with `action === 'upload'`; the fork's rewrite (verified fork hunk vs 8a744c1b31) removed that branch — every handler now gets `createRequest(...)` (generateClient.ts:83-118). The Upload operator still tags handlers with action: 'upload' (operators/http.ts:235) and createUploader is still exported by the runtime, but nothing generates calls to it; a client calling `upload({ files })` hits createRequest's payload switch (data/body/formData/formUrlencoded) where `files` matches nothing and is silently dropped. The bff-hono integration test confirms the breakage was papered over: the page calls the generated `upload(...).catch(() => undefined)` and the actual assertion uploads via raw fetch('/bff-api/upload') instead (upload/page.tsx:53-79). Separately, createUploader itself attaches no envelope/identity/operation-context headers, so even re-wired it would be denied by any policy-enabled server.

**Direction:** Restore createUploader emission for action === 'upload' in generateClient (including requestId/operationContext so it passes the producer policy), or formally drop the Upload client lane: make codegen fail loudly on upload handlers and delete createUploader from create-request. Then make the bff-hono integration test assert the generated client path instead of swallowing its rejection.

**Verifier:** Fully verified, and it is a genuine regression vs upstream. Baseline generateClient.ts has `if (action === 'upload')` emitting createUploader (baseline lines 86-115); the fork's rewritten loop destructures `action` but emits createRequest for every handler with no upload branch. plugin-bff/src/loader.ts imports generateClient from @modern-js/bff-core, so this is the live codegen path. operators/http.ts:235 still tags action:'upload'; createUploader is still exported (node.ts:707, browser.ts:648, runtime/create-request re-export) but nothing generates calls to it. browser createRequest's payload switch handles only data/body/formData/formUrlencoded (browser.ts:508-540) — `files` is silently dropped. The bff-hono upload page calls generated upload() and falls back to raw fetch('/bff-api/upload') on rejection (.catch(() => uploadMockImageViaFetch(mockImage)); click handler swallows with .catch(() => undefined)), so the integration test passes regardless of the generated client working — the 'papered over' characterization is fair. Restoring the upstream branch would also REDUCE divergence from vanilla Modern.js.

### bff-lane#5 [MAJOR/duplication/consolidate] ~450 lines of fork-added policy/identity/envelope logic duplicated verbatim between create-request browser.ts and node.ts

**Files:** `packages/server/create-request/src/browser.ts:124-308`, `packages/server/create-request/src/node.ts:137-321`, `packages/server/create-request/src/browser.ts:59-108`, `packages/server/create-request/src/node.ts:56-103`

**Evidence:** browser.ts grew 179→675 lines and node.ts 173→726 vs baseline. The fork-added core is byte-identical or near-identical in both: buildOperationContext (browser 124-180 / node 137-193), all five error classes (182-241 / 195-254), validateOperationContract (243-295 / 256-308, differing only in the `target` literal), getConfiguredRequest, configure, normalizeRequestOptions, and the header helpers findHeaderKey/readHeader/writeHeader/deleteHeader/toOrigin/isSecuredRequestId (59-108 / 56-103). The package already extracts shared fork logic into transport.ts, traceparent.ts and requestContext.ts, so the seam exists — this duplication is just unfinished consolidation and every policy fix currently needs to land twice (and can drift silently).

**Direction:** Extract a shared module (e.g. src/policyCore.ts) exporting the error classes, header utils, buildOperationContext and validateOperationContract parameterized by { target, getIncomingHeaders }; keep browser/node as thin senders over it, mirroring how transport.ts already works.

**Verifier:** Verified. Line counts match exactly (browser.ts 179→675, node.ts 173→726 vs 8a744c1b31). Diffing browser.ts:124-308 against node.ts:137-321 yields only three trivial differences (a type cast on firstHeaderValue, the 'browser'/'server' target literal, fetch vs Fetch type) across 185 lines covering buildOperationContext, the five error classes, validateOperationContract, and getConfiguredRequest; the header helpers (browser 59-108 / node 56-103) are likewise twinned. The package already has fork-added shared modules (transport.ts, traceparent.ts, requestContext.ts), so the proposed extraction follows the existing seam. All duplicated code is fork-added (baseline files had none of it). Keeping major: this is security-relevant logic where silent drift between targets has real consequences.

### bff-lane#6 [MAJOR/architecture/consolidate] Two parallel, non-interoperable envelope/contract systems; Effect clients emit both, Effect servers validate only one

**Files:** `packages/server/bff-core/src/security/crossProjectPolicy.ts:1-4`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/runtime/data-platform/index.ts:197-199`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/utils/effectClientGenerator.ts:464-514`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/utils/effectClientGenerator.ts:691-765`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/runtime/effect/handler.ts:597-659`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/runtime/effect/operation-context.ts:48-123`

**Evidence:** System A (bff-core/create-request): x-modernjs-bff-envelope + x-operation-id + x-modernjs-bff-operation-context, sha256 route-table hash, enforced only in express/koa. System B (data-platform): x-modernjs-data-envelope with FNV-1a inputHash/selectionHash/scopeKey, enforced only by the Effect handler (handler.ts:597-659). The generated Effect client configures create-request with strict System A defaults (effectClientGenerator.ts:464-514 — requireEnvelope, identityBinding, operationContract) AND attaches the System B data envelope per request (691-765), so every call carries two redundant envelope headers with two different operation-id schemes and two different hash algorithms. The Effect server parses System A headers into EffectContext.operationContext (operation-context.ts:48-123) but never validates them. Each system has its own duplicated trace-context plumbing and namespace concept.

**Direction:** Pick one canonical envelope. Most plausible split: keep data-platform as the Effect-lane contract and stop the Effect client generator from configuring System A (requireEnvelope/operationContract) — those headers are pure overhead there; or unify by making the Effect handler validate the bff-core operation contract and demote the data envelope to cache metadata. Either way, document the boundary in ADR-0005/0013 so the two stop growing in parallel.

**Verifier:** Verified. System A (x-modernjs-bff-envelope / x-operation-id / x-modernjs-bff-operation-context, sha256 route-table hash) is enforced only in fork-added express/koa plugins. System B (x-modernjs-data-envelope, data-platform/index.ts:197) is validated only by the effect handler (validateDataPlatformRequestEnvelope, handler.ts:597-659). The generated effect client both configures create-request with strict System A defaults when __REQUEST_ID is set (effectClientGenerator.ts:464-513) and attaches the System B envelope in __prepareEffectRequest (691-765), with senders built on createRequest (lines 357-368, 444), so cross-project effect calls carry both header families with different operation-id schemes and hash algorithms. effect/operation-context.ts:48-123 parses System A headers into context without validating, and grep confirms no evaluateCrossProjectPolicy/checkCrossProjectPolicy in the effect runtime. One precision: the dual-header overhead applies to cross-project (requestId-configured) effect clients; same-project clients emit only System B. All files fork-added; direction is fork-local.

### bff-lane#7 [MAJOR/architecture/refactor] effectClientGenerator embeds ~330 lines of untyped runtime JS as template strings and emits type declarations that erase the typed client

**Files:** `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/utils/effectClientGenerator.ts:435-781`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/utils/effectClientGenerator.ts:784-844`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/runtime/data-platform/index.ts:710-730`

**Evidence:** renderEffectClientCode returns one giant template literal containing the full client runtime: __normalizeRequest, __prepareEffectRequest, envelope construction, origin resolution, batch-transport bootstrap and producer configure() defaults (lines 435-781). This logic is untyped, unlintable, untestable except through string-snapshot tests, duplicated into every generated app bundle, and re-implements data-platform's resolveRuntimeOrigin (574-594 vs data-platform/index.ts:710-730). The only generation-time-variable parts are the endpoint manifest and config constants. Meanwhile renderEffectClientDeclaration (784-844) types the result as Record<string, Record<string, (request?: unknown) => Promise<unknown>>> — the Effect HttpApi's typed contract is fully erased for consumers, undermining the lane's headline value.

**Direction:** Move the runtime into a real module (e.g. export createGeneratedEffectClient(manifest, config) from plugin-bff/data-platform or a new ./effect-client-runtime export) and shrink codegen to: import + manifest literal + one call. Then generate real per-endpoint types from the reflected HttpApi metadata (group/endpoint names are already collected in collectEffectEndpoints) instead of Record<string, unknown> declarations.

**Verifier:** Verified. renderEffectClientCode returns a single template literal from line 435 through ~781 containing the full client runtime (__normalizeRequest, __prepareEffectRequest, envelope construction, __resolveOrigin/__resolveTargetOrigin, batch-transport bootstrap, producer configure() defaults) — untyped generated JS duplicated into every consumer bundle. __resolveOrigin (574-594) is a near-verbatim re-implementation of data-platform's resolveRuntimeOrigin (index.ts:710-730), differing only in fallback default. renderEffectClientDeclaration (784-843) types client as Record<string, Record<string, (request?: unknown) => Promise<unknown>>> via EffectClient/EffectClientGroup/EffectClientOperation, erasing the HttpApi contract for consumers. File is fork-added (A). Proposed direction (real runtime module + per-endpoint types from collectEffectEndpoints metadata) is sound and fork-local.

### bff-lane#8 [MINOR/duplication/consolidate] Generator-layer duplication: getPackageName x4, secure-defaults bootstrap template x3, prefix helpers x2, effect entry resolution x2

**Files:** `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/utils/clientGenerator.ts:70-80`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/utils/runtimeGenerator.ts:10-19`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/utils/effectClientGenerator.ts:149-159`, `packages/server/bff-core/src/client/generateClient.ts:15-24`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/utils/runtimeGenerator.ts:46-79`, `packages/server/bff-core/src/client/generateClient.ts:129-163`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/utils/effectClientGenerator.ts:464-514`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/cli.ts:39-47`

**Evidence:** Four copies of getPackageName with two different mechanisms (fs.readJSONSync in clientGenerator/effectClientGenerator, bare require() in runtimeGenerator/bff-core generateClient). Three string-template copies of the identical defaultSecureOptions producer bootstrap (requireEnvelope/identityBinding/operationContract merge) in runtimeGenerator.ts:46-79, generateClient.ts:129-163, effectClientGenerator.ts:464-514 — a policy-default change must be replicated in three generated-code templates. normalizePrefixList/getPrimaryPrefix duplicated cli.ts:39-47 vs server.ts:39-47. Effect entry resolution + the JS_OR_TS_EXTS list implemented twice (effectClientGenerator.resolveEffectEntryFile vs EffectAdapter.resolveEntryFile), and prefix-strip helpers (normalizePrefix/removePrefixFromPath/createRequestForMountedPrefix) duplicated between effect/adapter.ts and effect/edge.ts.

**Direction:** Create a small shared utils module in plugin-bff/src (entry resolution, prefix helpers, getPackageName) and export the producer secure-defaults object from @modern-js/create-request so all three generators emit `configure({ ...PRODUCER_SECURE_DEFAULTS, ...options })` instead of inlining the object; effect/edge.ts and effect/adapter.ts should import the prefix helpers from one place.

**Verifier:** All instances verified by direct read: four getPackageName copies with two mechanisms (fs.readJSONSync in clientGenerator.ts:70-80 and effectClientGenerator.ts:149-159; bare require in runtimeGenerator.ts:10-19 and bff-core generateClient.ts:15-24); the identical defaultSecureOptions producer bootstrap emitted as strings in runtimeGenerator.ts:46-79, generateClient.ts:129-163, and effectClientGenerator.ts:464-513; normalizePrefixList/getPrimaryPrefix duplicated cli.ts:39-47 vs server.ts:39-47 (both fork-added — baseline server.ts used `prefix || '/api'` inline); resolveEffectEntryFile (effectClientGenerator.ts:862-881) vs EffectAdapter.resolveEntryFile (adapter.ts:185-203) including the JS_OR_TS_EXTS list; normalizePrefix/removePrefixFromPath/createRequestForMountedPrefix duplicated between effect/adapter.ts:52-79 and effect/edge.ts:47-86. All fork-added hunks. Minor is the right severity; consolidation direction is fork-local. Caveat for the secure-defaults extraction: exporting PRODUCER_SECURE_DEFAULTS from create-request adds public API surface to a package upstream also ships — house it in plugin-bff or a fork-only export path to keep merge friction low.

### bff-lane#9 [MINOR/dead-code/delete] bff-core eventContracts module is dead runtime code on the public API

**Files:** `packages/server/bff-core/src/contracts/eventContracts.ts`, `packages/server/bff-core/src/index.ts:5`, `packages/toolkit/types/common/moduleSdk.d.ts:94-101`

**Evidence:** defineEventContract/createEventEnvelope/isEventEnvelope (87 lines, exported via `export * from './contracts/eventContracts'` in index.ts:5) have zero consumers outside their own unit test — repo-wide grep finds only tests/eventContracts.test.ts. The one place that conceptually needs the types, toolkit/types/common/moduleSdk.d.ts, re-declares structural twins (ModuleEventContract/ModuleEventEnvelope at lines 88-101) instead of importing, so even the type surface is unused. This is MV event-bus machinery shipped ahead of any event bus.

**Direction:** Delete src/contracts/eventContracts.ts and its test (or, if the MV event lane is imminent, reduce it to types co-located with moduleSdk.d.ts so there is one definition). Re-introduce runtime helpers when a producer/consumer actually exists.

**Verifier:** Verified. eventContracts.ts is 87 lines, fork-added (A), exported via `export * from './contracts/eventContracts'` at bff-core src/index.ts:5. Repo-wide grep finds consumers only in its own tests/eventContracts.test.ts. toolkit/types/common/moduleSdk.d.ts declares structural twins ModuleEventContract/ModuleEventEnvelope (lines ~87-101) without importing, confirming even the type surface is duplicated rather than consumed. Speculative machinery shipped ahead of any event bus; deletion is fork-local with zero upstream-merge cost since the file does not exist upstream.

### bff-lane#10 [MINOR/upstream-compat/small-fix] bff-runtime's public API silently jumped a farrow major (1.x to 2.x) behind `export *` at the monorepo's patch-level version

**Files:** `packages/server/bff-runtime/package.json:38-41`, `packages/server/bff-runtime/src/index.ts:1`

**Evidence:** The fork bumps farrow-api/farrow-pipeline/farrow-schema ^1.12 to ^2.3 (majors) while src/index.ts:1 keeps upstream's `export * from 'farrow-schema'`, so everything farrow-schema 2.x changed is re-exported as @modern-js/bff-runtime's own public API — at version 3.2.1, indistinguishable from upstream's farrow-1.x-surfaced 3.2.1. FORK-DIVERGENCE.md covers the merge-conflict handling (keep package.json + source together) but not the semver/publishing consequence: any external consumer or upstream-ecosystem package resolving @modern-js/bff-runtime gets breaking schema-API changes without a major signal.

**Direction:** Replace `export * from 'farrow-schema'` with an explicit re-export list of the symbols Modern.js BFF schema mode actually documents (the match.ts surface already does this for local exports). That pins the package's public API independent of farrow's surface area and makes the 2.x coupling auditable at the export site.

**Verifier:** Core claim verifies and the operative change (package.json farrow-api/pipeline/schema ^1.12→^2.3, all majors) is a fork hunk; src/index.ts:1 retains upstream's `export * from 'farrow-schema'` and package version stays 3.2.1, so farrow 2.x surface is re-exported under an upstream-identical version. One evidence correction: FORK-DIVERGENCE.md:98 does state 'the package's public API surface follows farrow 2.x' — so the API-surface consequence IS documented; what is missing is only the semver/publishing implication, making the doc-gap claim overstated. The external-consumer risk is also speculative for a private fork (publishConfig exists but no evidence of publishing). Minor is correct. Direction caveat: replacing the upstream-owned `export *` line with an explicit re-export list creates a small permanent merge conflict on that line — acceptable, but it should be acknowledged; an alternative with zero merge cost is bumping the package major or documenting the surface change in FORK-DIVERGENCE.md.

### bff-lane#11 [MINOR/dx/small-fix] defineEffectBff returns a non-optional typed `client` that is undefined at runtime

**Files:** `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/runtime/effect/handler.ts:731`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/runtime/effect/handler.ts:240-246`, `packages/document/docs/en/guides/advanced-features/bff/frameworks.mdx:113`

**Evidence:** handler.ts:731: `const client = undefined as unknown as EffectApiPromiseClientFromApi<TApi>;` — EffectBffRuntime types `client` as a fully working promise client (240-246). It only becomes real when the webpack loader swaps the api/effect entry for generated client code (docs show `effectBff.client.hello.ping({})` working via `@api/effect/index`). Anyone importing the entry module directly (server code, scripts, tests, non-bundled consumers) gets `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined` with zero indication that the property is loader-materialized.

**Direction:** Replace the cast with a Proxy/getter that throws a descriptive error ('client is only available through the @api/effect/* transformed import; on the server use HttpApiClient or the layer directly'), keeping the type intact for the codegen path.

**Verifier:** Verified. handler.ts:731: `const client = undefined as unknown as EffectApiPromiseClientFromApi<TApi>;` returned as part of the runtime object, and EffectBffRuntime (lines 240-246) types client as non-optional. The docs (frameworks.mdx:113) show effectBff.client.hello.ping({}) working only through the @api/effect/index transformed import, where the loader swaps in generated client code. Direct import of the entry module (server code, tests, scripts) yields `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading '<group>')` with no hint that client is loader-materialized. File is fork-added; the proposed Proxy-with-descriptive-error fix is small, type-preserving, and fork-local. Minor DX severity is right.

Rejected during verification: browser create-request accepts allowedHeaders config that is never read

## app-tools-fork-lane  (13 confirmed)

The app-tools fork lane is functionally solid and unusually well-tested (the 945-line Cloudflare worker entry has a 1180-line behavioral test suite, baseline/preset/deploy all have dedicated tests), but it carries one very invasive upstream-file hunk (getBuilderEnvironments.ts grew from 93 to 423 lines of inline Cloudflare/Effect-BFF wiring), a 275-line design-system validator with zero consumers, and a hardcoded wildcard-CORS policy that contradicts the otherwise careful worker security config surface. The deploy trust contract (runtimeDigest/integrity/attestation) is consumed end-to-end by plugin-garfish, but nothing ever computes the integrity hash at build time, so the producer-side contract is hand-maintained and drifts on every build. Several smaller items: dead dependencies, a dead --json flag, stale @default JSDoc, an unrecorded upstreamable bug fix, and a preset that re-enables RsDoctor in production despite ADR-0001 being formally reverted.

### app-tools-fork-lane#1 [MAJOR/architecture/refactor] 330 lines of Cloudflare/Effect-BFF worker wiring inlined into upstream getBuilderEnvironments.ts

**Files:** `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/builder/generator/getBuilderEnvironments.ts:15-125`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/builder/generator/getBuilderEnvironments.ts:200-415`

**Evidence:** Upstream file at merge-base 8a744c1b31 is 93 lines; fork version is 423. The fork hunk adds Effect BFF worker entry resolution (getEffectBffEntry, lines 97-118), Cloudflare deploy detection (isCloudflareWorkerDeploy, 120-125), ten resolvePackageFile lookups (206-262), and a ~115-line bundlerChain block (296-411) setting module-worker output, ~20 resolve aliases (react/react-dom/loadable/tanstack/rsc), and node-builtin fallbacks. It also couples the builder generator to deploy-plugin internals via CLOUDFLARE_WORKER_COMPAT_TEMPLATE_DIR = path.resolve(__dirname, '../../plugins/deploy/platforms/templates') (lines 18-21). Every upstream change to this file will conflict with the largest single fork hunk in app-tools. The fork already proved the clean seam pattern with the fork-owned builderPluginAdapterPrecompress (builder/shared/builderPlugins/adapterPrecompress.ts) registered via one line in generator/index.ts.

**Direction:** Extract the cloudflare-module-worker environment mutation (entry remapping, bundlerChain aliases, compat-template resolution) and the Effect BFF worker-entry logic into a fork-owned module (e.g. src/ultramodern/cloudflareWorkerEnvironment.ts or a builderPluginAdapterCloudflareWorker following the adapterPrecompress pattern), leaving only a 5-10 line conditional call in getBuilderEnvironments.ts. Move the worker compat shims (cloudflare-worker-*.mjs) out of deploy/platforms/templates into the same fork-owned module's directory so the deploy plugin and builder no longer share a relative-path coupling.

**Verifier:** Verified. Upstream file at 8a744c1b31 is 93 lines; fork version is 422 (finding said 423 — off by one, immaterial). All cited hunks confirmed fork-added: getEffectBffEntry (97-118), isCloudflareWorkerDeploy (120-125), ten resolvePackageFile lookups (206-271, finding said 206-262 — slightly understated), ~115-line bundlerChain (296-411), and the deploy-plugin coupling via CLOUDFLARE_WORKER_COMPAT_TEMPLATE_DIR = path.resolve(__dirname, '../../plugins/deploy/platforms/templates') (18-21). The adapterPrecompress precedent is real: builderPluginAdapterPrecompress is fork-owned and registered with one line at builder/generator/index.ts:86. Proposed extraction keeps a thin conditional in the upstream file, which preserves vanilla behavior and reduces merge conflicts — direction is sound.

### app-tools-fork-lane#2 [MAJOR/security/rewrite] Cloudflare worker entry hardcodes wildcard CORS on all responses including BFF APIs and SSR HTML, with no config escape hatch

**Files:** `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/plugins/deploy/platforms/templates/cloudflare-entry.mjs:6-10`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/plugins/deploy/platforms/templates/cloudflare-entry.mjs:903-942`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/types/config/deploy.ts:58-80`

**Evidence:** CORS_HEADERS (access-control-allow-origin: *, allow-headers: *, allow-methods: 'GET, HEAD, OPTIONS') is applied unconditionally: to BFF responses (withCorsHeaders(bffResponse), line 906), route-worker SSR responses (line 925-930), and 404s (916-918, 940). The federated-assets use case justifies CORS on static assets only, yet the wildcard also makes every BFF API response readable from any origin. It is also internally inconsistent: dispatchBffRequest serves POST (Effect handlers), but the preflight response only allows GET/HEAD/OPTIONS, so cross-origin POST is half-broken anyway. Meanwhile CloudflareWorkerSecurityConfig (types/config/deploy.ts:58-80) provides carefully reasoned, configurable CSP/noindex/header policies with 'reason' audit fields — but no CORS knob exists at all.

**Direction:** Scope the wildcard CORS to asset responses (withAssetHeaders path) where the federated-loading use case needs it; drop it from BFF, SSR, and 404 responses by default. Add a deploy.worker.security.cors config (allowed origins, methods) consistent with the existing security surface, and align allow-methods with what the BFF actually serves.

**Verifier:** Verified. cloudflare-entry.mjs (file is fork-added, A) applies CORS_HEADERS (ACAO *, allow-headers *, allow-methods GET/HEAD/OPTIONS, lines 6-10) unconditionally to BFF responses (906), route-worker SSR (925-930), and 404s (916-918, 940); withAssetHeaders also wraps withCorsHeaders (181-182). dispatchBffRequest (836+) dispatches any HTTP method to the Effect handler while the preflight (197-206) only advertises GET/HEAD/OPTIONS — internal inconsistency confirmed. CloudflareWorkerSecurityConfig (deploy.ts:58-80, fork-added; upstream MicroFrontend/DeployUserConfig has none of it) has CSP/noindex/header knobs but no CORS knob. One evidence correction: 'every BFF API response readable from any origin' is only true for non-credentialed requests — ACAO * without allow-credentials means browsers block reading cookie-authenticated responses, so the worst-case data-theft scenario is overstated. The core claim (hardcoded wildcard default with no opt-out on a production deploy path, plus inconsistent methods) stands; severity stays major as a default-on security posture defect.

### app-tools-fork-lane#3 [MAJOR/dead-code/delete] src/ultramodern/designSystem.ts: 275-line design-system contract validator with zero consumers

**Files:** `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/ultramodern/designSystem.ts`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/index.ts:48`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/tests/ultramodern/designSystem.test.ts`

**Evidence:** Repo-wide grep for validateDesignSystemContract / DesignSystemContract / DESIGN_SYSTEM_MODES finds exactly two source hits: the module itself and its own test. No CLI command, build gate, create-template, or contract-gate consumes it, yet `export * from './ultramodern/designSystem'` (index.ts:48) puts 15+ types and 3 functions on app-tools' public API. It implements ADR-0013 (mv-ds-platform-contract) validation but the wiring that ADR implies (a gate that runs it) was never built — an abandoned experiment living in the framework's main entry.

**Direction:** Delete the module and its test (it can be restored from history when the ADR-0013 gate is actually built), or at minimum remove the `export *` from index.ts so it stops being public API. If the MV DS gate is imminent, move it next to the other contract-gate code (server-runtime-extensions or the create-workspace contracts) where the consumer will live.

**Verifier:** Verified. File is fork-added (A, 275 lines). Repo-wide grep for validateDesignSystemContract/DesignSystemContract/DESIGN_SYSTEM_MODES finds only the module itself, its own test (tests/ultramodern/designSystem.test.ts), and the `export * from './ultramodern/designSystem'` at index.ts:48 (the only other hits are built doc_build static JS artifacts, not consumers). No CLI command, gate, or create-template consumes it. ADR-0013-mv-ds-platform-contract.md exists, confirming the abandoned-wiring narrative. Major is defensible because the dead module is star-exported from the framework's main entry, making it removable-only-as-breaking-change public API; direction (delete or unexport) is safe and fork-local.

### app-tools-fork-lane#4 [MAJOR/correctness/rewrite] Deploy trust contract: integrity/runtimeDigest are hand-typed config strings; no build step computes or stamps them, and the host fallback accepts remote self-attestation

**Files:** `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/types/config/deploy.ts:14-27`, `packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/cli/index.ts:116-165`, `packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/trust.ts:118-122`

**Evidence:** deploy.microFrontend.integrity is documented as an SRI hash of the remote entry (deploy.ts:19-22) and is baked into the remote bundle via define ('process.env.MODERN_MF_REMOTE_ENTRY_INTEGRITY', garfish cli/index.ts:151-157). Repo-wide grep for createHash/sha256 confirms no deploy or build step ever computes a hash of the built remoteEntry.js — the only hashing in app-tools is RouterPlugin's md5 for route manifests. Since the remote entry's content changes on every build (and embedding the value changes the bundle), a hand-maintained hash is stale by construction unless external tooling rewrites config per build, which does not exist in the repo. Worse, host-side verification falls back to `app.integrity || app.runtimeMetadata?.integrity` (trust.ts:118) — runtimeMetadata is exported by the remote entry itself, so an unpinned host verifies tampered bytes against the attacker's own declared hash. ADR-0002 §10-11 documents this ordering, so the code matches a flawed spec.

**Direction:** Add a deploy-time stamping step: in the deploy presets' writeOutput/genEntry (and the garfish CLI afterBuild), hash the emitted remoteEntry.js and write the SRI into mf-manifest.json / a sidecar trust file, treating the config field as an explicit override only. On the host side, never fall back to remote self-attested integrity for the verification path (keep it for telemetry/diagnostics only) — coordinate with the plugin-garfish owner.

**Verifier:** Verified end to end. deploy.ts:14-27 fields (runtimeDigest/integrity/attestation) are fork-added (upstream MicroFrontend has only enableHtmlEntry/externalBasicLibrary/moduleApp). The config value is baked into the remote bundle via define at plugin-garfish cli/index.ts:143-163 (finding cited 151-157 — within the block). Grep confirms the only hashing in app-tools is RouterPlugin's md5 for route manifests; the only producers of MODERN_MF_REMOTE_ENTRY_INTEGRITY are the config define and the remote-side env reads in cli/utils.ts — no build/deploy step computes an SRI of remoteEntry.js. Host fallback confirmed: getAppIntegrity (trust.ts:117-119) returns app.integrity || app.runtimeMetadata?.integrity, where runtimeMetadata is exported by the remote bundle itself (cli/utils.ts sets provider.runtimeMetadata/__GARFISH_EXPORTS__.runtimeMetadata from the baked define), and enforceRemoteTrustPolicy (trust.ts:472-491) feeds that into verifyIntegrity — so an unpinned host verifies fetched bytes against the remote's self-declared hash. ADR-0002 lines 156-158 document exactly this ordering (app integrity, then runtimeMetadata.integrity), confirming code-matches-flawed-spec. Direction (build-time stamping; demote self-attested integrity to telemetry) is sound and fork-local; both files are fork-added so no upstream-merge impact.

### app-tools-fork-lane#5 [MAJOR/architecture/small-fix] baseline preset re-enables RsDoctor for every production build, contradicting the formally reverted ADR-0001

**Files:** `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/baseline.ts:143-149`, `docs/super-app-rfc-adr/ADR-0001-rsdoctor-default-on.md`

**Evidence:** ADR-0001 status is 'Reverted (2026-04-20, commit a210ac658d)' with the reversal note 'The implicit RsDoctor default was removed because of build-time overhead... Current behavior is opt-in.' Yet createAppBaselineConfig sets performance.rsdoctor.enabled = (NODE_ENV === 'production') (baseline.ts:146) with no AppBaselineOptions toggle (unlike telemetry/MF-SSR which each have enable* options). Every workspace generated by `create` uses presetUltramodern, so all generated apps pay RsDoctor overhead on every production/CI build — exactly the cost the revert removed. FORK-DIVERGENCE.md:35 already flags the stale builder JSDoc from the same revert; the preset is the remaining drift.

**Direction:** Either remove the rsdoctor block from the baseline (making it opt-in like the framework default, per the reverted ADR) or add an enableRsdoctor option defaulting to false. If production-default-on is genuinely intended for the preset despite the revert, amend ADR-0001's reversal note to scope it, since right now code and ADR disagree.

**Verifier:** Verified. baseline.ts (fork-added file) sets performance.rsdoctor.enabled = (process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production') with disableClientServer: true at lines 143-149, and AppBaselineOptions (lines 6-48) has toggles for telemetry/BFF/MF-SSR but none for rsdoctor. ADR-0001 line 3: 'Status: Reverted (2026-04-20, commit a210ac658d "refactor(builder): remove implicit rsdoctor default")' with current behavior documented as opt-in. FORK-DIVERGENCE.md's builder section explicitly notes the post-revert stale JSDoc, corroborating that opt-in is the intended state. presetUltramodern is what create templates (packages/toolkit/create templates/handlebars), sandpack-react, docs, and local-test-project use, so generated apps do get production-default-on RsDoctor. Code/ADR contradiction confirmed; severity major is fair (build-cost regression reintroduced on all generated apps plus governance drift). Direction is fork-local and safe.

### app-tools-fork-lane#6 [MINOR/correctness/rewrite] resolveESMDependency rewritten as a partial hand-rolled package-exports parser; replaced dependency left in package.json

**Files:** `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/plugins/deploy/utils/index.ts:59-126`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/package.json:111`

**Evidence:** The fork replaced upstream's spec-compliant import-meta-resolve call with a hand parser that only understands string targets and the fixed condition keys node.import / import / default at one nesting level (utils/index.ts:113-116). Common shapes — root-sugar exports without a '.' key, `import: { types, default }` nesting, target arrays — silently fall through to the CJS require.resolve path (line 122) or to undefined via the blanket catch (123-125). Callers (node.ts:52, vercel.ts:123, netlify.ts:118-121) feed the result into generated ESM deploy entries and ndepe dependency tracing, so a wrong CJS path degrades the ESM deploy output without any error. Meanwhile import-meta-resolve@^4.2.0 is still declared in package.json:111 with zero imports left in src/ or tests/.

**Direction:** Delete the hand parser and resolve via the still-installed import-meta-resolve (or Node's import.meta.resolve, available in the toolchain's Node version); if the rewrite was motivated by ESM-loading constraints in the CJS dist, vendor the resolution through createRequire + the 'node','import' conditions of require.resolve instead of reimplementing exports semantics. If the hand parser stays, remove the unused dependency.

**Verifier:** Verified. Upstream resolveESMDependency used import-meta-resolve's moduleResolve with node/import/module/default conditions; the fork hunk (utils/index.ts:59-126) replaces it with a hand parser that handles only string targets and exportConfig.node?.import || .import || .default at one nesting level (113-116), falling back to localRequire.resolve's CJS resolution (94/122) or undefined via the blanket catch (123-125). Root-sugar exports (string or conditions object without a '.' key), nested condition objects whose import key is itself an object, and array targets all fall through as claimed. import-meta-resolve@^4.2.0 remains at package.json:111 with zero imports in src/tests (it was an upstream dep, but the fork orphaned it by removing the only usage — fork-owned deadness). Minor severity appropriate: degrades ESM deploy entry resolution silently rather than breaking builds. Note for direction: import-meta-resolve was already used via dynamic import in upstream's CJS-compatible code, so restoring it has no toolchain blocker.

### app-tools-fork-lane#7 [MINOR/hygiene/small-fix] Dead dependencies in fork-modified package.json: import-meta-resolve, @swc/core, esbuild-register

Repo greps over the entire package (src, tests, configs; excluding dist/node_modules) find zero references to any of the three — including string references that register hooks might use for esbuild-register. @swc/core and import-meta-resolve predate the fork as declarations, but their deadness is caused by fork hunks and the fork actively bumped @swc/core, so the cleanup is fork-owned.
