### app-tools-fork-lane#8 [MINOR/correctness/small-fix] baseline react-router bridge aliases force the production build in dev and resolve from process.cwd()

**Files:** `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/baseline.ts:50-91`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/baseline.ts:151-153`

**Evidence:** setReactRouterBridgeSafeAliases sets `react-router$` → dist/production/index.mjs unconditionally (baseline.ts:82), so every dev build of a preset app loses react-router's development build (dev-only warnings/invariant messages); only the deep path 'react-router/dist/development/index.js' keeps a dev mapping (87-90). The alias is applied through tools.bundlerChain (151-153) so it hits all environments including the node SSR server bundle. Resolution is rooted at process.cwd() (lines 51, 55) rather than the app directory, so invoking the CLI from a monorepo root can resolve a different react-router copy than the app's own. The chain parameter is typed `any` (line 67).

**Direction:** Pick the production/development entry based on the build mode inside the bundlerChain callback (it receives environment/mode context), resolve from the app directory (available via the config-time context, or accept it as an option on createAppBaselineConfig), and type the chain parameter as RspackChain.

**Verifier:** Verified. baseline.ts (fork-added): 'react-router$' → dist/production/index.mjs unconditionally (line 82); only the deep path 'react-router/dist/development/index.js' maps to the development entry (87-90); applied via tools.bundlerChain (151-153) which runs for all environments including the node/server bundle; resolveReactRouterPackageDir roots resolution at process.cwd() (lines 51, 55) rather than appDirectory; chain parameter typed any (line 67). Even granting that the alias exists to avoid dual react-router copies for the MF bridge, forcing the production entry in dev mode and cwd-rooted resolution are real correctness costs, and the proposed mode-aware/app-dir-aware fix preserves the single-copy intent. Fork-local, no upstream-merge impact.

### app-tools-fork-lane#9 [MINOR/duplication/consolidate] presetUltramodern.ts is a pure rename wrapper over baseline.ts; both APIs are exported publicly

**Files:** `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/presetUltramodern.ts`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/baseline.ts:165-169`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/index.ts:45-48`

**Evidence:** presetUltramodern.ts is 17 lines that re-export baseline behavior under new names (createPresetUltramodernConfig = createAppBaselineConfig, presetUltramodern = withAppBaseline, PresetUltramodernOptions extends AppBaselineOptions with no members). index.ts exports both modules (`export * from './baseline'` and `export * from './presetUltramodern'`), so the public API has two names for every concept. Repo-wide grep shows no consumer outside app-tools uses withAppBaseline/createAppBaselineConfig — all templates, sandpack, local-test-project, and integration tests use presetUltramodern. tests/presetUltramodern.test.ts exists solely to assert the wrapper equals the baseline.

**Direction:** Collapse baseline.ts into presetUltramodern.ts (keep the implementation, keep only the presetUltramodern* names public), drop the `export * from './baseline'` from index.ts, and delete the equality-asserting wrapper test.

**Verifier:** Verified. presetUltramodern.ts is exactly 17 lines of pure delegation (createPresetUltramodernConfig → createAppBaselineConfig, presetUltramodern → withAppBaseline, PresetUltramodernOptions extends AppBaselineOptions with no members). index.ts:45 and :47-48 star-export both modules. Grep confirms withAppBaseline/createAppBaselineConfig have no consumers outside app-tools' own src/tests, while presetUltramodern is used by create templates, sandpack-react, docs, and local-test-project. tests/presetUltramodern.test.ts asserts wrapper-equals-baseline via toEqual. All files fork-added; consolidation direction is fork-local and safe.

### app-tools-fork-lane#10 [MINOR/dead-code/small-fix] --json flag on `modern runtime status|fallback-signal` is dead: both printOutput branches are identical

**Files:** `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/commands/runtime.ts:106-112`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/commands/runtime.ts:160`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/commands/runtime.ts:220`

**Evidence:** printOutput(payload, jsonOnly) executes console.log(JSON.stringify(payload, null, 2)) in both the jsonOnly and default branches — the parameter has no effect. Both subcommands advertise '--json: output as JSON format for machine reading' (lines 160, 220) and the ai-capabilities scripts invoke `modern runtime status --json`, but output is always identical JSON. Also commands/index.ts:127-132 adds a runtimeOperationsCommand wrapper whose body is a single pass-through call to runtimeCommand.

**Direction:** Either remove the --json option (output is already machine-readable JSON) or implement a human-readable default rendering so the flag means something; inline the runtimeOperationsCommand pass-through.

**Verifier:** Verified. runtime.ts (fork-added) printOutput (106-112): both the jsonOnly branch and the default branch execute the identical console.log(JSON.stringify(payload, null, 2)). Both subcommands declare --json with the 'machine reading' description (lines 160, 220). docs/super-app-rfc-adr/contracts/ai-capabilities.json:39 invokes 'modern runtime status --json'. runtimeOperationsCommand (commands/index.ts:127-132, fork hunk in an M file) is a single pass-through to runtimeCommand as claimed. Direction note: since the ai-capabilities contract pins '--json', removing the flag would break that contract — implementing a human-readable default and keeping --json is the safer of the two suggested options.

### app-tools-fork-lane#11 [MINOR/hygiene/small-fix] Config-surface rot: output.precompress JSDoc claims '@default true' but default is off; deploy.worker.security.cookies is write-only

**Files:** `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/types/config/output.ts:61-66`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/builder/shared/builderPlugins/adapterPrecompress.ts:78-89`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/types/config/deploy.ts:71-78`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/plugins/deploy/platforms/cloudflare.ts:211-216`

**Evidence:** OutputUserConfig.precompress documents '@default true', but resolvePrecompressConfig treats undefined as disabled (adapterPrecompress.ts:79-81) and tests/config/default.test.ts:4-14 explicitly asserts 'should leave precompress unset in core defaults' — only the ultramodern baseline turns it on. Same stale-JSDoc family as the RsdoctorUserConfig note in FORK-DIVERGENCE.md:35. Separately, CloudflareWorkerSecurityConfig.cookies is typed `mutateSetCookie?: false` (a field that can only hold one value) plus a reason string; createCloudflareWorkerSecurityPolicy always emits the constant block into the manifest (cloudflare.ts:211-216), and grep shows cloudflare-entry.mjs never reads `security.cookies` — pure documentation theater shipped as config and manifest bytes.

**Direction:** Fix the precompress JSDoc to '@default false (enabled by presetUltramodern)'. Drop the cookies config key and manifest field — the guarantee 'worker does not mutate Set-Cookie' belongs in docs/ADR text, not in a config type that admits exactly one value and is consumed by nothing.

**Verifier:** Verified, both halves. (1) output.ts:61-66 (fork-added field; upstream output.ts has no precompress) documents '@default true', but resolvePrecompressConfig (adapterPrecompress.ts:73-86, fork-added file) returns false for undefined, and tests/config/default.test.ts asserts precompress is unset in core defaults — only baseline.ts:141 turns it on. (2) CloudflareWorkerSecurityConfig.cookies (deploy.ts:71-78, fork-added) types mutateSetCookie as the literal `false`; createCloudflareWorkerSecurityPolicy (cloudflare.ts:211-216, fork-added file) always emits the constant cookies block with a default reason string into the manifest, and grep shows zero 'cookies' references in cloudflare-entry.mjs — write-only config confirmed. Direction is doc/type-level and fork-local.

### app-tools-fork-lane#12 [MINOR/upstream-compat/small-fix] rsbuild.ts swaps a typed import for require() plus a hand-written duplicate of createConfigOptions' signature

**Files:** `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/rsbuild.ts:24-40`

**Evidence:** The fork hunk replaces upstream's `import { createConfigOptions } from '@modern-js/plugin/cli'` + `createConfigOptions<AppTools>` with a local 17-line CreateConfigOptionsFn type and `const { createConfigOptions } = require('@modern-js/plugin/cli')`. The duplicate type declares `getAppContext: () => any`, losing the AppTools generic the real API provides, and will silently drift whenever upstream changes the createConfigOptions signature — the exact failure mode hand-copied types create in a fork that merges upstream.

**Direction:** Restore the typed import; if the require() was needed to break an ESM/CJS or module-cycle issue under the new toolchain, keep the runtime require but derive the type from the source of truth (`typeof import('@modern-js/plugin/cli').createConfigOptions`) instead of re-declaring it.

**Verifier:** Verified. Upstream rsbuild.ts imports createConfigOptions from '@modern-js/plugin/cli' and calls it as createConfigOptions<AppTools>; the fork hunk (rsbuild.ts:24-40) declares a local CreateConfigOptionsFn with getAppContext: () => any and obtains the function via require(). Drift risk is real. One additional fact supporting the direction: the fork's actual createConfigOptions (packages/toolkit/plugin/src/cli/run/create.ts:192) is still exported, properly generic over CLIPluginExtends, and accepts the internalPlugins option the fork now passes — so `typeof import('@modern-js/plugin/cli').createConfigOptions` would type-check today; only the runtime require (if needed for CJS interop) needs to stay. Fork-owned hunk in an M file.

### app-tools-fork-lane#13 [MINOR/upstream-compat/small-fix] Upstreamable bug fixes hidden in fork hunks and missing from the FORK-DIVERGENCE ledger (adapterHtml mountId, bff-prefix-array compress filter)

**Files:** `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/builder/shared/builderPlugins/adapterHtml.ts:77`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/builder/generator/createBuilderProviderConfig.ts:66-78`, `FORK-DIVERGENCE.md:134-141`

**Evidence:** Upstream at merge-base has `mountId: modernConfig.html.templateParameters` (verified via git show 8a744c1b31) — a genuine upstream bug assigning the templateParameters object to mountId in bottom-template params; the fork fixes it to `modernConfig.html.mountId`. Similarly createBuilderProviderConfig's dev compress filter now handles bff.prefix arrays instead of calling startsWith on an array. Neither fix is mentioned in FORK-DIVERGENCE.md's app-tools section (lines 134-141), which is the ledger that decides conflict resolution on sync — an [M]-style 'resolve toward upstream' merge would silently drop both fixes, and the upstream-PR opportunity (the ledger's stated mechanism for shrinking divergence) is being missed.

**Direction:** Add both as [U] entries under the app-tools section of FORK-DIVERGENCE.md and queue the adapterHtml mountId one-liner for an upstream PR alongside the render.ts matchRoute headline fix.

**Verifier:** Verified. git diff 8a744c1b31..HEAD shows the fork hunk in adapterHtml.ts changing `mountId: modernConfig.html.templateParameters` to `mountId: modernConfig.html.mountId` — the upstream line is a genuine bug (assigns the templateParameters object to mountId). createBuilderProviderConfig.ts fork hunk wraps bff.prefix into an array and uses .some(prefix => url?.startsWith(prefix)), fixing startsWith-on-array for array prefixes. FORK-DIVERGENCE.md's app-tools section lists getBuilderEnvironments, deploy, commands, analyze, and esm hooks but mentions neither fix — no [U] entry exists, so a resolve-toward-upstream merge would drop both. Direction (ledger entries + upstream PR) actively improves upstream-merge safety.

## server-lane  (6 confirmed)

The live server lane is healthy: prod-server's real entry (typed createProdServer, netlify entry, apply.ts plugin assembly delegating to fork-owned @modern-js/server-runtime-extensions) is well-factored, server-core render/type hunks are mostly minimal plumbing, and code-tools is a legitimately consumed, tested package. The rot is concentrated in a resurrected pre-v3 lane: a 1000-line legacy Server class in prod-server that cannot compile or run (it imports modules and exports that do not exist, and its server factory unconditionally throws), the worker-lane "pilot" that only that dead class consumes — making the typed workerLane config and documented env var silent no-ops in production — and two zombie BFF adapter packages (plugin-koa, plugin-express) that no configuration path can reach in the v3 hono/effect pipeline. Roughly 2,500+ lines of fork-owned server code exists only to be exercised by its own test harnesses.

### server-lane#1 [MAJOR/dead-code/delete] Resurrected legacy prod-server harness is dead, unbuildable, and unreachable — delete the whole cluster

**Files:** `packages/server/prod-server/src/server/index.ts`, `packages/server/prod-server/src/server/modernServerSplit.ts:1`, `packages/server/prod-server/src/libs/loadConfig.ts:8`, `packages/server/prod-server/src/libs/metrics.ts`, `packages/server/prod-server/src/utils.ts`, `packages/server/prod-server/tests/runtimeSignals.test.ts:12`, `packages/server/prod-server/tsconfig.json:17`

**Evidence:** src/server/index.ts (1004 lines, resurrected from pre-v3 upstream; absent at merge-base 8a744c1b31 which had only apply/index/netlify/types) imports `AppContext, ConfigContext, loadPlugins, serverManager` from '@modern-js/server-core' (src/server/index.ts:1-7) — none exist anywhere in packages/server/core/src — and types from '../type' (src/server/index.ts:61-66), a file that does not exist (only src/types.ts, which defines none of those types). Its server factory `serverImpl = createProdServer` comes from modernServerSplit.ts, which unconditionally throws ('not available in this source runtime'), so Server.init() can never succeed; before that it would already crash on `serverManager.clear()` (src/server/index.ts:930, undefined under CJS interop). It is unreachable from package exports ('.' and './netlify' only, package.json). The only consumer is tests/runtimeSignals.test.ts, which never calls init() and instead pokes private fields via `as any` (line 80: `server.runtimeFallbackSignalConfig = {...}`). Nothing catches the breakage: the build emits dts with `noCheck: true` (scripts/tsgo-dts.mjs:35) and prod-server is absent from scripts/tsgo-critical.txt, while the type-only '../type' import is erased at transpile time. libs/loadConfig.ts also imports `flatted` (line 8), which is not in prod-server's dependencies (phantom dep). All runtime behavior it claims to provide (telemetry init, canary, autopilot, runtime status + fallback-signal endpoints) already exists in the live lane as hono middlewares in packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:1905-2160, registered via apply.ts:105.

**Direction:** Delete src/server/ (both files), src/libs/loadConfig.ts, src/libs/metrics.ts, src/utils.ts, src/libs/runtimeFallbackWorkerLane.ts (see worker-lane finding), tests/runtimeSignals.test.ts and tests/runtimeFallbackWorkerLane.test.ts, and drop the corresponding tsconfig.json include entries. The live endpoint behavior is already covered by runtime-extensions tests; port any unique assertion from runtimeSignals.test.ts onto the telemetry-plugin middlewares in runtime-extensions instead.

**Verifier:** Every load-bearing claim verifies. src/server/index.ts (exactly 1004 lines, fork-added; baseline 8a744c1b31 had only apply/index/netlify/types/modern-app-env) imports AppContext/ConfigContext/loadPlugins/serverManager from '@modern-js/server-core' — none exist anywhere in packages/server/core/src — and types from nonexistent '../type'. modernServerSplit.ts unconditionally throws, so Server.init() can never succeed (and serverManager.clear() at line 930 would crash first). Package exports are only '.' and './netlify' and src/index.ts never touches src/server/, so it is unreachable; the sole importer is tests/runtimeSignals.test.ts which never calls init() and pokes server.runtimeFallbackSignalConfig via `as any` at line 80. flatted phantom dep confirmed (loadConfig.ts:8, absent from package.json dependencies). Gates confirmed: tsgo-dts.mjs sets noCheck:true and prod-server is not in tsgo-critical.txt. Live equivalents confirmed in runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts (runtime-fallback-signal middleware at 2021-2060, runtime-status at 2062+) registered via injectTelemetryPlugin() in apply.ts. src/utils.ts and libs/metrics.ts are consumed only by the dead server module. Proposed deletion removes only fork-added files and shrinks divergence — no vanilla breakage, merges get easier. The direction's caveat to port unique assertions from runtimeSignals.test.ts is appropriate since that test exercises real runtime-extensions auth/trust primitives through the legacy handlers.

### server-lane#2 [MAJOR/correctness/delete] workerLane config and MODERN_RUNTIME_FALLBACK_WORKER_LANE are silent no-ops in the live server — abandoned pilot wired only into dead code

**Files:** `packages/server/core/src/types/config/server.ts:169`, `packages/server/prod-server/src/libs/runtimeFallbackWorkerLane.ts:106`, `packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:2021`, `packages/server/runtime-extensions/README.md:56`, `docs/super-app-rfc-adr/IMPLEMENTATION-BACKLOG.md:52`

**Evidence:** server.telemetry.canary.autopilot.runtimeFallbackSignal.workerLane is a typed public config surface (fork hunk in server.ts:166-183, ServerTelemetryCanaryRuntimeFallbackSignalWorkerLaneUserConfig), and IMPLEMENTATION-BACKLOG.md:52 claims the worker-lane pilot 'is implemented in @modern-js/prod-server with opt-in workerLane.enabled'. But the live runtime-fallback-signal handler — the hono middleware in runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:2021-2060 — never reads workerLane (zero grep hits in that file), and `persistRuntimeFallbackContractGateInWorker` is referenced only by the dead legacy Server class and its own unit test. So a user setting workerLane (or MODERN_RUNTIME_FALLBACK_WORKER_LANE=true, which runtime-extensions/README.md:56-58 itself admits is consumed only by 'the @modern-js/prod-server legacy harness') gets nothing. The implementation is also questionable if revived: it spawns a fresh eval'd-string Worker per signal (runtimeFallbackWorkerLane.ts:23-97, 120-123) doing unlocked read-modify-write of the same contract-gates JSON file the ContractGateAutopilot and main-thread persist path write, so concurrent signals can lose updates. RFC-0002 Workstream D's 'Selective Worker Lanes' actually describes client-side lanes (data transforms, grid/chart aggregation, OffscreenCanvas) — the server JSON-write pilot is a category mismatch with the documented intent.

**Direction:** Decide the pilot's fate explicitly: either wire persistRuntimeFallbackContractGateInWorker into the telemetry middleware in runtime-extensions (moving the file there, adding file-locking or routing all writes through ContractGateSnapshotStore), or — given persisting a small JSON file does not need an off-main-thread lane — delete runtimeFallbackWorkerLane.ts, remove ServerTelemetryCanaryRuntimeFallbackSignalWorkerLaneUserConfig from server-core's config types (also shrinking the M-file hunk), and correct README.md and IMPLEMENTATION-BACKLOG.md.

**Verifier:** Verified. The workerLane field and ServerTelemetryCanaryRuntimeFallbackSignalWorkerLaneUserConfig are fork-added hunks in server-core's types/config/server.ts. Repo-wide grep confirms workerLane/persistRuntimeFallbackContractGateInWorker/MODERN_RUNTIME_FALLBACK_WORKER_LANE appear only in: server-core types, the dead legacy Server (env var read at prod-server src/server/index.ts:379), the lib itself, and its tests — zero hits in runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts, whose runtime-fallback-signal middleware (2021-2060) never consults workerLane. README.md:56 admission and IMPLEMENTATION-BACKLOG 'implemented in @modern-js/prod-server with opt-in workerLane.enabled' claim both verified; RFC-0002 Workstream D indeed describes client-side lanes (data transforms, grid/chart aggregation, OffscreenCanvas). Implementation critique verified: eval'd-string Worker spawned per call (runtimeFallbackWorkerLane.ts:120-123) doing unlocked read-modify-write of the snapshot JSON (worker script lines 48-80). One evidence supplement, not a refutation: benchmark/runtime-resilience/workerLane.js plus the benchmark:runtime-resilience:worker-lane-gate script re-implement the lane standalone and do NOT import the prod-server lib, so they are not consumers either — the user-facing config and env var remain inert. Proposed direction (decide: wire into runtime-extensions or delete config+lib+docs) is sound and shrinks the server-core M-hunk.

### server-lane#3 [MAJOR/dead-code/delete] plugin-koa and plugin-express are zombie v2 BFF adapters no real pipeline can reach — delete both packages

**Files:** `packages/server/plugin-koa/src/plugin.ts:246`, `packages/server/plugin-express/src/plugin.ts:284`, `packages/server/core/src/types/config/bff.ts:3`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/server.ts:107`, `packages/server/plugin-koa/tests/runtimeHarness.ts:33`, `packages/server/plugin-koa/package.json`, `packages/server/plugin-express/package.json`

**Evidence:** Both packages were resurrected from pre-v3 upstream (absent at merge-base; version 3.1.3 vs workspace 3.2.1), are private:true with no build script, no main/exports fields, and zero consumers anywhere in the monorepo (repo-wide grep finds only self-references plus ADR-0005 §13). They cannot work in the v3 pipeline: BffRuntimeFramework is 'hono' | 'effect' (bff.ts:3) with no koa/express option; the only prepareApiServer pipeline call site (plugin-bff/src/server.ts:107-145) runs solely when runtimeFramework === 'hono' and invokes the result as a hono middleware `handler(c, next)`, while these plugins return a node `(req: IncomingMessage, res: ServerResponse)` handler smuggled through an `as unknown as` double-cast (koa plugin.ts:246-251, express plugin.ts:284-288 — the in-code comment at koa plugin.ts:30-35 admits the shape mismatch). Their taps also return without calling next(), which would short-circuit plugin-bff's tap that populates apiRouter/apiHandlerInfos. They are exercisable only via a vendored fake of the removed v2 plugin manager (tests/runtimeHarness.ts re-implements serverManager/ConfigContext and feeds handlers to supertest in node style). ADR-0005 §13 cites them as 'framework-level enforcement middleware in both API runtimes', but the fork's real runtimes are hono and effect; hono-side cross-project-policy enforcement already lives in plugin-bff (crossProjectApiPlugin) and the evaluator plus parity scenarios live in fork-owned @modern-js/bff-core (src/adapter-kit/parity.ts, tests/adapterKit.test.ts). They also pin a parallel dependency surface (koa 3.2.1, @koa/router 15, koa-body 8, express, cookie-parser, supertest) and occupy 2 of the 7 slots in scripts/tsgo-critical.txt.

**Direction:** Delete packages/server/plugin-koa and packages/server/plugin-express entirely (27 files), remove their lines from scripts/tsgo-critical.txt, and update ADR-0005 §13 to point validation coverage at bff-core's adapter-parity/adapterKit tests plus plugin-bff's hono enforcement. If express/koa BFF compat is ever genuinely wanted, it must be rebuilt against the hono prepareApiServer contract (e.g., via a node-to-fetch bridge), not the v2 node seam.

**Verifier:** Core claim verifies; two evidence details need correction. Verified: both packages are all-A in the fork diff (27 files), private:true, version 3.1.3 vs workspace 3.2.1, no build script and no main/exports; zero consumers outside themselves and ADR-0005 §13 ('framework-level enforcement middleware in both API runtimes' — confirmed at ADR-0005 line 155-156). BffRuntimeFramework = 'hono' | 'effect' (bff.ts:3); the only production prepareApiServer.call site (plugin-bff/src/server.ts:107-118) runs only under runtimeFramework==='hono' and invokes the result as hono middleware handler(c,next), while koa returns a node `(req,res)` handler (plugin.ts:198) registered through `as unknown as` double-casts (koa 246-251, express 284-288) with the admitting comment at koa plugin.ts:30-35. runtimeHarness.ts vendors a TestServerManager/ConfigContext fake; supertest/koa/express dep surface confirmed. Corrections: (1) scripts/tsgo-critical.txt has 14 entries, not 7 — they occupy 2 of 14 slots; (2) the 'would short-circuit plugin-bff's tap that populates apiRouter/apiHandlerInfos' sub-claim is backwards: both plugins declare pre:['@modern-js/plugin-bff'], so plugin-bff's tap runs upstream and populates apiRouter before the koa/express tap returns; a producer tap returning without next() is normal pipeline termination. Neither correction saves the packages — the handler shape mismatch alone makes them non-functional in the v3 pipeline. Nuance for the direction: their adapterParity tests do exercise bff-core's resolveCrossProjectPolicy against real koa/express apps, so before deletion confirm bff-core's parity/adapterKit tests cover those scenarios (the proposed direction already says this). Deletion is fork-only and cannot affect vanilla or upstream merges.

### server-lane#4 [MINOR/testing/move] prod-server hosts the test suite for runtime-extensions, partially duplicating its in-package tests

**Files:** `packages/server/prod-server/tests/telemetry.test.ts:1`, `packages/server/prod-server/tests/contractGateAutopilot.test.ts:1`, `packages/server/runtime-extensions/tests/telemetry.test.ts`, `packages/server/runtime-extensions/tests/telemetryAutopilot.test.ts`

**Evidence:** prod-server/tests/telemetry.test.ts (456 lines, 20 tests) and tests/contractGateAutopilot.test.ts (203 lines) import exclusively from '@modern-js/server-runtime-extensions' — they touch nothing in prod-server src. At least three test names are verbatim duplicates of runtime-extensions/tests/telemetry.test.ts ('applies redaction and emits dropped-count metric under backpressure', 'otlp exporter sends envelope batch with JSON body', 'victoria metrics exporter emits prometheus lines'), while the remainder (SLO alerts, queue stats, canary promote/rollback thresholds, startup health failLoud) exist only in the wrong package. This is leftover from extraction commit 5f9dff870e ('extract fork plugins from server-core into @modern-js/server-runtime-extensions'), which moved the source but not these tests, so coverage for runtime-extensions' core behaviors silently depends on another package's CI lane.

**Direction:** Move the non-duplicated tests (SLO alert emission, canary orchestrator thresholds, startup health, queue stats) into packages/server/runtime-extensions/tests/ and delete the verbatim duplicates. prod-server should keep only tests of its own surface (applyPlugins.test.ts is the correct model — it tests src/apply.ts).

**Verifier:** Evidence verifies: prod-server/tests/telemetry.test.ts (456 lines) and contractGateAutopilot.test.ts (203 lines) import exclusively from '@modern-js/server-runtime-extensions' and touch nothing in prod-server src. The three named tests are verbatim duplicates of runtime-extensions/tests/telemetry.test.ts ('applies redaction and emits dropped-count metric under backpressure', 'otlp exporter sends envelope batch with JSON body', 'victoria metrics exporter emits prometheus lines'). Canary promote/rollback, SLO alerts, queue stats, and startup-health failLoud tests exist only in prod-server; runtime-extensions' own telemetryAutopilot.test.ts covers the runtime-signal/middleware surface but not the orchestrator. Commit 5f9dff870e's stat confirms sources were moved while these test files were only edited in place. Severity downgraded major→minor: both suites still run in CI (per-package rstest), so no coverage is lost today — this is ownership/organization hygiene whose main risk is accidental coverage loss if prod-server is restructured (e.g., per finding 1's deletion). ### server-lane#5 [MINOR/upstream-compat/move] 518 lines of fork-only config interfaces live inside upstream-owned server-core type files

**Files:** `packages/server/core/src/types/config/server.ts:30`, `packages/server/core/src/types/config/bff.ts:3`, `packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:7`

**Evidence:** The fork hunk in types/config/server.ts is +370 lines of telemetry/canary/autopilot/runtime-fallback interfaces (ServerTelemetryUserConfig and 10 sibling interfaces) and bff.ts is +148 lines (crossProjectPolicy, runtimeFramework, effect options) — together the largest remaining server-core divergence by far (diff stat: 551 insertions across the [F] hunks, 518 of them in these two files). The implementations live entirely in fork-owned packages: runtime-extensions imports ServerTelemetryUserConfig back from server-core (telemetry.ts:7-8), and the cross-project evaluator lives in bff-core. The hunks are purely additive (only a one-line loaderFailureMode tweak touches upstream lines), so merge risk is moderate rather than high, but every upstream reshuffle of these files (upstream reorganizes config types regularly) will conflict against 500+ fork lines.

**Direction:** Shrink the M-file hunks: move the interface definitions into the fork-owned packages that implement them (ServerTelemetry* into @modern-js/server-runtime-extensions, BffCrossProjectPolicy/effect types into bff-core or plugin-bff) and leave server-core with either a one-line re-export or a `declare module '@modern-js/server-core'` augmentation shipped by runtime-extensions. Both prod-server and app-tools already depend on the fork packages, so the types stay in scope for config authors. Do this opportunistically — the FORK-DIVERGENCE ledger already tags these [F], and the change is mechanical.

**Verifier:** Verified with one numeric correction: git diff 8a744c1b31..HEAD --stat shows server.ts +370 and bff.ts +148 = 515 insertions (not 518) against 3 deletions; purely additive except the loaderFailureMode line, exactly as described. runtime-extensions imports ServerTelemetryUserConfig back from server-core (telemetry.ts:7-8) and bff-core hosts the cross-project evaluator (resolveCrossProjectPolicy.ts references BffCrossProjectPolicyUserConfig structurally). Direction caveat to record: the hunk cannot shrink to zero, because ServerUserConfig gains `telemetry?: ServerTelemetryUserConfig` (fork diff line +389) — that key must remain in server-core or come via `declare module` interface merging; and the augmentation route makes the config types visible only to consumers whose type graph includes runtime-extensions (true for prod-server/app-tools today, but a re-export stub in server-core is the safer mechanical form). Moving fork-only interfaces out reduces upstream merge conflict surface, so the direction helps rather than harms merges. Minor severity is correct.

### server-lane#6 [MINOR/hygiene/small-fix] Dead-broken source ships in the published prod-server artifact and is structurally invisible to every gate

**Files:** `packages/server/prod-server/package.json:24`, `scripts/tsgo-dts.mjs:35`, `scripts/tsgo-critical.txt`

**Evidence:** Three independent gaps let the broken legacy cluster (finding 1) survive: (1) rslib builds the whole src tree, so dist for the publicly published @modern-js/prod-server (publishConfig.access: public) includes the dead server/ and libs/ modules even though no export reaches them; (2) the only 'typecheck' in the package build is dts emission with noCheck: true (scripts/tsgo-dts.mjs:35), which happily emits declarations for files whose imports do not resolve; (3) the repo's actual typecheck gate, validate:tsgo, runs only the 7 tsconfigs listed in scripts/tsgo-critical.txt — prod-server is not among them, while the two zombie adapter packages are. The net effect: a package can contain a module importing a nonexistent file ('../type') for months with green CI.

**Direction:** After deleting the dead cluster, add packages/server/prod-server to scripts/tsgo-critical.txt (it is the production-serving package; the two koa/express slots it would replace should be removed with their packages). Longer-term, consider a cheap repo-wide `tsgo --noEmit` lane or making tsgo-dts run without noCheck for packages whose tsconfig.include is the full src, so unresolvable imports fail the build that produces the published artifact.

**Verifier:** All three gaps verify, with one count correction. (1) The shared rslib config (scripts/rslib/src/index.ts) is bundle:false with outBase './src', i.e., full-tree bundleless transpile; dist/cjs/server/ and dist/cjs/libs/ on disk contain the dead modules, and publishConfig.access is 'public' (the package.json:24 line cite is off — publishConfig is near the end of the file — but the claim is accurate). (2) scripts/tsgo-dts.mjs hard-sets noCheck:true in normalizeCompilerOptions, so dts emission cannot catch the unresolvable '../type' / serverManager imports. (3) validate:tsgo (scripts/tsgo-critical.mjs) runs exactly the configs in tsgo-critical.txt — which has 14 entries, not 7 — and prod-server's tsconfig is absent while plugin-koa/plugin-express tsgo configs are present. Correction: '7 tsconfigs' should read '14'. Fork-owned: both scripts are A in the fork diff and prod-server/package.json's build hunks are fork-added. Proposed direction (add prod-server to the critical list after deleting the dead cluster, drop the koa/express slots with their packages) is fork-internal tooling and merge-neutral. Note the dead cluster currently fails tsgo typecheck by construction, so prod-server can only be added to tsgo-critical.txt after finding 1's deletion lands.

## scripts-superapp  (9 confirmed)

All four directories are fully fork-owned (git A-status vs 8a744c1b31) and were built in a single burst (2026-05-01), then never touched. The area is dramatically over-built relative to what actually executes: the only load path that really runs is the 1k-line Node fetch runner (superapp-load) invoked by load.test.ts; the 5.2k-line superapp-k6 lane is structurally incapable of executing in CI (k6/autocannon are installed nowhere, the certification commands point at a server that is never started, and every failure mode is converted to exit 0), yet the readiness report counts its skipped artifacts as passed performance evidence. There are three parallel copies of the process-orchestration toolkit (waitForHttp/reservePort/stopChildProcess/log capture), a release gate that can never fail, and ~1.4k lines of test suites that no runner ever executes.

### scripts-superapp#1 [CRITICAL/correctness/delete] The entire superapp-k6 load lane (5.2k lines) can never execute: k6/autocannon are installed nowhere and every invocation silently exits 0 against a server that is never started

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/superapp-k6/run-superapp-k6.js:1371-1377`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/superapp-certification/run-superapp-certification.js:110-154`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/mise.toml`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/superapp-certification.yml:44-77`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/superapp-k6/__tests__/certification-profile.test.js:42-45`

**Evidence:** Three independent facts make the lane decorative: (1) k6 and autocannon are not dependencies (no match in any package.json or pnpm-lock.yaml), not in mise.toml (pnpm only), and no CI workflow installs them; (2) no caller ever passes --require-k6/--require-autocannon (repo-wide grep outside scripts/superapp-k6 returns nothing), so resolveK6Binary failure takes the 'skipped' path with exitCode 0 (run-superapp-k6.js:1371-1377) — certification-profile.test.js:42-45 even asserts the require flags are absent; (3) even with binaries present, superappLoadThresholdCommands (run-superapp-certification.js:110-154) builds run-superapp-k6.js commands without --app-dir or --base-url, so k6 would hit DEFAULT_BASE_URL http://localhost:8080 where certification never starts a server — the runner's own --app-dir orchestration feature (run-superapp-k6.js:1637-1745) is used by no caller. The scheduled nightly certification (superapp-certification.yml cron, PROFILE_INPUT default 'nightly') therefore runs 4 'threshold gate' commands per night that always skip-pass. The lane has never been run end-to-end.

**Direction:** Decide explicitly: either make it real (add a k6 install step + autocannon devDependency to the certification workflow, pass --app-dir tests/integration/superapp-portfolio and --require-k6/--require-autocannon in superappLoadThresholdCommands) or delete scripts/superapp-k6 entirely and keep the Node-based superapp-load runner, which is the only load path that actually executes. Given a month of zero execution, deletion is the cheaper honest option.

**Verifier:** Verified on all three legs. k6/autocannon absent from every package.json, pnpm-lock.yaml, and node_modules/.bin; the mise config (actual path is .mise.toml, not mise.toml — content claim correct) pins only pnpm; superapp-certification.yml installs nothing else and its cron defaults PROFILE_INPUT to 'nightly'. --require-k6/--require-autocannon are referenced only inside scripts/superapp-k6 (incl. certification-profile.test.js:42,45 asserting their absence). superappLoadThresholdCommands (run-superapp-certification.js:110-154) omits --app-dir/--base-url so DEFAULT_BASE_URL http://localhost:8080 (run-superapp-k6.js:33) is targeted with no server; missing-binary preflight returns status 'skipped'/exitCode 0 (lines 1371-1377 k6, 1400-1407 autocannon) and main propagates exit 0. Nightly = release(2)+nightly(2) = 4 skip-passing commands, confirmed at lines 279/357. Two minor caveats that don't refute: (a) the workflow can run on self-hosted runners (vars.SELF_LINUX_LABELS) that might have k6 on PATH, but even then runs would hit a nonexistent localhost:8080 server — never a meaningful execution; (b) 'has never been run end-to-end' is an inference, though consistent with all repo evidence. All cited files are fork-added (A in diff vs 8a744c1b31); proposed direction touches no upstream code.

### scripts-superapp#2 [MAJOR/correctness/small-fix] Readiness report converts skipped/unknown load artifacts into 'passed' performance evidence

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/superapp-certification/generate-readiness-report.js:149-161`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/superapp-certification/generate-readiness-report.js:123-137`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/superapp-k6/run-superapp-k6.js:1236-1237`

**Evidence:** When k6 is missing the runner writes summary.json with status 'unknown' (run-superapp-k6.js:1237 'isSkipped ? unknown : ...'). generate-readiness-report.js walks all summary.json files but normalizeSummaryStatus (lines 149-161) never reads summary.status — it derives status from failedCount/failedCommandCount/unexpectedErrorCount, all absent on a skipped k6 artifact, returning 'passed'. suiteDimensions (lines 130-131) maps suite 'superapp-k6-load' via /stress|load/ to ['stress','performance']. Net effect: the nightly readiness report marks stress and performance dimensions 'passed' and overall 'ready' from load runs that never happened, defeating the report's stated purpose of evidence-based certification.

**Direction:** In normalizeSummaryStatus, honor the artifact envelope's own status field first (map 'unknown'->'skipped', 'failed'->'failed', 'warning'->'passed-with-warnings'), and make createReadiness treat dimensions whose only evidence is skipped as 'incomplete', not 'passed'.

**Verifier:** Verified, and actually understated: normalizeSummaryStatus (generate-readiness-report.js:149-161) ignores summary.status AND budgetFailures, so even a genuinely failed k6 envelope (status 'failed' set via budgetFailures, which the envelope never mirrors into failedCount) would normalize to 'passed' — the laundering applies to failures too, not just skips. run-superapp-k6.js:1237 confirmed ('isSkipped ? unknown'); the envelope (artifact-schema.js:20-58) carries no failedCount/failedCommandCount/unexpectedErrorCount fields; suiteDimensions line 130 maps /stress|load/ to ['stress','performance']. One slight overstatement: in a nightly run the stress/performance dimensions also receive genuine passed evidence from rstest stress/soak suites, so 'overall ready' is not produced solely by the phantom load runs — but the skipped artifacts are still counted as false 'passed' evidence and would mask regressions. Core claim verifies; fork-added file; direction is sound and fork-local.

Only nuance: main().catch can set exitCode 1 on an environmental exception (e.g., reservePort failure), so 'can never fail' strictly means 'its designed check can never fail the gate' — which is the substance of the claim. ### scripts-superapp#7 [MINOR/correctness/small-fix] Upstream-drift gate selection via commands.slice(0, 4) is a fragile positional coupling, and the smoke profile re-runs gates CI already runs

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/superapp-certification/run-superapp-certification.js:459-466`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/superapp-certification/run-superapp-certification.js:518-530`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/superapp-certification/run-superapp-certification.js:157-163`

**Evidence:** runUpstreamDrift picks 'root gates' to run inside the merge worktree as commands.slice(0, 4) (lines 460 and 519), silently depending on lint/changeset/package-json/dependencies being the first four entries of the smoke list (lines 158-163). Inserting or reordering a smoke command changes drift-gate coverage with no error. Those same four commands also duplicate the standard PR CI gates, so the smoke certification profile mostly re-runs what every PR already runs.

**Direction:** Tag gate commands explicitly (e.g., driftGate: true on the command objects) and filter by tag instead of slicing; optionally drop lint/changeset/package-json/dependencies from the certification smoke profile since CI owns them.

**Verifier:** Verified. slice(0, 4) appears at run-superapp-certification.js:460 (dry-run) and 519 (live), and the first four smoke commands are lint/changeset/package-json/dependencies (lines 159-162) with nothing enforcing that ordering. The CI-duplication half also checks out: lint-Linux.yml runs lint:package-json/lint/check-changeset (lines 47-53) and check-dependencies.yml runs check-dependencies (line 33) on PRs. File is fork-added; tagging commands instead of slicing is a fork-local fix with no upstream impact. Minor severity is right — it's a latent maintenance trap, not a current failure.

Ran 'node --test scripts/mv-integration-pilot/__tests__/*.test.js': exactly 31 tests, 31 pass — wiring rot confirmed, not breakage. bf7dc130e0 commit message confirms the superapp-soak/destroy orphan-deletion precedent (~5.8k LOC, 'zero inbound references') and that nightly (ultramodern-nightly.yml:39) already runs test:scripts, making the one-line fix viable. ### scripts-mv#3 [MAJOR/correctness/rewrite] mv-ci-hardening certifies a phantom CI: every check command it budgets is executed by nothing

**Files:** `scripts/mv-ci-hardening/mv-ci-hardening-profile.json:37`, `scripts/mv-ci-hardening/mv-ci-hardening-profile.json:49`, `scripts/mv-ci-hardening/mv-ci-hardening-profile.json:61`, `scripts/mv-ci-hardening/validator.js:184`, `.github/workflows/mv-ci-hardening.yml`

**Direction:** Make the profile load-bearing: have validate-ci-hardening.js resolve each check.command's file arguments and fail if targets are missing, and add a mode (used by mv-ci-hardening.yml or nightly) that actually executes profile.checks with their timeoutMinutes — turning the declared budgets into enforcement. CI-HARDENING-0001 is 'Status: Active' (line 3). The 'deleting the __tests__ trees would still pass this gate' claim is logically sound given the validator. Fork-added (profile, validator, workflow all new). Direction is fork-local and safe.

### scripts-mv#4 [MAJOR/architecture/refactor] Two incompatible 'topology manifest' schemas, both schemaVersion 1, inside the same script family

**Files:** `scripts/mv-integration-pilot/reference-topology.js:229`, `scripts/mv-integration-pilot/reference-topology.js:303-310`, `scripts/mv-zephyr-profile/validate-zephyr-profile.js:807`, `docs/super-app-rfc-adr/contracts/mv-topology-manifest.schema.json`

**Evidence:** The canonical ADR-0012 schema (docs contract, validated by mv-zephyr-profile) has shape {profile, environments, topology:{shells,remotes,services}, policies}. mv-integration-pilot/reference-topology.js defines a second, incompatible shape {preset:'presetUltramodern', shell, remotes, effectServices} also at SCHEMA_VERSION=1, and all four drill scripts build on it via loadReferenceTopology. Worse, the pilot validator hardwires its own fixture's cardinality — 'topology must define exactly two vertical remotes' and 'exactly one horizontal design-system remote' (reference-topology.js:303-310) — so it can never validate any real topology other than the committed fixture. mv-production-rollout's certification profile then pins the pilot shape as the 'mv-trust-topology-contract' (production-certification-profile.json:39-47), entrenching the non-canonical schema.

**Direction:** Converge on the canonical contract schema: port the drill family to read the ADR-0012 manifest shape (mv-zephyr-profile's validateTopologyManifest already implements it), drop the fixture-cardinality assertions or move them into the fixture's own test, and put the shared topology validation in scripts/lib so both families consume one implementation.

**Verifier:** Verified with one evidence correction. reference-topology.js: SCHEMA_VERSION=1 (line 5, checked at 231), preset 'presetUltramodern' (line 237), shape {shell, remotes, effectServices}; canonical ADR-0012 schema is {profile, environments, topology:{shells,remotes,services}, policies} also schemaVersion const 1, validated by validate-zephyr-profile.js:807 (SCHEMA_VERSION=1 at line 7). Cardinality hardwiring confirmed at reference-topology.js:303-311 ('exactly two vertical remotes', 'exactly one horizontal design-system remote') — it genuinely can only validate the committed fixture's shape. production-certification-profile.json pins reference-topology.js as 'mv-trust-topology-contract' (lines ~39-47). CORRECTION: only 3 of 4 drill scripts use loadReferenceTopology (remote-failure, rollback-kill-switch, vertical-extraction); design-system-bad-release-drill.js does not import reference-topology at all. Core claim stands. Fork-added; convergence direction is fork-local.

### scripts-mv#6 [MAJOR/testing/rewrite] Drill 'evidence' is hand-authored fixture data, yet ADRs cite it as production proof

**Files:** `scripts/mv-integration-pilot/rollback-kill-switch-drill.js:220-247`, `scripts/mv-integration-pilot/__fixtures__/rollback-kill-switch.json`, `docs/super-app-rfc-adr/SUNSET-DECISION-0001-compatibility-lanes.md`

Real integration machinery exists (tests/integration/superapp-*, scripts/superapp-local-control-plane) but is not connected.

**Direction:** Pick a lane: (a) make drills real — have the superapp integration suites or local-control-plane emit drill-report JSON as artifacts and point the drill validators at those outputs instead of __fixtures__; or (b) relabel the scripts as contract-shape documentation, move fixtures under the schema contracts, and amend the evidence docs to stop citing fixture validation as drill results.

The evidence doc rollback-evidence.md states outright 'Rollback behavior is certified from uw2-05-rollback-kill-switch-slo in scripts/mv-integration-pilot/__fixtures__/rollback-kill-switch.json' and lists the fixture numbers as 'certified rollback properties'. SUNSET-DECISION-0001 line 38 cites that evidence file as 'proves fallback ordering, LKG selection, revocation precedence, and mitigation within SLO budget'. Gates can only fail by editing fixtures — confirmed. Fork-added (drills, fixtures, evidence docs all new). Direction is fork-local.

CANONICAL_FALLBACKS in remote-failure-drills.js duplicates the MV_TIMEOUT/MV_ENTRY_LOAD_FAILED/MV_INTEGRITY_MISMATCH codes and the 'modernjs:mv-runtime-parity' event name whose source of truth is packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/fallbackTelemetry.ts (DEFAULT_EVENT_NAME). reference-topology.js:154 hardcodes the event name a third time. None import or cross-check the others, so a runtime rename silently invalidates every 'canonical' gate.

### scripts-mv#10 [MINOR/correctness/small-fix] Zephyr source scan flags URLs inside comments: stripComments exists but is not applied in validateSourceConstraints

**Files:** `scripts/mv-zephyr-profile/validate-zephyr-profile.js:33`, `scripts/mv-zephyr-profile/validate-zephyr-profile.js:213-229`

**Evidence:** validateWithZephyrPlacement runs stripComments(content) before pattern checks (line 75), but validateSourceConstraints applies urlLiteralPattern and the boot-hack patterns to the raw file content (lines 214-247). Any quoted https URL in a comment or license header of a scanned app file (e.g. // see 'https://example.com/docs') produces a hardcoded-url violation, and document.write/createElement('script') examples in comments trip the boot-hack rules. Latent today only because the tool is orphaned (see finding 1), but it makes the scanner unusable on real codebases if revived.

**Direction:** Apply stripComments (already defined at line 33) to content before the urlLiteralPattern/bootHackPatterns loops in validateSourceConstraints — note line numbers must then be computed against the stripped offsets or comments replaced with equal-length whitespace to keep detectLine accurate.

**Verifier:** Verified. stripComments defined at validate-zephyr-profile.js:33-34 and applied in validateWithZephyrPlacement (line 76: const source = stripComments(content)), but validateSourceConstraints reads raw content (fs.readFileSync at ~214) and runs urlLiteralPattern plus all bootHackPatterns against it unstripped (lines ~215-248). A quoted URL in a comment matches urlLiteralPattern (the pattern only requires quote-delimited https?://), and document.write( / createElement('script')...remoteEntry in comments trips forbidden-boot-hack. Correctly scoped as latent (tool is orphaned per finding 1) and minor. The direction's caveat about detectLine offsets is accurate — stripComments as written shortens content, so comments must be replaced with equal-length whitespace to keep line reporting correct. Fork-added; fix is fork-local.

## scripts-ultramodern  (10 confirmed)

The publish lane (ultramodern-publish + production-readiness) is the healthy core: the scope-renaming full-cohort republish to @bleedingdev genuinely cannot be done with pnpm/changesets, it is wired into CI, and its trust gates are defense-in-depth. Everything else in scope is in varying states of rot: contract-doctor validates a workspace shape two generations old and fails 15 checks against a freshly generated workspace (verified by running it), preflight — the documented readiness gate — therefore fails on a clean tree, and zephyr-live-evidence, cloudflare-ssr-validation, and version-switching are one-shot evidence harnesses pinned to retired topologies (or, in version-switching's case, a self-referential simulation that can never fail). None of the eight directories' ~2.7k lines of tests are wired into test:scripts or any workflow, which is why all of this drift went unnoticed.

### scripts-ultramodern#1 [MAJOR/correctness/rewrite] contract-doctor validates an obsolete workspace shape and fails every freshly generated workspace

**Files:** `scripts/ultramodern-contract-doctor/run-contract-doctor.js:5`, `scripts/ultramodern-contract-doctor/run-contract-doctor.js:15-32`, `scripts/ultramodern-contract-doctor/run-contract-doctor.js:321-327`, `scripts/ultramodern-contract-doctor/run-contract-doctor.js:632`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/versions.ts:6`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/contracts.ts:115`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/descriptors.ts:95`

**Evidence:** Doctor pins EXPECTED_TANSTACK_ROUTER='1.170.8' (run-contract-doctor.js:5) while the generator emits 1.170.15 (versions.ts:6, set 2026-06-03); doctor reads topology.remotes (run-contract-doctor.js:321) while contracts.ts:115 writes 'verticals'; doctor expects remote-commerce/remote-identity under apps/remotes/ (run-contract-doctor.js:15-32) but 'commerce' no longer appears anywhere in create/src — default workspaces are shell-super-app + on-demand verticals/<domain> (descriptors.ts:95). Running `node scripts/ultramodern-preflight/run-preflight.js --json` on the clean tree: contract-doctor step fails with 15 failing checks (topology-remotes, tanstack-version-shell-super-app, all full-stack-* checks). The doctor also re-pins zephyr-rspack-plugin '1.1.1' (line 632) independently of versions.ts. Its only consumer is preflight; no CI runs it.

**Direction:** Delete the doctor's duplicated checks and make preflight rely on the workspace's own generated scripts/validate-ultramodern-workspace.mjs (maintained in lockstep with the generator and currently passing). If a repo-side doctor is still wanted, rebuild it to import TANSTACK_ROUTER_VERSION/ZEPHYR_RSPACK_PLUGIN_VERSION and topology shape from @modern-js/create instead of hardcoding a snapshot that drifts.

**Verifier:** Core claim verified by execution: I ran `node scripts/ultramodern-preflight/run-preflight.js --json` on the clean tree; the contract-doctor step fails with exactly 15 failing checks matching the cited IDs (topology-remotes, tanstack-version-shell-super-app, full-stack-* for remote-commerce/remote-identity). All cited evidence is accurate: run-contract-doctor.js:5 pins '1.170.8' vs versions.ts:6 '1.170.15'; line 321 reads topology.remotes while contracts.ts:115 emits 'verticals'; FULL_STACK_VERTICALS (lines 15-32) expects apps/remotes/remote-commerce|remote-identity and 'commerce' appears nowhere in packages/toolkit/create/src; line 632 hardcodes zephyr-rspack-plugin '1.1.1'; the only consumer is run-preflight.js:243-245, no CI. Two corrections: (1) the zephyr '1.1.1' pin currently MATCHES versions.ts, so that is duplication risk, not active drift; (2) fixing the doctor alone will not make preflight pass — preflight's own smoke checks (>=4 control-plane processes, design-system remote, 2 full-stack verticals) also fail because the default workspace now generates only a shell. Severity downgraded critical→major: the tool is wholly broken but nothing in CI consumes it, so blast radius is the documented dev command and developer trust, not publish/production. Proposed direction is fork-internal and creates no upstream-merge friction.

### scripts-ultramodern#2 [MAJOR/testing/small-fix] validate:ultramodern-preflight (the documented readiness gate) fails on a clean tree and its tests run nowhere

**Files:** `scripts/ultramodern-preflight/run-preflight.js:243`, `scripts/ultramodern-preflight/__tests__/run-preflight.test.js:8`, `package.json:80`, `docs/super-app-rfc-adr/PREFLIGHT-0001-ultramodern-superapp-readiness.md:32`

**Evidence:** `node --test scripts/ultramodern-preflight/__tests__/run-preflight.test.js` fails both tests on main-ultramodern ('fail' !== 'pass'), because run-preflight.js:243 runs the drifted contract-doctor. PREFLIGHT-0001 documents `pnpm run validate:ultramodern-preflight` as the readiness command, but no workflow runs it, and the root test:scripts glob (package.json:80) covers lib/module-sdk-contracts/boundary-guards/mv-*/release-gates/ai-capabilities only — none of the 8 scripts/ultramodern-* test suites (~2.7k test lines, including the npm-publish pipeline's own tests in scripts/ultramodern-publish/__tests__/) are wired into test:scripts or any workflow. ultramodern-nightly.yml:39 runs test:scripts, so adding the globs is cheap.

**Direction:** Add scripts/ultramodern-publish, ultramodern-production-readiness, ultramodern-preflight (and whatever survives the deletions below) to the test:scripts glob so nightly exercises them; wire validate:ultramodern-preflight into ultramodern-nightly.yml once the doctor drift is fixed. A committed-broken gate test is worse than no gate — it trains people to ignore the command the ADR advertises.

**Verifier:** Verified by execution: `pnpm run validate:ultramodern-preflight` (package.json:57) exits 1 on the clean tree; `node --test scripts/ultramodern-preflight/__tests__/run-preflight.test.js` fails 3 of 4 tests (the suite has 4 tests, not 2 — minor count error in the evidence; only 'live mode requires an existing installed workspace' passes). package.json:80 test:scripts glob covers exactly lib/module-sdk-contracts/boundary-guards/mv-lane-policy/mv-ci-hardening/release-gates/ai-capabilities — none of the 8 ultramodern-* suites (2736 test lines, confirmed by wc) are wired in; no workflow references ultramodern-preflight; ultramodern-nightly.yml:39 runs test:scripts. PREFLIGHT-0001 documents the command (~line 32). One correction to the causal claim: the failure is NOT solely the drifted contract-doctor — preflight's own smoke checks (control-plane-process-count expects >=4, control-plane-roles expects design-system-remote + 2 Effect-BFF verticals) independently fail against the current shell-only default workspace, and test 2 also asserts the retired remote-commerce/remote-identity process IDs. So the proposed 'wire into nightly once the doctor drift is fixed' is insufficient — preflight's smoke expectations and its tests need the same generator-realignment. Severity major stands; fork-owned (package.json entries and all files fork-added).

### scripts-ultramodern#3 [MINOR/dead-code/delete] ultramodern-version-switching is a tautological self-simulation that can never fail — delete it

**Files:** `scripts/ultramodern-version-switching/run-version-switching-proof.js:7-20`, `scripts/ultramodern-version-switching/run-version-switching-proof.js:204-216`, `scripts/ultramodern-version-switching/run-version-switching-proof.js:355-375`, `docs/super-app-rfc-adr/OPERATIONS-0001-micro-vertical-certification-and-operations.md:182`

**Evidence:** runScenario spins up a local node:http server (createProofServer) whose manifest/ui/api responses are generated from the same MARKERS constant table (lines 7-20) that the assertions then compare against (lines 355-375) — both sides of every assertion come from the same literal, so the 'proof' exercises nothing real: no build artifact, no Zephyr, no generated workspace. The package name '@modern-js-ultramodern/commerce-vertical' matches no generator output. 717 lines (571 script + 146 test) referenced by no workflow, package.json script, or runnable doc; OPERATIONS-0001:182 itself states simulated Zephyr evidence 'is not a substitute for live version-switching proof'. Last touched 2026-05-26.

**Direction:** Delete scripts/ultramodern-version-switching/ entirely. Real version-switching evidence belongs in the generated workspace's own proof scripts (cloudflare:proof / browser smoke) where actual artifacts exist, or in a rebuilt live-evidence harness (see zephyr-live-evidence finding).

**Verifier:** Evidence verified: createProofServer serves manifest/ui-marker/api-marker responses generated from the same MARKERS constant (lines 7-20) the assertions compare against (runScenario lines 346-375), so no build artifact, Zephyr resolution, or generated workspace is exercised; '@modern-js-ultramodern/commerce-vertical' (line 22) matches nothing in packages/toolkit/create/src; line counts exact (571+146=717); zero references in package.json, workflows, or docs (grep confirms); last commit 2026-05-26; OPERATIONS-0001 quote exists (~line 181). 'Can never fail' is mild hyperbole — the skew negative control is designed to fail and does — but the core characterization (a proof that can detect no real regression) is accurate. Severity downgraded major→minor: the script is referenced by nothing at all, so its blast radius is zero until someone runs it and mistakes the output for evidence; deletion is cleanup, not risk mitigation. Delete direction is safe (fork-owned directory, no upstream counterpart).

### scripts-ultramodern#4 [MINOR/duplication/delete] ultramodern-cloudflare-ssr-validation duplicates the workspace template's cloudflare proof and targets the retired Tractor layout — delete

**Files:** `scripts/ultramodern-cloudflare-ssr-validation/validate-cloudflare-ssr.js:126-165`, `scripts/ultramodern-cloudflare-ssr-validation/README.md:8-14`, `packages/toolkit/create/templates/workspace-scripts/ultramodern-cloudflare-proof.mjs`, `scripts/ultramodern-production-readiness/run-published-create-proof.mjs:688`

**Evidence:** The 563-line repo script and the 809-line workspace template ultramodern-cloudflare-proof.mjs implement the same validation: import .output/server/index.mjs as a Worker, emulate the ASSETS binding (validate-cloudflare-ssr.js:126-165), fetch SSR/BFF/MF-manifest routes, and extract build markers (findBuildMarker vs the template's markerFromJson). The template copy is generated into every workspace and exercised by CI via `pnpm cloudflare:proof` (run-published-create-proof.mjs:688); the repo copy is referenced by no workflow or package.json script, and its README/examples target `apps/remotes/remote-explore` and explore/decide/checkout — a workspace layout the current generator cannot produce. Only ADR docs (CLOUDFLARE-ZEPHYR-0001, ADOPTION-0001) still point at it.

**Direction:** Delete scripts/ultramodern-cloudflare-ssr-validation/ and update CLOUDFLARE-ZEPHYR-0001 + ADOPTION-0001 to point at the workspace-owned scripts/ultramodern-cloudflare-proof.mjs (and proof-cloudflare-version.mjs) that generated apps already carry.

**Verifier:** Partially verified; one material evidence error. Verified: 563 vs 809 lines exact; only the three ADR docs reference the repo script (no workflow/package.json); README and the script's own defaults target the retired layout (--bff defaults to '/explore-api/effect/explore/readiness' at lines 15 and 522, README example uses apps/remotes/remote-explore); the template copy is generated into workspaces and exercised via `pnpm cloudflare:proof` from run-published-create-proof.mjs:~688. ERROR in the duplication claim: the template ultramodern-cloudflare-proof.mjs does NOT import .output/server/index.mjs as a Worker or emulate the ASSETS binding — it is HTTP-fetch-only against deployed public URLs (driven by proof-cloudflare-version.mjs reading the generated contract). Only the repo script does in-process Worker + ASSETS emulation, i.e. local no-deploy validation, a capability the workspace proof does not replace. The overlap is in assertion intent (SSR/BFF/MF-manifest routes, markers, security headers), not mechanism. Also the repo script is parameterizable, so 'targets the retired layout' is true of defaults/docs, not a hard incompatibility. Kept because the dead-code/stale-doc core verifies, but severity downgraded major→minor and the delete direction should acknowledge it removes the only no-deploy local validation path the ADRs document.

## scripts-misc  (10 confirmed)

The gate-validator families (boundary-guards, release-gates, module-sdk-contracts, lib) are well-tested and share a deliberate zero-dependency kit, but the surrounding wiring has decayed: the fork-added prepare-root.mjs silently turned the PR type-check and integration-test build steps into no-ops, the release-candidate gate's PR lane requires untracked dist artifacts so it cannot pass on a fresh checkout, and the ai-capabilities family is largely theater (a tautological nightly parity check plus an MCP bridge that speaks the wrong wire framing and so cannot work with any real MCP client). On top of that, ~600 lines of ownership/blast-radius gating in boundary-guards have no contract instance or profile wiring anywhere, and the 65-script root surface contains verified-dead entries while CI re-spells other entries inline, inviting drift.

### scripts-misc#1 [CRITICAL/correctness/rewrite] prepare-root.mjs CI skip silently disabled the Type Check gate and the integration-test build step

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/prepare-root.mjs:15`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/type-check.yml:59`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/integration-test-Linux.yml:61`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/integration-test-Windows.yml:70`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/package.json:24`

**Evidence:** Fork-added scripts/prepare-root.mjs:15-20 exits immediately when process.env.CI === 'true' ('Skipping root prepare in CI; workflows run explicit build gates'). GitHub Actions always sets CI=true, yet three workflows still use `pnpm run prepare` as their only build/type-check step: type-check.yml:59 (the job's entire purpose — upstream at 8a744c1b31 ran `prepare` = 'npm run prepare-build && husky install', i.e. a full nx build acting as the type check), integration-test-Linux.yml:61 and integration-test-Windows.yml:70 (which then run test:framework against never-built packages). prepare-root.mjs was added 2026-05-27 in commit e3e6f23813 'fix: unblock bleedingdev trusted publish' — fixing the publish lane by making prepare CI-aware instead of using `--ignore-scripts` (which upstream type-check.yml:57 already used before the fork hunk removed it). No workflow except ultramodern-nightly.yml (lines 179/238/314) calls `pnpm run prepare-build` explicitly, so PR-level type checking is now a no-op, and `validate:tsgo` (the fork's actual type gate, package.json:75) is wired into zero workflows.

**Direction:** Restore the build in CI: change type-check.yml and both integration-test workflows to run `pnpm run prepare-build` (the pattern nightly already proves), or revert `prepare` to upstream semantics and give the publish workflow `pnpm install --ignore-scripts` instead of the CI guard in prepare-root.mjs. Also wire `validate:tsgo` into type-check.yml so the tsgo lane is actually gated on PRs.

**Verifier:** Verified end to end. prepare-root.mjs:15-20 (fork-added in e3e6f23813, 2026-05-27) exits when CI==='true'; GitHub Actions always sets CI=true. type-check.yml's only check step (line 57-59) is `pnpm run prepare`, now a guaranteed no-op, so the PR Type Check job passes green while checking nothing — a silent gate disable. The fork diff also removed upstream's `pnpm install --ignore-scripts` in all three workflows, so even the install-time prepare lifecycle hits the same CI skip. Both integration workflows' Prepare step is likewise a no-op (their failure mode is loud — tests against unbuilt dist — but the build step is still disabled as claimed). validate:tsgo (package.json:75) is in zero workflows; prepare-build appears only in ultramodern-nightly.yml:179/238/314. Proposed direction is sound and merge-friendly: reverting `prepare` to upstream semantics plus `--ignore-scripts` in the publish lane actually reduces divergence from vanilla Modern.js.

### scripts-misc#2 [MAJOR/correctness/rewrite] MCP CLI bridge implements LSP Content-Length framing instead of MCP newline-delimited stdio — interoperates with nothing

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/ai-capabilities/mcp-cli-bridge.js:48`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/ai-capabilities/adapter.js:154`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/ai-capabilities/__tests__/adapter.test.js:123`

**Evidence:** createTransport (mcp-cli-bridge.js:48-109) reads `Content-Length: N\r\n\r\n`-framed messages and writes replies with Content-Length headers (lines 52-58, 64-90). That is LSP framing; the MCP stdio transport for the protocol version the bridge itself advertises (PROTOCOL_VERSION '2024-11-05', line 13) is newline-delimited JSON-RPC. A conformant client (Claude Desktop, mcporter — which adapter.js:178-185 generates a config for via createMcporterConfig) sends `{...}\n`; the bridge waits forever for `\r\n\r\n` (headerEnd === -1 → return, line 64-67) and never answers initialize, while its replies would be unparseable anyway. No test exercises the transport: __tests__/adapter.test.js only tests invocation-building and manifest shape (the only bridge references are bridgeArgs strings at lines 123/134). The generated artifacts (.modern/mcp/adapter-manifest.json, .modern/mcporter.json) have zero consumers in the repo — evidence the bridge has never been run against a real client.

**Direction:** Rewrite createTransport to newline-delimited JSON-RPC (readline over stdin, `JSON.stringify(message) + '\n'` on stdout) and add one end-to-end test that spawns the bridge and round-trips initialize/tools-list/tools-call. If nobody is actually consuming the MCP surface yet, delete mcp-cli-bridge.js and generate-mcp-adapter.js instead and keep only the contract validation (see the parity finding).

**Verifier:** Verified. createTransport (mcp-cli-bridge.js:48-109) reads Content-Length/\r\n\r\n framed input and writes Content-Length+Content-Type headers — LSP framing. The bridge advertises PROTOCOL_VERSION '2024-11-05' (line 13), whose MCP stdio transport is newline-delimited JSON-RPC; a conformant client's `{...}\n` never produces a \r\n\r\n, so parseMessages returns at headerEnd === -1 and initialize is never answered. adapter.test.js references the bridge only as bridgeArgs strings (lines 123/134); no test spawns it. Generated artifacts (.modern/mcp/adapter-manifest.json, .modern/mcporter.json per generate-mcp-adapter.js:15-16) have no consumers. All ai-capabilities files are fork-added (A). One wrinkle for the delete option: rc-contract-profile.json's mcp-cli-bridge-contract target asserts the literal 'Content-Length' snippet in the bridge source, so either fix would also need that profile entry updated.

### scripts-misc#3 [MAJOR/dead-code/delete] Nightly 'MCP/CLI capability parity' gate is tautological — it can never fail on parity

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/ai-capabilities/validator.js:99`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/ai-capabilities/validator.js:146`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/ai-capabilities/validate-mcp-cli-parity.js:33`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/ultramodern-nightly.yml:68`

**Evidence:** validateMcpCliParity first calls loadCapabilityContract → validateContractShape → validateCapabilityShape, which unconditionally calls validateCliDescriptor (validator.js:99) and throws if any capability lacks cli.command/bin/args. generateParityReport then computes missingCli as capabilities with mcp but no cli.command (validator.js:146-153) — by construction always empty for any contract that survived loading, so report.passed is always true and parityCoverage always 1. The nightly job (ultramodern-nightly.yml:41-68) therefore only re-validates JSON shape, which `test:scripts` already covers. ADR-0009 §2.4 promises release-time parity checks for 'schema, behavior, and errors' against the real CLI; nothing compares the contract's argMap to the actual `modern runtime` command surface in packages/solutions/app-tools/src/commands/runtime.ts, which the contract duplicates by hand (flags like --token-env/--header-name at runtime.ts:150-215).

**Direction:** Either make parity real — load the runtime command definitions (or `modern runtime status --help` output) and diff flags against cli.argMap in the contract — or delete validate-mcp-cli-parity.js and the nightly mcp-cli-parity job, keeping contract-shape validation in the existing test:scripts suite. The current job costs a CI lane to assert a constant.

**Verifier:** Verified. validateMcpCliParity → loadCapabilityContract → validateContractShape → validateCapabilityShape → validateCliDescriptor (validator.js:99, 31-59) throws on any capability lacking cli.command/bin/args, so generateParityReport.missingCli (146-153) is empty by construction for any contract that loads; report.passed is always true and the failure branch in validate-mcp-cli-parity.js (lines 37-41) is unreachable. The nightly mcp-cli-parity job (ultramodern-nightly.yml:41-68) thus re-asserts shape validation that test:scripts (package.json:80 includes ai-capabilities/__tests__) and the nightly script-validators job already run. ADR-0009 promises behavior/exit-code/error-shape parity (lines 33, 93-97); runtime.ts flags --token-env/--header-name at lines 150/155/210/215 are duplicated by hand in the contract with no automated diff. Fork-owned (all files A). Note the rc-contract gateCommands also run validate:mcp-cli-parity (rc-contract-profile.json:407), so the tautology is asserted in two gate systems.

### scripts-misc#4 [MAJOR/dead-code/delete] Ownership/blast-radius gate: ~600 lines in boundary-guards with no contract instance, no profile wiring, no changed-paths producer

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/boundary-guards/validator.js:233`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/boundary-guards/validator.js:805`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/boundary-guards/profile.json:1`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/docs/super-app-rfc-adr/contracts/mv-ownership.schema.json:1`

Keeping a fully built but unwired gate makes ADR-0015 look enforced when it is not.

**Verifier:** Verified. scripts/boundary-guards/profile.json (the only profile) has no ownershipGate key; the ownership path in runBoundaryGuardChecks executes only `if (profile.ownershipGate)`. Repo-wide grep for ownershipGate/pathRules hits only validator.js, its tests, and mv-ownership.schema.json — no contract instance exists and nothing produces changedPaths. Schema drift confirmed: mv-ownership.schema.json line 16 requires 'extractionBoundaryChecks', a string absent from validator.js entirely. Ownership functions span roughly lines 233-973 of the 1110-line file, matching the ~600-line estimate. Fork-owned (all boundary-guards files and the schema are A). ### scripts-misc#6 [MINOR/testing/consolidate] Gate-snapshot CI step validates a file the same job just wrote — tautological self-check

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/release-gates/validate-gate-snapshot.js:1`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/release-gates/validator.js:314`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/module-certification-gates.yml:105`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/release-contract-gates.yml:118`

**Evidence:** validate-release-candidate-gates.js always persists .modern/contract-gates.json via writeGateSnapshot (validator.js:314-375) on both pass and fail (validate-release-candidate-gates.js:157-199); the very next workflow step in both gate workflows runs validate-gate-snapshot.js against that same path with --required-gate. Neither workflow uploads the snapshot as an artifact (no upload-artifact step in either file), and the runtime consumer (packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/contractGateSnapshotStore.ts via MODERN_CONTRACT_GATES_FILE) reads a deployment-local file, never the CI workspace copy. So the step is a round-trip test of release-gates' own serializer, executed as a CI gate on every run.

**Direction:** Remove the 'Validate gate snapshot artifact shape' step from both workflows and keep the writeGateSnapshot/validateGateSnapshotFile round-trip in scripts/release-gates/__tests__/validator.test.js where it belongs. Keep validate-gate-snapshot.js only if something will validate snapshots produced elsewhere (e.g. a deployed app's .modern dir); otherwise delete the CLI and the root `validate:gate-snapshot` script too.

**Verifier:** Verified. validate-release-candidate-gates.js persists the snapshot on success (main → persistGateSnapshot passed:true) and in the catch path on failure; the next step in both module-certification-gates.yml and release-contract-gates.yml runs validate-gate-snapshot.js against the same .modern/contract-gates.json with --required-gate. Since the validate step only runs after the gate step succeeded, the required gate entry always exists with passed=true — a pure round-trip of the family's own serializer. grep confirms no upload-artifact step in either workflow; packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/contractGateSnapshotStore.ts reads a deployment-local path (default '.modern/contract-gates.json' via MODERN_CONTRACT_GATES_FILE), never the CI copy. Fork-owned (all files A). Minor is the right severity — wasted CI step, not a correctness hole.
