### scripts-misc#8 [MINOR/duplication/refactor] scripts/lib under-extraction: 12 hand-rolled parseArgs loops and families re-implementing kit helpers they already import

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/lib/validation-kit.js:1`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/boundary-guards/validator.js:76`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/ai-capabilities/adapter.js:8`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/test-orchestrator/run-parallel-tests.js:76`

**Evidence:** validation-kit.js (174 lines) is the only shared module, yet 12 scripts each define their own `const parseArgs = argv => {...}` switch-loop with identical '--flag value' / 'Unknown argument' semantics (ai-capabilities x3, boundary-guards, module-sdk-contracts, mv-ci-hardening, mv-lane-policy, mv-zephyr-profile, release-gates x2, superapp-load, test-orchestrator) despite engines requiring Node >=20 where node:util parseArgs exists. boundary-guards/validator.js imports validation-kit (lines 3-7) but re-implements isObject/isNonEmptyString/assertStringArray (lines 76-94) duplicating ensureObject/ensureString/ensureNonEmptyStringArray; ai-capabilities defines isRecord twice (validator.js:15, adapter.js:8); test-orchestrator re-implements readJson (run-parallel-tests.js:76) vs validation-kit readJsonFile. The lib README's own consolidation note shows the team already values byte-stable behavior, so the remaining duplicates are just unfinished extraction.

**Direction:** Add scripts/lib/cli-args.js (thin wrapper over node:util parseArgs preserving the 'Unknown argument: X' message) and migrate the 12 CLIs; replace the boundary-guards/ai-capabilities local guards with validation-kit equivalents (the kit's error messages are already the asserted contract). This is mechanical and keeps the zero-dependency rule.

**Verifier:** Verified exactly. grep finds precisely 12 files defining `const parseArgs = argv` across the named families (ai-capabilities x3, release-gates x2, boundary-guards, module-sdk-contracts, mv-lane-policy, mv-ci-hardening, mv-zephyr-profile, superapp-load, test-orchestrator). validation-kit.js is 174 lines and exports ensureObject/ensureString/ensureNonEmptyStringArray/readJsonFile; boundary-guards/validator.js imports the kit (lines 3-7) yet defines local isObject/isNonEmptyString/assertStringArray (76-94); isRecord duplicated at ai-capabilities/validator.js:15 and adapter.js:8; test-orchestrator readJson at run-parallel-tests.js:76. All fork-added. Direction caveat the finding itself acknowledges: scripts/lib/README.md says error messages are byte-asserted by the test suites, so any cli-args extraction must preserve the exact 'Unknown argument: X' strings.

### scripts-misc#9 [MINOR/dx/small-fix] test-orchestrator: unpinned pnpm via `npm exec pnpm@11`, POSIX-only pkill, and a documented 'runaway' lane no script uses

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/test-orchestrator/run-parallel-tests.js:404`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/test-orchestrator/run-parallel-tests.js:283`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/test-orchestrator/run-parallel-tests.js:212`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/test-orchestrator/README.md:34`

**Evidence:** The orchestrator is a healthy dev-only tool (not in CI; root scripts test:all:parallel*), but: every test script is spawned as `npm exec --yes pnpm@11 -- --dir ...` (run-parallel-tests.js:404-415), which resolves the latest pnpm 11.x from the network instead of the repo-pinned 11.5.3 (mise.toml) and can violate engines `pnpm >=11.5.2 <11.6.0` (package.json:84) once 11.6 ships; killProcessTree shells out to pkill (lines 283-301), dead on the Windows CI matrix this repo otherwise supports; and detectRunawayScript (line 212-215) plus the README flag --timeout-runaway-ms gate on a `test-orchestrator-runaway` marker that matches zero package.json scripts repo-wide — a dead lane and a stale doc promise. The 983-line file also has no tests of its own despite test:scripts covering every sibling family.

**Direction:** Spawn plain `pnpm` (mise guarantees the pinned version on dev machines) instead of npm exec; guard the pkill path with process.platform checks (or use taskkill on win32); drop the runaway lane and its README section until something marks a script. Extract parseLogInsights/laneForCommand into a testable module and cover them in test:scripts.

**Verifier:** Core claims verified: spawn uses `npm exec --yes pnpm@11 --` (run-parallel-tests.js ~404-415), which floats within 11.x while engines pin `>=11.5.2 <11.6.0` (package.json:84) and the repo pins 11.5.3 — though in `.mise.toml` plus packageManager, not 'mise.toml'. killProcessTree uses pkill for tree-kill (283-301); on Windows the spawnSync failure is swallowed and a plain process.kill fallback still fires, so it degrades (no child-tree cleanup) rather than being fully 'dead', and since the orchestrator is dev-only and not in any workflow, the 'Windows CI matrix' framing overstates it — it affects Windows dev machines. `test-orchestrator-runaway` matches zero package.json scripts repo-wide (dead lane confirmed; README flag is line 33, not 34). 983 lines, no own tests, absent from test:scripts — confirmed, though 'every sibling family' is slightly broad (mv-zephyr-profile/superapp-load also lack coverage in test:scripts). Fork-owned (A). Keep with these corrections.

### scripts-misc#10 [MINOR/hygiene/small-fix] boundary-anti-patterns.yml: Node 18 vs engines >=20, and a full pnpm install for validators designed to need none

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/boundary-anti-patterns.yml:38`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/boundary-anti-patterns.yml:44`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/lib/README.md:14`

**Evidence:** The workflow pins node-version 18 (boundary-anti-patterns.yml:38-41) while root engines require node >=20 (package.json:83) and every other fork workflow uses 20/22/24; it also uses unpinned jdx/mise-action@v3 (line 34) where sibling workflows SHA-pin. It then runs `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` (line 44) before invoking two validators whose whole design point — per scripts/lib/README.md 'Plain CommonJS, Node builtins only ... without any install or build step' and the bun-smoke lane that proves it — is zero dependencies. The install is the slowest step of the job and adds supply-chain surface for no benefit; check-boundary-violations.js and validate-module-sdk-contracts.js require only repo files and node builtins.

**Direction:** Bump to Node 20+, SHA-pin mise-action (or drop it — without the install step pnpm is not needed at all), and delete the Install Dependencies step so the job is checkout + node, matching the family's zero-dep contract and cutting job time substantially.

**Verifier:** Verified: node-version 18 at line 41 vs engines node >=20 (package.json:83); jdx/mise-action@v3 unpinned at line 34 while sibling workflows SHA-pin it; `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` at line 45 before validators that scripts/lib/README.md:14-15 explicitly documents as runnable 'without any install or build step' under node and bun. One correction: 'every other fork workflow uses 20/22/24' is not strictly true — bun-superapp-smoke.yml:52 also pins Node 18 — but that does not rescue this workflow's mismatch. Workflow is fork-added (A). Direction note: the job invokes the validators via `pnpm run ...`, so dropping the install step is fine but pnpm (via mise) and the setup-node `cache: pnpm` line need adjusting together; alternatively call `node scripts/...` directly and drop pnpm entirely.

## ci-workflows  (11 confirmed)

The fork-added gate/certification workflows are individually well-built (SHA-pinned sensitive lanes, harden-runner, trusted publishing via OIDC environment), but the CI system as a whole has two load-bearing failures: the root `prepare` script no-ops under CI=true so the type-check and integration build steps silently pass without doing anything, and every upstream-derived PR gate (lint/type-check/unit/integration/builder-e2e) is branch-filtered to `main` while the fork develops on `main-ultramodern` — meaning fork PRs get only the contract validators, no tests. Around that, there is heavy duplication (two near-identical evidence-gate workflows plus a bun lane re-running both; nightly and scheduled certification re-running the same superapp suites an hour apart), and a security-posture inversion where the fork un-pinned 17 upstream-pinned actions and dropped --ignore-scripts while its own validator only enforces pinning on 5 hard-coded files and literally requires the weaker `egress-policy: audit`. All wiring otherwise resolves: every referenced script, profile, and test path exists.

### ci-workflows#1 [CRITICAL/correctness/small-fix] Root `prepare` is a CI no-op, silently neutering type-check and the integration-test build step

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/prepare-root.mjs:15-22`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/type-check.yml:59`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/integration-test-Linux.yml:61`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/integration-test-Windows.yml:70`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/package.json:24`

**Evidence:** Upstream baseline had `"prepare": "npm run prepare-build && husky install"`, and the type-check/integration workflows use `pnpm run prepare` as their actual build/type-check step (type-check.yml's only meaningful step, named 'Type Check', is `mise exec -- pnpm run prepare`). The fork replaced prepare with fork-added scripts/prepare-root.mjs, which at lines 15-20 does `if (process.env.CI === 'true') { console.log('Skipping root prepare in CI; workflows run explicit build gates.'); process.exit(0); }`. GitHub Actions always sets CI=true, so the 'Type Check' step exits 0 having checked nothing, and integration-test-* 'Prepare' builds nothing (the Nx Cache restore step in those workflows is also dead since nx never runs). tests/package.json `test:framework` is plain `rstest` with no build, and commit 74f54c4ca1 confirms suites expect an external prepare-build. Any green run of these gates is a false positive.

**Direction:** In the fork hunks of type-check.yml and integration-test-*.yml, replace `pnpm run prepare` with an explicit `pnpm run prepare-build` (matching what ultramodern-nightly.yml:179 already does), or remove the CI guard from prepare-root.mjs. Then the existing Nx Cache steps become useful again.

**Verifier:** Verified. scripts/prepare-root.mjs:15-20 exits 0 when CI === 'true' (GitHub Actions always sets CI=true); package.json:24 fork hunk rewired 'prepare' from upstream's 'npm run prepare-build && husky install' to this script; type-check.yml:59 and integration-test-*.yml run 'pnpm run prepare' as their build/type-check step. prepare-root.mjs was introduced 2026-05-27 (e3e6f23813); green Type Check runs from 2026-04-30 show the step taking ~2.5 min (real build), so any run after the change exits green having checked nothing. One correction: for the integration lanes the failure mode is a loud red (missing dist), not a silent green — 'any green run is a false positive' strictly applies to type-check; integration would fail rather than falsely pass. Proposed direction (explicit pnpm run prepare-build, matching ultramodern-nightly.yml:179/238/314) is sound and keeps the mise hunks small.

### ci-workflows#2 [CRITICAL/architecture/small-fix] Every upstream-derived PR/push gate (lint, type-check, unit, integration, builder-e2e) never triggers on the fork's branch, yet the fork actively maintains modifications in all of them

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/type-check.yml:4-5`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/lint-Linux.yml:4-6`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/ut-macOS.yml:4-6`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/ut-Windows.yml:4-6`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/test-builder-e2e.yml:2-4`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/check-dependencies.yml:2-8`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/integration-test-Linux.yml:5-7`

**Evidence:** The fork repo's default/dev branch is main-ultramodern (`git ls-remote --symref bleedingdev HEAD` -> refs/heads/main-ultramodern), but all upstream-derived gates filter `pull_request: branches: [main]` / `push: branches: [main]` (trigger lines are upstream-identical). So PRs to and pushes on main-ultramodern get NO lint, type-check, unit tests, integration tests, builder e2e, or dependency checks — the only PR gates that actually run are the fork-added contract validators (which have no branch filter). Meanwhile the fork carries substantial M-hunks in every one of these dead workflows (mise migration, action bumps, dropped --ignore-scripts, removed `cache: 'pnpm'` on integration-test-Linux/type-check) — maintenance cost on lanes that never execute. The fork clearly knows the correct branch: docs-pages.yml:4-6 and workflow-security.yml:11-13 trigger on main-ultramodern.

**Direction:** Add main-ultramodern to the branch filters inside the already-M files (one-line hunks), restoring real PR coverage for unit/lint/type/integration suites; alternatively decide these lanes are dead on the fork and revert all fork hunks in them to shrink divergence. Combined with finding 1, fixing only the triggers without fixing `prepare` would still yield no-op type-check runs — do both together.

**Verifier:** Verified. Remote default branch is main-ultramodern (ls-remote --symref confirms); all eight cited workflows filter pull_request/push on 'main' only, while docs-pages.yml and workflow-security.yml correctly target main-ultramodern. Live run history confirms: Type Check and Integration Test(Linux) only ran on main-ultramodern via manual workflow_dispatch (last 2026-04-30); the only automatic main runs are upstream-mirror pushes (v3.2.2 on 2026-06-04, failed). No merge_group runs exist, so the unfiltered merge_group trigger provides no actual coverage either. Fork-ownership caveat: the trigger lines themselves are upstream-identical hunks, but the defect cannot exist in vanilla Modern.js (whose default branch IS main) — it was created by the fork's branch rename while the fork carries M-hunks in every one of these files, so it is fork-owned in substance. Direction note: adding main-ultramodern to the filters is a tiny fork hunk and does not impede upstream merges; per the investigator's own caveat it must land together with the finding-1 fix or type-check stays a no-op.

### ci-workflows#3 [MAJOR/security/small-fix] Fork downgraded upstream's SHA-pinned actions to floating tags in 17 places, including github-script@v7 holding a repo-scoped token

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/issue-label.yml:12`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/integration-test-Linux.yml:47`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/type-check.yml:44`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/test-builder-e2e.yml:23,32`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/boundary-anti-patterns.yml:34`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/bun-superapp-smoke.yml:40,45`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/module-certification-gates.yml:59`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/release-contract-gates.yml:72`

**Evidence:** Upstream baseline pinned every action to a full commit SHA (e.g. actions/cache@6f8efc29..., actions/github-script@d7906e4a... in issue-label.yml). Fork hunks replaced them with mutable tags: `jdx/mise-action@v3` (9 workflows), `actions/cache@v4` (4), `oven-sh/setup-bun@v2`, and worst, issue-label.yml:12 `actions/github-script@v7` which receives `secrets.REPO_SCOPED_TOKEN` — a tag-hijack of that action exfiltrates a repo-scoped PAT. This directly contradicts the fork's own posture: .github/renovate.json extends helpers:pinGitHubActionDigests, and scripts/security/validate-github-workflows.mjs:113-122 enforces SHA pinning — but only for 5 'sensitive' files. Inconsistently, the same mise-action IS SHA-pinned (5ac50f77...) in docs-pages/nightly/publish workflows.

**Direction:** Pin all 17 floating refs to the SHAs already used elsewhere in the repo (mise-action 5ac50f77...=v3.2.0 is already in 6 workflows), and extend validate-github-workflows.mjs SHA enforcement to all workflows (see next finding) so this cannot regress.

**Verifier:** Core claim verified; inventory count of 17 floating refs is exact (jdx/mise-action@v3 x11, actions/cache@v4 x4, oven-sh/setup-bun@v2 x1, actions/github-script@v7 x1), though the per-action breakdown in the evidence is slightly off (mise@v3 appears in 11 workflows, not 9). issue-label.yml:12 is a literal downgrade from upstream's github-script@d7906e4a SHA to @v7 with secrets.REPO_SCOPED_TOKEN passed in — confirmed in the 8a744c1b31 diff. Renovate config does extend helpers:pinGitHubActionDigests and validate-github-workflows.mjs only enforces SHA pinning on 5 sensitive files (line 103 early return), so the inconsistency claim holds; the same mise-action is SHA-pinned (5ac50f77) in 7 other workflows. Wording caveat: for the fork-ADDED workflows there was no upstream pin to 'downgrade' — those are fork-introduced unpinned refs — but that does not weaken the security claim. Direction is correct and merge-safe.

### ci-workflows#4 [MAJOR/security/rewrite] validate-github-workflows.mjs enforces the weaker egress mode and covers only 5 hard-coded files, leaving the gaps it exists to prevent

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/security/validate-github-workflows.mjs:10-22`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/security/validate-github-workflows.mjs:103-122`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/security/validate-github-workflows.mjs:5`

**Evidence:** Three defects: (1) requiredSensitiveTokens (line 21) demands the literal string 'egress-policy: audit' — if anyone hardens a publish workflow to `egress-policy: block`, the security gate FAILS; the check enforces the weaker mode. Audit mode on publish-bleedingdev.yml only logs egress, it blocks nothing. (2) Line 103 returns early for all but 5 hard-coded sensitiveWorkflowPaths, so SHA pinning, permissions blocks, persist-credentials and timeouts are unenforced for the other 17 workflows — which is exactly where the 17 unpinned actions and 7 missing permissions blocks live. (3) Line 5 computes repoRoot from `new URL(...).pathname`, which breaks on Windows drive letters and percent-encoded paths (should be fileURLToPath); the repo runs Windows CI lanes.

**Direction:** Accept 'egress-policy: (audit|block)' — or require block for the publish workflow specifically; apply pinning + `permissions:` checks to every workflow file (the repo is one renovate pass away from full compliance); use node:url fileURLToPath for repoRoot.

**Verifier:** All three defects verified in the fork-added script: (1) requiredSensitiveTokens line 21 requires the literal 'egress-policy: audit', so hardening any sensitive workflow to 'block' fails the gate (publish-bleedingdev.yml uses audit in all 3 jobs — log-only); (2) line 103-104 early-returns for everything outside the 5 sensitiveWorkflowPaths, so SHA pinning/permissions/persist-credentials/timeout checks never apply to the other 17 workflows — exactly where the 17 unpinned refs (finding 3) and the 7 missing permissions blocks (finding 9) live; (3) line 5 uses new URL(...).pathname instead of fileURLToPath, which breaks on Windows drive letters/percent-encoded paths. Minor framing correction on (3): the script itself only runs in the Linux workflow-security lane, so the Windows issue affects local Windows devs, not CI lanes. Proposed direction is sound.

### ci-workflows#5 [MAJOR/duplication/consolidate] module-certification-gates.yml and release-contract-gates.yml are ~95% copy-paste with unused workflow_call surfaces, and bun-superapp-smoke re-runs both profiles again

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/module-certification-gates.yml:32-108`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/release-contract-gates.yml:45-121`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/bun-superapp-smoke.yml:58-59`

**Evidence:** The two gate workflows are byte-similar (same triggers shape, same inputs, same RUN_COMMANDS shell juggling, same two validation steps) differing only in profile JSON path, default evidence dir, and --required-gate name. Both declare `workflow_call` (module-certification-gates.yml:32, release-contract-gates.yml:45) but `grep 'uses: ./'` across .github/workflows returns nothing — no caller exists, dead trigger surface. On top, package.json's validate:bun-smoke (run by bun-superapp-smoke.yml) executes validate-release-candidate-gates.js for BOTH profiles plus module-sdk-contracts and boundary-guards, so a PR touching scripts/release-gates/** runs the same dependency-free validations in 3 separate workflows (4 counting boundary-anti-patterns for the contracts part).

**Direction:** Consolidate into one 'contract-gates' workflow with a 2-entry job matrix (profile, required-gate, evidence-dir); delete the unused workflow_call blocks; scope bun-superapp-smoke down to a pure bun-runtime-compat check of one validator rather than a superset re-run.

**Verifier:** Verified by reading both fork-added workflows side by side: identical job structure, identical RUN_COMMANDS shell logic, identical two validation steps; only profile path, default evidence dir, and --required-gate differ (plus a longer pull_request paths list in release-contract-gates). grep for 'uses: ./' across .github/workflows and the template-workspace returns nothing — both workflow_call surfaces are dead. package.json validate:bun-smoke confirmed to run validate-release-candidate-gates.js for BOTH profiles (--skip-commands) plus module-sdk-contracts and boundary-guards, so a scripts/release-gates/** PR triggers the same dependency-free validations in module-certification-gates, release-contract-gates, and bun-superapp-smoke. Consolidation via a 2-entry matrix is reasonable and purely fork-local (no upstream merge impact).

### ci-workflows#6 [MAJOR/duplication/consolidate] Two scheduled heavy lanes (ultramodern-nightly and superapp-certification cron) run overlapping superapp suites and drift probes ~1h apart; the certification lane is missing the build/puppeteer steps the same suites need in nightly

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/ultramodern-nightly.yml:5-6,139-272`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/superapp-certification.yml:31-77`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/superapp-certification/run-superapp-certification.js:156-366`

**Evidence:** ultramodern-nightly (cron '23 1 * * *') runs superapp-erp, superapp-portfolio (including nightly.test.ts at line 264) and superapp-browser-matrix as jobs, each doing pnpm install + nx cache + `pnpm run prepare-build` + puppeteer postinstall. superapp-certification (cron '17 2 * * 1-5', profile defaults to 'nightly' on schedule at line 60) re-runs the same suites via run-superapp-certification.js — erp/portfolio smoke (script lines 162-171), browser-matrix-smoke (line 206), portfolio nightly.test.ts again (line 324) — on weekdays one hour later. Both lanes also run upstream-drift probes (nightly's superapp-upstream-drift job = `--profile smoke --drift-only`; certification defaults RUN_UPSTREAM_DRIFT=true). Worse, superapp-certification.yml's job (steps at lines 39-77) only does `pnpm install` — no prepare-build, no nx cache restore, no puppeteer install — though the identical suites in nightly require all three (ultramodern-nightly.yml:166-196), so the 180-minute certification lane either fails on missing dist or rebuilds the world uncached.

**Direction:** Drop the schedule trigger from superapp-certification.yml (keep workflow_dispatch for release-evidence runs) and let ultramodern-nightly be the single scheduled signal; or make nightly invoke the certification script once instead of duplicating the suite list inline. Whichever survives must gain the prepare-build/nx-cache/puppeteer steps.

**Verifier:** Core claims verified: nightly cron 23 1 * * * runs erp/portfolio/browser-matrix jobs with pnpm install + nx cache + prepare-build; superapp-certification cron 17 2 * * 1-5 defaults to the 'nightly' profile (line 60) and run-superapp-certification.js re-runs the same suites (erp/portfolio smoke lines 162-172, browser-matrix-smoke ~206, portfolio nightly ~324); both lanes run upstream-drift probes. The certification job (lines 39-77) indeed has no prepare-build and no nx cache restore. Empirical confirmation that the lane is broken: every scheduled run fails in ~1 minute (currently at the very first command, 'pnpm run lint', biome CLI/schema version mismatch — it never even reaches the missing-dist problem), and ultramodern-nightly has never executed (workflow registered 2026-06-11, zero runs). Two corrections: the 'no puppeteer install' sub-claim is weak — certification's pnpm install runs without --ignore-scripts and pnpm-workspace.yaml allowBuilds includes puppeteer, and tests/package.json prepare installs puppeteer; and the '~1h apart' overlap is prospective since nightly has not yet run. Consolidation direction is sound.

Node 18 pins confirmed at boundary-anti-patterns.yml:41 and bun-superapp-smoke.yml:52 against engines node>=20/pnpm 11.5.3, while sibling gates use Node 20/24. One caveat the direction already handles: module/release gate workflows DO need the install on the workflow_dispatch run_commands=true path (the command matrix runs real tests), so the install must be gated, not deleted.

### ci-workflows#9 [MINOR/security/small-fix] Most fork-added gate workflows lack permissions blocks; publish workflow grants id-token: write to all jobs

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/boundary-anti-patterns.yml`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/bun-superapp-smoke.yml`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/module-certification-gates.yml`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/release-contract-gates.yml`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/superapp-certification.yml`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/mv-ci-hardening.yml`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/mv-lane-policy.yml`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/publish-bleedingdev.yml:15-17`

**Evidence:** Seven fork-added workflows define no `permissions:` block at all (verified via grep -L), inheriting whatever the repo default grants, while the fork's own hardened workflows (ultramodern-nightly.yml:8-9, workflow-security.yml:22-23, publish) all set `permissions: contents: read`. The security validator only enforces this on its 5 hard-coded paths. Separately, publish-bleedingdev.yml grants `id-token: write` at workflow level (lines 15-17), so the publish-security and prepare-packages jobs receive an OIDC-mint capability only the `publish` job (which alone has `environment: npm-publish`) needs.

**Direction:** Add `permissions: contents: read` to the seven workflows; move `id-token: write` from workflow level into the publish job in publish-bleedingdev.yml; make validate-github-workflows.mjs enforce a permissions block repo-wide.

**Verifier:** Verified via grep -L: exactly the seven cited fork-added workflows (boundary-anti-patterns, bun-superapp-smoke, module-certification-gates, release-contract-gates, superapp-certification, mv-ci-hardening, mv-lane-policy) have no permissions: block, while docs-pages, diff, publish, nightly, and workflow-security set one. publish-bleedingdev.yml:15-17 grants workflow-level contents: read + id-token: write, so publish-security and prepare-packages jobs inherit OIDC-mint capability that only the publish job (the sole one with environment: npm-publish, line 138) needs. Note for the direction: moving id-token: write into the publish job still satisfies validate-github-workflows.mjs's substring check (line 125), so no validator change is required for that part.

### ci-workflows#11 [MINOR/hygiene/small-fix] docs-pages deploy installs with --no-frozen-lockfile, making published docs builds non-reproducible

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/docs-pages.yml:37`

**Evidence:** The fork-added Pages deploy runs `mise exec -- pnpm install --no-frozen-lockfile` — the only workflow in the repo that deliberately ignores the lockfile — so the deployed docs can resolve different dependency versions than anything CI ever tested, and a registry-published bad version flows straight to the public site. Every other fork workflow uses --frozen-lockfile. (Rest of the workflow is healthy: DOCS_BASE is genuinely consumed by packages/document/rspress.config.ts, actions are SHA-pinned, permissions are scoped.)

**Direction:** Switch to `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`; if the lockfile genuinely cannot satisfy the docs project, that is a lockfile bug to fix, not to bypass at deploy time.

**Verifier:** Verified. Fork-added docs-pages.yml:37 runs 'mise exec -- pnpm install --no-frozen-lockfile' — the only explicit lockfile opt-out in any workflow (other lanes use --frozen-lockfile or bare 'pnpm install', which defaults to frozen in CI), feeding a public GitHub Pages deploy. The 'rest is healthy' characterization also checks out (SHA-pinned actions, scoped permissions, DOCS_BASE resolution). Minor severity and the proposed frozen-lockfile fix are appropriate and fork-local.

Rejected during verification: Fork hunks removed upstream's `--ignore-scripts` from CI pnpm installs, enabling arbitrary dependency postinstall execution for no benefit

## tests-fork-suites  (13 confirmed)

The fork's integration suites are comprehensive in intent (TanStack lane, Effect BFF lane, SuperApp chaos/load/security) but structurally heavyweight and partially broken: three fork-owned test files call the removed `jest` global under the rstest runner and currently fail at file level (their tests never execute), the same app fixtures are rebuilt by up to six parallel test files coordinated only by ad-hoc locks and copy-pasted 4-attempt retry loops (the root of the recurring 'dist race' the fork keeps patching), and ~10k lines of superapp-portfolio 'workload/contract artifact' modules are largely self-referential fixture data tested against hand-duplicated literals. There is also substantial copy-paste boilerplate (4 lock implementations, 6 identical applyBaseConfig copies, 8 identical type-test copies, 16 hand-written .js-to-.ts re-export shims) and one whole suite (bff-effect-lambda-only) that duplicates coverage already in bff-effect.

### tests-fork-suites#1 [CRITICAL/correctness/small-fix] Three fork suites are dead under rstest: bare `jest.setTimeout` throws ReferenceError in beforeAll

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/routes-tanstack/tests/index.test.ts:36`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/routes-tanstack-create-routes/tests/index.test.ts:26`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/i18n/mf/test/app-level-ssr-serve.test.ts:68`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/utils/setSuiteTimeout.ts`

**Evidence:** The test runner is rstest (tests/rstest.config.mts, `globals: true`), and rstest 0.10.3's injected globals are only test/describe/it/expect/assert/hooks/rs/rstest (node_modules/@rstest/core/globals.d.ts) — no `jest`. Empirically verified with the repo's own rstest binary and an equivalent config: a beforeAll containing `jest.setTimeout(...)` fails with `ReferenceError: jest is not defined`, the file is marked failed and all its tests are skipped (probe output: failedFiles:1, skippedTests:1). These three fork-owned files contain exactly that pattern, so the flagship routes-tanstack browser E2E suite (9 tests incl. SSR bootstrap, redirects, mutations, blockers), the create-routes E2E suite, and the i18n MF app-level SSR serve suite never execute under `pnpm test:framework`. The fork even built `tests/utils/setSuiteTimeout.ts` precisely to abstract jest/vi/rstest timeouts and uses it in 25+ other files — these three were missed when upstream migrated tests to rstest (18671f635e, 2026-04-03; the jest call predates it, ed88dd46c2, 2026-02-06).

**Direction:** Replace the three bare `jest.setTimeout(...)` calls with `setSuiteTimeout(...)` from tests/utils/setSuiteTimeout.ts (already imported in sibling suites), then run the three files once to confirm they actually pass — they have not executed since the rstest migration, so latent assertion failures may surface. Add a lint guard (or grep in CI) banning the `jest.` global under tests/integration.

**Verifier:** Verified empirically: reproduced with the repo's own @rstest/core@0.10.3 and an equivalent globals:true config — a bare jest.setTimeout in beforeAll throws ReferenceError, the file is marked failed and its tests are skipped (failedFiles:1, skippedTests:1, exit code 1). All three cited files contain the pattern at the cited lines (routes-tanstack/tests/index.test.ts:36, routes-tanstack-create-routes/tests/index.test.ts:26, i18n/mf/test/app-level-ssr-serve.test.ts:68), rstest.config.mts has no setupFiles defining a jest global, and all three files plus tests/utils/setSuiteTimeout.ts are A (fork-added) vs 8a744c1b31. tests/package.json test:framework runs the default rstest config whose include matches all three. One framing correction: the suites are not silently dead — the hook error fails the whole framework run (CI integration-test-Linux/Windows run test:framework with no continue-on-error), so this is a loud, every-run failure (or the lane is being ignored), which if anything supports critical. Proposed direction (setSuiteTimeout, already used by ~21 sibling files) is correct and fork-local.

### tests-fork-suites#2 [MAJOR/architecture/refactor] Same-fixture rebuilds race across parallel test files; locking is inconsistent and retry loops are copy-pasted as a substitute for a build-once design

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/superapp-portfolio/tests/index.test.ts:486`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/superapp-portfolio/tests/browser-runtime.test.ts:569`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/superapp-portfolio/tests/chaos-toggles.test.ts:465`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/superapp-portfolio/tests/effect-bff-contracts.test.ts:174`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/superapp-portfolio/tests/effect-tanstack-contract-behavior.test.ts:524`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/routes-tanstack-mf/test/index.test.ts:434`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/routes-tanstack-mf/test/deploy-certification.test.ts:80`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/routes-tanstack-mf/tests/tanstack-mf-contract.test.ts:96`

**Evidence:** Six superapp-portfolio test files each run `modernBuild(appDir)` on the SAME fixture in beforeAll, but only browser-runtime-matrix.test.ts takes `acquireFixtureLock` (line 361); rstest runs files in a parallel worker pool, so the other five can clobber each other's dist. In routes-tanstack-mf, the host+remote+remote-2 triple build is implemented three separate times — `buildFederatedFixtureApp` (test/index.test.ts:434-456), `buildApp` (deploy-certification.test.ts:80-108) and `ensureTanstackMfDistFixtures` (tanstack-mf-contract.test.ts:101-146) — each a hand-rolled 4-attempt rm-dist/rebuild loop keyed on ENOTEMPTY, plus a 100x100ms poll for dist/api/effect/index.js (index.test.ts:467-481) after a successful build. The comments themselves name the cause: 'transient fs race while replacing the fixture's own dist'. Commit 74f54c4ca1 added 270 lines of reader/writer locking to modernTestUtils.js to fight the same class of race. The framework run pays for ~9 MF builds plus 6 portfolio builds of identical artifacts.

**Direction:** Make 'build this fixture's dist exactly once per run' a first-class primitive: either an rstest globalSetup that pre-builds each fixture used by multiple files, or a `buildFixtureOnce(appDir)` helper in tests/utils/modernTestUtils.js that holds the existing fixture lock and memoizes on a dist marker file. Then delete all three per-file retry loops and the waitForEffectEntry poll. This removes the standing flake source instead of retrying around it, and cuts the largest single chunk of suite runtime.

**Verifier:** Verified. All six superapp-portfolio files build the same appDir (path.resolve(__dirname,'../')); only browser-runtime-matrix.test.ts imports/takes acquireFixtureLock (line 361, and it builds into separate dist-browser-runtime-matrix-* roots); the other five (index:486, browser-runtime:569, chaos-toggles:465, effect-bff-contracts:174, effect-tanstack-contract-behavior:524) build/serve the shared dist in beforeAll with no lock and no env gate, under rstest's parallel file pool. modernTestUtils' reader/writer lock (added by fork commit 74f54c4ca1, absent upstream) protects packages/*/dist only, not the fixture's own dist, so the cross-file clobber hazard is real. The three copy-pasted 4-attempt rm-dist/ENOTEMPTY retry loops and the 100x100ms waitForEffectEntry poll exist exactly as cited. One correction: all three routes-tanstack-mf files DO hold acquireFixtureLock (index:848, deploy-certification:304, tanstack-mf-contract beforeAll), so the mf retry loops guard against external writers (prepare-build/nx cache restores per the in-code comment), not unlocked cross-file races — the inconsistency claim applies chiefly to superapp-portfolio. Also '~9 MF builds of identical artifacts' is slightly overstated: each file passes its own env to the builds. Direction (build-once primitive) is sound and fork-local; all flagged files are A vs baseline.

### tests-fork-suites#3 [MAJOR/duplication/consolidate] Four parallel file-lock implementations in fork test code

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/utils/fixtureLock.ts`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/i18n/test-utils.ts:13`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/utils/modernTestUtils.js:39`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/utils/modernTestUtils.js:97`

**Evidence:** fixtureLock.ts (acquireFixtureLock: mkdir lock in os.tmpdir keyed by sha1 of dir, 200ms poll, 10min stale reap), i18n/test-utils.ts:13-62 (acquireTestLock: open-wx lockfile in __dirname/.locks, 200ms poll, 15min stale reap — fork-added hunk in an M file, verified via git diff vs 8a744c1b31), modernTestUtils.js:39-95 (acquireWorkspacePackageBuildLock: near-byte-identical to fixtureLock but in JS), and modernTestUtils.js:97-210 (a reader/writer dist lock). The first three are the same mkdir/poll/stale-reap algorithm written three times in two languages with three different stale windows; subtle behavior differences (handle.close after unlink vs rm -rf, mtime refresh) make their interactions hard to reason about — which is how the dist races of finding 2 survived multiple fix rounds.

**Direction:** Consolidate to a single lock module in tests/utils (the TS fixtureLock.ts implementation, parameterized by key and stale age); port i18n's acquireTestLock call sites to it and re-express modernTestUtils' package-build lock on top of it. The reader/writer dist lock can stay separate but should reuse the same primitive.

**Verifier:** Verified by reading all four: tests/utils/fixtureLock.ts (mkdir lock, sha1-keyed tmpdir, 200ms poll, 10min stale), i18n/test-utils.ts:13-62 acquireTestLock (open-wx file in __dirname/.locks, 200ms poll, 15min stale), modernTestUtils.js acquireWorkspacePackageBuildLock (lines 48-86, near-identical mkdir algorithm, 10min stale), and the reader/writer dist lock (lines 88-274). Fork ownership confirmed: fixtureLock.ts is A; upstream modernTestUtils.js at 8a744c1b31 contains zero lock code (all hunks fork-added); upstream i18n/test-utils.ts had no acquireTestLock. Consolidation direction is fork-local and would not affect upstream merges (it concentrates fork hunks). Keeping major: the divergent stale windows/release semantics are the substrate for the dist-race fixes referenced in finding 2.

Line counts match (workload-domain-catalog 1454, workload-scenario-profiles 1262, effect-tanstack-contract-map 981, workload-generated-data 908, workload-chaos-failure-taxonomy 828, workload-validation-artifact 734, effect-tanstack-contract-coverage-artifact 686). workload-validation-artifact.test.ts hardcodes the 13-entry expectedEntityCounts + totalRecords 106960; workload-data.test.ts hardcodes the same entity-count literal a second time; effect-tanstack-contract-map.test.ts:18-40 re-lists every endpoint id and publicPath verbatim. These tests import only from ../shared/ (no packages/ code), so they pin fixture data against a hand-maintained copy of itself. Entire suite is A vs 8a744c1b31. ### tests-fork-suites#5 [MAJOR/testing/move] Committed generated TanStack mirrors (router.gen.ts / register.gen.d.ts / @mf-types) verified by string-contains contract tests — order-dependent double maintenance

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/routes-tanstack/tests/tanstack-data-flow-contract.test.ts:31`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/routes-tanstack/src/modern-tanstack/string/router.gen.ts`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-tanstack/src/cli/index.ts:225`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/routes-tanstack-mf/tests/tanstack-mf-contract.test.ts:490`

**Evidence:** plugin-tanstack writes router.gen.ts into the fixture's src during every build (cli/index.ts:225-233 writeFileIfChanged), and these generated files are committed (routes-tanstack/src/modern-tanstack/{string,stream}/router.gen.ts, mf-host/src/modern-tanstack/index/router.gen.ts, register.gen.d.ts, plus 9 @mf-types d.ts files in mf-host). tanstack-data-flow-contract.test.ts:31-50 asserts ~16 exact substrings of the committed file WITHOUT building first, so in the release-gate lane (rstest.superapp-contracts.config.mts runs only the contract files; invoked from scripts/release-gates/rc-contract-profile.json:410) it certifies the last-committed mirror, not the current generator — generator regressions pass until someone happens to rebuild and commit. Conversely in the full framework run the sibling index.test.ts build may rewrite the file before/after the contract test reads it depending on worker scheduling, and any template change dirties the working tree mid-CI. The same contract files are also matched by the default framework include, so tanstack-mf-contract's 3-app build runs twice across the two lanes.

**Direction:** Make the contract independent of committed mirrors: move the loader-bridge string contracts into plugin-tanstack's own unit tests that call generateTanstackRouterTypesSourceForEntry directly on a fixture route tree (the generator is a pure function of routes), and keep at most one integration assertion that the built fixture's generated file matches the generator output. Then either stop committing router.gen.ts/register.gen.d.ts in fixtures (generate at build, like dist) or add a CI cleanliness check that fails when a build dirties them.

**Verifier:** Verified. plugin-tanstack cli/index.ts writeTanstackRouterTypesForEntries writes router.gen.ts into appContext.srcDirectory on build (writeFileIfChanged ~line 226); routes-tanstack/src/modern-tanstack/{string,stream}/router.gen.ts + register.gen.d.ts are git-tracked, plus 9 @mf-types d.ts files A in mf-host. tanstack-data-flow-contract.test.ts asserts ~16 substrings of the committed file with no modernBuild anywhere in the file. rstest.superapp-contracts.config.mts includes exactly the contract files and is invoked from rc-contract-profile.json line 410 ('pnpm --dir tests run test:superapp-contracts'), so the release-gate lane certifies the last-committed mirror, not the current generator. The same files also match the default framework include ('integration/**/*.(spec|test).[jt]s?(x)'), so tanstack-mf-contract's 3-app build runs in both lanes. All cited files (incl. plugin-tanstack itself) are A vs baseline — entirely fork-owned. Direction is fork-local; no upstream-merge impact.

### tests-fork-suites#6 [MINOR/architecture/move] MF remote-load reliability logic (ADR-0003 surface) lives only inside the mf-host fixture, unit-tested in the integration tree, shipped nowhere

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/routes-tanstack-mf/mf-host/src/routes/mf/remoteLoaderCore.ts`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/routes-tanstack-mf/test/remote-loader-reliability.test.ts:1`

**Direction:** Promote remoteLoaderCore into a fork-owned package (natural home: @modern-js/plugin-tanstack runtime helpers or a small @modern-js/mf-reliability module under packages/runtime), move remote-loader-reliability.test.ts next to it as a package unit test, and have the mf-host fixture import the published API — turning the fixture into a true consumer test. **Verifier:** Facts verify: remoteLoaderCore.ts is 173 lines of retry/timeout/classification/contract-validation logic, remote-loader-reliability.test.ts (108 lines) imports it from the fixture path, and grep across packages/ and docs/ finds no other reference — nothing exports it. Fork-owned (routes-tanstack-mf is entirely A). Severity downgraded: ADR-0003 ('Decision Type: Data contract and testing strategy') explicitly prescribes exactly this shape — 'Add unit tests for remote loader timeout/retry/contract errors' plus 'dedicated integration fixture' — so the fixture-only placement is documented intent, not an overlooked shipping gap. The finding's residual value is the strategic question (promote to a package or accept fixture-only and simplify), which its own proposed direction already acknowledges as a team decision. Keep as minor with the 'inverse of test coverage' framing softened.

### tests-fork-suites#8 [MINOR/duplication/delete] 16 hand-written `.js -> export * from './x.ts'` shim files mirror every shared fixture module

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/superapp-portfolio/shared/portfolio-state.js`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/superapp-erp/shared/superapp-state.js`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/bff-effect/shared/effect-api.js`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/superapp-portfolio/tsconfig.json:6`

**Evidence:** 12 shims in superapp-portfolio/shared, 2 in superapp-erp/shared, 2 in bff-effect/shared — each a one-line `export * from './<name>.ts';` so that source files importing with NodeNext-style '.js' specifiers (e.g. api/effect/index.ts:20 `from '../../shared/portfolio-state.js'`) resolve under both the bundler and rstest. But the fixtures' tsconfigs use `"moduleResolution": "Bundler"` (superapp-portfolio/tsconfig.json:6, bff-effect/tsconfig.json:5), which permits extensionless relative imports — the .js suffix convention is the only reason the shims exist. Every new shared module requires remembering to add a mirror shim; forgetting one yields a resolution error only at integration-run time.

**Direction:** Drop the '.js' extension from the ~40 relative import sites in these three fixtures (Bundler resolution handles it in tsc, rspack and rstest alike) and delete all 16 shim files.

**Verifier:** Verified: exactly 12 shims in superapp-portfolio/shared, 2 in superapp-erp/shared, 2 in bff-effect/shared, each a one-line `export * from './<name>.ts';`, and all three fixtures' tsconfigs set moduleResolution Bundler. All fork-added. One caution on the direction: before deleting, confirm the bundler (rspack server/api compilation) resolves extensionless relative imports in these api/ trees the same way — the shims most plausibly exist because rspack resolves the literal '.js' specifier to the shim file rather than extension-aliasing to '.ts'; dropping the extension should work under Bundler resolution but needs one fixture build to confirm. Fork-local either way.

### tests-fork-suites#10 [MINOR/dx/consolidate] tsgo typechecks embedded in integration beforeAll across 8 suites — duplicated lane, inconsistent invocation, repeated per run

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/routes-tanstack/tests/index.test.ts:40`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/bff-runtime-parity/tests/index.test.ts:74`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/superapp-portfolio/tests/index.test.ts:482`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/superapp-erp/tests/index.test.ts:367`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/bff-effect-lambda-only/tests/index.test.ts:25`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/routes-tanstack-mf/test/index.test.ts:852`

**Evidence:** Eight fork suites run a full tsgo --noEmit over their fixture inside test setup (routes-tanstack, routes-tanstack-mf x3 apps via runTypecheck, bff-effect, bff-runtime-parity, bff-effect-lambda-only, superapp-erp, superapp-portfolio, deploy-server). Three invocation styles coexist: execFileSync(process.execPath, [tsgoBin...]) with require.resolve, execSync('pnpm exec tsgo ...') (spawns pnpm per test run), and execSync('pnpm tsgo'). Each adds tens of seconds per suite per framework run and re-runs on every retry, while the dedicated type-check workflow (.github/workflows/type-check.yml) doesn't cover fixtures at all — so fixture type safety is enforced only as a side effect of slow integration jobs.

**Direction:** Extract a single 'fixture typecheck' lane: one test file (or a CI step in type-check.yml) that iterates the fixture tsconfig list and runs tsgo once per fixture, then delete the per-suite beforeAll typechecks. Keeps the signal, removes the duplication and the pnpm-spawn inconsistency, and decouples typecheck failures from integration flake retries.

**Verifier:** Verified. Eight suites embed a tsgo --noEmit in test setup (routes-tanstack, routes-tanstack-mf, bff-effect, bff-effect-lambda-only, bff-runtime-parity, superapp-erp, superapp-portfolio, deploy-server) in exactly the three claimed styles: execFileSync(process.execPath,[tsgoBin...]) (routes-tanstack:41, bff-runtime-parity:78, deploy-server:23, routes-tanstack-mf:185), execSync('pnpm exec tsgo ...') (bff-effect:35, bff-effect-lambda-only:26, superapp-erp:367), execSync('pnpm tsgo --noEmit') (superapp-portfolio:482). .github/workflows/type-check.yml only runs 'pnpm run prepare' (package builds) — no fixture typecheck lane. deploy-server's tsgo hunk is fork-added (0 tsgo hits in the upstream file); all other suites are A. Extraction direction is fork-local.

### tests-fork-suites#12 [MINOR/upstream-compat/rewrite] bff-corss-project misspelling is upstream-inherited (do not rename); the fork's effect mirror inside it is hand-rolled JS duplicating every lambda endpoint

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/bff-corss-project`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/tests/integration/bff-corss-project/bff-api-app/api/effect/index.js`

**Evidence:** git ls-tree 8a744c1b31 confirms tests/integration/bff-corss-project exists at the upstream baseline — the misspelled name is vanilla Modern.js's, so renaming it in the fork would convert every future upstream change to this suite into rename conflicts for zero functional gain. What IS fork-owned: 10 added files including bff-api-app/api/effect/index.js — ~150 lines of plain JavaScript (with `/** @type {any} */` JSDoc casts) implementing a regex-based router (`/^\/user\/([^/]+)$/` etc.) that manually re-implements every api/lambda endpoint (hello, context, user/[id], upload with formData parsing and a silent fallback to mock values on parse failure) so the effect runtime mirrors the lambda app. Every lambda endpoint change now needs a synchronized hand edit in this regex router or the cross-project parity assertions drift; the silent catch-and-mock in the upload handler can also mask real multipart regressions.

**Direction:** Leave the directory name alone (document it in FORK-DIVERGENCE if needed). Convert bff-api-app/api/effect/index.js to TypeScript like every sibling effect fixture (mf-host/api/effect/index.ts etc.), replace the regex router with the same defineEffectBff HttpApi group pattern used elsewhere, and remove the catch-all mock fallback in the upload handler so parity failures surface instead of being faked.

**Verifier:** Verified. git ls-tree 8a744c1b31 confirms tests/integration/bff-corss-project exists upstream (rename advice correct). bff-api-app/api/effect/index.js is A (fork-added), 156 lines of JSDoc-typed JS that wraps defineEffectBff's handler with a manual pathname/regex router (/^\/user\/([^/]+)$/, /^\/hello\/([^/]+)$/, plus literal-path branches for /, /context, /upload) re-implementing the lambda endpoints, and the upload branch's catch falls back to fileName 'mock_image.png' / params {a:1,b:2} — which are exactly the values the cross-project test asserts (tests/index.test.ts:193 etc., client uploads a real file named mock_image.png), so a formData parsing regression would be invisible. Minor correction: the file is not purely hand-rolled — two endpoints (hello, traceHeader) already use the HttpApiBuilder group pattern; the manual router covers the remaining five. Direction is fork-local and sound.

## toolkit-docs-hygiene  (10 confirmed)

The small toolkit forks are mostly disciplined (mechanical churn plus a few well-scoped additions like sanitizeSSRPayload, which is tested), and the ADR set is unusually well-curated — statuses including the ADR-0001 revert and ADR-0017's grep-verified claims check out against code. The real problems are leftovers and ledger drift: a fork merge resurrected a dangling `export * from './babel'` in published @modern-js/types, plugin-runtime ships a dead 4-file legacy SSR remnant hidden behind a fork-added tsconfig exclude, the worker async-storage variant has zero consumers plus a concurrency-unsafe fallback, and FORK-DIVERGENCE.md's toolkit/tsconfig entries are factually wrong in ways that would mislead the next upstream sync (notably tagging the tsgo-critical base tsconfig change as take-upstream-safe). The 30-changeset backlog is a deliberate pre-V4 accumulation and mostly accurate, with one superseded February entry; hygiene nits include three tracked .codex session artifacts that survived an explicit untracking commit.

### toolkit-docs-hygiene#1 [MAJOR/correctness/small-fix] @modern-js/types re-exports a deleted module: `export * from './babel'` resolves to nothing

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/toolkit/types/common/index.d.ts:1`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/toolkit/types/common/moduleSdk.d.ts`

**Evidence:** common/index.d.ts:1 is a fork-added hunk (`+export * from './babel';` in diff 8a744c1b31..HEAD) but `packages/toolkit/types/common/` contains only index.d.ts and moduleSdk.d.ts — no babel.d.ts exists anywhere in the tree. Upstream commit 860596b344 ('chore: remove tools.babel #8107', in HEAD's history) deleted both the file and this line; a fork merge resolution resurrected the line without the file. The error is masked only because packages/tsconfig/base.json sets skipLibCheck:true; any consumer compiling with skipLibCheck:false gets 'Cannot find module ./babel' from a published .d.ts package.

**Direction:** Delete line 1 of common/index.d.ts (re-aligns with upstream, which removed it). Add a trivial lint/test in toolkit/types that resolves every relative re-export in shipped .d.ts files so merge resolutions can't silently resurrect dangling exports again.

**Verifier:** Fully verified. `+export * from './babel';` is a fork-added hunk vs 8a744c1b31 (baseline common/index.d.ts blob 5256cb4c58 has no such line); packages/toolkit/types/common/ contains only index.d.ts and moduleSdk.d.ts, no babel.d.ts anywhere. Upstream 860596b344 ('chore: remove tools.babel (#8107)') is an ancestor of HEAD and its stat shows it deleted babel.d.ts (80 lines) and 2 lines from index.d.ts — the merge resolution resurrected only the export line. skipLibCheck:true confirmed at packages/tsconfig/base.json:9, so the dangling re-export is masked in-repo but ships in a published .d.ts package. Direction (delete the line) re-aligns with upstream and eases merges. Major is fair for a published broken type entry.

### toolkit-docs-hygiene#2 [MAJOR/dead-code/delete] plugin-runtime src/ssr/ is a dead, broken 4-file remnant masked by a fork-added tsconfig exclude, yet shipped in dist

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/ssr/index.node.tsx:2`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/ssr/serverRender/renderToString/entry.ts:80`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/tsconfig.json:12`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/toolkit/types/server/context.d.ts:94`

**Evidence:** All four src/ssr files are A (fork-added) vs 8a744c1b31. Nothing imports them: the only repo reference to 'src/ssr' is the fork-added tsconfig exclude (`"exclude": ["src/ssr/**/*"]`), and the look-alike tests (tests/ssr/serverRender/**) actually import src/core/server/* (entry.test.ts:1-3 imports src/core/server/string/ssrData). The files cannot compile: index.node.tsx imports './serverRender', './serverRender/tracker', './utils' which don't exist; entry.ts:80 reads `SSRServerContext['metrics']` after the fork's own hunk in toolkit/types/server/context.d.ts removed `metrics?: Metrics` from BaseSSRServerContext (baseline has it at the same spot). The package's `./ssr` export maps to dist/esm/core/server, not these files — yet rslib's bundleless transpile still emits them into dist/esm/ssr/, so broken dead modules are published.

**Direction:** Delete packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/ssr/ entirely and drop the `src/ssr/**/*` exclude from tsconfig.json so the whole package typechecks again. While there, decide the BaseSSRServerContext `metrics` removal: either restore `metrics?: Metrics` (upstream still publishes it; removal is silent API breakage for downstream apps) or document it as intentional in FORK-DIVERGENCE.md.

**Verifier:** Verified on every point. All 4 src/ssr files are A vs baseline; the tsconfig diff shows `"exclude": ["src/ssr/**/*"]` is fork-added. Only repo reference to src/ssr is that exclude (src/cli/ssr is a different dir); tests/ssr/serverRender/renderToString/entry.test.ts:1-3 imports src/core/* and src/router/*. index.node.tsx imports './serverRender', './serverRender/tracker', './utils' — none exist (serverRender/ holds only types.ts, entry.ts, buildTemplate.after.ts). entry.ts:80 is exactly `public metrics: SSRServerContext['metrics'];` where SSRServerContext = BaseSSRServerContext & {...} and the fork hunk in context.d.ts replaced `metrics?: Metrics` with cacheConfig/enableUnsafeCtx/unsafeHeaders. package.json `./ssr` maps to dist/esm/core/server; dist/esm/ssr/ exists with index.node.mjs and .npmignore does not exclude dist, so it ships (though the exports map seals deep imports — the shipped modules are inert dead weight rather than executable breakage). The bundled metrics question is real: its removal from BaseSSRServerContext is a published type-surface change. Direction is sound and merge-friendly.

### toolkit-docs-hygiene#4 [MAJOR/upstream-compat/rewrite] FORK-DIVERGENCE.md toolkit/tsconfig entries are factually wrong, including a mis-tag that invites a toolchain-breaking merge resolution

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/FORK-DIVERGENCE.md:176`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/FORK-DIVERGENCE.md:171`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/FORK-DIVERGENCE.md:157`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/FORK-DIVERGENCE.md:7`

**Evidence:** (1) Lines 176-178 claim packages/tsconfig/base.json '[M] adds ignoreDeprecations: "6.0"' — the actual diff is `module: commonjs→preserve` and `moduleResolution: node→bundler`, and no ignoreDeprecations key exists in that file. The [M] tag ('prefer upstream content' per line 19/203) is dangerous: resolving toward upstream's commonjs/node10 breaks every package under the tsgo/TS7 toolchain (node10 resolution removed). This is [F]. (2) Line 173 claims utils src/cli/constants.ts is '[F] fork constants (NESTED_ROUTE_SPEC_FILE, etc.)' — the diff is pure import re-sorting; those constants exist in both versions. (3) Lines 157-159 describe runtime-utils url/loaderContext/async-storage/fileReader diffs as [F], but each is mechanical reordering; the real fork content (node/sanitize.ts, async_storage.server.worker.ts) is unmentioned. (4) Counts drifted: ledger says plugin-i18n 20 files / 539 total (line 23, line 67); actual is 21 / 540. (5) The regen command (lines 7-9) uses `git merge-base origin/main HEAD`, which exits 1 in this clone — origin/main is not fetched.

**Direction:** Rewrite the three entries: tsconfig/base.json → [F] 'preserve+bundler for tsgo; never take upstream'; utils constants.ts → [M]; runtime-utils → [M] with a note that the fork content lives in A files. Refresh counts and pin the regen command to the merge-base SHA (8a744c1b31) or add a fetch step, since the documented command currently fails.

**Verifier:** All five sub-claims verified. (1) FORK-DIVERGENCE.md:176-178 says '[M] base.json adds ignoreDeprecations: 6.0'; the actual diff is module commonjs→preserve and moduleResolution node→bundler, no ignoreDeprecations key — and the legend (line 19) says [M] = 'prefer upstream content', which would reinstate node10 resolution and break the tsgo/TS7 toolchain. (2) utils src/cli/constants.ts diff is pure import re-sorting of re-exports that exist on both sides. (3) runtime-utils M diffs are mechanical (url.ts = one removed biome-ignore comment; loaderContext/fileReader/async_storage.server = re-sorting; node/index.ts merely adds exports of fork A files node/sanitize.ts and the worker storage, which the ledger never mentions). (4) Actual counts: 540 M total, 21 plugin-i18n M files vs ledger's 539/20. (5) `git merge-base origin/main HEAD` exits 1 — origin (web-infra-dev) exists as a remote but origin/main is not fetched. FORK-DIVERGENCE.md is A (fork-owned). Direction is correct and merge-protective; major is right since the [M] mis-tag is an active landmine for the next sync.

### toolkit-docs-hygiene#3 [MINOR/dead-code/delete] async_storage.server.worker.ts has zero consumers and its fallback leaks request context across concurrent requests

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/toolkit/runtime-utils/src/universal/async_storage.server.worker.ts:19`

**Evidence:** Fork-added file (A vs baseline), 86 lines. A repo-wide grep for 'async_storage.server.worker' across all ts/mts/tsx/js/mjs/json (excluding dist/node_modules) matches only the file itself and its own build outputs — no export condition in runtime-utils package.json, no alias in rslib.config.mts (which only externalizes './async_storage.server'), no builder/app-tools wiring. Worse, if it ever were wired: createFallbackStorage (lines 19-43) is a plain stack, so when globalThis.AsyncLocalStorage is absent (Cloudflare Workers without nodejs_compat) two concurrent requests in one isolate interleave pushes/pops and getStore() returns another request's context — which carries `headers`, `request`, and `serverPayload` (lines 68-78), i.e. cross-user data leakage instead of the loud 'out of scope' error the node variant gives.

**Direction:** Delete the file. If a worker-safe storage is actually needed for the Cloudflare lane, wire it explicitly (exports condition or build alias) and replace the stack fallback with a hard throw when AsyncLocalStorage is unavailable — silently approximating per-request isolation is worse than failing.

**Verifier:** Evidence verifies: file is A vs baseline, 86 lines matching the description; repo-wide grep finds zero references; runtime-utils rslib.config.mts only externalizes './async_storage.server'; the exports map has no worker subpath (and no wildcard), so the file is unreachable even via deep import from the published package. createFallbackStorage (lines 19-43) is indeed a plain stack whose getStore() would interleave concurrent requests, and the storage shape carries headers/request/serverPayload (lines 68-78). Severity downgraded to minor: the leak is entirely hypothetical because nothing can reach the module today — actual present impact is dead code in a published dist. The 'hard throw instead of stack fallback' direction is correct if it is ever wired.

### toolkit-docs-hygiene#5 [MINOR/duplication/consolidate] Runtime-resilience benchmark measures two hand-copied clones of plugin-garfish cachePolicy, and its CI 'gate' can never fail

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/benchmark/runtime-resilience/workerLane.js:4`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/benchmark/runtime-resilience/workerLane.js:80`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/cachePolicy.ts:3`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/ultramodern-nightly.yml:76`

**Evidence:** workerLane.js contains the MF cache-policy logic (CACHE_VERSION_QUERY_KEY 'mfv', resolveVersionToken, applyVersionQuery) twice — once as plain JS (lines 4-78) and once re-inlined inside the WORKER_SCRIPT template string (lines 80-144) — both manual copies of the production implementation in plugin-garfish/src/runtime/cachePolicy.ts (fork-owned, A). Any change to the real cachePolicy silently desynchronizes the benchmark; nothing imports the production module. Additionally, the nightly job runs `--fail-on-worker-lane-gate` (root package.json:62) but the job sets `continue-on-error: true` (ultramodern-nightly.yml:76), so the gate flag is inert in CI.

**Direction:** Import the built `@modern-js/plugin-garfish` cachePolicy (or a shared CJS build of it) in both the main-thread path and the worker (load via file instead of an inlined string), deleting both copies. Either let the gate actually gate (drop continue-on-error once thresholds stabilize) or rename it from 'gate' to 'probe' in script and workflow to match reality.

**Verifier:** Duplication verified: workerLane.js lines 4-78 (plain JS) and the WORKER_SCRIPT template (lines 80+) both replicate plugin-garfish/src/runtime/cachePolicy.ts ('mfv' key, resolveVersionToken chain, applyVersionQuery) — three copies of one algorithm; the benchmark imports nothing from the production module (whose real consumers are plugin.tsx and cachePolicy.test.ts). All files A vs baseline. CI claim verified: package.json:62 passes --fail-on-worker-lane-gate but ultramodern-nightly.yml line 76 sets continue-on-error: true on the job. One correction: the workflow carries an explicit comment that the lane is 'advisory-only and intentionally excluded from the nightly signal', so the inert gate is documented intent rather than an oversight — which makes the finding's rename-to-'probe' option the right half of the proposed direction. Minor stands.

Same ledger gap applies to analyze/utils.ts's @swc/core→esbuild parseModule swap (utils.ts:46-64), which is real dependency divergence in an upstream file the ledger never mentions.

**Direction:** Add explicit ledger lines for both hunks: tag getHtmlTemplate's resolveConfiguredTemplate as [U] and PR it upstream; tag the analyze/utils.ts esbuild transform swap as [F]-coupled-dependency (same family as bff-runtime/plugin-polyfill — package.json @swc/core removal and source must be taken together on sync).

**Verifier:** Verified. Fork diff adds resolveConfiguredTemplate (file lines ~76-96) and the precedence check in the entrypoint loop (~126-135), using only the upstream AppNormalizedConfig['html']['template'] surface — no fork lanes. FORK-DIVERGENCE.md's app-tools section (lines ~134-141) tags `src/plugins/analyze/*` only as '[F] entry/routes-owner integration' with no mention of this hunk. The analyze/utils.ts claim also verifies: @swc/core transform replaced with esbuild transform in parseModule (utils.ts diff), a dependency divergence the ledger never lists. Direction (ledger lines + upstream PR for the template fix) is low-risk documentation work.

### xcut-divergence-footprint#10 [MINOR/hygiene/small-fix] rscManifest is plumbed through stream/shared.tsx via an inline intersection cast instead of the fork-extended type

**Files:** `packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/server/stream/shared.tsx:19-24`, `packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/server/stream/shared.tsx:178-180`

**Evidence:** The fork added RscManifest and put rscManifest?: RscManifest on CreateReadableStreamFromElementOptions (shared.tsx:37), but createRenderStreaming reads it off its own options via `(options as typeof options & { rscManifest?: RscManifest }).rscManifest` (shared.tsx:178-180) because the field was never added to the RenderStreaming options type the fork already modifies. The cast is a fork hunk in an upstream file that exists only to dodge declaring the field where the fork already owns the type extension.

**Direction:** Add rscManifest to the fork-extended RenderOptions/resource type (the fork already touches packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/server/shared.ts types for moduleFederationCssAssets) and delete the cast. Two-line cleanup that removes a confusing hunk from the M file.

**Verifier:** Core claim verified: shared.tsx:178-179 reads `(options as typeof options & { rscManifest?: RscManifest }).rscManifest`; RscManifest is fork-declared at :19-24 and the field is on CreateReadableStreamFromElementOptions:37; baseline stream/shared.tsx has zero rscManifest references; real producers/consumers exist (cli/template.server.ts:41, createReadableStream.worker.ts:27/45). Correction to the proposed direction: it claims the fork 'already touches packages/runtime/plugin-runtime/src/core/server/shared.ts types for moduleFederationCssAssets' — false; that file is byte-identical to baseline. The fork-extended type actually lives in packages/server/core/src/types/requestHandler.ts, where the fork adds Resource.moduleFederationCssAssets and where upstream's rscClientManifest/rscSSRManifest/rscServerManifest fields already sit on RequestHandlerOptions; adding `rscManifest?` there (1-line hunk in an already-touched upstream file) is the correct fix and still strictly better than the cast.
