## xcut-scripts-duplication  (10 confirmed)

scripts/ holds ~33k fork-added lines across ~25 families. The gate-validator tier is in decent shape: scripts/lib/validation-kit.js consolidation landed recently and 7 families use it with centrally-tested CLIs. But the runner/orchestration tier (superapp-k6, superapp-load, superapp-certification, local-control-plane, ultramodern-* proofs) never got the same treatment: ~1,500 lines of hand-rolled arg parsing in 30 files, a copy-pasted server-lifecycle stack (waitForHttp/stopChildProcess/killChild/reservePort x3), 6 duplicated run()/writeJson helpers with semantic drift, two fully dead families (~2,000 lines), ~5,100 lines of tests no runner ever executes, and one real gate-breaking constant-drift bug in contract-doctor.

### xcut-scripts-duplication#1 [MAJOR/duplication/consolidate] 30 hand-rolled parseArgs implementations (~1,495 lines), zero use of node:util parseArgs

**Files:** `scripts/superapp-k6/run-superapp-k6.js:117`, `scripts/superapp-load/run-superapp-load.js:32`, `scripts/ultramodern-publish/prepare-bleedingdev-packages.mjs:35`, `scripts/boundary-guards/check-boundary-violations.js:6`, `scripts/module-sdk-contracts/validate-module-sdk-contracts.js:10`, `scripts/mv-ci-hardening/validate-ci-hardening.js:5`, `scripts/ultramodern-production-readiness/run-published-create-proof.mjs:48`, `scripts/superapp-certification/run-superapp-certification.js:16`

**Evidence:** 30 files each define their own parseArgs with the identical for-loop/switch/'Unknown argument' shape (e.g. boundary-guards/check-boundary-violations.js:6-28 vs module-sdk-contracts/validate-module-sdk-contracts.js:10-45 vs mv-ci-hardening/validate-ci-hardening.js:5-31 are byte-near-identical patterns). Measured total: ~1,495 lines of flag parsing (superapp-k6 alone: 258 lines). The only node:util import in all of scripts/ is promisify (prepare-bleedingdev-packages.mjs:7); nothing uses the built-in util.parseArgs available since Node 18, and scripts/lib has no arg helper.

**Direction:** Add scripts/lib/cli-kit.js: a thin wrapper over node:util parseArgs encoding the shared conventions (--help, 'Unknown argument: X' error, '--flag requires a value' error, repeatable flags, env-var defaults). Port the 8 small gate-validator CLIs first — they are already covered end-to-end by scripts/lib/__tests__/validator-clis.test.js which asserts exit codes and message prefixes, so the port is regression-safe. Then port the big runners family by family. Expected deletion: ~1,000+ lines.

**Verifier:** Verified. grep finds exactly 30 parseArgs definitions across 29 files (one is the parseArgsListEnv helper in run-superapp-k6.js), all hand-rolled for-loop/switch with the 'Unknown argument: X' throw; boundary-guards:6-28, module-sdk-contracts:10-45 and mv-ci-hardening:5-31 confirmed near-identical. The only node:util import in scripts/ is promisify (prepare-bleedingdev-packages.mjs:7). My independent measurement of parseArgs function bodies sums to ~1,495 lines; superapp-k6's parseArgs is 272 lines (finding said 258 — trivial). All files are fork-added (A in diff vs 8a744c1b31). Corrections to direction: scripts/lib/__tests__/validator-clis.test.js covers SIX gate validator CLIs per its own header, not 8, and it asserts exit codes plus '[family] validation failed' prefixes, not the 'Unknown argument' path specifically — still substantial regression cover. Direction touches only fork-owned scripts, no upstream-merge impact.

### xcut-scripts-duplication#2 [MAJOR/duplication/refactor] Server-lifecycle stack copy-pasted across 3-4 families with drifting behavior (waitForHttp/stopChildProcess/killChild/reservePort/sleep/log-tail)

**Files:** `scripts/superapp-certification/production-server-controller.js:93`, `scripts/superapp-k6/run-superapp-k6.js:1862`, `scripts/superapp-local-control-plane/run-local-control-plane.js:334`, `scripts/superapp-k6/run-superapp-k6.js:1901`, `scripts/superapp-local-control-plane/run-local-control-plane.js:396`, `scripts/superapp-certification/production-server-controller.js:386`, `scripts/ultramodern-production-readiness/run-browser-smoke.mjs:266`

**Evidence:** waitForHttp is implemented 3x near-identically (production-server-controller.js:93, run-superapp-k6.js:1862, run-local-control-plane.js:334) plus a waitForTarget variant (run-browser-smoke.mjs:266); stopChildProcess+killChild 3x (run-superapp-k6.js:1901-1938, run-local-control-plane.js:396-427, production-server-controller.js:386); reservePort via net.createServer 2x (production-server-controller.js:8, run-superapp-k6.js:1828); sleep 5x; stdout/stderr tail-capture 2x. The copies have silently drifted: PROCESS_LOG_TAIL_LIMIT is 64_000 in run-superapp-k6.js:42 but 4000 in run-local-control-plane.js:18; readiness accepts status <500 in two copies but <400 in run-local-control-plane.js:362. production-server-controller.js already exists as a reusable module, and superapp-k6 already does cross-family imports from superapp-certification (artifact-schema at run-superapp-k6.js:12) — yet re-implements the entire lifecycle anyway.

**Direction:** Extract scripts/lib/process-kit.js (spawn-with-tail-capture, waitForHttp, stopChildProcess/killChild, reservePort, sleep) and make production-server-controller.js, run-superapp-k6.js, run-local-control-plane.js and run-browser-smoke.mjs consume it. Tail limits and readiness predicates become options. This also shrinks the 2,079-line run-superapp-k6.js monolith.

**Verifier:** Verified. Three near-identical waitForHttp copies confirmed (production-server-controller.js:93, run-superapp-k6.js:1862, run-local-control-plane.js:334) plus waitForTarget (run-browser-smoke.mjs:266). Drift confirmed exactly: PROCESS_LOG_TAIL_LIMIT 64_000 (run-superapp-k6.js:42) vs 4000 (run-local-control-plane.js:18); readiness status<500 in two copies vs status<400 in run-local-control-plane.js (~line 362). reservePort 2x, sleep 5x (incl. configure-bleedingdev-trusted-publishing.mjs:97), tail-capture 2x, cross-family artifact-schema import at run-superapp-k6.js:12, run-superapp-k6.js is 2,079 lines — all confirmed. One precision fix: production-server-controller.js has no named stopChildProcess; its equivalent is the structurally identical inline SIGTERM-then-SIGKILL stop() closure at ~193-212 plus killChild:386, so 'stopChildProcess+killChild 3x' is 2 named + 1 inline. The <400 readiness in the control plane may be a deliberate per-context predicate; the direction already preserves that by making it an option. All files fork-added.

### xcut-scripts-duplication#3 [MAJOR/correctness/small-fix] Contract-doctor pins TanStack Router 1.170.8 while create generates 1.170.15 — doctor fails every freshly generated workspace, and preflight gates on it

**Files:** `scripts/ultramodern-contract-doctor/run-contract-doctor.js:5`, `scripts/ultramodern-contract-doctor/run-contract-doctor.js:531`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/versions.ts:6`, `packages/toolkit/create/template-workspace/pnpm-workspace.yaml:30`, `scripts/ultramodern-preflight/run-preflight.js:154`, `scripts/ultramodern-contract-doctor/__tests__/run-contract-doctor.test.js:103`

**Evidence:** run-contract-doctor.js:5 hardcodes EXPECTED_TANSTACK_ROUTER = '1.170.8' and the tanstack-version-${id} check (lines 531-543) requires generated apps' dependencies to equal it exactly; overall doctor status fails on any failing check (line 796, exitCode at line 850). But the create generator emits 1.170.15 (packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/versions.ts:6 TANSTACK_ROUTER_VERSION = '1.170.15'; template-workspace/pnpm-workspace.yaml:30 override). run-preflight.js generates a workspace with create (line 243) then gates on doctor.status === 'pass' (lines 154-155), so 'pnpm validate:ultramodern-preflight' (package.json:57) is broken. git history: doctor pinned 1.170.8 on 2026-05-26 (b3dd9ac352); create bumped to 1.170.15 by 2026-06-06 (d0e3d468c9). The doctor's own test masks the drift because it builds fixtures from the exported constant itself (run-contract-doctor.test.js:103,134) — a circular test.

**Direction:** Stop duplicating the version constant: have run-contract-doctor.js read the expected version from the create package (require packages/toolkit/create template metadata, or parse template-workspace/pnpm-workspace.yaml) so there is one source of truth. Fix the test to assert against the create package's value, not the doctor's own constant.

**Verifier:** Core claim empirically CONFIRMED: I generated a workspace with packages/toolkit/create/bin/run.js and ran the doctor — it fails with 'tanstack-version-shell-super-app expected 1.170.8 actual 1.170.15'; run-preflight.js gates on doctor.status via the doctor-pass smoke check and 'pnpm validate:ultramodern-preflight' exits 1 (reproduced). Version constants, exact-equality check (run-contract-doctor.js:531-543), circular test (test imports EXPECTED_TANSTACK_ROUTER at lines 7/103/134), and git dates (b3dd9ac352 2026-05-26 pin; d0e3d468c9 2026-06-06 bump) all verified. Severity adjusted critical→major for two reasons: (1) the preflight lane is NOT wired into any CI workflow — it is a manual package.json script plus a docs reference, so nothing automated is blocked; (2) my run shows the doctor/preflight also fail multiple topology and full-stack checks against the current default create output (topology-remotes expects >=3 remotes, control-plane expects >=4 processes, actual 1), so the version pin is one of several breakages and the proposed single-source-of-truth fix is necessary but not sufficient to restore the gate. Direction is sound and fork-local; note the broader doctor-vs-create drift should be fixed alongside.

All fork-added. Moving to scripts/lib is fork-local; no upstream-merge concern.

### xcut-scripts-duplication#10 [MINOR/dx/rewrite] Module-system split (.mjs ESM vs .js CJS) blocks helper reuse; ESM scripts re-implement validation-kit primitives

**Files:** `scripts/ultramodern-publish/validate-source-create-proof.mjs:85`, `scripts/lib/README.md:14`, `scripts/ultramodern-production-readiness/run-published-create-proof.mjs:181`, `scripts/lib/validation-kit.js:154`

**Evidence:** Four families are ESM .mjs (ultramodern-publish 6 files, ultramodern-production-readiness 2, security, top-level tsgo-*/prepare-root) while the other ~18 are CJS .js. scripts/lib is deliberately CJS for node+bun (README.md:13-15), and zero .mjs files import it; instead they re-implement isObject/assert/assertString/readJson/writeJson (validate-source-create-proof.mjs:66-96 mirrors validation-kit's ensureObject/ensureString). Node 20 statically resolves named imports from a module.exports object literal, so `import { readJsonFile } from '../lib/validation-kit.js'` would work today — the split is convention drift, not a technical barrier. There is also no shared runner convention: some families log '[family] ...' prefixes (9 distinct prefixes found), others raw console.log/JSON.

**Direction:** Document one convention in scripts/lib/README.md (keep lib CJS for bun, state that .mjs scripts import it directly) and port the ESM scripts' duplicated primitives onto validation-kit as part of finding #6's cleanup; standardize the '[family]' log prefix in the shared CLI helper.

**Verifier:** Verified. Exactly 12 .mjs files in the four claimed groups (ultramodern-publish 6, ultramodern-production-readiness 2, security 1, top-level prepare-root/tsgo-critical/tsgo-dts 3). scripts/lib/README.md:14-15 states the plain-CommonJS node+bun rule. Zero .mjs files import validation-kit (grep confirms). validate-source-create-proof.mjs:66-96 re-implements readJson/writeJson/isObject/assert/assertString mirroring validation-kit's ensureObject/ensureString. The technical claim is correct: validation-kit.js ends in a module.exports object literal (lines 154-178), which Node's cjs-module-lexer statically resolves, so named ESM imports of it work today — the split is convention drift, not a barrier. The title's word 'blocks' is therefore slightly self-contradicted by the finding's own evidence ('would work today'), but the substantive claim (split caused re-implementation) holds. Fork-added throughout; direction is docs+fork-local consolidation, no upstream impact.

## xcut-concept-coherence  (11 confirmed)

The fork's concept vocabulary has badly outgrown its code. Real, working concepts (SuperApp fixtures, telemetry runtime-extensions, garfish trust, release-gates engine, presetUltramodern) are surrounded by ~18.5k lines of fork-owned validation scripts in which at least five distinct "certification/gate" systems overlap, several governance lanes validate only their own static JSON/markdown, and two whole script packages are referenced by nothing. The most damaging pattern is parallel sources of truth: the contract-doctor/preflight lane hardcodes a copy of the workspace generator's expectations and has already drifted (validate:ultramodern-preflight fails today with ~20 failing checks), and string-pin "migration contracts" duplicate behavior that the same profiles also test with real test commands. Config surface is similarly incoherent: trust/digest knobs are spread across three namespaces, and presetUltramodern's defaults (fail-loud telemetry against localhost exporters) are so hostile the fork's own generator has to disable them.

### xcut-concept-coherence#1 [MAJOR/architecture/consolidate] Five overlapping gate/certification systems with no shared spine

**Files:** `scripts/release-gates/validate-release-candidate-gates.js:1`, `scripts/release-gates/module-certification-profile.json`, `scripts/superapp-certification/run-superapp-certification.js:156`, `scripts/ultramodern-production-readiness/run-published-create-proof.mjs`, `scripts/mv-production-rollout/production-certification-profile.json`, `packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/contractGateAutopilot.ts`, `.github/workflows/module-certification-gates.yml:89`, `.github/workflows/release-contract-gates.yml:102`

**Evidence:** The word 'certification' alone names five different mechanisms: (1) release-gates engine with module-certification-profile.json + rc-contract-profile.json driving two near-identical workflows (module-certification-gates.yml and release-contract-gates.yml both call validate-release-candidate-gates.js then validate-gate-snapshot.js); (2) superapp-certification runner with its own smoke/release/nightly profile system (run-superapp-certification.js:156-360); (3) mv-production-rollout/production-certification-profile.json, referenced only from mv-ci-hardening-profile.json, never executed; (4) SUPERAPP_MF_CERTIFICATION/deploy-certification.test.ts and SUPERAPP_K6_CERTIFICATION env-gated test modes; (5) runtime telemetry canary contractGates + ContractGateAutopilot in server runtime-extensions. superapp-certification/generate-readiness-report.js adds separate "readiness" vocabulary.

**Direction:** Consolidate to two layers: the release-gates engine (profiles in one directory) for all static/CI gating, and superapp-certification for runtime suites. Merge module-certification-gates.yml + release-contract-gates.yml into one workflow with a profile matrix. Delete mv-production-rollout's paper profile or fold it into release-gates profiles. Rename so 'certification' means exactly one thing.

**Verifier:** Verified. Both module-certification-gates.yml (line 89/101/105) and release-contract-gates.yml (102/114/118) run validate-release-candidate-gates.js then validate-gate-snapshot.js with only the profile/evidence-dir differing. superapp-certification has its own smoke/release/nightly profile system (run-superapp-certification.js certificationCommands). mv-production-rollout/production-certification-profile.json is referenced by nothing except its own __tests__ (which is itself only named inside mv-ci-hardening-profile.json command strings that are never executed and absent from the test:scripts glob). SUPERAPP_MF_CERTIFICATION/SUPERAPP_K6_CERTIFICATION env-gated modes and ContractGateAutopilot/canary contractGates in runtime-extensions all exist. generate-readiness-report.js confirms the extra 'readiness' vocabulary. All cited files are A in the baseline diff. Proposed consolidation touches only fork-added scripts/workflows, no upstream-merge impact.

### xcut-concept-coherence#6 [MAJOR/correctness/rewrite] presetUltramodern defaults crash production boots and contradict the reverted ADR-0001; the fork's own generator overrides them

**Files:** `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/baseline.ts:101`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/baseline.ts:145`, `packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:927`, `packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/module-federation.ts:232`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/presetUltramodern.ts:10`

**Evidence:** createAppBaselineConfig defaults enableTelemetry=true, enableTelemetryExporters=true, failLoudStartup=true with exporter endpoints defaulting to http://127.0.0.1:4318 and http://127.0.0.1:8428 (baseline.ts:101-127). startupHealthCheck throws TelemetryStartupHealthError when any exporter probe fails and failLoud is set (telemetry.ts:927-928), so any presetUltramodern({}) app without a local OTLP collector AND VictoriaMetrics fails to boot. The fork's own workspace generator works around this by emitting telemetryFailLoudStartup:false (module-federation.ts:232) — the flagship preset's default is unusable without the override. Separately, baseline.ts:145-148 enables RsDoctor whenever NODE_ENV==='production', while ADR-0001 is 'Status: Reverted' with 'Current behavior is opt-in'; FORK-DIVERGENCE.md:35 already flags the stale JSDoc on the same feature. Finally presetUltramodern.ts is a pure re-export alias of withAppBaseline — two public names ('baseline' vs 'presetUltramodern') for one concept.

**Direction:** Flip exporter defaults: enableTelemetryExporters should default false (or failLoudStartup false) unless endpoints are explicitly configured; remove the generator's workaround once fixed. Decide rsdoctor production default against ADR-0001's reversal and align baseline.ts + the RsdoctorUserConfig JSDoc. Collapse the baseline/presetUltramodern duality: keep presetUltramodern as the only public name and make baseline.ts internal.

**Verifier:** Mechanics verified: createAppBaselineConfig defaults enableTelemetry/enableTelemetryExporters/telemetryFailLoudStartup all true with 127.0.0.1:4318 and :8428 endpoints; prod-server/src/server/index.ts:303 calls registry.startupHealthCheck({failLoud: telemetryConfig.failLoudStartup ?? true}) and telemetry.ts throws TelemetryStartupHealthError on any failed exporter probe, so presetUltramodern({}) without both collectors fails prod boot. Generator override telemetryFailLoudStartup:false confirmed at module-federation.ts ~232. presetUltramodern.ts is a pure alias of baseline. ADR-0001 'Status: Reverted ... Current behavior is opt-in' and FORK-DIVERGENCE.md stale-JSDoc note confirmed. Two framing corrections justify downgrade to major: fail-loud startup is deliberate, documented, opt-out-able design (not an accidental crash), and baseline enabling rsdoctor is arguably preset-level opt-in rather than a strict contradiction of the ADR (which reverted the builder-wide implicit default). The verified incoherence — the fork's flagship generator disabling its own flagship preset's default — stands. All files fork-added; direction is fork-only, though flipping defaults changes behavior for existing fork preset users.

### xcut-concept-coherence#9 [MAJOR/testing/delete] Evidence-gate layer is process theater: self-authored markdown satisfies 'minimumReviewers: 2'

**Files:** `scripts/release-gates/rc-contract-profile.json:4`, `docs/super-app-rfc-adr/evidence/release-candidate/current/review-evidence.md:1`, `docs/super-app-rfc-adr/evidence/module-certification/current/`

(review-evidence.md:1-7). The gate validates the form of review evidence while the content is written by the same agent that wrote the code, and the pinned commit_sha goes stale with every change. This is governance ceremony with no verification value, but it costs maintenance on every gate-profile change.

### xcut-concept-coherence#10 [MAJOR/architecture/refactor] Trust/digest configuration split across three config namespaces with no single source of truth

**Files:** `packages/solutions/app-tools/src/types/config/deploy.ts:17`, `packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/trust.ts:415`, `packages/server/core/src/types/config/server.ts:230`, `packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:1940`

**Evidence:** The MF trust contract (ADR-0002 §10-11) is configured in three unrelated places that must agree by hand: producer-side deploy.microFrontend.{runtimeDigest,integrity,attestation} (deploy.ts:17-27), host-side garfish remoteTrust policy with per-app attestations (trust.ts:415), and server-side server.telemetry.canary.runtimeFallbackSignal.trustPolicy.expectedRuntimeDigests/enforceRuntimeDigest (server.ts:230-236). The same runtimeDigest value lives in deploy config, runtime config, and telemetry config. More broadly, server.telemetry has absorbed a deployment control plane — canary rollout/rollback orchestration, contract-gate autopilot, signal auth, rate limiting (server.ts:180-345; telemetry.ts:1940-2215) — concerns that belong to a platform/control-plane layer, not a per-app framework config key named 'telemetry'. presetUltramodern configures only a thin slice (enabled + exporters), so there is no single coherent config story for the trust/canary surface.

**Direction:** Introduce one trust-contract config object (e.g. superApp.trust or a shared schema in @modern-js/types) that deploy, garfish runtime, and the fallback-signal endpoint all read, so a digest is declared once. Split server.telemetry into telemetry (envelopes/exporters/SLO) and a separately-named canary/rollout key — or move canary orchestration entirely into runtime-extensions config — so the name matches the concern.

**Verifier:** Verified. deploy.ts microFrontend gains fork-added runtimeDigest/integrity/attestation (confirmed as + hunks in baseline diff); plugin-garfish trust.ts (fork-added file) validates per-app attestations against host remoteTrust policy; server.ts (fork-added hunks, 26 +Telemetry type lines) defines ServerTelemetryCanaryRuntimeFallbackSignalTrustPolicyUserConfig with expectedRuntimeDigests/enforceRuntimeDigest — the same digest declared in three namespaces. server.telemetry indeed absorbs canary rollout/rollback orchestration, contract-gate autopilot, signal auth, and rate limiting (server.ts canary/autopilot keys; telemetry.ts ~1940+ instantiates TelemetryCanaryOrchestrator/ContractGateAutopilot). Direction caveat: server.ts is an upstream-modified file, so restructuring/renaming the server.telemetry surface should be additive (aliases) to limit fork-user breakage and keep the upstream diff small; vanilla Modern.js has no server.telemetry, so no vanilla behavior breaks.

### xcut-concept-coherence#11 [MINOR/dx/rewrite] Overloaded vocabulary: 'lane', 'contract gates', and 'bun superapp smoke' each mean multiple unrelated things

**Files:** `scripts/mv-lane-policy/lane-policy.json:3`, `packages/server/prod-server/src/libs/runtimeFallbackWorkerLane.ts`, `scripts/superapp-k6/README.md:3`, `.github/workflows/release-contract-gates.yml:1`, `packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/contractGateSnapshotStore.ts:311`, `.github/workflows/bun-superapp-smoke.yml:31`, `scripts/release-gates/validate-gate-snapshot.js:6`

**Evidence:** 'Lane' means: governance tiers golden/compat/experimental (mv-lane-policy), the off-main-thread worker lane (runtimeFallbackWorkerLane.ts), feature lanes in docs/FORK-DIVERGENCE ('Effect BFF lane', 'TanStack lane'), and the 'torture load lane' (superapp-k6 README:3). 'Contract gates' means both runtime canary gates (server.telemetry.canary.contractGates, snapshot store) and the CI release gate workflow (release-contract-gates.yml) — and both write/read the same `.modern/contract-gates.json` filename: in release-contract-gates.yml, validate-release-candidate-gates.js writes that snapshot (validate-release-candidate-gates.js:96) and the very next step validates the file it just wrote (validate-gate-snapshot.js), a circular check. 'Bun SuperApp Smoke' (ADR-0008) never runs a SuperApp — it only re-runs three gate validator scripts under bun (bun-superapp-smoke.yml:58-59).

**Direction:** Add a concept glossary to docs/super-app-rfc-adr/README.md and rename: 'canary gates' for runtime, 'release gates' for CI; rename bun-superapp-smoke to bun-gate-scripts-smoke or fold it into the nightly script-tests job; reserve 'lane' for the documented golden/compat/experimental policy and rename the worker lane/load lane usages. Drop the redundant validate-gate-snapshot step that re-validates a file written seconds earlier by the same module.

**Verifier:** Verified. 'Lane' overload confirmed (mv-lane-policy golden/compat/experimental tiers; runtimeFallbackWorkerLane.ts fork file; superapp-k6 README 'torture load lane'; FORK-DIVERGENCE 'Effect-first BFF lane'). Shared '.modern/contract-gates.json' filename confirmed in contractGateSnapshotStore.ts:8, validate-release-candidate-gates.js:13, validate-gate-snapshot.js:6; release-contract-gates.yml writes the snapshot then validates the just-written file in the next step (lines ~114-122) — circular as claimed, though it is a deliberate artifact-shape self-check, so dropping it is a judgment call rather than a bug fix. bun-superapp-smoke.yml runs only 'pnpm run validate:bun-smoke' = bun re-runs of module-sdk-contracts, boundary-guards, and release-gates validators with --skip-commands; no SuperApp is run. Minor line-number drift in citations (snapshot store constant at line 8 not 311) is immaterial. All fork-added; glossary/rename direction is fork-only.

## xcut-dead-features  (10 confirmed)

The fork already ran one orphan-deletion pass (commit bf7dc130e0, 2026-06-11, deleted ~5.8k LOC of superapp-soak/destroy), so the scripts/ tree is healthier than it looks — most validators are CI-wired via package.json + the fork workflows. What remains dead clusters around three patterns: (1) a resurrected pre-2024 prod-server "ModernServer" lane (~1.2k LOC) that cannot boot and is reachable only from its own unit tests, yet is institutionalized in the RC release gate; (2) "proof"/"drill" scripts that simulate everything in-process and are referenced only by one-shot evidence documents or by declaration-validators that shape-check JSON without ever executing the declared commands; (3) packages and templates (plugin-express/koa, the 131KB sandpack web-app template) maintained at real CI cost with no consumer story in the fork. About 14 of 22 scripts __tests__ directories are wired to no runner at all.

**Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/prod-server/src/server/index.ts`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/prod-server/src/server/modernServerSplit.ts:1`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/prod-server/src/libs/runtimeFallbackWorkerLane.ts`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/prod-server/tests/runtimeSignals.test.ts:12`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/server/prod-server/tests/runtimeFallbackWorkerLane.test.ts`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/scripts/release-gates/rc-contract-profile.json:406`

(src/index.ts) and './netlify'; no file in src/ imports './server' (grep returns zero); the only importer is tests/runtimeSignals.test.ts. **Files:** `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/benchmark/runtime-resilience/workerLane.js:1`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/benchmark/runtime-resilience/run.js:6`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/cachePolicy.ts:3`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/.github/workflows/ultramodern-nightly.yml:100`, `/Users/satan/side/experiments/modernjs/benchmark/runtime-resilience/__tests__`

**Direction:** Either delete benchmark/runtime-resilience and the nightly gate (it gates nothing real), or make it import applyCachePolicy from the built @modern-js/plugin-garfish dist so the gate measures shipped code, and add its __tests__ to the test:scripts glob. The proposed direction (import from built plugin-garfish dist, or delete) does not affect upstream merges since everything is fork-added.

Verify by loading the docs page; the 131KB inline string in a published package is wrong in either case.

Package is publishable (no private flag). The 'cannot run' claim is inferred rather than demonstrated by loading the page, but the requirements (mise toolchain, pnpm workspace, git-clone postinstall, Node 26) are categorically outside Sandpack/Nodebox capability, so the inference is sound. Rewrite direction matches upstream's own generate-from-template approach (fork even keeps scripts/template.mts), so no merge harm.

### xcut-security#1 [MAJOR/security/rewrite] Runtime-fallback-signal endpoint is enabled and unauthenticated by default and can flip canary/contract gates (rollback DoS)

**Files:** `packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:1989`, `packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:1517`, `packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:1571`, `packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:1722`, `packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:1839`

**Evidence:** When telemetry.canary is enabled, autopilot defaults on (canaryConfig.autopilot?.enabled ?? true) and the POST /_modern/contract-gates/runtime-fallback middleware is registered because runtimeSignalEnabled = runtimeSignalConfig?.enabled ?? true (telemetry.ts:1989). Auth defaults OFF: normalizeRuntimeFallbackSignalAuthConfig sets enabled = configured?.enabled === true (1517). The trust policy defaults to an empty allowedApps/allowedEntryOrigins list, and enforcement is skipped when those lists are empty (allowedApps check at 1665 only runs `if length > 0`, 1571-1575). So by default any unauthenticated caller can POST a payload; persistRuntimeFallbackContractGate (1839) then writes the runtime-mf-fallback-health gate as passed:false for failureHoldMs (default 5 min), which the canary autopilot consumes to hold/rollback a deploy. The only throttle is a 30/window rate limit whose source key is `${appName}@${entryOrigin}` (1722) — both fields come straight from the attacker-controlled JSON body (normalizeRuntimeSignalAppName/normalizeRuntimeSignalOrigin), so rotating appName/entry trivially resets the budget. Net: an external request can force a healthy SuperApp into a failed-gate / rollback loop. validate-github-workflows.mjs does not cover this (server code, not CI).

**Direction:** Make the signal endpoint secure-by-default: require auth (or a non-empty allowedApps/expectedRuntimeDigests allowlist) before the middleware is registered, i.e. flip runtimeSignalEnabled to default false unless auth.enabled or a trust allowlist is configured. Key the rate limiter on a server-trusted value (remote IP / authenticated identity) rather than body-supplied appName/entryOrigin.

**Verifier:** Verified end-to-end against fork-added telemetry.ts (status A). runtimeSignalEnabled = runtimeSignalConfig?.enabled ?? true (1989); autopilotEnabled ?? true (1974); auth.enabled defaults false (normalizeRuntimeFallbackSignalAuthConfig, configured?.enabled === true, 1517); trust policy defaults to empty allowedApps/allowedEntryOrigins and the allowlist checks are skipped on length===0 (1665-1685); persistRuntimeFallbackContractGate writes passed:false with expiresAt=now+failureHoldMs (default 5min, const 210) keyed on gate 'runtime-mf-fallback-health' (209); rate limiter sourceKey=`${appName}@${entryOrigin}` from body (1722), default 30/60s window (213-214). I also confirmed the impact path the finding asserts: ContractGateAutopilot.readSnapshot -> orchestrator.addRequiredContractGate(gate.name) + setContractGate(passed=false) (contractGateAutopilot.ts:98/104), so an attacker-written gate is ADDED as a required failing gate even if not originally configured, driving hold/rollback. One honest precondition the title compresses: the whole machinery only registers when telemetry.canary.enabled is true (1947) and the endpoint must be network-reachable; the evidence body does state 'When telemetry.canary is enabled'. Major is appropriate (not critical) given that opt-in gate. Proposed direction (default-off unless auth/allowlist; key rate-limit on remote identity) is fork-local and does not affect vanilla Modern.js.

### xcut-security#2 [MAJOR/security/rewrite] MF remote-trust integrity verification is TOCTOU — hashes a separate fetch, not the bytes the module-federation runtime actually executes

**Files:** `packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/trust.ts:239`, `packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/trust.ts:294`, `packages/runtime/plugin-garfish/src/runtime/trust.ts:456`

**Evidence:** enforceRemoteTrustPolicy -> verifyIntegrity does its own `fetch(app.entry)` (trust.ts:248/253), reads response.text() and SHA-digests it (294-299), then compares to the expected SRI token. But this fetch is independent of the fetch the MF/Garfish runtime uses to actually load and execute the remote entry. A malicious or compromised remote (or a network MITM on the http(s) entry) can serve benign content to this verification request and different content to the real module load — classic time-of-check/time-of-use. Unlike a browser-native SRI attribute (which the browser enforces atomically on the same response that executes), this check provides no binding guarantee to the executed code, so a deploy that advertises `deploy.microFrontend.integrity` is getting weaker assurance than the SRI-style `sha256-...` token implies (deploy.ts:19-22 markets it as 'SRI-style integrity').

**Direction:** Bind integrity to the actual asset load rather than a side-channel fetch: emit a native SRI integrity attribute on the injected remoteEntry <script>/import where the loader supports it, or have the MF runtime hash the exact bytes it loads. If a pre-fetch must remain, document it explicitly as best-effort (warn-grade) and stop presenting it as cryptographic integrity for the executed remote.

**Verifier:** Verified against fork-added trust.ts (status A). verifyIntegrity does its own fetch(app.entry) (248/253), response.text() + subtle.digest (294-299), compares to parsed SRI token (301). Call sequence confirmed in plugin.tsx: enforceRemoteTrustPolicy runs at line 130, Garfish loads/executes the entry separately via registerApp at 237 — so the hashed bytes are not bound to the executed bytes (genuine TOCTOU). deploy.ts:19 does market it as 'SRI-style integrity token'. Two honest nuances that temper but do not refute: (1) integrity verification is opt-in — verifyIntegrity defaults to true only under the 'strict' production profile (plugin.tsx:119-121); (2) the check still catches a STATIC/persistent compromise (both fetches return the same malicious bytes -> mismatch -> reported); it only fails against an adaptive attacker serving different content per request or a MITM. So it is weaker than browser-native SRI as marketed but not entirely useless. Major is defensible because the control is presented as cryptographic integrity. Proposed direction (native SRI attribute on injected script / hash exact loaded bytes, else relabel as best-effort) is fork-local.

### xcut-security#3 [MAJOR/security/refactor] PR-triggered fork CI workflows run untrusted PR code on self-hosted runners with no least-privilege permissions

**Files:** `.github/workflows/boundary-anti-patterns.yml:26`, `.github/workflows/boundary-anti-patterns.yml:45`, `.github/workflows/bun-superapp-smoke.yml:32`, `.github/workflows/module-certification-gates.yml:51`, `.github/workflows/module-certification-gates.yml:70`, `.github/workflows/release-contract-gates.yml:64`

**Evidence:** boundary-anti-patterns, bun-superapp-smoke, module-certification-gates and release-contract-gates all trigger on `pull_request` (fork PRs included), run on `${{ fromJSON(vars.SELF_LINUX_LABELS || '"ubuntu-latest"') }}` (self-hosted if the repo variable is set), have no top-level or job-level `permissions:` block (so GITHUB_TOKEN keeps repo-default scopes), use `actions/checkout` without `persist-credentials: false`, and then run `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` WITHOUT `--ignore-scripts` (boundary:45, bun:56, module-cert:70, release:83). A PR can edit package.json + lockfile + a workspace package's postinstall, so checking out and installing the PR head executes attacker-controlled lifecycle scripts. GitHub explicitly warns against running PR workflows on self-hosted runners for exactly this persistence/lateral-movement reason. scripts/security/validate-github-workflows.mjs does not flag any of this — it only enforces permissions/pinning on a hardcoded sensitiveWorkflowPaths set (validate-github-workflows.mjs:10-16) that excludes every PR-triggered workflow here.

**Direction:** Add `permissions: contents: read` (top-level) to every PR-triggered workflow, restore `--ignore-scripts` on installs in PR context (or split: an unprivileged ephemeral runner for PR jobs, self-hosted only for trusted push/dispatch), and set `persist-credentials: false`. Extend validate-github-workflows.mjs to require an explicit minimal `permissions:` block and ephemeral runners on any `pull_request`-triggered workflow, not just the sensitive allowlist.

**Verifier:** All four workflows are fork-added (status A). Verified: pull_request triggers (boundary:5, bun:5, module-cert:4, release:4); runs-on ${{ fromJSON(vars.SELF_LINUX_LABELS || '"ubuntu-latest"') }} (boundary:26, bun:32, module-cert:51, release:64); no top-level or job-level permissions block in any of the four; actions/checkout without persist-credentials:false; pnpm install --frozen-lockfile without --ignore-scripts (boundary:45, bun:56, module-cert:70, release:83). The lifecycle-script vector is real: --frozen-lockfile permits a PR that edits package.json+lockfile consistently, and pnpm still runs the project's/workspace packages' own postinstall scripts. validate-github-workflows.mjs does not cover these (sensitiveWorkflowPaths set excludes them, early return at 103-105). One correction to keep honest: for FORK PRs the GITHUB_TOKEN is automatically read-only and secrets are withheld (this is pull_request, not pull_request_target), so the 'keeps repo-default scopes' concern mainly applies to same-repo branch PRs; and the self-hosted exposure is conditional on SELF_LINUX_LABELS being set. The primary risk (untrusted code execution on self-hosted runners + install scripts) stands, and the finding already emphasizes that angle. Major holds. Proposed hardening is fork-local and good hygiene.

### xcut-security#4 [MINOR/security/small-fix] validate-github-workflows.mjs leaves unpinned third-party actions and missing-permissions outside its allowlist uncaught

**Files:** `scripts/security/validate-github-workflows.mjs:10`, `scripts/security/validate-github-workflows.mjs:103`, `.github/workflows/boundary-anti-patterns.yml:34`, `.github/workflows/bun-superapp-smoke.yml:40`, `.github/workflows/release-contract-gates.yml:72`

**Evidence:** The SHA-pinning loop (validate-github-workflows.mjs:113-122) and the requiredSensitiveTokens permission/persist-credentials/timeout/egress checks only run after the early return at line 103-105 for any path not in sensitiveWorkflowPaths (the 5 hardcoded files). Consequently jdx/mise-action@v3 (boundary:34, bun:45, module-cert:59, release:72, superapp-cert:45) and oven-sh/setup-bun@v2 (bun:40) — unpinned third-party actions in PR/dispatch-triggered workflows — pass validation. The global checks the validator does apply to ALL workflows are only `pull_request_target` and NPM_TOKEN string scans (94-101). Renovate's digest-pinning helper (enforced in renovate.json) only pins on its own future PRs, so these tags are mutable today: moving the v3 tag to malicious code executes in CI.

**Direction:** Make SHA-pin enforcement (and a minimal-permissions requirement) global for all workflows rather than gated on sensitiveWorkflowPaths; allow only an explicit local `./` exception. Pin jdx/mise-action and oven-sh/setup-bun to commit SHAs now.

**Verifier:** Verified against fork-added validate-github-workflows.mjs (status A). sensitiveWorkflowPaths is a hardcoded 5-file Set (10-16); validateWorkflow returns early at 103-105 for any path not in that set, so the SHA-pin loop (113-122) and requiredSensitiveTokens checks (107-111) never run for the PR-triggered workflows. Only pull_request_target and NPM_TOKEN/NODE_AUTH_TOKEN string scans (94-101) are global. Confirmed unpinned third-party actions pass validation: jdx/mise-action@v3 (boundary:34, bun:45, module-cert:59, release:72) and oven-sh/setup-bun@v2 (bun:40) — mutable tags. Renovate's helpers:pinGitHubActionDigests (renovate.json, enforced at validator line 71) only pins on future Renovate PRs, so existing tags remain mutable. Severity minor is honest. Proposed direction (global SHA-pin + minimal-permissions enforcement with only ./ local exception) is fork-local and does not impede upstream merges.

### xcut-security#5 [MINOR/security/small-fix] Cross-project BFF policy is a spoofable self-asserted-header gate presented as a security control

**Files:** `packages/server/bff-core/src/security/crossProjectPolicy.ts:103`, `packages/server/bff-core/src/security/crossProjectPolicy.ts:144`, `packages/server/bff-core/src/security/crossProjectPolicy.ts:242`

**Evidence:** evaluateCrossProjectPolicy lives under src/security/, returns code 'BFF_CROSS_PROJECT_POLICY_DENIED' with HTTP 403, and validates requestId/namespace/schemaHash/operationVersion entirely from caller-supplied headers: the envelope is a plaintext JSON header parsed at 144-157, namespace is derived from the envelope's own requestId (extractNamespace), and schemaHash/operationVersion come from the x-modernjs-bff-operation-context-detail header (242-260). There is no signature, HMAC, or shared-secret binding anywhere — any caller that can reach the BFF and set headers (the schemaHash is the published operation contract value, the requestId format is documented) passes the gate. ADR-0005 itself scopes this as 'Reliability and DX hardening', not authz, but the security/ placement + 403 DENIED + UNTRUSTED_SOURCE-style reasons invite reliance on it as an authorization boundary it cannot enforce.

**Direction:** Either add a real cryptographic binding (sign the envelope with a per-producer key the consumer verifies) or relabel: move out of security/, document in code that it is a contract-conformance/defense-in-depth gate, not authentication, and that perimeter network controls remain responsible for who may call the BFF.

**Verifier:** Verified against fork-added crossProjectPolicy.ts (status A). Lives in src/security/, returns code 'BFF_CROSS_PROJECT_POLICY_DENIED' (97) with DEFAULT_DENY_STATUS=403 (34). All checks read caller-supplied headers: envelope JSON parsed from header (132-157), requestId from envelope (159), namespace via extractNamespace(requestId) (172), schemaHash/operationVersion from the operation-context-detail header (242-260). grep for hmac/signature/sign/secret/crypto/timingSafe returns nothing — no cryptographic binding exists, so the core 'spoofable' claim verifies. Minor naming nit (does not refute): the actual detail header value is 'x-modernjs-bff-operation-context' (BFF_OPERATION_CONTEXT_DETAIL_HEADER, line 3-4), not the '...-detail' string the finding wrote. Caveat worth recording: this leans toward intended behavior — the finding itself concedes ADR-0005 scopes it as reliability/DX hardening, and the code never claims to authenticate, so there is no authz BYPASS of an intended control, only a labeling/misuse-invitation concern. Kept as a defensible minor hardening/clarity gap; proposed direction (relabel out of security/ or add per-producer signature) is fork-local.

### xcut-security#6 [MINOR/security/small-fix] Runtime-status endpoint discloses telemetry/canary/trust internals and uses non-constant-time token comparison

**Files:** `packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:2062`, `packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:2069`, `packages/server/runtime-extensions/src/telemetry.ts:1540`

**Evidence:** The GET /_modern/runtime/status middleware (telemetry.ts:2062) only enforces a token when runtimeFallbackSignalConfig?.auth.enabled is true (2069) — which defaults false — so by default it returns queue stats, exporter health, full canary status, and the entire runtime-fallback trust policy (allowedApps, allowedEntryOrigins, enforceRuntimeDigest, expectedRuntimeDigestsCount, window/rate-limit tuning) to any unauthenticated client (2076-2126), handing an attacker the exact config needed to craft accepted fallback signals. Separately, when auth IS enabled, enforceRuntimeFallbackSignalAuthToken compares the bearer token with `token !== authConfig.expectedValue` (1540) — a non-constant-time string compare on a static shared secret.

**Direction:** Gate the status endpoint behind auth by default (or behind the same allowlist as the signal endpoint), and reduce its default response to a health boolean unless authenticated. Replace the token equality check with crypto.timingSafeEqual over fixed-length encodings.

**Verifier:** Verified against fork-added telemetry.ts (status A). GET /_modern/runtime/status (DEFAULT_RUNTIME_STATUS_ENDPOINT, 208) only enforces a token when runtimeFallbackSignalConfig?.auth.enabled is true (2069), which defaults false (1517). Unauthenticated response (2076-2126) discloses queueStats, exporterHealth, full canary status, and trustPolicy fields (allowedApps, allowedEntryOrigins values, enforceRuntimeDigest, expectedRuntimeDigestsCount [count only, not digest values — finding states this accurately], maxSignalsPerWindow, windowMs, dedupeWindowMs). Non-constant-time compare confirmed: enforceRuntimeFallbackSignalAuthToken uses token !== authConfig.expectedValue (1540). This compounds finding #1 (when an allowlist IS configured, this leaks the values needed to forge accepted signals; when it is not, #1's signals are accepted regardless). Minor is appropriate. Proposed direction (auth-gate by default, reduce default body to a health boolean, crypto.timingSafeEqual on fixed-length encodings) is fork-local.

## gap-1-builder-toolchain-lane-packages-cli-buil  (6 confirmed)

The builder lane has one genuine correctness regression and a cluster of abandoned-experiment debris. The worst issue: the fork re-added user postcss.config loading that upstream deliberately removed in cherry-picked breaking fix #8310, and I empirically confirmed every plugin from a user's postcss.config now runs TWICE per CSS file. The rsdoctor story is internally inconsistent across four layers (reverted ADR-0001, Active DIAG-0001, default-on user docs, opt-in code), anchored by a 5-file orphan cluster in packages/builder/ that cannot compile yet is the sole thing the release-contract gate validates. The tsgo default swap works as designed but is an undocumented engine change pinned to a nightly build, and the FORK-DIVERGENCE ledger omits it entirely.

### gap-1-builder-toolchain-lane-packages-cli-buil#1 [CRITICAL/correctness/delete] User postcss.config plugins are applied twice per CSS file — fork re-added postcssrc loading that upstream deliberately removed

**Files:** `packages/cli/builder/src/plugins/postcss.ts:64-92`, `packages/cli/builder/src/plugins/postcss.ts:173`, `packages/cli/builder/src/plugins/postcss.ts:186-197`, `tests/e2e/builder/cases/postcss/postcss-config-file/index.test.ts:18-22`

**Evidence:** Upstream commit 7a278a6060 'fix(builder)!: align postcss object behavior with Rsbuild (#8310)' (cherry-picked into this fork) removed Modern.js's own loadUserPostcssrc because @rsbuild/core already loads postcss.config.* itself (verified in @rsbuild/core 2.0.11 dist: getPostcssLoaderOptions seeds postcssOptions with the loaded user config before reducing tools.postcss). Fork commit ec7abd37fa re-added a copy of loadUserPostcssrc (postcss.ts:73-92) and merges it again: mergedOptions.plugins = [...userPlugins, ...existingPlugins, ...defaults] (postcss.ts:192-196), where existingPlugins already contains the core-loaded user plugins. Empirically verified against the built dist: a marker plugin from postcss.config.cjs appears twice in the resolved postcss-loader plugins array for every CSS rule. Non-idempotent plugins (e.g. prefixers) produce wrong CSS; tailwind compiles twice on every build. The e2e assertion was simultaneously loosened from toEqual(exact CSS) to toContain (ec7abd37fa), masking the regression, and the unit snapshot suite never has a postcssrc file in scope so it cannot catch it. Bonus dead code in the same file: loadByResolver's catch branch (postcss.ts:19-27) re-resolves with the same resolver and can only rethrow the identical error.

**Direction:** Delete loadUserPostcssrc, userPostcssrcCache, clonePostCSSConfig and the userOptions merge (postcss.ts:50-92, 173, 184-197), restoring the upstream #8310 function body that only pushes the builder's default plugins into opts.postcssOptions.plugins. Keep the app-root require fallback if monorepo resolution needs it, but delete the no-op retry in loadByResolver. Restore the exact-match e2e assertion as the regression test.

**Verifier:** Verified end-to-end. Upstream #8310 (7a278a6060, in-range cherry-pick) removed loadUserPostcssrc with the explicit rationale of aligning with Rsbuild; fork commit ec7abd37fa (Petr Glaser) re-added it plus the [...userPlugins, ...existingPlugins, ...defaults] merge at postcss.ts:186-197. @rsbuild/core 2.0.11 dist (756.js getPostcssLoaderOptions) confirms core seeds postcssOptions with the postcssrc-loaded user config before reducing tools.postcss, so existingPlugins already contains the user plugins. Independently reproduced against the built dist (dist/esm-node): a marker plugin from postcss.config.cjs appears twice in the resolved postcss-loader plugins for the CSS rule. The e2e assertion loosening (toEqual exact CSS -> toContain) happened in the same commit ec7abd37fa — verified in the diff. loadByResolver's catch branch is indeed a no-op retry (resolve+require repeats the identical failure). Proposed direction restores upstream #8310 behavior, so it eases rather than hardens upstream merges. Critical stands: silent double-application for every app with a postcss config — non-idempotent plugins (prefix-wrappers, rtl flippers) emit wrong CSS, everyone else pays 2x postcss work, and the regression test was loosened to mask it.

### gap-1-builder-toolchain-lane-packages-cli-buil#2 [MAJOR/dead-code/delete] Orphan packages/builder cluster (5 files, ~957 lines): cannot compile, invisible to the workspace, exists only to green-light a release gate

**Files:** `packages/builder/builder/src/plugins/performance.ts:1-7`, `packages/builder/builder-shared/src/schema/performance.ts`, `packages/builder/builder-shared/src/types/config/performance.ts`, `packages/builder/builder/rstest.config.mts`, `packages/builder/builder/tests/plugins/performance.test.ts`, `scripts/release-gates/rc-contract-profile.json:288-291`

**Evidence:** Root cause confirmed as the 'resurrected legacy layout' pattern: upstream deleted the entire legacy builder tree in 4df6c876aa 'chore: remove all legacy builder packages (#5408)' long before the v3.2.1 baseline (git ls-tree 8a744c1b31 packages/builder/ is empty), yet fork commit 4ec9133799 'feat(builder): backport rsdoctor default-on diagnostics' (2026-02-21) wrote new code into the v2-era paths, and later toolchain commits (a60ed49587 added rstest.config.mts, 5585d66e88) kept grooming it. The cluster cannot build: performance.ts:1-7 imports '@modern-js/builder-shared' which exists nowhere (zero pnpm-lock.yaml entries, no node_modules dir), and the two builder-shared files import '../types', '../utils', '../zod' which are not on disk. Neither directory has a package.json, so the pnpm-workspace.yaml 'packages/builder/*' glob ignores them and no nx/CI target can run the test. Its isRsdoctorEnabled/getRsdoctorPluginOptions (performance.ts:157-188) duplicate the live packages/cli/builder/src/plugins/rsdoctor.ts:4-29. The ONLY repo reference is rc-contract-profile.json target 'rsdoctor-artifact-contract' pointing at the dead file. 12 sibling empty dirs (builder-cli, builder-webpack-provider, ...) also linger untracked on disk.

**Direction:** Delete all 5 tracked files and the empty packages/builder/* directories, and remove or retarget the 'rsdoctor-artifact-contract' entry in scripts/release-gates/rc-contract-profile.json (see separate finding). If RSDOCTOR_DIAGNOSTICS_CONTRACT_FILE behavior is still wanted, it belongs in packages/cli/builder/src/plugins/rsdoctor.ts.

**Verifier:** Core verified, but three evidence claims need correction. Verified: all 5 files are fork-added into a tree empty at baseline (git ls-tree 8a744c1b31 packages/builder/ is empty; upstream removed it in 4df6c876aa); 4ec9133799 was committed on a pre-removal v2-era base (its tree still contains packages/builder/builder/package.json, which the later merge f3c61a9888 dropped) — the resurrected-legacy-layout root cause holds; '@modern-js/builder-shared' has zero pnpm-lock entries; builder-shared files import ../utils, ../zod, ../../../compiled/webpack-bundle-analyzer which do not exist; no package.json in either dir so the pnpm workspace glob skips them; builderPluginPerformance has zero live importers; isRsdoctorEnabled/getRsdoctorPluginOptions duplicate packages/cli/builder/src/plugins/rsdoctor.ts; rc-contract-profile.json:288-294 points at the dead file. CORRECTIONS: (1) 'invisible to the workspace / no nx/CI target can run the test' is wrong — root rstest.config.mts has projects: ['packages/**/rstest.config.{ts,mts}'], which discovers packages/builder/builder/rstest.config.mts, and ut-macOS.yml/ut-Windows.yml run `pnpm run test:ut`; I executed the suite and it PASSES, because performance.ts's broken imports are all type-only and erased at runtime. (2) 'cannot compile/cannot build' holds only for tsc/publish — nothing type-checks or builds it, but the code executes fine under rstest. (3) 10 empty sibling dirs, not 12 (12 total including builder/ and builder-shared/). Net effect is arguably worse than claimed: dead code that consumes CI time and produces false green. Deletion direction is sound and merge-friendly (upstream already deleted the tree); deleting the files also removes the rstest discovery automatically.

### gap-1-builder-toolchain-lane-packages-cli-buil#3 [MAJOR/correctness/rewrite] DIAG-0001 diagnostics contract is vaporware: CI release gate validates substrings in the dead orphan file while the live plugin never writes the artifact

**Files:** `scripts/release-gates/rc-contract-profile.json:288-291`, `scripts/release-gates/validator.js:204-260`, `packages/cli/builder/src/plugins/rsdoctor.ts:40-51`, `docs/super-app-rfc-adr/DIAG-0001-rsdoctor-diagnostics-contract.md`

**Evidence:** DIAG-0001 (Status: Active) promises a machine-readable '.rsdoctor/ultramodern-diagnostics.json' artifact for agents, and .github/workflows/release-contract-gates.yml:102 runs validate-release-candidate-gates.js against rc-contract-profile.json whose target 'rsdoctor-artifact-contract' points at packages/builder/builder/src/plugins/performance.ts with includes ['ultramodern-diagnostics.json', '.rsdoctor']. validateMigrationContracts only checks the file exists and contains those substrings — so the gate passes forever on a file that cannot compile and never executes. The live plugin (cli/builder/src/plugins/rsdoctor.ts) registers RsdoctorRspackPlugin but contains no contract writer; grep confirms 'ultramodern-diagnostics' has zero consumers outside the orphan file, docs, and the gate profile. DIAG-0001 also still asserts 'RsDoctor still defaults on in production', contradicting its own dependency ADR-0001 (Status: Reverted) and tests/rsdoctor.test.ts which asserts default-off. The live RsdoctorUserConfig (types.ts:117-130) also lacks the reportDir/mode/loaderInterceptorOptions surface DIAG-0001 specifies.

**Direction:** Decide the contract's fate explicitly: either (a) implement the ~40-line done-hook contract writer inside packages/cli/builder/src/plugins/rsdoctor.ts and point the gate at that file plus an integration test that asserts the artifact exists, or (b) mark DIAG-0001 Reverted alongside ADR-0001 and delete the gate target. Either way the gate must stop validating a dead file.

**Verifier:** Verified. release-contract-gates.yml:102/114 runs validate-release-candidate-gates.js with rc-contract-profile.json; validateMigrationContracts (validator.js:204+) only does fs.existsSync + content.includes(snippet), so 'rsdoctor-artifact-contract' (profile lines 288-294: path packages/builder/builder/src/plugins/performance.ts, includes 'ultramodern-diagnostics.json', '.rsdoctor', 'schemaVersion: 1') passes forever against source text. The live pluginRsdoctor (cli/builder/src/plugins/rsdoctor.ts, 51 lines) only registers RsdoctorRspackPlugin — no contract writer; grep confirms 'ultramodern-diagnostics' appears only in the orphan file, two ADR docs, two orphan builder-doc pages, and the gate profile — no live producer or consumer. DIAG-0001 line 3 'Status: Active' and line 58 'RsDoctor still defaults on in production' contradict ADR-0001 'Status: Reverted (a210ac658d)' and tests/rsdoctor.test.ts which asserts default-off in both modes. RsdoctorUserConfig (types.ts) only has enabled/disableClientServer — no reportDir/mode/loaderInterceptorOptions. One nuance: 'never executes' should read 'never executes in a real build' — the orphan file's contract-writer functions do run inside the CI unit test (root rstest glob), which only deepens the false-green problem. All cited files fork-added (scripts/release-gates and docs/super-app-rfc-adr absent at baseline). Direction (implement-or-revert, stop validating the dead file) is sound.

### gap-1-builder-toolchain-lane-packages-cli-buil#4 [MINOR/hygiene/consolidate] rsdoctor 'default on in production' still claimed in shipped user docs and JSDoc after the ADR-0001 revert; heavy plugin remains a hard prod dependency of an opt-in feature

**Files:** `packages/document/docs/en/configure/app/performance/rsdoctor.mdx:17`, `packages/cli/builder/src/types.ts:120-123`, `packages/cli/builder/package.json:43`, `packages/cli/builder/tests/default.test.ts:43-47`, `packages/document/builder-doc/docs/en/config/performance/rsdoctor.md`

**Evidence:** Post-revert behavior is opt-in (pluginRsdoctor returns null for undefined config, rsdoctor.test.ts asserts off in dev AND prod; only app-tools withAppBaseline opts in for NODE_ENV=production at baseline.ts:145). But the live doc page states 'Default: true in production build, false in development mode' (rsdoctor.mdx:17), the RsdoctorUserConfig JSDoc repeats the same claim (types.ts:122, already flagged stale in FORK-DIVERGENCE.md), and two orphan builder-doc pages (in a doc package that does not exist at baseline — git ls-tree 8a744c1b31 has no packages/document/builder-doc — and which sits outside the rspress root 'docs/') document reportDir/mode/loaderInterceptorOptions options the live type does not accept. tests/default.test.ts:43-47 etc. carry vestigial 'rsdoctor: false' opt-outs left over from the default-on era that are now no-ops. Meanwhile '@rsdoctor/rspack-plugin' ^1.5.12 sits in dependencies (package.json:43), so every consumer installs it even though the feature is opt-in and dynamically imported. Similarly orphaned: packages/document/main-doc/docs/{en,zh}/guides/get-started/ultramodern.mdx are stale, diverged duplicates of the live pages under packages/document/docs/.

**Direction:** Fix rsdoctor.mdx (en+zh) and the types.ts JSDoc to state opt-in default (on only via withAppBaseline in production); delete the four orphan doc files under packages/document/builder-doc and packages/document/main-doc; drop the vestigial rsdoctor:false config from default.test.ts; move @rsdoctor/rspack-plugin to optionalDependencies or peerDependenciesMeta-optional with a clear install error.

**Verifier:** Verified. rsdoctor.mdx states 'Default: true in production build, false in development mode' while pluginRsdoctor returns null for undefined config and rsdoctor.test.ts asserts default-off in dev and prod; only app-tools baseline.ts:145 opts in. types.ts RsdoctorUserConfig JSDoc repeats the stale default-on claim (and FORK-DIVERGENCE.md indeed already flags it as stale). @rsdoctor/rspack-plugin ^1.5.12 is in dependencies (package.json line 48, not 43 — trivial citation slip) despite being opt-in and dynamically imported. packages/document/builder-doc and main-doc are absent at baseline (ls-tree confirms only docs/), sit outside the rspress root (root: docs), and the builder-doc pages document reportDir/mode/loaderInterceptorOptions the live type lacks; main-doc/docs/{en,zh}/guides/get-started/ultramodern.mdx exist and diverge from the live pages (diff confirms). default.test.ts:44-46 carries a now-no-op rsdoctor:false. All fork-owned. Direction is fine; only caveat: moving @rsdoctor/rspack-plugin to optional/peer changes consumer install behavior and needs the clear runtime error it proposes.

### gap-1-builder-toolchain-lane-packages-cli-buil#5 [MINOR/upstream-compat/refactor] tsgo type-checking default silently swaps the engine for every app and ships a pinned nightly compiler as a hard prod dependency, with no user-facing documentation

**Files:** `packages/cli/builder/src/shared/tsgo.ts:26-53`, `packages/cli/builder/src/shared/parseCommonConfig.ts:242-252`, `packages/cli/builder/package.json:49`

**Evidence:** Type checking is on by default (disableTsChecker undefined at parseCommonConfig.ts:66, plugin pushed at :242), and withTsgoDefaults prepends {typescript:{tsgo:true, typescriptPath}} to every user chain (tsgo.ts:37-38), so all existing apps silently move from tsc to the TypeScript-Go preview — whose diagnostics differ from tsc and whose unsupported tsconfig options can change CI results. The fallback path hard-pins '@typescript/native-preview' 7.0.0-dev.20260610.1 (a daily dev snapshot) in dependencies (package.json:49), adding ~3MB JS + ~28MB platform binary to every consumer install; and because tryResolve prefers the project's own copy (tsgo.ts:30-31), two machines can type-check with different nightly compilers depending on local installs. The opt-out (tools.tsChecker.typescript.tsgo:false, handled at tsgo.ts:40-51) appears nowhere in packages/document user docs (grep for 'tsgo' in main docs: zero hits). The opt-out logic itself is sound: @rsbuild/plugin-type-check 1.4.0 reduces the chain with deepmerge (verified in dist), matching the ts-deepmerge mirror in tests/tsgo.test.ts. Note the plugin already natively resolves the project's native-preview when tsgo:true, so the fork wrapper's only real value is the builder-bundled fallback.

**Direction:** Document the default and the opt-out in the user docs; centralize the nightly pin (catalog or single constant) and prefer a stable dist-tag once TS7 has one; consider demoting the builder-bundled fallback to an optionalDependency with a clear 'install @typescript/native-preview or set tsgo:false' error, which removes ~30MB from default installs while keeping the tsgo-by-default bet.

**Verifier:** Partially REFUTED — the 'no user-facing documentation' prong is false. packages/document/docs/{en,zh}/configure/app/tools/ts-checker.mdx (commit 2e773c6418 'feat(builder): support TypeScript Go checks (#8673)', 2026-06-10, i.e. before this audit) has a full 'TypeScript Go by Default' section documenting the default, the typescriptPath resolution to @typescript/native-preview, the bundled fallback, and the tsgo:false opt-out with a code sample — the investigator's 'grep for tsgo in main docs: zero hits' is simply wrong. What DOES verify, all fork-owned (tsgo.ts added in 4ffb0c8a89, 2026-06-11): type-check on by default (parseCommonConfig.ts:66/242-252), withTsgoDefaults prepends {typescript:{tsgo:true, typescriptPath}} to every user chain (tsgo.ts:37-38), tryResolve prefers the project's own native-preview (tsgo.ts:30-31) so machines can check with different nightlies, and '@typescript/native-preview': '7.0.0-dev.20260610.1' is a hard dependency (package.json:49; verified 3.0MB JS + 28MB darwin-arm64 binary in node_modules). The opt-out merge soundness claim also checks out. Kept as minor: the engine swap is the fork's deliberate, changeset-and-doc-documented headline bet (also covered by FORK-DIVERGENCE [F] tsgo toolchain framing), so the actionable residue is dependency hygiene — the daily-snapshot pin, install weight, and version-drift between bundled and project copies. Direction item 'document the default and opt-out' is already done; demoting to optionalDependency would contradict the doc's 'ships with the builder, no extra install needed' promise and needs that doc updated in the same change.

### gap-1-builder-toolchain-lane-packages-cli-buil#6 [MINOR/hygiene/small-fix] FORK-DIVERGENCE ledger omits the builder lane's two biggest divergences and mislabels the postcss change as upstreamable

FORK-DIVERGENCE.md 'builder (19 files)' section names neither src/shared/tsgo.ts, src/plugins/rsdoctor.ts (only the config surface in createBuilder/parseCommonConfig/types is mentioned), tests/{tsgo,rsdoctor}.test.ts, nor package.json; the diff shows 23 changed files under packages/cli/builder (recounted: exactly 23). The postcss entry reads '[U] resolves postcss/tailwind plugins from the app root...' — confirmed it omits the dominant loadUserPostcssrc/merge hunk (the #8310 reversion proven buggy in the duplication finding) and overstates the resolver (builderRequire is tried first, postcss.ts:29-33, so app root is a fallback). grep confirms zero 'packages/builder' orphan-cluster entry anywhere in the ledger, while Appendix A keep-deleted entries exist at lines 182-197 as the template to mirror. FORK-DIVERGENCE.md is fork-added (status A). ## gap-2-published-docs-site-packages-document-co  (9 confirmed)

The published docs site (packages/document, deployed by .github/workflows/docs-pages.yml) has good en/zh parity and mostly-real config surfaces, but it systematically documents behavior the fork does not deliver: every CLI example was renamed to a nonexistent `ultramodern` binary across ~37 files, the rsdoctor pages still document the reverted ADR-0001 default-on behavior, and the BFF/data-platform pages promise trace continuity, a working `.client`, and server-side cross-project enforcement that verified findings show are broken. Separately, four files were committed under a v2-era main-doc/builder-doc layout that the rspress build never reads, creating diverged double-maintenance copies, and one fork-added root script is dead-on-arrival.

### gap-2-published-docs-site-packages-document-co#1 [MAJOR/correctness/rewrite] Docs rebrand every CLI command to a nonexistent `ultramodern` binary across ~37 published pages

**Files:** `packages/document/docs/en/apis/app/commands.mdx`, `packages/document/docs/zh/apis/app/commands.mdx`, `packages/document/docs/en/components/serve-command.mdx`, `packages/document/docs/en/components/debug-app.mdx`, `packages/document/docs/en/components/build-output.mdx`, `packages/document/docs/en/configure/app/usage.mdx`, `packages/solutions/app-tools/package.json:78`

**Evidence:** The fork-modified hunks rename every command example (`modern dev` -> `ultramodern dev`, `npx modern new` -> `npx ultramodern new`, `Usage: ultramodern build [options]`, anchor `#ultramodern-build`) across 37 en+zh files (grep for 'ultramodern dev|npx ultramodern|ultramodern build' over packages/document/docs). But no package defines an `ultramodern` bin: app-tools/package.json:78-80 ships only `modern`/`modern-app`, and generated workspaces' scripts are `dev: 'modern dev'`, `serve: 'modern serve'` (packages/toolkit/create/src/ultramodern-workspace/package-json.ts:405-417). docs/en/components/debug-app.mdx even claims `pnpm run dev` prints `> ultramodern dev`, which the generated apps never print. Every copy-pasted command from the published site fails.

**Direction:** Pick one: (a) add `"ultramodern": "./bin/modern.js"` as a bin alias in @modern-js/app-tools and update generated workspace scripts/output to match, or (b) mass-revert the command rename in docs back to `modern ...`. Option (b) also shrinks divergence on 30+ upstream M files, making future upstream doc merges near-conflict-free; the rebrand can live in prose, not in command names.

**Verifier:** Verified. 36 (not 37 — immaterial) en+zh doc files under packages/document/docs use `ultramodern dev/build/new/serve`; the rename hunks are fork-added (confirmed via diff of en commands.mdx, debug-app.mdx). No package in the repo defines an `ultramodern` bin: app-tools/package.json:78-80 ships only `modern`/`modern-app`, toolkit/create ships `create`/`modern-js-create`, and generated workspace scripts are `dev: 'modern dev'`, `serve: 'modern serve'` (create/src/ultramodern-workspace/package-json.ts:405,418). debug-app.mdx:5 indeed claims `pnpm run dev` prints `> ultramodern dev`, which generated apps never print. quick-start.mdx:51 also advertises `ultramodern dev`/`ultramodern build`. `npx ultramodern new` would resolve to an unrelated/nonexistent npm package — arguably worse than failing. Proposed direction (b) is the safer one for upstream mergeability; direction (a) would also work without breaking vanilla since it only adds a bin alias.

### gap-2-published-docs-site-packages-document-co#3 [MAJOR/correctness/rewrite] rsdoctor docs (config page, debug guide, ultramodern diff page) document the reverted ADR-0001 default-on behavior

**Files:** `packages/document/docs/en/configure/app/performance/rsdoctor.mdx:17`, `packages/document/docs/en/guides/basic-features/debug/rsdoctor.mdx`, `packages/document/docs/en/guides/get-started/ultramodern.mdx:33`, `packages/cli/builder/src/plugins/rsdoctor.ts:4`, `packages/cli/builder/src/types.ts:122`

Code disagrees: pluginRsdoctor returns null when performance.rsdoctor is undefined (packages/cli/builder/src/plugins/rsdoctor.ts:4-7,34-36), and ADR-0001 is marked 'Status: Reverted (2026-04-20, commit a210ac658d)' with rsdoctor.test.ts asserting default-off. Only the opt-in presetUltramodern/withAppBaseline path enables it in production (app-tools/src/baseline.ts:145). The stale JSDoc at builder/src/types.ts:122 ('By default, Modern.js enables Rsdoctor in production builds') repeats the same lie. zh pages mirror all three claims. Fix the types.ts:122 JSDoc in the same change.

Code: pluginRsdoctor returns null when performance.rsdoctor is undefined (builder/src/plugins/rsdoctor.ts:4-7,34-36, fork-added file), so default is off; ADR-0001 is 'Status: Reverted (2026-04-20, commit a210ac658d)'. The stale JSDoc 'By default, Modern.js enables Rsdoctor in production builds' at builder/src/types.ts:~122 is in the fork-added RsdoctorUserConfig hunk. baseline.ts:145 confirms only the preset path enables it. ### gap-2-published-docs-site-packages-document-co#4 [MAJOR/correctness/rewrite] frameworks.mdx canonical browser example uses `effectBff.client.*`, which defineEffectBff hardcodes to undefined

**Files:** `packages/document/docs/en/guides/advanced-features/bff/frameworks.mdx:108`, `packages/document/docs/zh/guides/advanced-features/bff/frameworks.mdx:105`, `packages/cli/plugin-bff/src/runtime/effect/handler.ts:731`

**Evidence:** The fork-added section 'Call Effect endpoints from the browser via `@api/effect/index`' (frameworks.mdx:108-114) shows `import effectBff from '@api/effect/index'; await effectBff.client.hello.ping({})` as the canonical usage. Cross-referencing the verified finding: defineEffectBff returns `const client = undefined as unknown as EffectApiPromiseClientFromApi<TApi>` (handler.ts:731-737) — a type-level lie. Anywhere the import resolves to the real module (SSR of that route, node tests, server code copying the doc pattern) `effectBff.client` is undefined and the call throws. The doc presents this as the framework's primary type-safe client story on both en and zh pages, with no caveat about which contexts resolve to the generated client.

**Direction:** Until the client-undefined defect is fixed, change the doc example to the import path that actually yields a working client, and document explicitly that the defineEffectBff return value's `client` is browser-bundle-only (or remove `.client` from the server return type entirely, per the lane-owning area's fix). Docs and the defineEffectBff fix should land together.

**Verifier:** Verified. The fork-added section at en frameworks.mdx:108-114 and zh:108-113 shows `import effectBff from '@api/effect/index'; await effectBff.client.hello.ping({})` as the canonical pattern (confirmed the hunk is fork-added via diff). handler.ts:731 (fork-added file) has `const client = undefined as unknown as EffectApiPromiseClientFromApi<TApi>` returned as `client`. Combined with the brief's confirmed finding that defineEffectBff's client is undefined at runtime, any context resolving the real module (SSR, node tests, server code) throws on `.client.hello`. The doc presents this with no caveat about bundle-context-dependent resolution. Direction is sound and tied to the lane-owning fix; no upstream impact (entire Effect lane is fork-only).

### gap-2-published-docs-site-packages-document-co#5 [MAJOR/security/rewrite] data-platform.mdx and effect.mdx promise trace continuity and server-boundary enforcement that the shipped client/policy do not deliver

**Files:** `packages/document/docs/en/guides/advanced-features/bff/data-platform.mdx:16`, `packages/document/docs/en/guides/advanced-features/bff/data-platform.mdx:58`, `packages/document/docs/en/guides/advanced-features/bff/data-platform.mdx:86`, `packages/document/docs/en/configure/app/bff/effect.mdx:19`, `packages/document/docs/zh/guides/advanced-features/bff/data-platform.mdx`

**Evidence:** data-platform.mdx sells: 'Trace context continuity for distributed observability' (line 16), envelope 'includes ... optional trace context' (lines 64-70), and 'Trace continuity: batch gateway forwards traceparent from item headers or envelope metadata' (line 86). The verified finding is that the generated Effect client silently drops operationContext/traceparent/locale, so the documented continuity chain is broken at its first link. Worse, effect.mdx:19,107 documents `requireTraceContext` — a user enabling it per the docs would have the server reject requests from the framework's own generated client (handler.ts:547 rejects envelopes without traceparent). The page also frames `validateOrigin`/`expectedNamespace` as 'strict envelope enforcement at the server boundary' (lines 14, 58, 111-118), but per the verified cross-project finding the policy is client-asserted: both the envelope header and the Origin value it is checked against originate from the caller, so this is hygiene, not a trust boundary — yet 'Recommended policy' presents it as the production security posture.

**Direction:** Re-scope both pages (en+zh) to actual guarantees: state that envelope validation is an integrity/correctness aid for cooperating clients, not authentication or tenant isolation; remove or caveat `requireTraceContext` and the trace-continuity bullets until the client forwards traceparent; re-sync once the client-side dropping bug is fixed.

**Verifier:** Verified with one citation correction. All cited doc lines check out: data-platform.mdx:16 'Trace context continuity', :14 'strict envelope enforcement at the server boundary', :58 validateOrigin vs incoming Origin header, :62-70 envelope 'optional trace context', :86 'Trace continuity: batch gateway forwards traceparent', :111-118 'Recommended policy' with validateOrigin/expectedNamespace; effect.mdx:19,107 document requireTraceContext. Correction: the requireTraceContext rejection is at runtime/data-platform/index.ts:547 (`if (options.requireTraceContext && !envelope.traceparent)`), not handler.ts:547. The client-asserted-policy and traceparent-dropping premises are the brief's confirmed findings; consistent with what I saw (getExpectedEnvelopeOrigin in handler.ts:508-514 trusts the caller's Origin header; generated client only embeds static config traceContext, not per-request traceparent). All files A-status fork additions. Security categorization is apt: the doc frames client-asserted hygiene as a production trust boundary.

## gap-3-unaudited-mid-size-fork-hunks-in-upstrea  (8 confirmed)

The four packages split cleanly: toolkit/plugin and the six "mechanical" packages are genuinely toolchain-only churn (the one semantic toolkit/plugin change — createConfigOptions honoring internalPlugins with duplicate detection — is sound and tested; no new hook names were added there, so the registryHooks/hoc issues are not rooted in this package). server/utils' tsgo rewrite does still honor tsconfig paths (post-emit matcher, covered by tests) but ships two real hazards: an undeclared runtime dependency on @typescript/native-preview and a fragile regex-based post-emit specifier rewriter. The deleted upstream typescriptLoader.ts resolved `typescript` with `paths: [appDirectory]` and threw a friendly install hint. Document the requirement in the BFF guide.

But the evidence contains a factual error: packages/cli/builder/package.json declares @typescript/native-preview as a REGULAR dependency ('dependencies': '7.0.0-dev.20260610.1'), and app-tools depends on @modern-js/builder — so the package is present in every app-tools install tree and resolves under default pnpm hoisting (node_modules/.pnpm/node_modules) and npm/yarn flat installs, independent of whether the fork's create tool generated the workspace. The real residual breakage is limited to strict-isolation installs (pnpm hoist=false / hoist-pattern overrides, Yarn PnP), plus the lost actionable error message. Downgraded major→minor for the narrowed blast radius. Proposed direction (declare the dep on server-utils itself or peer + paths-based resolve with friendly error) remains correct and is fork-local (this whole compile path is a fork rewrite; vanilla uses the typescript package).
